# DECISIONS (nfl4)

Each entry: ID, date, decision, reason, what would show it wrong (test or phase). Standing owner decisions are copied here once so the game folder is self-contained.

## Standing decisions (apply to every game; never asked again)
- **D-001 (2026-10-12) Original wavs ship in the repo and installer on the owner's own responsibility** (CLAUDE.md section 10). Copy only the wavs into the clone's assets, hash-checked against `original/`. README follows section 10 wording (Q-017 for the permission sentence). Rights holder can ask for removal.
- **D-002 (2026-10-13) Rules 0.9 to 0.11 apply.** Start the clone from the starter kit (`shared\starter-kit`); fixes are recorded once in `fixesforgames.md`; clean uninstall and clean exit; sound math with `audioop-lts`, no numpy unless the user chooses it (propose in S6.1 if the game needs it: this game's sounds are plain wav playback with volume, pan, frequency, so none is expected); audio output stays the pygame-ce mixer (B-A8). (P6 S6.1, 2026-10-14: confirmed, no numpy and no proposal, D-036.)
- **D-003 (2026-10-13) "Sounds start right away" is never the default** (B-S16): the default is the original's wait for the spoken prompt; the player may switch it in the settings.
- **D-004 (2026-10-13) JAWS support: yes** (CLAUDE.md section 1). The clone gets the JAWS backend from the kit (7.3.1).

## This game
- **D-005 (2026-10-13) The original is not run; desk work only.** Reason: `dx8vb.dll` and the DirectX 8 class IDs are not registered on this PC (Q-005), and the Windows Sandbox is not available on Windows 11 Home (ENVIRONMENT.md). Installing a 2001 DirectX runtime needs administrator rights and is not needed to read the code. Behavior comes from the recovered code (P3), the readme and the exe's help strings, and listening to the clone. Wrong if: a question in P4 cannot be settled from code (then plan a VM with the owner, 6.1).
- **D-006 (2026-10-13) The clone does not launch `gamemenu.exe`.** It is another program (the Kitchensinc game menu, its own project, 0.2 and 6.5); the clone's exit just ends the program (0.10). Wrong if: P3 shows the exit path does something the player relies on (Q-013).
- **D-007 (2026-10-13) Clone name.** Working title for the 0.5.1 format: "NFL Football 4 by Josh Kennedy, inspired by Jim Kitchen" (the original says "NFL football By Jim Kitchen" and speaks "Jim's N. F. L. Football, version 4"). Folder and Python package `nfl4`; repo name `nfl-football-4-audio-game` (B-R6 pattern `<game>-audio-game`). Original strings go in STRINGS.md next to the new ones. Cheap to change until S7.1.
- **D-008 (2026-10-13) Personal data.** Jim's e-mail, phone number and town (football.txt and probably the exe) never go into the clone, README or spec that is published (B-R2); the clone's help text points to the repository instead. The roster surnames stay as the original data until the owner decides otherwise (Q-016).
- **D-009 (2026-10-13) Layout.** The original files were moved (not copied) from the game folder root into `original\` after hashing them; a second hash after the move was identical (`spec\ORIGINAL_SHA256.txt`); the read-only attribute is set. No loose duplicates remain. `work\football.exe` is the working copy for tools. A Python 3.10 `-I` run is used for the analysis tools (ENVIRONMENT.md).
- **D-010 (2026-10-13) Recovery strategy for P3** (455 KB of native code, 11 times Brain): see PROJECT_STATE.md "Plan for Phases 2 and 3". In short: map method and event tables first; export every procedure with Ghidra (`ExportVB6.java`) into `work\ghidra\`; condense with `vb6_asm_condense.py`; read by feature (menus and start-up, keys, play engine, commentary, season, files), not by file order; write the recovered logic into the spec as it is read; split the two giant procedures by statement counters and GoSub blocks; build a small VB6 pseudo-code lifter (`tools\vb6_lift.py`) in S3.1 if the first batch shows the reading volume is too large for hand reading. Wrong if: S3.1's batch timing shows more than about 25 batches.
- **D-011 (2026-10-13) Ghidra project folders live outside OneDrive and outside any folder that starts with a dot** (`C:\Users\joshk\kitchensinc-ghidra\<slug>`): Ghidra refuses a path element that starts with ".", and `.kitchensinc-builds` is such a folder (fixesforgames B-T13).
- **D-012 (2026-10-13) The assembly export is the gap-filled one.** Ghidra's headless export left 35,883 bytes of p_43a860 (two runs: 0x446e29 to 0x44dfda and 0x451b54 to 0x4533d7) and 2 to 3 short stretches (50 to 130 bytes) in every other procedure undisassembled (found when 700 strings had no code reference). `tools\asm_fill_gaps.py` (Capstone linear sweep of every byte not in the export, marked `; [fill]`) merged 9,200 instructions with no undecodable bytes. The raw export is kept in `work\ghidra_raw\`, the complete one is `work\ghidra\` (104,410 lines; p_43a860 is now 32,992). Run the tool after every Ghidra export (fixesforgames B-T17). Wrong if: a later reading finds a `[fill]` run that is data, not code (look for decoding that turns into nonsense after a jump table).
- **D-013 (2026-10-13) Randomness in the clone.** The original reseeds from the clock (`Randomize Timer`, 13 sites) and draws `Int(Rnd * N) + 1`; the clone uses the system random generator seeded from the clock, with the same ranges and the same choice structure, and a fixed seed in tests (5.5). Wrong if: P3 finds a draw whose distribution is not uniform (for example sums of two draws).
- **D-014 (2026-10-13) Blitz help text.** The exe's string ends "... a very long gain on the " (cut off); the readme has "play." at the end. The clone speaks the full sentence. Wrong if: never matters for play. (S5.1: Q-020 answered from the lifted SayHints, the original speaks the cut text; this stays the clone's choice, listed in GAME_SPEC 17.1 and 17.3 for the owner's veto, D-033.)
- **D-015 (2026-10-13) Crude banter lines.** Kept as the original text in the game; README gets a content note; the owner decides at S11.1 (Q-018) whether crude lines become switchable. (S5.2: D-035 makes the banter a player file `banter.txt` in which every line can be switched off, so the S11.1 answer only chooses which original lines SHIP switched off.)
- **D-016 (2026-10-13) Shipped data files.** The clone creates every data file itself (as the original code does during play) and treats a missing file as the original does; the first-run copy routine (p_41c000) is not reproduced, because nothing we have shows a shipped file (Q-019). Wrong if: P3 finds a reader that cannot start without a shipped file. Confirmed in S3.6 (Q-019 answered): every reader is read; the only one that reads a file no code has written yet (pog reads footbal3.dat in week 1) works because the VB runtime creates it empty (B-P31).
- **D-018 (2026-10-13) Key model of the clone: global keys at every wait.** The original runs F5, F6, F7, F8, F11, F12 and Ctrl inside Form_KeyDown, so they work at any wait, as nested modal menus. The clone's key loop dispatches the same global keys at every wait point to the same menus (same observable behavior, no nested event loops), and after such a menu returns it speaks the interrupted prompt again and continues the wait. Wrong if: P3 shows (Q-023) that the interrupted wait ends instead; the conformance tests (S8.1) cover it either way. Updated 2026-10-13 (S3.1, Q-023 answered): the original ends every global-key menu with `g = "112"` (F1), so the interrupted wait ends as if F1 had been pressed and its own F1 handling repeats the prompt. The clone does exactly that: after a global-key menu it hands F1 to the interrupted wait (no separate "repeat" logic), so a wait that treats F1 differently behaves as the original. Test in S8.1: a global key during each kind of wait.
- **D-019 (2026-10-13) Structure deliverables are generated.** `extracted\resources\structure.json` and `structure.md` come from `tools\vb6_structure.py` (a reusable tool for any one-form VB6 native game), `spec\PROCEDURE_INDEX.md` and `extracted\resources\call_graph.md` from `work\make_procedure_index.py` (ROLES table in the script holds the roles, tags and status; edit the table and rerun, do not edit the generated files). After every P3 batch update the ROLES entry and status of the procedures read.
- **D-020 (2026-10-13, S3.1) P3 reads lifted code, not raw assembly.** `tools\vb6_lift.py` turns the gap-filled asm into pseudo-VB lines (`work\lift\`), 104,410 lines to 10,591 (9.9 times shorter; the test procedure of about 1,000 lines, SayScedual, 1,001 to 113). The plan's threshold was 3 times. It removes the compiler's error branches (bounds, HRESULT, FPU checks), follows registers, the push stack and frame slots across blocks, names form calls, form variables, SAPI calls, file statements and VB runtime functions, and shows path-dependent values as {a | b}. Checked: the nine procedures read by hand in P2 come out the same; a completeness check (every form call, Open, Close, Get, Put, Print, Kill, For, Shell and Rnd of the reachable asm appears once in the lifted text) passes for all 64 procedures. Reading rule: trust statements, check anything that decides behavior against the asm at the same address; tag such findings CODE (lifted). Rerun the tool (and regenerate `work\lift\`) after any change to it. Wrong if: a batch finds a lifted line that contradicts the asm; then fix the tool, rerun, add a fixesforgames entry, and re-check that batch's findings. (S3.2, S3.3: fixed, B-P16, B-P19. S3.4: the GoTo after a GoSub Return was dropped when other code jumps to it, pag's next-play jump at 0x451b53; fixed, B-P22; the completeness check now also lists every reachable JMP without a lifted statement; the diff changed one line, the S3.1 to S3.3 findings are unaffected. S3.6: a move a few instructions into a shared tail was shown as `?arg` in 11 places of finplay17 and the report listed only the 3 most common warnings; fixed, B-P30: the 11 lines now show the winner's team per path, nothing else changed, every warning is listed.)
- **D-021 (2026-10-13, S3.1) P3 batch plan.** Five reading batches after S3.1, by feature (`spec\FEATURE_MAP.md`): S3.2 keys, speech, global-key menus and sound, with the DirectX 8 method names (Q-024, Q-009), about 1,300 lifted lines; S3.3 start-up details, files and season set-up (getkp, GetCoach, Getqbs, ChooseTeam, GetTeams, makes, SayScedual, SayStand, SayScore, SayFinalScore, sgc, Form_Load details) plus the surname table by script, about 1,300 lines (Q-007, Q-019, Q-014); S3.4 the play engine main body (pag lifted lines 1 to 2280); S3.5 the play engine's eight GoSub routines, kicksnap, halftime (about 1,660 lines); S3.6 the season engine and play-offs (pog, finplay17, superbowl, about 2,200 lines; split in two if needed). Models: S3.2 and S3.3 Sonnet 5.5 at high (reading short lifted procedures and settings menus is now routine; the cost of a silent mistake is caught by the completeness check and the S5.2 audit), S3.4 to S3.6 Opus 5.5 at high (game and season logic, the judgment work of P3). Wrong if: a batch does not finish in one session (then split it and record why), or Sonnet misreads lifted code twice (then move that batch to Opus, 3.4.3). Updated 2026-10-13 (S3.4 done in one session on Opus 5.5 at high; kicksnap, halftime and superbowl were read with it because the main body needed their effects): S3.5 (pag's GoSub routines: hand-off and drop-back phrase rules, the set-up with footplay.txt and the rosters, the 142 banter lines; about 1,265 lifted lines not yet read) moves to Sonnet 5.5 at high, because the game rules are now in the spec and what is left is phrase tables and set-up with simple choice rules; S3.6 (pog, finplay17, 2,111 lines) stays Opus 5.5 at high. Update 2026-10-14: S3.5 was done on Sonnet 5.5 at high in one session with no escalation (no check failed twice; the lifted text agreed with the assembly everywhere it was checked). Update 2026-10-14 (S3.6): pog and finplay17 were read in one session on Opus 5.5 at high (no split needed: the two engines repeat the same blocks, and the spoken sentences were checked by a path script instead of by eye); Phase 3 is complete.
- **D-022 (2026-10-13, S3.2) Keys, typed names and voices in the clone.** (1) Typed names (coach, quarterbacks) follow the original rule exactly: only letters, digits and spaces are kept; fewer than 2 characters typed, only spaces left, or fewer than 2 characters left give the default ("coach" for the coach; the quarterback default is the one Getqbs keeps, S3.3); the original has no maximum length and the clone sets none (an on-screen limit is a S7.4 matter, not a rule change). Shift makes the next typed letter a capital; Backspace speaks the removed character; F1 repeats the prompt and clears the text. (2) The original types the character of the key CODE for every key: keypad 1 to 9 type a to i, the arrow keys type % & ' ( (each is spoken, then removed by the name check), F2 to F4 type q, r, s; the clone types the digit a keypad key shows and ignores keys that show no character while typing. Wrong if: never (no player wants the letters; the keys of the main and play menus are separate and are kept exactly, S3.3 to S3.5). (5) F11 and F12 leave the speech cache LastVoice unchanged although they end with the announcer voice loaded, so in the original a quarterback line spoken right after a hot-key menu could come out in the announcer voice (say3 would not reload); the clone picks the voice for every speaker call and does not reproduce this. Related: the original loads a voice (and applies its role's volume and the rate) only when the voice NUMBER changes, so two roles that use the same voice share the volume of whichever role loaded it; the clone applies each role's own volume. (3) Speech rate: the original lets the rate counter grow without a limit (SAPI accepts any number; tested 2026-10-13 on this PC: Rate = 100 is stored without an error); the clone keeps the counter in the SAPI range -10 to 10. (4) Voice choice (F12 and first start): nothing happens when only one SAPI voice exists, exactly as the original (S5.2: in the original F12 then reaches the interrupted wait as an ordinary key, because ChangeVoice returns without the F1 hand-back, quirk Q59, NOT reproduced: the clone's F12 ends with the F1 hand-back like every global key, D-034); the original then opens footbal0.dat, which was never written, For Binary Access Read (S3.2 inferred run-time error 53 and a crash; CORRECTED in S3.6 from the VB6 runtime's own code: the file is created empty, the reads change nothing, so the original keeps voice 0 and the voice's own rate, and the empty file means the first-run welcome is not repeated); the clone does the same with real values: voice 0, the default rate, and it writes the file. The five-voice choice only makes sense for the SAPI backend; with NVDA or JAWS speech the clone asks it only when SAPI is the active backend (S7.1 decides the wiring; the player can still pick voices with F12). Wrong if: S7.1 finds the starter kit has no voice list. Test: S8.1 hot-key tests.
- **D-023 (2026-10-13, S3.2) Sound model of the clone: same parameters, original quirks kept, two arithmetic slips not.** The clone plays every wav with the original's volume (hundredths of a dB attenuation, amplitude = 10 ^ (v / 2000), plus the player's sound volume wavol 0 to -5000), pan (the opposite channel attenuated by |pan| hundredths of a dB) and frequency (resampling; the +1 Hz the original adds to most sounds is inaudible and not reproduced), with the original's loops, waits, key interruptions and crowd ramps (WAV_EVENT_MAP.md sections 1 to 4). Kept as in the original: c2uh never ramps (its end value is always below its start), play waits to the end and play2 stops on a key, PlayTackle waits 2.5 s, PlayRan's 5 s start and 3 to 10 s spacing, foot10's pan-dependent volume, a key pressed during the F11 demo sound is lost. Not reproduced: the double counting of the entry value of wavol in the F11 sound step (the crowd gets quieter by that value after each use) and the 1 Hz. Wrong if: the owner hears that the crowd after F11 should be as quiet as in the original; then add the slip back (one line). Test: S8.1 and the listening round of P4 S4.1.
- **D-024 (2026-10-13, S3.3) Program flow, state and data in the clone.** (1) The original plays ONE game per run: after the game, the season engine and the standings it says "Thank you for playing" and ends (Form_Load 0x48a042, GAME_SPEC section 3 step 8). The clone returns to the week announcement and the main menu after a game (the exit is menu choice 4); menu choices 2 and 3 return to the week announcement as in the original; "Save Game" in the play menu still ends the program (the saved game is loaded at the next start and the menu is skipped, exactly as the original, otherwise the menu would resume the game at once). (2) Everything the original reset by ending is rebuilt at every game start: the surname pool, the voice cache, the replay list, the crowd volumes, the clock variables. (3) The clone keeps its data in its own per-user folder (the starter kit's, S7.1 names it, README says where, rule 0.10), not in the original's Documents\kitchensinc, and never reads or writes the original's files; a player may copy files across. (4) Files keep the original's value order (GAME_SPEC 12); the binary ones are written as Variant records (2-byte type, then the value) and read tolerantly (Integer, Long, Single, Double), so a season or save of the original can be copied in; footplay.txt keeps its text layout and headings because the player edits it (7.9). S7.5 may choose another container for a file if it finds a reason and records it. (5) A missing or unreadable file never ends the program (S3.3 said the original ends with run-time error 53 for football.gtp, footbal2.dat and footbal4.dat; CORRECTED in S3.6: the VB6 runtime creates such a file empty and the original goes on with week 0 or a stale opponent, GAME_SPEC 12; the clone's rule is unchanged): footbal0.dat, football.vol and footplay.txt give defaults or are drawn again; a missing or damaged season file restarts the season with a spoken notice (7.10; the wording in S7.5). Wrong if: the owner wants one game per start (cheap setting) or the original's folder shared; tests: S8.1 menu-flow test (play, return, schedule, exit), S8.3 damage tests.
- **D-025 (2026-10-13, S3.3) Quirks of the season, schedule, standings, clock and name procedures (B-P18).** Kept as the original (the player hears or sees them): the week line and today's game are spoken again after the schedule and the standings; the schedule maker's rules exactly (GAME_SPEC 5.1: the first division game is refused in weeks 1 and 2, no rule against other neighbors); the standings order by fewest losses only, equal ones in the team order; the quarterback of the player is asked once per season and kept as the last item of footplay.txt, the opponent's name is asked every game; the clock text with no seconds when they are 0; the surnames, including odd ones (D-026). Not reproduced: (a) F1 in the main menu repeats the whole announcement (week line and today's game) in every week, not only the play-off week name; (b) the clock at play counter 0 before any sgc call speaks the start-of-quarter time instead of " remaining in the first quarter." with no time (S3.4 shows whether it can happen); (c) what the original does with a missing football.gtp, footbal2.dat or footbal4.dat (S3.3 said it crashes; S3.6 found it goes on with week 0 or a stale opponent; either way the clone repairs or restarts the season, D-024 item 5); (d) the silent loss of the player's edits in footplay.txt when one heading or line is wrong: the clone keeps every valid section, regenerates only the broken one and tells the player (Q-014: limits are 33 names in fixed headings and order, a name is any text without a comma); (e) the MsgBox of the no-voice start is spoken through the active speech backend and the program ends (D-022) (S5.2 note: superseded by D-031 item 6 and D-032 item 3: the clone shows the same message box and speaks it through a screen reader when one runs, G-F9, GAME_SPEC Q12). The doubled spaces of Str$ ("game number  1") are not kept: the spoken words are the same. (S4.3 REVERSED this one remark in D-031 item 2: the clone builds every text exactly as the original does, doubled spaces included; the test showed no audible difference, and exact strings make the conformance tests plain.) Wrong if: the owner or the listening round finds one of the dropped quirks was wanted. Tests: S8.1 conformance (menu, schedule, standings, clock), S8.3 (files).
- **D-026 (2026-10-13, S3.3) Roster data.** The 32 team nicknames and the 1,708 surnames stay as the original data (spec\SURNAMES.tsv becomes the clone's surname asset, UTF-8, two Latin-1 letters, `Jones"` and `Maddox)` kept as in the original data, 50 phonetic or two-part spellings kept). Team names are not customizable in the original and stay fixed; the player's own names live in footplay.txt (D-025 d). Q-016 stays an accepted default; new Q-026 (the name "Redskins") is asked once at S11.1 with Q-017 and Q-018. Wrong if: the owner changes the data at S11.1 (then edit the asset and the strings).
- **D-027 (2026-10-13, S3.4) The play engine in the clone.** (1) The clone plays the game exactly as GAME_SPEC 4.14 to 5.12 describe: the coin toss and menus with their keys, the computer's play choice and fourth-down rules, the 30-entry outcome table (yards, sack and completion percentages, tackle sounds), fumbles 1/50, the interception rule, field goals by five draws, punts, kick-offs and returns, the extra point, the two-point try, safeties, overtime, the statistics, every text with its spelling and spaces (" its" in the F1 sentence, " it's" after a play, "7yards", "30yard line" and " 30 yard line" where the original has them) and every sound in its order; random draws as D-013. Where football.txt and the code differ the code wins (30 to 37 plays per quarter, Q13); the help text stays the original text. (2) Kept quirks (the player hears them, or they are rules of this game): Q14, Q15, Q17, Q18, Q19, Q20, Q21, Q22, Q29, Q31, and the overtime kick-off going to the side that had the ball at the end of regulation. (3) Not reproduced: Q23 (a turnover ends the two-point try with no points, then the kick-off), Q24 (a safety ends overtime like every other score), Q25 (every kick-off, overtime included, sets the receiving side's half, so a return starts at its own goal line), Q26 (the statistics name the right quarter), Q27 (the Super Bowl presentation runs once, in place of the final score, before the statistics), Q32 (keypad digits choose in the play menus and the main menu like the main-row digits; this extends D-022 item 2 to menus), Q28 for the second-half kick-off (the clone writes the side that received the opening kick-off as one more Variant after the original 36 values and the name in football.sav; a file without it, such as one from the original, behaves as in the original; the original reads only its own values, so a clone save stays readable by it). Q16, Q30, Q33 need nothing. (4) The event names in WAV_EVENT_MAP.md were renamed from the code context found in S3.4 (foot4 hit_pileup, foot5 hit_two_tacklers, foot6 hit_single, foot11 whistle, foot12 ball_contact, foot13 bleep); the listening round of P4 S4.1 may rename them again. Wrong if: the owner or the listening round wants a dropped quirk back (each is one condition in the clone) or a kept one removed; tests in S8.1: the outcome table and the computer's choices with a fixed seed against the tables of 5.7 and 5.9, overtime and sudden death, save and load (also a football.sav written in the original's layout), the concede path.
- **D-028 (2026-10-14, S3.5) The play engine's GoSub routines in the clone: phrases, rosters, banter.** (1) Hand-off and drop-back sentences exactly as GAME_SPEC 5.9: the value draws (group and phrase), the history of the last 16 values, the group memory as the original codes it (Q34: the hand-off test compares with a text and never rejects after the first call, the drop-back test works), and the spoken text always one of the nine (eleven) plain phrases. KEPT AS HEARD (Q35): the fake texts (" He fakes a hand off to ...", " He fakes a pass to ...", " He pump fakes a pass and then ", "fakes a hand off and then", "fakes to ...") are never spoken because the original overwrites them; the clone keeps them in the spec and the string list only. If the owner or a tester ever asks for them, it is one switch (speak the fake text before the phrase) and the odds already exist in the draws. (2) Rosters (5.5 step 2 to 4, 11): the player's own 33 names come from footplay.txt when it is valid, else 33 random unused surnames written to the file; the opponent's 33 names are drawn every game, never stored (Q39 kept: after loading a saved game they are drawn again); the pool is drawn without replacement and a name read from the file blanks the first equal pool entry (exact case); the clone's file keeps the original's headings, counts and order (D-024 item 4), is read as UTF-8 with a cp1252 fallback (a file written by the original with an accented name) and written as UTF-8. Q36 (the original ends with run-time error 9 or 62 on most damaged files) is NOT reproduced: D-025 d stands (repair only the broken section and tell the player). (3) Banter (5.13): the 142 lines of the table with their spelling, the draw Int(Rnd * 142) + 1 without repeats in a game (the history is not saved, Q37 kept), <NAME> from the 58-name list (the 29 running backs, receivers and defensive players of both teams), the opponent's quarterback in line 45, the second texts (alternatives of lines 101 and 110 equally likely), foot13 after the first text of lines 69, 72, 75 (twice), 83 and 140, line 113 one rate step faster (Q38: always applied, clamped), nothing at all when comments are off (F8, which also silences the five bleeped lines). (4) The crude lines stay as they are (D-015); Q-018 is asked at S11.1 with the evidence of GAME_SPEC 5.13 (a keyword scan flags 35 of the 142 lines, `work/banter_keywords_s35.txt`). (5) Not drawn in the clone: L31c and L390 (they only name the unspoken fake texts, Q35 and Q40). Wrong if: a listener or the owner misses the fakes (Q35), wants the same opponent names after a save (Q39) or finds a line misplaced; tests in S8.1: the phrase and group draws with a fixed seed against 5.9 (history of 16, first-call rule), the banter: each line at most once per game, the five bleeped lines, no banter with comments off, 15 lines with a name from the 58-name list, the roster draws without replacement, footplay.txt read, repaired and written (also a cp1252 file).
- **D-029 (2026-10-14, S3.6) The season engine and the play-offs in the clone.** (1) The clone runs the season exactly as GAME_SPEC 5.14 to 5.17 describe: the other 15 games of a week with random pairings and the 32-entry score table (no tie, the higher score wins), the records (footbal3.dat), the division winners by fewest losses, the wild cards, the seeds by wins (fewest first) with the byes and the fixed bracket, the computer's play-off games as coin flips with table scores, the waits with F1 and F2, the order of the sentences and their texts ("<winner> over the <loser> by the score of <w>, to <l>. " in a regular week, " The <winner> won over the <loser> by the score of <w> to <l>. " in the play-offs, the division winner and wild card lines, " ... did not make it into the playoffs. ", every heading of each engine with its own spelling), the celebration after a Super Bowl without the player; random draws as D-013. (2) Kept (the player hears them, or they are the season's rules): Q41 (a tie goes to the opponent; cannot happen), Q42, Q43 (the player wins ties for the division and the second wild card), Q44, Q45, Q47, Q49 (the remaining rounds are played and spoken at once when the player is out or loses), Q50 (the celebration again after a replay, with the 5-second pause), Q51 (no standings after week 16 with a bye or when out). (3) Not reproduced: Q46 (every play-off result is spoken as its own sentence with " The ", also the first A. F. C. wild card game when the player plays the second and the player's own A. F. C. championship; the two A. F. C. wild card results become two sentences); Q48 (after a conceded Super Bowl the clone speaks " In the Super Bowl. " and " The <opponent> won over the <player's team> by the score of <o> to <p>. ", plays the celebration and the wait, and the season ends; no repeated championship, no game of the opponent against itself). (4) The end of a season: when gtp becomes 0 (after the player's Super Bowl, or at the end of finplay17) the clone deletes its season data as the original's exit does (GAME_SPEC 3 step 9) and, because the clone returns to the menu after a game (D-024 item 1), it starts the new-season set-up (coach, team, schedule) at the next week announcement, as the original's next start does. (5) Files: football.gtp, footbal3.dat, footbal4.dat and footbal5.dat keep the original's value order with Integer Variants (D-024 item 4); footbal4.dat and footbal5.dat are replaced, not overwritten in place (the original deletes them first); a missing or damaged season file restarts the season with a notice (D-024 item 5). (6) Correction recorded (S3.6, the VB6 runtime's own code, fixesforgames B-P31): a missing file opened For Binary Access Read is created empty, not run-time error 53; D-022 item 4, D-024 item 5, D-025 (c), GAME_SPEC 3, 4.7, 12 and Q7 were corrected; no clone decision changes. Wrong if: the owner or a playtest wants a dropped slip back (each is one condition) or a kept rule changed (the coin-flip play-offs and the random pairings are the most likely to be questioned). - **D-031 (2026-10-14, S4.3) The gaps: speech, pauses, keys, sound slots, program flow, season end, files, errors (details and evidence per item: `spec\GAP_REGISTER.md`, 34 entries G-S1 to G-E1 (the counts in this entry are those of S4.3; S4.4 added G-A5 to G-A7 and S5.2 settled G-S5 by a test: now 37 entries, SETTLED 6, DEFAULT 26, NOTED 5, GAME_SPEC 18.3); S4.2 is merged here, the original cannot be run, D-005).** Nothing needs the owner. (1) Speech (G-S1, G-S2, G-S11, quirk Q52): one first-in-first-out speech queue for all roles; asynchronous = queue and return, synchronous = queue and wait for the empty queue (a barrier for everything queued before it), stop = purge; nothing is cut by a voice change (the original cuts the tail of an unguarded " Hike! "; not reproduced); pauses dy and rady run on a monotonic clock while queued speech plays, an audible gap only after a synchronous line; the pauses are not shortened, the pace control is the rate (Ctrl) as in the original, and no "sounds start right away" setting is added in S7 (D-003 stands; the lines before sounds are the broadcast, S8.2 decides). (2) Texts (G-S3, G-S9, supersedes the remark in D-025): every text is built exactly as the original builds it, Str$ doubled spaces included (tested: no audible difference); a per-engine respelling table only if a listening round finds a wrong reading. (3) Voices (G-S4 to G-S8, G-S10): the clone's speech layer gets a role argument and one SAPI voice object per distinct voice id inside one playback queue (S7.1 extends the kit); the default speech mode is SAPI 5 (the five voices are part of the game), automatic, NVDA and JAWS are one setting away in the F9 speech settings; NVDA and JAWS ignore roles; the rate counter changes only the pauses and the spoken number with NVDA and JAWS; the key echo is spoken at rate + 5 on SAPI; He and She come from the voice token's Gender, else He; the voice choice (F12 and first start) is the 7.4.2 engine and voice picker per role; footballlog.txt is kept (data folder, 1 MB cap). (4) Keys (G-K1 to G-K6, quirk Q54): the kit's `KeyInput` unchanged (synchronous speech does not pump messages, tested, so a key typed during narration acts at the next key wait with its code only); F9 opens the added functions (speech settings, sounds, customize, about) as a global key; Shift, Ctrl, Insert and CapsLock count as keys as in the original (watch item for S8.2); Alt+F4 or the close button ends the program at once without saving; typed names are cut at 100 stored characters. (5) Sound (G-A1 to G-A4): the mixer has the original's seven buffer slots and a new sound in a slot cuts that slot's sound; the play-noise loop is stopped at every key wait as a safety net and the rig test looks for a path that needs it; no sound device = the game goes on silent with one spoken notice. (6) Program flow (G-F1 to G-F9, quirks Q53, Q55): the spoken title " NFL Football 4 by Josh Kennedy, inspired by Jim Kitchen " at every launch (first run: in place of the original title), after a game the standings and the one key wait only when standings were spoken, then the week loop; at the season's end the original's deletion of the season files and footplay.txt, but footplay.txt is first saved as footplay.previous.txt (new random names each season as the original, edits never lost), the clone says " The season is over. A new season begins. " and runs the new-season set-up; a damaged or stale football.sav is set aside as football.sav.damaged1 with one spoken notice; the data folder is `%APPDATA%\Kitchensinc Revival\NFL Football 4` with the kit's settings file (no footbal0.dat or football.vol, no import of the original's files); no voice at all = a message box (and speech through a screen reader when one runs), then the end; no guard against a second copy. (7) Data and tests (G-D1, G-D2, G-C1): every redraw loop is bounded (10,000 tries); one random generator from the clock behind one function with the lifted ranges; scripted and seeded generators in the tests; a property test of 10,000 simulated games checks the invariants. Wrong if: the owner or the S8.2 playtest wants a dropped quirk back or a default changed; each default has its closing session in the register (S7.1 5, S7.2 1, S7.4 5, S7.5 3, S7.7 1, S8.1 13, S8.2 12, S8.3 4, S9.2 1 entries). Tests of this session: `work\sapi_sync_pump_test_s43.txt` (a synchronous Speak delivered no messages in 24.1 s), `work\sapi_release_cut_test_s43.txt` (releasing a speaking voice stops it), `work\sapi_spaces_test_s43.txt` (56 of 56 equal lengths), `work\pause_table_s43.txt` (68 pause sites).

- **D-036 (2026-10-14, P6 S6.1 and S6.2 merged) Language and runtime: Python 3.14.8 with pygame-ce 2.5.8, no GUI toolkit, no numpy; two environments, both proven.** Nothing needs the owner; the spec is not touched (GAME_SPEC stays version 1.0). (1) LANGUAGE (playbook 7.1): stay in Python, as the starter kit is. None of the three reasons to leave Python holds: (a) no hard real-time need: nfl4 is a turn-based speech and key game, and the heaviest sound work is one render at a sound's start, measured here with the whole audioop path on all 26 original wavs at original + 1 Hz: 40 ms in total in 64-bit and 46 ms in 32-bit, worst 6 ms (foot1, 430 KB 16-bit stereo); the mixer reports a sound's end within 14 ms of the computed length on the real device at channel volume 0 (three sounds, both bitnesses); sleep(1 ms) takes 1.53 ms; (b) the owner set no size limit (expected install about 25 MB 64-bit and 13 MB installer as Brain, plus the 2.8 MB of wavs); (c) no mobile target is decided (S12.1 is asked once at S11.4, nothing here). The window is the kit's ctypes Win32 module (proven in both bitnesses in one process with the mixer); the audio output stays the pygame-ce mixer (B-A8). (2) NUMPY: not used, and no proposal is made (rule 0.11, 7.1.1): the game only plays wavs with volume, pan and frequency, adds no generated sound or music (D-032), and needs no filter, echo, FFT or per-sample mixing in Python (the mixer mixes the seven slots and the two crowd beds). The 7.1.1 cases that would call for it do not occur. Proof: the environments have no numpy and the kit's whole suite passes with numpy blocked (below). The owner can still ask for the numpy variant (`new_clone_from_kit.py ... --numpy-audio` at S7.1, about 28 MB more in a 64-bit build); nothing indicates it. (3) PYTHON VERSION: 3.14.8 in both bitnesses (the newest 3.14 on python.org, already installed, nothing installed or changed). Python 3.15.0 exists and all six pinned packages have cp315 wheels (`games\tools\pypi_wheels.py`, `work\pypi_wheels_s61.txt`; `work\python_releases_s61.py` lists python.org), but the kit's proof (516 tests in both bitnesses, build, installer, size and latency numbers) is on 3.14.8; a Python move is a kit-wide change with its own trial of both kit variants (B-T9), not a per-game choice (fixesforgames B-B9). (4) PACKAGES, the kit's pins, each also the newest on PyPI on 2026-10-14: pygame-ce 2.5.8, audioop-lts 0.2.2, pywin32 312, pyinstaller 6.22.3, pytest 9.1.1, pefile 2024.8.26 (`work\requirements_nfl4_s61.txt`, installed with `--only-binary=:all:`); environments `C:\Users\joshk\.kitchensinc-venvs\nfl4-64` and `nfl4-32` (B-B8: not under AppData\Local, not in OneDrive); versions in `tools\ENVIRONMENT.md`; S7.1 brings the clone's own `requirements.txt` and `tools\setup_envs.ps1` from the kit (same pins, slug nfl4). (5) PROOFS: `work\verify_runtime_s62.py` (`work\runtime_check_s62_64.txt` and `_32.txt`): 12 of 12 in each bitness: imports with numpy absent, all 26 wavs (7 formats: 8-bit and 16-bit, mono and stereo, 8363 to 44100 Hz; S7.2 correction, D-038: 8 formats, the list in fixesforgames B-A13 always named 8, `test_every_shipped_wav_loads_and_matches_the_standard_reader` checks the set) decoded and rendered, pitch lengths exact at -2000 and +1900 Hz (floor 8000), pan +1400, -1500 and 0 within 3 of 32768, a stereo source keeps its channels, the mixer on SDL's dummy driver (7 of 7 slots busy, all idle at the end, a loop stops) and on the real device at volume 0, SAPI at Volume 0 (1,811 voices, default FlexVoice2 Bill) and FIVE voice objects at once into memory streams with equal byte counts in both bitnesses (the role voices of G-S6 can be separate objects), the kit's ctypes window, the NVDA controller client (x64 and x86 DLLs load, NVDA runs), `FreedomSci.JawsApi` registered in both registry views and creatable (D-004; no call made), sleep precision. The kit's own suite from a throwaway clone (`new_clone_from_kit.py demo demo <folder> --with-reference`, since deleted), run from its clone folder with numpy blocked: 516 passed, 164 skipped (no sound files), 0 failed in each environment. (6) FOR S7.1: the 16-bit stereo files (foot1 to foot9, foot11, foot12, foot14) use the stereo path of `render.py` (per-channel `tomono`, `tostereo`, `add`), proven here with the plain audioop calls; the kit's `tools\verify_runtime.py` is Brain-specific, so S7.1 copies `work\verify_runtime_s62.py` into the clone's tools (package-agnostic) and refreshes the kit for both variants (B-X16). (7) Not tested by design: real NVDA and JAWS speech (it would be heard on the owner's speakers; the owner's playtests S8.2 cover it), the built exe (P9). (8) The spec checker's generated region CHECKS (GAME_SPEC 18.3) quotes the number of decisions, so it went stale when this entry was written; it was refreshed with `work\check_spec_s51.py --write` after a byte copy (`work\GAME_SPEC_before_s62.md`): the diff is ONE line (" Decisions: D-001 to D-036, 36 entries"), the checker passes again (regions up to date, RESULT PASS), and the spec stays version 1.0 because no statement about the original or the clone changed. Every later decision entry makes that line stale again until the checker is run with `--write`. Wrong if: a later phase finds a measured limit of 7.1 (S8.1 key and sound timing, S9.2 size above what the owner accepts), or the owner asks for numpy or another Python; then change it here with the measurement.
- **D-037 (2026-10-14, P7 S7.1) The clone's skeleton: the kit copied, five speakers on one speech queue, typed characters, nfl4's settings and data folder, the crash guard for every voice, the lifecycle.** Nothing needs the owner; the spec is not touched (GAME_SPEC stays version 1.0; only its generated CHECKS region was refreshed, item 12). (1) CREATION: the clone was made with `games\tools\new_clone_from_kit.py nfl4 nfl4 <game folder>` (default variant, no numpy, no reference game), AFTER a fix of that tool: a dry run showed its renaming step walked the WHOLE game folder and would have rewritten 17 files that are not the kit's (spec\STRINGS.md, the lifted code in work\lift, the playbook copies and the owner's markdown backup in work\md_backup_2026-10-14_s52); it now renames only what it copied (clone\, release\ and the four root files). Proof: SHA-256 of all 499 other files of the game folder before and after: identical (fixesforgames B-T30). (2) GIT: the repository is the GAME folder, not clone\ alone: the kit's `.gitignore` (adapted: football.exe, football.txt and foot*.wav at the root, nfl4-portable) and `clone\tools\make_public_commit.py` work from the game root, and it keeps clone\, spec\, release\ and the root notices in one history; original\, work\, extracted\, observations\, the state files and the playbook copies stay out; `core.autocrlf false` in this repository so files keep their bytes (the spec checkers compare bytes). Local commits after every working step; nothing pushed (public actions wait for S11.1). (3) SPEECH, D-031 items 1 and 3 built (GAP_REGISTER G-S1, G-S4, G-S6): the five speakers are named in `speech\roles.py` (the F12 order announcer, own quarterback, opponent quarterback, own punter, opponent punter; the F11 order; their spoken names in F11 and F12; say3's choice by side and quarterback or punter; GetSexOf's case-sensitive "Female" test). The SAPI backend (`speech\sapi5.py`, rewritten on the kit's) keeps one SpVoice object per DISTINCT voice, opened the first time a speaker with that voice talks, and ONE first-in-first-out queue: a text is made in memory and played (rendered voices) or spoken by SAPI itself (a voice too slow to render, or no sound device) only after the text before it has been heard to its end, so nothing overlaps and nothing is cut by a voice change; the speaker's volume and the rate vr (plus a per-text rate step, banter line 113) are set on the object just before each text; a text of spaces queues nothing (the barrier is the wait itself); stop purges the queue, the voice making or speaking and the player at once. `role_details` reads a speaker's voice id, name and Gender attribute from the voice token without opening the voice. The manager (`speech\manager.py`) passes `role` and `rate_step` only to an engine that takes them (SAPI 5); NVDA and JAWS get the text alone; `set_rate` reaches only SAPI 5 (G-S4); `role_gender` is "" unless SAPI 5 speaks now (G-S6: " He " with NVDA or JAWS); `last_role` serves say6; the default mode stays "sapi" (G-S7); every engine call stays guarded (B-X2). (4) CRASH GUARD for five voices (playbook 7.4.5): the backend arms each voice before it opens it or probes it again and clears it after its first text was made or spoken to the end (a fast voice by its speed probe); the first voice of SAPI's list is never armed; `VoiceGuard.clear(voice)` removes the trial only when it holds that voice; at start every speaker saved with a voice on the crashed list uses the first voice this run (the saved choice stays in settings.txt), and one notice per crashed voice is spoken; a saved voice that is not installed gives one notice for all speakers. (5) SETTINGS (G-F8): `%APPDATA%\Kitchensinc Revival\NFL Football 4\settings.txt` in the kit's plain-line format with healing: `speech_output` (sapi), `voice_<speaker>` (empty = the first voice), `volume_<speaker>` (95), `speech_rate` (original = the first voice's own rate, which is what the original keeps in vr at start, GAME_SPEC 3 step 3; -10 to 10), `speech_pitch` (0), `list_letters` (type), `sound_volume` (wavol in WHOLE DECIBELS, 0 to -50, the F11 step being 1 dB; stored internally as hundredths), `comments` (on); no `sound_start` (G-S11) and no footbal0.dat or football.vol. The kit's Brain audio fields stay in `AudioSettings` unsaved until S7.2 builds the seven slots. (6) WINDOW AND KEYS (G-K5, B-W3): `ui\window.py` (class GameWindow) calls TranslateMessage and hands over each key TOGETHER with the character it typed: TESTED that TranslateMessage posts the WM_CHAR behind keys already queued (five posted keys arrived as five keys, then five characters), so the loop takes it at once with PeekMessage (B-W4); `show()` comes to the front as the listening tool's window does. `game\keys.py`: the window's characters go into the same ordered queue as the keys; g and g2 stay the original's; `typed` and `take_typed()` serve the name prompts of S7.4. (7) APP SKELETON (`app.py`, `game\flow.py`, `__init__.py` VERSION 0.1.0, `__main__.py`, `tools\nfl4_entry.py`): the working directory leaves the program folder first, the window's close arms the exit deadline, the entry points end with finish(main()) (CLAUDE.md 0.10, playbook 7.2.1); the title " NFL Football 4 by Josh Kennedy, inspired by Jim Kitchen " (and the window title without the spaces) once per launch, synchronous, by the announcer (rule 0.5.1, G-F1), then the notices; then, ONLY in this skeleton, a key wait that Escape ends (S7.4 replaces it with the start-up, the week loop and the main menu; Escape does not end the original). No voice at all: the sentence through NVDA or JAWS when one runs and a plain box "Sorry but I couldn't find any voices to use" + "For help, see https://github.com/joshknnd1982/nfl-football-4-audio-game" (G-F9, D-008: the original's "Please contact Jim Kitchen" is not shown). No sound yet (S7.2). (8) TESTS (`clone\tests`, run from the clone folder, B-X12): the virtual-clock rig `gamerig.py` with scripted keys (B-X1; a one-character answer types the key and its character); fakes that behave like the measured engines (B-X3): `test_sapi_roles.py` gives every SpVoice its own object and a shared fake sound device that records every interval of sound, so overlapping voices would fail; new files test_sapi_roles (15), test_speech_roles_manager (14), test_flow_skeleton (7), test_keys (11), test_app_startup (6), test_voiceguard (7), and test_window (10), test_settings_file (28), test_real_engines (12, one new: the first five real voices as the five speakers, silent, their genders equal their tokens'); the start-up tests keep the window in the background (B-X17). Results: the whole suite with numpy blocked 294 passed, 39 skipped (the tests that need the shipped wavs, until S7.2 copies them), 0 failed in 64-bit and in 32-bit; every new test file was run first alone with a time limit (B-X7). Repeat runs of the 12 real-thread files (158 tests, B-X5): first loop, summaries only: 64-bit 15 saved runs (runs 6 to 20 lost to a file lock, B-T32), 14 fully passed; 32-bit 30 saved, 28 fully passed; the 3 failing runs had 1, 1 and 2 failures whose names were not saved; diagnostic loop with every run's output kept, 15 per bitness: 28 of 30 fully passed, the 2 failures were the two new character tests of test_window.py, whose expected letters assumed no Shift while the owner was typing (B-X18); the tests now accept what the key can type with and without Shift and Caps Lock (asked from Windows), a new test proves the cause by setting Shift in the window thread's keyboard state, and test_window.py then passed 30 of 30 runs in each bitness. No other test failed in any run whose output was kept. (9) RUNTIME CHECK: `work\verify_runtime_s62.py` copied into `clone\tools\verify_runtime.py` (defaults now the game's original folder and the clone's src; B-X16) and run from there: 12 of 12 in both bitnesses (`work\runtime_check_s71_64.txt`, `_32.txt`; the window check now uses GameWindow). (10) THE KIT is not refreshed in this session: the kit's reference game (Brain) uses the single-voice API, `BrainWindow` and Brain's settings, so a hand refresh would mean adapting it and proving both variants; it is left to the end of the game (B-T25), when `refresh_starter_kit.py` builds the kit from nfl4. Waiting for it (fixesforgames section C lists them): the window with typed characters, the key input with typed characters, the multi-voice SAPI backend and manager, the fake with speakers, `VoiceGuard.clear(voice)`, the package-agnostic verify_runtime (and its numpy-variant twin with the numpy check inverted). The tool fix of item 1 is already in `games\tools`. (11) NOT YET RENAMED (their sessions do it): the audio module's Brain sound ids (S7.2), the build, smoke, installer, portable and measuring tools (P9, P10; `tools\interrupt_latency_check.py` imports a module `mixerplayer` the kit does not have), the kit's help texts in `game\extra_texts.py` (S7.4; the about text is nfl4's already). (12) The spec checker's generated regions quote the gap register's count line and the number of decisions; both went stale (G-S1, G-S4, G-S6, G-K5 and G-F8 no longer name S7.1 as a closing session, and this entry); refreshed with `work\check_spec_s51.py --write` after a byte copy (`work\GAME_SPEC_before_s71.md`), the diff showing only those generated lines. - **D-038 (2026-10-14, P7 S7.2) The sound layer: the 26 original wavs, the IDs, the seven slots and the extra channel, levels applied by the mixer, the original's sound procedures, the sound-file notice.** Nothing needs the owner; the spec is not touched (GAME_SPEC stays version 1.0; only its generated lines were refreshed, item 14). (1) WAVS (D-001): the 26 files copied from original\ into `clone\assets\sounds` by `work\copy_wavs_s72.py`, every source and every copy proven by SHA-256 against `spec\ORIGINAL_SHA256.txt` (26 of 26, `work\copy_wavs_s72.txt`); the test `test_the_shipped_files_are_the_originals_byte_for_byte` repeats the proof in every suite run. (2) IDS (SOUND_DESIGN 6, playbook 7.7): `snd_foot0` to `snd_foot25` and `beep_exit` (`audio\ids.py`); a player's file is named like the original's file (`foot7.wav`, tried first) or like the ID (`snd_foot7.wav`), wav before ogg, flac and mp3, any case; a `sounds.txt` line may use either name; the owner-confirmed event names (WAV_EVENT_MAP 6) are only words in the notes of `sounds.txt` (`#   foot7: kick`), never IDs. Brain's numbered pool (brain1 to brainN, "numbers in a row") is gone: nfl4 has a fixed set. (3) SLOTS (G-A1): the seven original buffers are mixer channels 0 to 6 in the order of the form fields (one-shot Me.m220, crowd 1, crowd 2, hike, ambient, tackle, play noise), the extra channel is channel 7 (the sounds menu's previews and the exit beep, never used in play); starting a sound in a slot stops that slot's sound, different slots mix. (4) LEVELS: DirectSound's volume in hundredths of a decibel, the caller adds wavol (settings `sound_volume`, F11 part 2), gain = 10 ^ (v / 2000), -10000 and below is silence. The level is applied by the MIXER, not baked into the samples, because the crowd ramps, F11 and the start of a game change the level of a PLAYING loop and a re-render would restart it: SDL_mixer's chunk volume (128 whole steps) times its panning effect (a float multiply, 255 steps), shared by `output.split_gain`; within 0.5 percent down to -50 dB and within 1 dB at -79 dB (-2900 - 5000, the quietest the game sets). TESTED with SDL's disk driver, which writes the mixed samples to a file in real time (silent): the measured sample of a constant 16000 at 0, -100, -600, -1200, -1500, -2750, -2900 and -5000 is within 0.5 percent of 16000 x 10 ^ (v / 2000) and within 2 of the computed mixer arithmetic, and no other value is ever written (the level holds from each sound's first sample: an effect set on an idle channel stays for the next sound; SDL_mixer drops a channel's effect when the channel stops, so `play` stops, sets, then starts). The player's trim from `sounds.txt` (0 to 200 percent) stays baked into the samples (clipped). A master volume of 0 makes the real device silent for timing tests. (5) PAN AND FREQUENCY: pan is baked into the render, only the far channel attenuated (D-023; only foot10 uses it); frequency None = the file's own rate (the +1 Hz is not reproduced, D-023; crowd 1 and crowd 2 set none in the original either), the celebration sounds at own rate + 100 x k with the 8000 Hz floor, a frequency outside DirectSound's 100 to 200,000 Hz plays at the own rate with one log line. (6) PROCEDURES (`game\sounds.py`, class GameSounds, each read again in the lifted code this session): intro (foot0 once at wavol, waited, then looped at the same level), loop_foot0 (ChangeVoice), crowd1, crowd2, start_crowds in Form_Load's order (crowd1, crwd1 = -1200, crowd2), set_crwd1 (pag's -100), stop_crowds, c2uh/c2uf/c2dh/c2df as VB For loops (SetVolume then dy(0.1); c2uf from the base is 13 steps in 1.3 s, c2dh from full 7 steps in 0.7 s; a ramp that starts AT its end sets once and waits 0.1 s, one that starts past its end does nothing; c2uh's loop never runs, quirk kept), make_hike and make_play_noise (levels only), play_hike (foot3 then the play noise loop, no wait), play_tackle (foot n + 3 at -1500 + wavol, the play noise stops, 2.5 s whatever the length, keys kept), play (clk, waited to the end, keys kept), play2 (clk, wavol, own rate, until the end or a key; the key stays in g for the caller), celebration_sound (file, k drawn again while 0, floor, waited) and playcel (c2uf, 8 to 15 sounds, 0.4 s, play(12, -500), c2df), load_ambient, start_ambient_wait and play_ran (PlayRan: nothing while the ambient slot plays or before st4; foot8 -250, foot9 -1200, foot10 at -2900 + |pan| with pan (Int(Rnd * 30) - 15) x 100; then st4 = now + Rnd x 7 + 3), stop_play_noise (Q57's stop, G-A2), stop_all; `KeyInput.getkey2` (st4 = now + 5, clk, DoEvents and PlayRan on every pass, stop speech at the key). The random draws keep their lifted ranges and order (G-D2; `rng.random()` is VB's Rnd); halftime, superbowl and finplay17 are S7.3 logic that call celebration_sound and play. TESTED in passing: a key still WAITING in the queue when play starts survives clk (g is cleared, then DoEvents reads the waiting key, GAME_SPEC 8.1), as in the original; only a key already read is thrown away. (7) "off" in `sounds.txt` (G-A3: "a missing or silent sound waits nothing"): the sound is not played and nothing waits for it (the kit's "off" kept the sound's length as silence for Brain's numbered pool); a player who wants a pause supplies a silent wav of that length, which plays and is waited like any sound. (8) SOUND-FILE NOTICE (G-A6): the exe's unused string "Sorry but one or more of the wave files seem to be corrupt" (no spaces around it in the exe), a period, then the kit's "A sound file could not be used and was skipped, details are in the log.", ONCE per start, for a player's or a shipped file that cannot be decoded and for a sound with no usable file at all; never for an absent player file or a `file=` naming a missing file (logged and listed by check files). The program resolves all 27 sounds at start (7 ms in 64-bit, 10 ms in 32-bit, `clone\tools\sound_load_timing.py`), so the notice is spoken with the start-up notices. (9) NO DEVICE (G-A4, built early, closes S8.3): the silent timed output keeps every wait right and " The sound system could not be started. The game goes on without sounds. " is said once with the start-up notices. (10) SETTINGS: the kit's Brain-only `effects_volume`, `pitch_spread` and `start_early` are removed from AudioSettings; wavol (`sound_level`) is nfl4's only sound level (G-S11: no "sounds start right away"). (11) LONG FILES: the decoder accepts up to 120 s (the kit had 30 s, Brain's sounds were at most 250 ms; nfl4's longest is foot25, 11.5 s, and a player's crowd bed may be a long loop); longer files fall back. (12) RENDER CACHE: a sound rendered at a frequency and pan is kept for its next start (64 MB at most; the 26 files at their own rates are about 8 MB); all first renders take 44 ms in 64-bit and 55 ms in 32-bit (worst foot1, 6 to 7 ms). (13) TESTS (all run from the clone folder with numpy blocked): new `test_game_sounds.py` (42, the procedures on the virtual clock with a slot log), `test_audio_disk.py` (6, the mixed samples), `test_audio_realdevice.py` (9, the real device at volume 0: each sound's end within 30 ms of its length, play waits its length, play2 ends within 50 ms of a key from another thread, a loop runs on, the ramp takes 1.3 s), `test_audio_restore.py` (2); rewritten for nfl4 `test_audio_engine.py`, `test_audio_library.py`, `test_audio_mixer.py`, `test_audio_soundsconfig.py`; adapted `test_audio_render.py`, `test_audio_wavdata.py`, `test_app_startup.py` (the mixer on the dummy driver; two new start-up tests for G-A6 and G-A4), `test_jaws.py` (the removed field); the rig (`tests\gamerig.py`) has the real audio layer on a silent timed output on its virtual clock and a scripted getkey2. The kit's ogg, flac and mp3 tests needed `tests\data\tone.*`, which the kit never shipped: made with ffmpeg (440 Hz, 0.15 s, half scale). The whole suite: see item 15. (14) The spec checker's generated lines quote the number of decisions and the register's count line; refreshed with `work\check_spec_s51.py --write` after a byte copy (`work\GAME_SPEC_before_s72.md`). (15) RESULTS: the whole suite with numpy blocked 404 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed in 64-bit and in 32-bit (`work\suite_s72_64.txt`, `_32.txt`); the 15 real-thread files 30 times per bitness: no audio test failed in any run; the only failures were S7.1's window character tests, from the owner's Ctrl key (fixesforgames B-X21: the tests now wait until the modifier keys are up and report the keyboard state with a failure; test_window.py then 30 of 30 per bitness); details in PROJECT_STATE, Findings of P7 S7.2. Not built here (their sessions): the exit sequence and the crowd beds' restart after a game (S7.4, SOUND_DESIGN 4, G-A5), adjvol's crowd levels from the base values (S7.4, D-023), the F9 sounds menu (S7.4, G-K2), the play noise's safety net (S8.1, G-A2). Wrong if: the owner hears a level, a ramp or a cut that differs from the original (S8.2), the owner wants "off" to keep the sound's length (one line in `library.py`), or a sound device stutters with eight channels (S8.3).
- **D-039 (2026-10-14, P7 S7.3a) The season frame of the game logic: the one random function, the speakers, the league, the surname pool, the schedule maker, the standings, the game clock, the score speech and the game's set-up.** Nothing needs the owner. GAME_SPEC goes from 1.0 to 1.1 by two additions of words (item 8). (1) WHAT WAS BUILT, all in `clone\src\nfl4\game`, none of it with a window, a sound card or a screen reader in it: `rnd.py` (class GameRandom), `vbnum.py` (VB's Str$, Int, Trim$ and the Single rounding), `league.py` (the 32 teams and 8 divisions from GAME_SPEC 11; a `League` takes the player's lists later, S7.7), `names.py` (class NamePool over the 1,708 surnames, which are now the asset `clone\assets\data\surnames.txt`, made by `work\make_surnames_asset_s73a.py` from spec\SURNAMES.tsv and proven equal to it by a test), `schedule.py` (`makes`), `standings.py` (the records and SayStand's order and sentences), `gameclock.py` (`sgc`), `scorespeech.py` (the SayScore and SayFinalScore sentences), `setup.py` (pag's set-up GoSub: the rosters, the banter names, the plays of the five periods, the three warning lengths and the fixed word lists), `season.py` (the state of a season: coach, teams, week, schedule, records), `speakers.py` (say to say6, the replay list, the log hook, inrep, dy and rady) and `flow.py` (Game inherits Speakers and gets `say_stand`, `say_score`, `say_final_score`, `sgc`, `make_schedule`, `new_game_setup`; its S7.1 skeleton `run()` is unchanged until S7.4). (2) RANDOM (G-D2 and G-D1 built; both still close at S8.1): ONE `GameRandom` per program (made in app.py and handed to the sound procedures and the Game; the rig does the same); `rnd(n)` is Int(Rnd * n) + 1, `below(n)` is Int(Rnd * n) for the lines that add another offset (`below(8) + 30`), `random()` is Rnd; the source is `random.Random()` (seeded by the operating system, D-013) and the tests pass a seeded or a scripted one. Every "draw again while" loop of the lifted code that this session builds (the schedule's week, opponent and flag draws, the surname draws, the clock's seconds) goes through `redraw`: at most 10,000 tries, then the first valid candidate found by scanning, one warning in the log and a counter (`fallbacks`, 0 in every test on the real data; a source stuck on one number takes the fallbacks and still gives a valid schedule). A used-up surname pool is filled again with a warning (cannot happen: a game draws 70 of 1,708). Every draw keeps the range and the order of its lifted line: the schedule's 56 scripted draws (through every kind of refusal) and the set-up's 74 (33 + 33 + 5 + 3) prove the order. (3) NUMBER TYPES (found while writing sgc, TESTED, fixesforgames B-P40): the original's clock is evaluated in SINGLE precision because the plays of a quarter are a Single Variant (`Int(Rnd * 8) + 30` is stored with FSTP float, type word 4) and a Single with an Integer or a Single stays a Single through VarSub, VarMul, VarDiv and VarInt (the automation library's functions, which the runtime's `__vbaVar*` helpers call; only two Integers divided give a Double). `vbnum.single` rounds to 32 bits after every operation of the chain; compared with the library for all 3,588 inputs the game can give (`tests\oleauto.py`, `work\vb_variant_ops_s73a.txt`): 0 differences, while a Double chain differs in 57 (the first: total 30, played 10: 989 hundredths in a Single, 990 in a Double, so 53 seconds against 54 before the jitter). The runtime's own wrapper functions were not called (the library's are what they wrap); the types of the two arguments come from the asm of pag's set-up (0x458328 to 0x458338) and the constants (type word 2). (4) SPEAKERS (GAME_SPEC 9.1, 9.2; G-S1, G-S2, G-S4): say, say2, say3, say4, say5 and say6 on `Speakers` with the original's waits (say, say3 and say4 wait for everything queued, the others queue and return), logs (say, say2, say4, say5, say6 log while ptf is 1, say3 never) and replay list (say2 and say4 keep the line); say3 takes the speaker from the form variables `wisoff` and `qorp` (kept as attributes of the Game, as the original keeps them), say6 the speaker of the last text; the log goes to a `log_sink` hook (S7.5, G-S10 writes footballlog.txt; SayScore prints its two lines to it whatever ptf is); inrep speaks " here's the instant rea play " (waited, unless norep is not 0), pauses 0.2 s and queues every line of the list up to the one that equals " <coach> select your play "; dy is the kit's `KeyInput.dy`; rady waits Int(Rnd * 30) / (max(vr, 1) * 25) seconds with vr from a source the app sets from the speech-rate setting (S7.4; 0 until then). The original's replay list has 501 places (a store beyond it is run-time error 9, asm 0x42a6a3); the clone's list has no bound and records its longest use (`replay_longest`), which the S8.1 property test checks. (5) THE POOL'S TWO BLANK RULES, both kept: names read from footplay.txt blank the FIRST equal slot with exact case (a typed name that is not in the pool blanks nothing), a quarterback name blanks EVERY slot equal ignoring case; the set-up's draws blank the slot at once, Getqbs's default draw blanks it only when the name is accepted (S7.4 calls `draw_slot` and `blank`). (6) FILES AND UI STAY WITH THEIR SESSIONS: the schedule is a list of 16 `ScheduleEntry(division, team, flag)` and the records a `Records` with 64 file values; reading and writing footbal1.dat to footbal3.dat, football.gtp, footplay.txt (the set-up takes the player's names as a `Roster`, or None to draw them; `own_names_drawn` says the file has to be written), footballlog.txt and football.sav is S7.5; the typed prompts (coach, team, quarterback names, the schedule question), SayScedual's speech, the week loop's sentences and the main menu are S7.4; teams.txt, divisions.txt, surnames.txt and footplay.txt for the player are S7.7; pag, the banter's draw rule and the pause sites are S7.3b; pog and finplay17 are S7.3c. (7) GAP REGISTER: notes (before "Closes:", B-T28) for G-S1, G-S2, G-S3, G-D1 and G-D2; no entry is closed here, so the count line does not change. (8) GAME_SPEC 1.1: one sentence group in 5.3 (the number types and the evidence) and one in 9.2 (the replay list's size), the status line and the history line; every old word is kept (`work\spec_v11_s73a.py` proves it by undoing its four replacements, `work\check_spec_s51.py` passes); no quirk is added and no sentence, sound or rule of the original changes (the clock's rounding was always the original's). (9) TESTS (all new files first alone with a time limit, B-X7; the whole suite from `clone` with numpy blocked): test_rnd 9, test_league 9, test_names 12, test_schedule 5, test_standings 8, test_gameclock 17 (among them the exhaustive comparison with the automation library and whole quarters with the two-minute warning), test_setup 9, test_speakers 27, test_season_frame 12, test_vbnum 7, with the helpers `tests\randsources.py` (scripted, strict and stuck sources), `tests\oleauto.py` and `tests\specfiles.py` (every spoken text is looked up in spec\strings_all.tsv); one old test changed because the rig's generator is now the game's GameRandom (`test_game_sounds.py`: `rig.rnd.source.drawn`); `tests\gamerig.py` hands the same GameRandom to the Game and the sounds. Results: see PROJECT_STATE, Findings of P7 S7.3a. Wrong if: S8.1's property test finds a draw whose range or order differs from its lifted line, S7.3b finds another Variant chain whose type changes an Int (check each fractional step the same way, B-P40), or the owner's playtest hears a clock second that differs from the original (cannot be tested against the original here, D-005; the types come from the library and the asm).
- **D-040 (2026-10-14, OWNER REQUEST, given before P7 S7.3b) The finished clone gets seven extra in-game features, in this game only, specified in `spec\NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES.md`.** The owner's list: timeouts, replay challenges, QB keepers and onside kicks according to NFL rules; playbooks from Madden football as optional uses (research the current 2026 Madden guides, then implement in this game's style); a Franchise mode (a Madden feature, researched then implemented in this clone's style); penalties with accept or decline according to NFL rules. Decisions: (1) The features are requirements of the finished clone, kept in a companion file, never in the starter kit, `shared\`, the playbooks or another game (CLAUDE.md rule 0.13 points to the file, nfl4 only). (2) Classic stays exact: a setting "Rules" (Classic or N. F. L. rules); the S8.1 conformance tests run in Classic; S7.3b and S7.3c record a golden transcript of a seeded Classic game and the extras must never change it. (3) The classic engine is built with nine hooks (the file's section 5: one period clock, a play as choose, resolve and apply with an after-outcome hook, menus as data, a kick-off kind, a state record with an extras slot, one Rules object, a rating slot per player, outcome tables as data with a modifiers argument) so the extras need no rewrite; S7.3b's NEXT_PROMPT names them. (4) A new phase PX with sessions SX.0 (rules and Madden research from official sources, read-only, can run in parallel) to SX.9 (integration) sits between P7 and P8; the Windows release waits for it unless the owner says otherwise. (5) Accepted defaults (Q-030, file section 15): QB keepers are read as designed quarterback runs under the NFL rules for a runner (NQ1); N. F. L. rules mode uses a real 15-minute clock calibrated to the original's plays per quarter (NQ3); overtime stays sudden death (NQ4); Classic is the only mode until the extras are finished and tested, then "N. F. L. rules" is the recommended default (NQ2); a handful of style playbooks assigned to the 32 teams (NQ5); the minimum Franchise first (NQ6). (6) Every NFL rule in the file is tagged SRC-OFFICIAL, SRC-SECONDARY or UNVERIFIED (today only search results were read: the 2026 rule changes, which say an onside kick may be declared at any time and say nothing new about timeouts or challenges); SX.0 replaces them with the rulebook. (7) Madden: inspiration only, no EA text or data copied, README and About say Madden NFL is a trademark of Electronic Arts and the project is not affiliated. (8) GAME_SPEC 1.2 (a pointer in 17.4). Wrong if: S7.3b finds a hook that cannot be built without changing Classic (the golden test fails), SX.1 finds the real clock cannot keep the original's range of 29 to 36 counted plays per quarter, SX.0 finds the rules differ from the outline in a way that breaks a requirement, or the owner reads "QB keepers" or any other line differently (change section 2 and 15 of the file).
- **D-041 (2026-10-14, OWNER DECISIONS at PX SX.1, asked once, one at a time, each with a recommended answer; the owner took all three recommendations) The extras' three open choices.** (1) Overtime in N. F. L. rules mode: the original's sudden death stays in both modes (NQ4 confirmed); the 2026 NFL overtime (both teams get a possession, a regular-season tie) is an optional companion, not built. N. F. L. rules mode takes from Rule 16 only the period lengths (10 minutes regular season, 15 play-offs, repeated until a score), the timeout counts and "no coach's challenge in overtime". (2) The onside kick question is asked only when it makes sense: the player's team trails in the third or fourth quarter, or leads by 1 to 8 points with at most 5:00 left in the fourth; a setting "Onside question: always" (off by default) asks at every kick-off and safety kick; never in Classic. (3) Franchise before the first Windows release: the core only (a league that lasts across seasons, history, ratings with bounded effect, progression and retirement, the draft, the spoken to-do list and news: N6.1 to N6.5, N6.9, N6.10); contracts with the salary cap and free agency, trades, injuries, coordinators and the job rating follow the release as updates (NQ6 answered; the release gate of NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES section 14 and NX-R3 changed). Wrong if: the owner later wants the NFL overtime, a different onside rule, or more Franchise before the release (each is a recorded change in NFL_EXTRAS_DESIGN and this list; nothing built depends on them yet).
- **D-042 (2026-10-14, PX SX.1) The design of the seven extra features: `spec\NFL_EXTRAS_DESIGN.md` version 1.0.** SX.1 ran before S7.3b on the owner's word (the next prompt of SX.0 placed it after S7.7); this is useful because S7.3b now builds the refined hooks directly. Decisions: (1) Hooks (design section 3): H1 names its calls; H2 is five steps (choose, before_snap, the original's snap-resolve-narrate up to a defined narration end point per kind of result, after_outcome, apply), so the original's interleaved draws, speech and pauses stay in their order; H4 has a decision point before every kick-off; H5's state record can be copied (the snapshot for challenges, kept until the next snap); new H10 between plays, H11 added statistics lines, H12 added help, H13 added F1 situation speech; all identities or no-ops in Classic, which consumes no extra random number. (2) The rules table `nfl_rules_2026` (design section 4) holds every NFL number with its citation; calibration start values live apart in a tuning module with their targets. (3) The clock (design section 5): live time per play kind plus a runoff by tempo (normal 27 to 37, hurry-up 12 to 20, milk 37 to 39 seconds), the runoff kept pending until the next snap so a timeout can cut it; out of bounds is a new draw (runs 8, completions 21, keepers 12, scrambles 34 percent, from the play-by-play data); the clock-stop list of Rule 4; the warning at the end of the last down before 2:00; H1's plays_left = time left / 38 for the computer's rules of 5.7; calibration band 29 to 36 counted plays per quarter. The tempo is automatic for both sides. (4) Timeouts and challenges (design 6): a ruling model (the engine knows the truth; a ruling error by category with start rates; clear and obvious at 85 percent; hint remarks that the computer hears as the player does), booth reviews before the result is applied, coach challenges at the next menu with a rollback to the snapshot, the computer's timeout rules in six steps and its challenge rule; the menus refuse anything the rules forbid, so the excess-timeout and illegal-challenge fouls cannot arise. (5) Quarterback runs (design 7): Sneak, Keeper and Draw as rows of the outcome table against the six calls, the scramble as one extra draw after the original's sack test or completion draw, the slide, the computer's sneak on short yardage; start values to calibrate in SX.5. (6) Onside kicks (design 8): the 2026 rule (any free kick, declared, no surprise), 8 percent recovery start value, the computer only when trailing in the fourth quarter (the 2024 to 2026 data). The original's kick-off itself (end-zone catch, run-out question, touch back to the 20) stays in both modes: the 2026 kickoff is not one of the seven features (companion list). (7) Penalties (design 9): 27 foul rows with yards, spots and the 2025 frequency mix; dead pre-snap fouls stop the snap after " hut 1 "; at-snap and live fouls at the narration end point; the accept or decline prompt speaks both outcomes and starts at the better one; the computer decides by points, turnover, first down, field position, down and distance, clock; target 12.8 accepted fouls per game, band 12.0 to 13.6. (8) Playbooks (design 10): Classic plus seven styles, offense and defense chosen separately, a shared catalog, bounded modifiers, a balance band of 45 to 55 percent against Classic; menus of at most 12 items with digits and the letters C, T and S; Save Game moves to the end of the list in N. F. L. rules mode (Classic keeps 8 and 7). (9) Franchise (design 11): chosen at a new season only with N. F. L. rules; ratings 40 to 99 (neutral 70), the defensive slot labels, a `franchise` folder; sessions SX.8a to SX.8f for the core. (10) Settings keys, the F9 Rules menu of 10 items (NQ7), the football.sav extras section after D-027's Variant (marker, format number, one JSON string), the Classic-save and damaged-save rules (design 12). (11) Every new draw and its order (design 15). (12) New spoken texts: `spec\strings_nx.tsv` is the master, `work\make_strings_nx.py` checks it (ids, placeholders, pools, menu length, agreement with the design's foul rows and named ids; it caught four planted faults) and writes `spec\STRINGS_NX.md` (195 texts, 8 remark pools). (13) CUSTOMIZABLE_CONTENT section 13 (remarks.txt, penalties.txt, playbooks, news.txt), nfl4 only. (14) NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES.md version 0.2 (the research corrections and tags, NQ4 to NQ7 answered). GAME_SPEC stays version 1.2 (it describes the original; 17.4 already points to the companion file): only the generated decision count of 18.3 was refreshed (D-040, 40 became D-042, 42; byte copy `work\GAME_SPEC_before_sx1.md`, word diff checked, `work\check_spec_s51.py` PASS). Wrong if: S7.3b cannot place the narration end point without moving a draw or a sentence of the original (then the hook moves, Classic never does); SX.2's calibration cannot reach the band by moving the runoff ranges by 4 seconds; any feature's simulation misses its NFL target band by a wide margin (the start values are then wrong, not the structure).

" and of the " " after the score, in the voice that spoke last, neither logged nor replayed), `saykey` (the echo: purge, then the character unless F1; control characters are not sent, SAPI has nothing to say for them and a screen reader would read their names; S8.2 listens), dy and rady record their site in the lifted code when `pause_log` is set, `GameRandom.trace` (the size of every draw) and `GameRandom.below_single`, `KeyInput.wait_key` (the bare DoEvents loop of the final score), `GameSounds.halftime`, `play_noise_running` and a bound on the celebration's pitch redraw (G-D1). (4) NUMBER TYPES (B-P40, read in the asm, TESTED against the automation library, `clone/tests/test_pag_numbers.py`): the sack and completion tests compare Rnd * 100 as a Single (FSTP float); the yards, the return length and the interception draw multiply a Single Variant by a Variant range with VarMul, so `below_single` rounds the product to a Single before Int; the constant-range draws multiply on the FPU and take Int of the exact product (`below`, exact for every 24-bit Rnd); the incompletion pause stays in Double (its multiplier is an Integer, a Single or a Double depending on how the play was chosen; at most 1/9 s on a set below 1e-5). (5) CLONE RULES OF EARLIER DECISIONS, built: Q23 (a turnover on a two-point try ends it: no spot is drawn, no possession sentence, 0 points, the score and the kick-off by the side that scored the touchdown), Q24 (a safety in overtime ends the game), Q25 (the overtime kick-off sets the receiving side's half), Q26 (the statistics name the period just ended: one less once an overtime has started), Q27 (the Super Bowl presentation once, in place of the final score), Q32 (keypad digits choose in the play menus), Q38 (line 113 always one step faster), Q57 (the play noise stopped after " is way to short"); Q54 comes from the kit's KeyInput unchanged. (6) TWO NEW QUIRKS of the original, found while every path was built and tested, both KEPT: Q61 (the kick-off's play noise loops under the player's run-out question: no PlayTackle comes before it) and Q62 (the return flag L30c left by a kick-off returned for a touchdown takes away the |yards| / 25 pause of the next play that ends in the field of play); GAME_SPEC 1.3 by adding words (`work/spec_v13_s73b.py`, byte copy `work/GAME_SPEC_before_s73b.md`, proof by undoing the seven replacements; `work/check_spec_s51.py` with Q61 and Q62 in its QUIRKS table). (7) G-A2: the safety net acts at every key wait of pag except the run-out question (Q61), counts (`PlayEngine.noise_net`) and logs; 0 in every test game. (8) SCOPE: halftime is BUILT here although the plan left it to S7.3c, because every game passes through it and the golden transcript must not change when S7.3c comes (it is the original's five lines on the S7.2 sound procedures); the Super Bowl presentation is S7.3c's (the stand-in does what pag depends on: the replay list emptied, the log flag on, gtp = 42); the typed quarterback names (Getqbs) are S7.4's and are handed in as `quarterback_names`; football.sav and footplay.txt are S7.5's (a `SavedGame` record in, `save_sink` out, the player's names as a Roster); footballlog.txt is S7.5's (`log_sink`); F8 is S7.4's (`comtog_source`).
