No fact about the original changed, so the spec has no new version (its checker's decision count was refreshed). (1) WHAT WAS BUILT, in `clone/src/nfl4/game`: `season_engine.py` (class `SeasonEngine`: `pog()` = GAME_SPEC 5.14 to 5.16 after a game that was not saved when gtp is not 0, with the game's final score in `Game.m414`; `finplay17()` = 5.17; `super_bowl_presentation(scores)` = superbowl, called by pag in place of the final score (Q27); the sentence builders `week_result`, `playoff_result`, `qualifier_line`, `out_of_the_playoffs`, `super_bowl_sentence`; the `SeasonStore` protocol with `NULL_STORE`; `SeasonStateError`), `playoffs.py` (the 32-entry score table, `original_sort`, `computer_game`, `TeamRef`, `PlayoffState` = footbal4.dat and footbal5.dat in memory with their file values, `division_winners`, `seeds`, `wild_cards`, `end_of_regular_season`), `season_texts.py` (27 texts, each a string of the exe, checked against spec/strings_all.tsv), `SeasonState.playoffs`, `own_team`, `opponent_team` and `set_playoff_opponent()` (the week loop's step 7c), `Game.season_engine`, `Game.pog()`, `Game.finplay17()`, `Game.superbowl()` (the S7.3b stand-in is replaced; it still ends with gtp = 42 and pag sets 0), `Game.season_store`, and `GameSounds.celebration_run()` (8 to 15 celebration sounds; playcel now calls it). (2) ONE CLASS, ONE RECORD (the request that the Franchise mode can wrap it later): the engine is one class whose whole state is the Game's `SeasonState` (week counter, records, play-off pairings and byes); what the original writes to its files at fixed moments (footbal3.dat after a regular week, football.gtp, footbal4.dat after k4, footbal5.dat) goes to a `SeasonStore` that S7.5 implements, in the original's order (`tests/seasonrig.py` `RecordingStore` proves the order and, for example, that football.gtp is not written when the player's team is out after week 16). No hook of the extras is touched (H1 to H11 and H13 exist since S7.3b, H12 comes with S7.4); the golden Classic transcript is unchanged (it plays week 1). (3) DRAWS (G-D2, G-D1, B-P45): every draw keeps the range and the order of its lifted line: a regular week's 15 games draw a free team (division Int(Rnd * 8) + 1 and place Int(Rnd * 4) + 1 together, again while it has played), its score index Int(Rnd * 32) + 1, the second team the same, then its index again while its score equals the first's; a computer game of the play-offs draws the coin Int(Rnd * 2) + 1, the winner's index again while its score is 0, the loser's again while its score is not lower; the Super Bowl runs Int(Rnd * 8) + 8 sounds, each Int(Rnd * 10) + 15 and a pitch Int(Rnd * 40) - 20 again while 0. Every Rnd of these procedures multiplies a CONSTANT, so the product is formed on the FPU and no Single rounding is needed (`work/rnd_check_pog_s36.txt`, `rnd_check_finplay17_s36.txt` and the new `rnd_check_superbowl_s73c.txt`: 0 mismatches of N; the constants of the new procedure: `const_check_superbowl_s73c.txt`, the only misses are the subscript 1 and the `Global <> 0` path test). The redraw loops are bounded (`GameRandom.redraw`): the team draw, the unequal score and both play-off scores; a stuck source ends through the scan and counts a fallback (0 in every seeded season). (4) TEXTS: a regular week's results are `<winner> over the <loser> by the score of <w>, to <l>. ` with Trim$ numbers and no " The "; the division winner and wild card lines `<team>. at <w> and <l>. ` with Trim$ numbers; the play-off results ` The <winner> won over the <loser> by the score of  <w> to <l>. ` with Str$ numbers (a leading space each, so two spaces before the winner's score and `to 0` for a shutout); the line of a team that is out ` The Bears at  7 and  9.  did not make it into the playoffs. ` (two spaces after the full stop); the Super Bowl ` The <winner> have Won the super Bowl!  They beat the <loser> by the score of <w>, to <l>. ` (two spaces after the exclamation mark). Each engine keeps its own headings (Q47: pog's lower-case "division" and "championship  game" with two spaces, finplay17's capital D and "Championship"). (5) D-029 AS BUILT: Q46 is not reproduced (every play-off result of pog's play-off weeks is its own sentence with " The ", each spoken right after its draws; the first A. F. C. wild card result is not lost, the player's own A. F. C. championship result is spoken); Q48 is not reproduced (a conceded Super Bowl, which reaches pog with gtp 20, says " In the Super Bowl. " and " The <opponent> won over the <player's team> by the score of <o> to <p>. ", then finplay17's Super Bowl ending with the celebration, the wait and gtp = 0, and writes football.gtp = 0); Q41 (a tie goes to the opponent), Q42 to Q45, Q47, Q49 to Q51 are kept. (6) WAITS: every wait is the lifted one: a regular week's `clk`, `getkey`, F1 or F2 speak the 17 lines again with say2 (not inrep) and wait again; the end-of-season waits and the rounds' waits use `norep = 1`, `getkey`, F1 or F2 `inrep` (no introduction) and wait again, some with a `clk` first (0x46fbc0, 0x46fd35) and some without (0x46fa4b, 0x47338a, 0x476e33); the Super Bowl ending after a result without the player waits 0.2 s (site 0x483f1a), runs the celebration, and after F1 or F2 replays, waits 5 s (the same site) and runs it again with new draws (Q50); finplay17 starts with its own 0.2 s (site 0x47df85). `ptf` stays 1 when pog returns (the week loop sets 0, as Form_Load does). (7) WHAT THE CALLERS DO (S7.4, S7.5): S7.4's week loop sets tn3, tn4 and vtrnt each week with `SeasonState.set_week_opponent` (1 to 16) or `set_playoff_opponent` (17 to 20), calls `Game.pog()` after a game that was not saved when gtp is not 0, sets ptf = 0, calls `say_stand` (silent above 17, Q51) and waits one key only when the standings were spoken (G-F2), starts a new season when `PogResult.season_over` or gtp is 0 (G-F3, D-029 item 4) and turns a `SeasonStateError` into a restart with a notice (D-024 item 5); S7.5 implements `SeasonStore` (the Variant files of GAME_SPEC 12, via `Records.as_file_values`, `PlayoffState.pairing_values` and `bye_values`) and reads them into the state at the start. An independent review by a fresh Opus 5.5 subagent that read only the code (`work/review_s73c_findings.md`: the port against the lifted pog, finplay17 and superbowl in seven categories) found nothing that would change what the player hears or the season's result (one minor, three cosmetic); it led to one change, the bye teams are checked before a round is drawn and spoken (`SeasonEngine._byes`, two tests added: 115 new tests in all), and to one note kept on purpose: the celebration draws are bare Rnd calls with trace size 0 as S7.2 built them, because the golden Classic transcript holds those sizes. (9) THE PLAN FROM HERE: S7.4 is too big for one session (seven hot-key menus with nested waits, four typed prompts, the start-up and exit sequences, the main menu and the week loop with the season end), so it is SPLIT (CLAUDE.md 3: split huge sessions): S7.4a the key layer so that every global key runs as a nested menu inside any wait (D-018, B-P13), the hot-key menus F5 to F8, F11, F12 and Ctrl with the added help of the extras (H12) and the typed prompts (GetCoach, ChooseTeam, Getqbs, SayScedual with getter, ccn and cqn: GAME_SPEC 4.1 to 4.13 and 8), Opus 5.5 at high; S7.4b the start-up and exit sequences, the main menu, the week loop that calls `Game.pog` (the new season, the standings and their one-key wait, the crowd beds after a game: G-F1, G-F2, G-F3, G-F6, G-A5), Sonnet 5.5 at high. - **D-045 (2026-10-14, P7 S7.4a) The key layer, the seven hot-key menus and the typed prompts; GAME_SPEC stays 1.3.** Nothing needs the owner. No fact about the original changed, so the spec has no new version (its checker's decision count was refreshed). (1) THE KEY LAYER (D-018, B-P13; Form_KeyDown p_42e5e0): `KeyInput.pump()` is Form_KeyDown as well as DoEvents: Alt (18) and F10 (121) become the Escape code "27" with no character and run no menu (0x42e645); every other key sets g and g2 and then calls `key_handler(g)` inside the pump. `game/hotkeys.py` `HotKeys.dispatch` is that handler, with the original's chained tests in their order (F12, F11, F8, F7, F6, F5, Ctrl, each reading g after the menu before), so the menus run inside any wait that pumps (getkey, getkey2, the bare wait, dy, rady, waited sounds, clk), nest when a global key is pressed inside one, and end with clk and g = "112": the interrupted wait ends as F1 and repeats its prompt. A global key typed during a synchronous line runs its menu at the next pump (the first DoEvents of the next wait's clk) and that wait ends at once as F1 (TESTED on the rig, G-K1). `KeyInput.wait_speech` is the `Loop While g = "" And RunningState = 2` of F5 to F7. The Game re-attaches the handler whenever its key input is replaced (the rigs do). (2) F5, F6, F7 as lifted: their texts are GENERATED from the lifted speckeys, readins and SayHints by `work/make_help_texts_s74a.py` into `game/help_texts.py` (103 pieces, each a string of the exe used by its procedure; a test regenerates the module and compares it), F7 ends "... on the play." (D-014); F6 waits 0.3 s between its parts (site 0x42028d), F1 in part 1 starts part 1 again, Escape ends, another key goes to part 2 without the pause; any key in part 2 ends the menu except F1, which starts again at part 1; a key during F5 or F7 stops the speech and ends the menu. (3) F8 (ctog): comtog flips, " comments toggle off " or " comments toggle, ON! " is spoken waited, settings.txt is written (football.vol). (4) Ctrl (adjrate): as lifted (the instruction and the number spoken with the voice that spoke last, neither logged; Up and Down change vr, apply it, purge and speak the number; any other key speaks the instruction again; Enter: clk, F1, settings.txt (footbal0.dat)); vr is kept in -10 to 10 (D-022 item 3) and starts at the first voice's own rate when the setting is "original". (5) F11 (adjvol): part 1 walks the five speakers in the original's volume order, each prompt and answer spoken in that speaker's own voice (say6 with the speaker named, D-022 item 5), steps of 5, " volume maximum " when Up reaches 100, " volume minimum " when Down reaches 0; part 2 the sound volume in steps of 100 (1 dB), " volume lower " at exactly -5000 and " volume minimum " below it (S5.2), crowd 1 at -100 + wavol and crowd 2 ramped up while it runs, the crowd levels set from their BASE values after every change (D-023: no double counting of the entry wavol), the foot0 demo with play2 (a key stops it and is lost), and at Enter crowd 1 = crwd1 + wavol, crowd 2 = its level before the ramp with the new wavol, the hike buffer at -1600 + wavol, settings.txt written, g = "112" without clk as the original. (6) F12 (ChangeVoice): the 7.4.2 picker once per speaker in the original's order (`game/voice_choice.py`, G-S8): engines, then voices, Escape keeps that speaker's voice; the chosen voice says "Voice set to <name>. This is how this voice will speak in N. F. L. football." in that speaker's role, waited; SAPI's first voice is stored as no saved voice; then clk, F1, settings.txt, and GetSexOf of the game in progress (`Game.play_engine`); with fewer than two voices nothing is asked and F1 is handed back (Q59 not reproduced, D-034). F12 always offers the picker; the first-start choice only when SAPI is the active backend (D-022 item 4) is S7.4b's. (7) H12 (nfl4 only, NFL_EXTRAS_DESIGN 3): `Rules.extra_help(key)` is spoken with say5 after the original text of F5, F6 and F7; ClassicRules returns nothing; the hot keys read `Game.rules`, the program's rules, which `play_game` now uses when it is given none (CLASSIC by default, so the golden Classic transcript is unchanged). No extra feature is built. (8) A GAP FOUND WHILE BUILDING: the speech manager's `is_speaking` answered False for the NVDA and JAWS backends (they had none), so F5 to F7 would have stopped at once under a screen reader; both backends now answer it (NVDA: a call of ours in flight, queued or held; JAWS: before the estimated end of our speech), tested with their fakes (fixesforgames B-S23). (9) OPTIONS (`game/options.py`): the one object the menus change: vr, comtog, the five volumes, wavol, the five voices; `save()` writes settings.txt where the original wrote footbal0.dat or football.vol, and a failure is logged, never raised. app.py builds it with the settings store, the first voice's own rate, the voice list (read the first time F12 is pressed) and the crash guard; without one (the tests) the Game makes defaults that are saved nowhere and share the sounds' AudioSettings, with vr 0 and comtog 0 as before. The Game's `vr_source` and `comtog_source` read it. (10) THE PROMPTS (`game/prompts.py`): getter (F1 speaks the prompt again and empties the text; Shift makes the next key upper case; Backspace speaks and removes the last character; Enter ends; every other key is echoed and appended), ccn and cqn (pure functions), GetCoach, ChooseTeam (the wraps checked in the asm at 0x430b70 and 0x430c86), Getqbs (the own name from footplay.txt when pag read it, else asked and appended; the opponent's asked except for a loaded game whose footplay.txt was read, A-18 kept; a name nobody types is a free surname drawn Int(Rnd * 1708) + 1 again while blank; on acceptance the drawn slot or every equal entry ignoring case is blanked) and SayScedual (the schedule with its pauses at 0x42f30b, 0x42f5c6 and 0x42f673, the question before week 1 with Up, Down and Tab, F1 speaking the whole schedule again, n or Enter on no making a new schedule; the play-off round as one waited text). Typed names follow D-022 items 1 and 2 and G-K5: the original's character of every key that types something (LCase(Chr(KeyCode)), UCase after Shift, so Caps Lock types lower case as the original did: S8.2 watch item), the digit of a keypad key, nothing for a key that types nothing (arrows, F2 to F4, Escape, Tab; the original typed % & ' ( q r s and control characters that the name check removed again), at most 30 stored characters. pag's Getqbs is now this prompt (`Roster.quarterback` holds the last item of footplay.txt, filled by S7.5); a test may still hand the names in. (11) FILES: what these procedures wrote goes through a `SetupStore` (S7.5): footbal1.dat when the team is accepted, footbal2.dat when `makes` runs (`Game.make_schedule`), the "Quarterback:" line and the name appended to footplay.txt; reads come from the state, which S7.5 fills at the week loop. (12) NOT BUILT HERE (S7.4b, with the start-up): the F9 added functions (G-K2; the key layer has the slot `HotKeys.added`), the start-up and exit sequences, the main menu, the week loop, the first-start voice choice, the crowd beds after a game. (13) CHECKS: a call-site inventory by script (`work/callsites_s74a.py`, output `work/callsites_s74a.txt`, B-P48): 255 call sites of the 20 lifted procedures, 159 named by address in the port, 96 explained (60 the yes or no voice walk the picker replaces, 3 its foot0 loop, 30 file writes and 3 file reads), 0 missing. Tests: 85 new (`test_hotkeys` 30, `test_prompts` 41, `test_prompt_texts` 5, `test_options` 7, one each in `test_nvda_backend` and `test_jaws`), each file first alone with a time limit; the rig types a key during a menu's speech (("cut", seconds, key)); the whole suite 895 passed in each bitness with numpy blocked, the golden Classic transcript unchanged; the real-thread files whose code path changed (test_app_startup.py and test_keys.py, 19 tests) 30 times per bitness, each run's output in its own file (`work/repeat_s74a/`): 30 of 30 runs passed in 64-bit and 30 of 30 in 32-bit. Wrong if: the owner's playtest (S8.2) finds the Ctrl key, which screen reader users press to silence speech, opening the rate menu too often (the original does it; G-K3), Caps Lock should type capitals, or five pickers in a row too long for F12; S8.1's conformance tests find a menu whose keys or texts differ from GAME_SPEC 4.2 to 4.13.
- **D-046 (2026-10-15, P7 S7.4b) The program: start-up, season set-up, week loop, main menu, what follows a game, the end of a season, the exit, and F9; GAME_SPEC stays 1.3.** Nothing needs the owner. No fact about the original changed (the only difference found, SOUND_DESIGN 4 step 1 against the lifted code, is a correction of that companion file), so the spec has no new version. (1) THE PROGRAM (`game/program.py`, class `Program`, run by `Game.run`; Form_Load p_486990 read in full, 494 lifted lines, the address of every call beside it): `startup` (the crowd beds, then the intro once to its end and looped, the hike and the play noise made, the title, the notices), `season_setup` (a loaded season, else GetCoach, ChooseTeam, makes, gtp = 1 and the football.gtp write), `week_loop` (the week line, the log, today's game, the main menu, the choice), `play_and_after` (pag, pog, the standings), `season_over`, `exit_program`. (2) THE FILES are one protocol (`game/program_store.py`, `ProgramStore`): first start, saved game present, the season loaded into the SeasonState once at the start (the original re-reads football.gtp, footbal2.dat and footbal3.dat at every pass; the state holds exactly those values and the engines keep it in step through the SeasonStore and SetupStore), the log opened once per run, footplay.txt's roster, football.sav taken (read and deleted, as pag does), Save Game's write, the deletion of the season files. S7.5 implements it over the real files; until then `MemoryProgramStore` keeps everything for one run, so a season, a saved game and the log live as long as the process and nothing is written to disk but settings.txt: the clone is playable but not yet persistent, and nothing is released before S7.5. (3) THE FIRST START is the start that made settings.txt (`SettingsStore.created`), the original's missing footbal0.dat (GAME_SPEC 3 step 6): " Welcome to ", the intro stops (Stop, DoEvents), the synchronous " " waits for the welcome, the title (rule 0.5.1) in place of " Jim's N. F. L. Football, version 4. ", the notices, 0.2 s, the F5 key list, ONE added sentence (" Press F9 for the added functions: the speech settings, the sounds and the about text. ", a CLONE text, GAP_REGISTER section 8: F9 is in no help text of the original and a blind player must find it), 0.2 s, and the five voices (F12's pickers) only when SAPI is the active engine (D-022 item 4: `takes_roles`). A later start says only the title, once, after the intro has played. A start that is quit during the voice choice does not repeat the welcome (the original repeats it because footbal0.dat is written at the end of ChangeVoice; settings.txt is made before the game starts); recorded, not worth a second marker file. (4) THE WEEK LOOP as lifted: the week line " This is week <Str$ n> of Jim's N. F. L. football " (say2), the log's " This is week #<n> ... ", the intro stopped (the week branch has two DoEvents around Set Nothing, the first start's copy one; kept), the synchronous " ", the opponent from the schedule; play-off weeks " This is the " then the week name kept as the menu's first line (L118, the name with a space in front, not the "This is the"); today's game (the opponent first when vtrnt = 1) with Str$ numbers; the main menu says " <coach> select " then the current choice only, Up and Down wrap, digits 1 to 4 set the choice (the keypad's too, Q32 not reproduced, D-027), Enter runs it, F1 and every hot-key menu repeat from the week text with the choice kept, any other key is echoed and the choice starts again at 1; Escape does nothing; a saved game skips the menu after the two lines; L118 is reset in a regular week (the original's local never survives its one game; the clone loops, B-P49). Choices 2 and 3 return to the week loop and so the week line is said again (D-025). (5) AFTER A GAME (G-F2, G-A5, Q53): Save Game ends the program with no pog and no standings (D-024); otherwise crwd1 = -1200, pog unless pag left gtp 0 (a Super Bowl game), ptf = 0, the standings, and the wait (the voice's speech or a key; stop the speech; one key when none came; F1 says them again) only when SayStand spoke (`say_stand` now returns whether it did); the crowd beds start again at the top of the next pass; a season that is over (gtp 0) has its files deleted by the store, says " The season is over. A new season begins. " (draft, GAP_REGISTER G-F3), resets the SeasonState in place (`SeasonState.reset_for_new_season`: default coach, first team, week 1, no schedule, records 0, no pairings or byes; the engine's player_won flag too) and runs the new-season set-up, then the week loop shows week 1; the log stays open. (6) THE EXIT (G-F6, SOUND_DESIGN 4): the files are deleted when gtp is 0, foot0 (waited), the thank-you (waited) with the crowd beds still playing, as the lifted code does (0x48a54f, 0x48a59a, then the release 0x48a5ed); SOUND_DESIGN 4 step 1 had the release first and is corrected; then every sound stops, Beep, dy(0.1), Beep, dy(0.1), the closing sentence, dy(0.2), the end. The closing sentence is queued and the program waits for the end of the speech or any key (B-X13; the original waits and nothing cuts it), with the hot keys switched off so a key only ends it; `Game.program` holds the running Program (its `ending` says "exit" or "saved"). Closing the window unwinds with no exit sequence. (7) F9 (`game/functions.py`, G-K2): the key runs inside every wait like F5 to F12 (`HotKeys.added`), clears the key, stops the speech, opens one spoken menu of three items and ends with clk and g = F1. Speech settings: the speech output (SAPI 5 voice only, NVDA only, JAWS only, automatic), the speech rate (the value Ctrl changes, -10 to 10, Enter = the first voice's own rate), the pitch, the letter keys of the lists, the five voices (F12's pickers), restore, the settings folder; sounds: the sound volume (wavol, Left and Right in steps of 5 decibels, a sample of F11's demo sound at the new level, Enter = full), listen to the 27 sounds one by one at the player's volume, reload, check, the sounds folder, restore the volume, restore one sound, restore all (one confirming Enter), and as the first item the sentence about a sound system that did not start (G-A4); about (rule 0.5). No export item: the game has no generated sound but the exit beep. Customize (7.9) joins in S7.7. A menu that raises is caught, logged and spoken as "Something went wrong", never the end of the game. (8) THE TESTS: `tests/programrig.py` (the Game on the virtual clock with the real sound layer, a `RigStore`, scripted keys with a policy, a stand-in pag whose outcomes the test scripts and a stand-in pog that moves the week), `test_program_start.py` 15, `test_program_menu.py` 29, `test_program_game.py` 15, `test_program_exit.py` 10, `test_program_texts.py` 4, `test_functions.py` 33, `test_pause_lengths.py` 7 (G-S2: the snap count's length at the rates); an independent read of the port against the lifted Form_Load by a fresh subagent (`work/review_s74b_findings.md`) found ONE defect, fixed and tested: the keypad's 5 to 9 (and 0) were taken as choices 5 to 9 and raised a KeyError, while the original tests only "1" to "4" (the keypad's 1 to 4 count, Q32); the other differences it listed are decided ones (notices spoken after the title, a clk and no hot keys before the closing sentence, the no-voice exit in app.py) or inaudible (the intro buffer's +1 Hz, D-023); the skeleton's `test_flow_skeleton.py` (7 tests) is gone with the skeleton; `test_app_startup.py` gained the first-start test and a fixture (a settings.txt that exists and a 0.05 s foot0.wav of the player's, because the intro jingle's 3.4 s now come before the first word and the windows of those tests close after a second); the rig's `read_key` takes a typed character that no menu asked for (it looped for ever on one left in the queue, B-X26). The call-site inventory of Form_Load by script (`work/callsites_s74b.py`, B-P48): 157 call sites, 75 named by address in the port, 82 explained by a rule, 0 missing; it found three wrong comment addresses and the second DoEvents of the intro stop. (9) HOOKS of the extras (rule 0.13): none built or changed (H12 exists since S7.4a, nothing else is in the program flow); the golden Classic transcript is unchanged; the Rules setting stays out of the menus until PX. Wrong if: the owner's playtest (S8.2) finds the 3.4 s before the title too long (the title could be said over the intro, one line), the added F9 sentence unwanted at the first start (one line), the beds under the thank-you wrong (one line in `exit_program`), or the spoken season-over sentence odd; S8.1's menu-flow test finds a difference from GAME_SPEC 3 and 4.
- **D-047 (2026-10-15, P7 S7.5) The player's files: the real stores, the log, football.sav, footplay.txt and the healing; GAME_SPEC stays 1.3.** Nothing needs the owner. No fact about the original changed, so the spec has no new version (its checker's decision count was refreshed). (1) WHAT WAS BUILT, in `clone/src/nfl4/data/files/` (one module for the Variant records and one for each file, each with pure encode and decode functions over bytes or text and ONE damaged-file error): `variant.py` (VB6 Variant records: written Integer, Long, Single and Double, read also Empty, Currency, Date, Boolean and Byte; `FileDamaged`), `coachteam.py` (footbal1.dat), `schedulefile.py` (footbal2.dat: Integer records for the six division games, Single for the ten others, as the original writes them), `weekfile.py` (football.gtp), `recordsfile.py` (footbal3.dat), `pairingsfile.py` (footbal4.dat), `byesfile.py` (footbal5.dat), `savedgame.py` (football.sav: the 36 values and the name in the original's order, D-027 item 3's opening receiver after them; an original's file without it is accepted), `playernames.py` (footplay.txt), `speechlog.py` (footballlog.txt) and `store.py` (`FileProgramStore`: the ProgramStore of D-046, the SeasonStore of D-044 and the SetupStore of D-045 over the files, plus `write_player_names`, `log_line`, `take_notices` and `close`); `data/healing.py` got `read_bytes` and `write_bytes_atomic`. app.py makes the store (`paths.user_data_dir()` and `paths.customize_dir()`, the first-start flag stays `SettingsStore.created`) and hands it to the Game as program store, season store, set-up store (`game.season_store = game.setup_store`) and log sink (`game.log_sink = store.log_line`), and as a notice source; `MemoryProgramStore` stays the tests' store. (2) WHERE: the season files, football.sav and footballlog.txt are in the data folder (D-024 item 3), footplay.txt and footplay.previous.txt in its `customize` folder (CUSTOMIZABLE_CONTENT 6 and 10; S7.7 builds the rest of that folder). Every write is a whole file through a temporary file and a rename; footbal4.dat and footbal5.dat are replaced, never overwritten in place (the original deletes them first). (3) HEALING (D-024 item 5, G-F5, playbook 7.10), decided once, when the store is made, so its notices come with the start-up notices and `has_saved_game` and `load_season` are plain answers: a MISSING footbal1.dat is a new season (the first start) and says nothing, season files left behind without it are removed quietly; a season file that cannot be used (footbal2.dat, football.gtp, a footbal3.dat from week 2 on, footbal4.dat from week 17 with the right number of games and the player's team in one of them, footbal5.dat in weeks 17 and 18; a record cut short, a value outside its range, an unknown type) gives <name>.damagedN (three kept), every season file removed, ONE notice (" The season files could not be read and were set aside. A new season begins. ") and a new season; the player's footplay.txt stays. A football.sav that cannot be read, or that belongs to no season, is set aside the same way with G-F5's notice (" The saved game could not be read and was set aside. "). A file held by another program is not damaged: nothing is copied or deleted, a new season starts this time and the store writes nothing this run (one notice, " The season files are in use by another program. A new season starts this time, and it will not be kept. "). A folder or file that cannot be written is told once (" The game could not write its files, so the season and the saved game will not be kept. ") and the game goes on. (4) TWO DECISIONS AGAINST THE LETTER OF THE ORIGINAL'S RULES, both about zero (B-P51, found by the fuzz test): the original treats a first value of 0 in footbal1.dat as "no season" and an Empty value reads as 0; that rule only ever met the empty file its own runtime created (B-P31), and in a clone it let a zeroed or emptied file end a season without a word. So a first value of 0 in footbal1.dat is a damaged file, a football.gtp that is an Empty Variant is damaged, and only a real 0 in football.gtp (a season that ended and was not cleaned up) is cleaned up, quietly, with the names kept as footplay.previous.txt. (5) footplay.txt (D-025 d, Q8, Q36): the text is read as UTF-8 with or without a BOM, UTF-16 with a BOM or the Windows code page; it is split at its headings (compared ignoring case and spaces), a section is good when it holds exactly its number of names and each name is 1 to 30 characters without a comma, a semicolon or a control character (every pool surname passes, tested); the good sections are kept, a wrong or missing one is left empty in the Roster and the SET-UP draws it again (`setup.adopt_partial_roster`: the good names blank the pool, the missing sections are drawn in the original's order, the quarterback of the file stays), pag writes the repaired file once (`roster_sink` = `store.write_player_names`, called when `own_names_drawn`; the original's write at 0x455908 to 0x456f0b), a copy of the file with a problem is kept as footplay.txt.damagedN and one notice says so (" The file footplay.txt had a problem. The part that was wrong got new random names, and a copy of the file was saved. ", or when nothing was usable " ... could not be read. It was replaced with new random names ... "); a file that is missing or complete changes nothing; a file without the quarterback (or with a wrong Quarterback section) makes Getqbs ask for him again (`prompts.getqbs`: asked when `own_from_file` is empty), and `append_quarterback` replaces the Quarterback section, never doubling it. At the end of a season `delete_season_files` first copies footplay.txt to footplay.previous.txt (replaced at each season end, never touched when there are no names, so calling it twice loses nothing: the program calls it at the end of the season and the exit calls it again when gtp is 0). (6) Save Game: `save_sink` may return False (the file could not be written): pag then says " The game could not be saved. " (CLONE, `program_texts.SAVE_FAILED`), does not say " Game Saved ", does not end and returns to the play menu; a sink that returns nothing is a save as before (the tests' list.append). The saved game is read at the start of a game and deleted (`take_saved_game`: pag's read and Kill 0x43afa4 and 0x43b884); one that went bad after the start is set aside then. (7) THE LOG: `FileProgramStore.open_log(fresh)` opens footballlog.txt (a new file when fresh, else appended; a file over 1 MB is started again, also while it grows), `log_line` writes UTF-8 with CRLF and flushes at every line (the process may end through `os._exit`); a write error switches the log off for the run, logged once. (8) NOTICES: the store's notices are spoken with the start-up notices; the Program now asks its notice sources again at the top of every week announcement (a write that failed in the set-up) and before every game (a footplay.txt problem or a saved game that went bad), which says nothing when there is nothing to say (`Program.week_announcement`, `Program.play_and_after`). (9) HOOKS of the extras (rule 0.13): none built or changed. H5's `SavedGame` record is what football.sav now holds (with D-027 item 3's opening receiver); H7's `Roster.ratings` is neither read nor written; the Rules setting is in no menu; the golden Classic transcript is unchanged. (10) TESTS (each new file first alone with a time limit, B-X7): `test_files_variant` 10, `test_files_season_files` 48, `test_files_savedgame` 11, `test_files_playernames` 22, `test_files_speechlog` 9, `test_files_store` 88 (every state, every season file by six kinds of damage, locked by another program through a real sharing violation, read-only, an unwritable folder, 150 random corruptions), `test_files_season_run` 14 (whole seeded seasons written through a tee of the recording store and the real store and read back by a second store after every engine call; the recorded events replayed into a fresh folder give the same bytes), `test_files_game` 9 (the real pag with the real footplay.txt, the quarterback, Save Game and its failure), `test_files_program` 11 (the program over the real files on the rig: a new season, a season in week 5, a play-off week, a damaged season, a saved game, Save Game, a season that ends, a footplay problem, an unwritable folder), 3 in `test_app_startup` and 1 in `test_prompts`; the call-site inventory of the files by script (`work/callsites_s75.py`: 160 file statements of 21 lifted procedures, each explained by the module that does it now); 1228 pass in each bitness with numpy blocked. (11) THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW (a fresh Sonnet 5.5 subagent that read only the code, the spec's file section and this decision; its eleven findings were all real: ten are fixed, one only in part, each with a test, and one is noted): a saved game made at the start of quarter 3 or of an overtime period has a play counter of 0 (`start_period(0)`), which the reader refused, so that valid save would have been set aside as damaged (B-P52; the range is 0 to 200); a season file that could not be read for any reason other than damage (held by another program, or any other OSError) no longer judges or deletes the saved game, no longer lets a temporary new season delete the real files at its season end (`delete_season_files` does nothing while the files are held) and no longer lets `write_player_names` or `append_quarterback` write while the names file was unreadable (`_hold_names`); every season file that is there is kept as a `.damagedN` copy before a damaged or stale season is removed (not only the one that was being read); the names stay where they are when their previous.txt copy cannot be made; every writer builds its bytes inside a guard, so a value that cannot be encoded or a lone surrogate in a name is a failed write (one notice) and never an exception; `take_saved_game` survives a file that vanishes or cannot be read; a loaded game whose footplay.txt has no quarterback asks for him but keeps the saved opponent's name (`prompts.getqbs`); `write_bytes_atomic` flushes and fsyncs before the rename. Not changed, noted: a read-only season file that cannot be removed reappears at each start with the notice, and a `.tmp` file left by a crash stays. (12) Wrong if: the owner's playtest (S8.2) finds the notices too long or too many at a start, a season restart after a damaged file too eager (a damaged footbal3.dat could restore zero records instead; one line in `_read_season`), or footplay.txt's 30-character limit too tight for names a player copies in; S8.3's resource-damage tests find a file kind that raises; S9.2's smoke test finds the data folder not writable in a built exe.
- **D-048 (2026-10-15, P7 S7.7) The customization files and their healing: teams.txt, divisions.txt, surnames.txt, banter.txt, the sounds folder, the F9 Customize item; GAME_SPEC stays 1.3.** Nothing needs the owner. No fact about the original changed, so the spec has no new version (its checker's decision count was refreshed). (1) WHAT WAS BUILT: `clone/src/nfl4/data/customlists.py` (the format and the healing of ONE list file, generic: no word of football in it; `parse_list`, `render_list`, `ListFile.load` that never raises and answers one of the statuses ok, created, reset, damaged, locked, unwritable, failed, `Problems` that counts entries, not messages), `data/customize.py` (`Customization`: the four lists, the `Snapshot` of their values, the sentences, `reload`, `check`, `reset`, `tend`, `take_notices`), `game/banter_markup.py` (the pieces of a banter line and the parser), `game/banter_pool.py` (the 142 built-in lines as pieces, `build_pool`), `game/banter.py` (the draw over the pool and the playing of a line), `audio/library.py` `pick_file` and `audio/engine.py` `play_file` (a player's sound file named by its place), `game/sounds.py` `play_file`, `Game.apply_customization`, `League.rename`, `NamePool.replace_names`, `game/functions.py` `customize_menu`, app.py (the Customization is made at the start and is a notice source), `clone/CUSTOMIZE.md` (the player's guide, with an example for every file). (2) THE FORMAT is CUSTOMIZABLE_CONTENT 10 as drafted (D-032 item 7): plain UTF-8 (a byte order mark, UTF-16 with one and the Windows code page are read), a header `# NFL Football 4 <list>, format 1.` on the first non-blank line, `#` notes, one `ID = value` per line, the ID not case sensitive and spaces around it ignored; first line of an ID wins, a repeated ID, an unknown ID, a line without the shape and an empty value are problems; section 14 of that file records the rest. (3) NAMES: 1 to 30 characters, no comma, semicolon or control character; the original data passes (tested); a team name that another entry already took ignoring case sends the later entry back to its original name, and an original name a player's name took becomes `<name> 2` (3, ...), in two passes in the order of the IDs, for teams and for divisions (a property test over 120 random files); surnames may repeat; counts never change. (4) THE BANTER: IDs B001 to B142 built in from `banter_lines.py` (written as markup by script, never typed), a player's line replaces, `off` removes, a new ID up to B9999 adds; the final markup words are `[name]`, `[opponent quarterback]`, `[bleep]`, `[sound file]`, `[pause]`, `[pause seconds]`, `[then]`, `[faster]`, `[one of: a | b | c]`; limits 500 characters, 20 pieces, 20 choices, pauses 0.1 to 10 seconds and 30 seconds a line, a sound name that is a name (no folder, no character a file name cannot have, no Windows device name such as CON or NUL), a unknown word spoken as text and counted, any other fault makes the line bad; a player's line that is exactly the original's markup IS the original. The draw is the original's over the pool (size in place of 142, lines told apart by number, `[one of:]` drawn after the pause in line order), a pool with nothing on says nothing, F8 silences everything; the playing keeps the original's three `clk` (before the line, after each group and once more after a one-group line), and one test per original line proves the same order of clk, say with its rate step, bleep and the same draw sizes as the lifted routine. (5) THE PLAYER'S SOUND: `customize/sounds/<file>`, the one-shot slot at the sound volume, waited like the bleep, a key does not cut it, a file that cannot be used plays nothing and gives the library's one notice (G-A6). (6) HEALING (7.10): every list by empty, random bytes, UTF-16 without a mark, over a megabyte, over eight megabytes (moved, not copied), a newer format, no header, another list's header, zero bytes, deleted, folder deleted, read-only (a fine one is read, a damaged one is not replaced), held open (not damage, nothing written), a bad line in the middle (counted), cut in half (not damage: the header is there; the rest uses the originals), and a fuzz of 300 corruptions; footplay.txt and the sound files are never touched by any of it (byte for byte in the fuzz); `<name>.damagedN` three kept; a missing file or folder is made again silently when the program next asks for notices (each week and before each game) and the loaded values stay in use; reset keeps the player's file as `<name>.before-reset`. (7) F9 now has four items (speech, sounds, customize, about); Customize has open folder, reload, check, reset one list, reset all (one confirming Enter); an action that raises says so and leaves the menu open. (8) footplay.txt is left as S7.5 made it (no header note: the original reads its layout); the check item reads it and says its problems, never changes it. (9) THE KIT: `customlists.py`, its test and `tests/filedamage.py` are generic and wait in fixesforgames section C to go back into the kit at the end of the game (D-037 item 10), with the pattern of `customize.py` as the model for the next game. (10) TESTS (each new file first alone): `test_customlists` 41, `test_banter_markup` 18, `test_banter_custom` 159 (one per original line), `test_customize` 40, `test_customize_damage` 56, `test_customize_game` 15, `test_customize_review` 21, 3 in `test_app_startup`, `test_functions` changed for four items; the golden Classic transcript unchanged. (11) HOOKS of the extras (rule 0.13): none built or changed; the Rules setting is in no menu. (12) THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW (a fresh Sonnet 5.5 subagent that read only the code and the contract after the tests were green; 14 findings, each checked against the code, 13 fixed with a test each in `test_customize_review.py`, `test_customlists.py` and `test_banter_markup.py`, one noted): a reset wrote the originals over the player's file even when the copy of it could not be made, and a second reset replaced the only copy with the originals (now nothing is written unless the copy was made, a file that already is the built-in text needs no copy, and three copies are kept: `.before-reset`, `.before-reset2`, `.before-reset3`); the reset sentences lost what the reload healed in other files (now said); a reload with a file held by another program, unreadable or damaged and not replaceable swapped the running game back to the originals (now the loaded values stay, `Snapshot.kept`, and the sentence says so; only the very first load uses the originals); the unwritable sentence was said twice or blamed files that were written; check said "no problems" of a file it did not read; an unreplaceable damaged file pushed the player's older copies out of the three (one copy of the same bytes); words after the version on the header line reset the file; a file that vanished between the test and the read was called locked; the superscript device names COM1 to LPT3; the sounds of one line could hold the game for 40 minutes (the player's sounds of a line are cut at 30 seconds together, like the pauses); a name of invisible characters; a tab inside a value (now a space). Not changed: the 1,000,000-byte limit of a list file (a banter file of thousands of 500-character lines would be set aside as damaged, with its copy; `CUSTOMIZE.md` says one megabyte). (13) Wrong if: the owner's playtest (S8.2) finds the F9 sentences too long, the markup words hard to remember (they are plain words and one place, `banter_markup.py`), the default `banter.txt` too long to read with a screen reader (its notes are 16 lines), or a name that collides given a number it should not have; S8.1 finds a difference of the default banter from the original; S8.3's stress tests find a file kind that raises.
- **D-049 (2026-10-15, PX SX.2, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The real clock, the timeouts and the two-minute warning of the N. F. L. rules mode; the Rules setting with its F9 menu; the extras section of football.sav; remarks.txt with the timeout pool.** Nothing needs the owner. GAME_SPEC stays 1.3 (it describes the original; only its checker's generated decision count was refreshed). (1) WHAT WAS BUILT, in `clone/src/nfl4/extras/` (a package the classic engine reaches only through the hooks of `game/rules.py`): `rules_2026.py` (the rules table's clock, period and timeout groups with their rule citations), `tuning.py` (the calibration values with their targets and history), `state.py` (ExtrasRecord: the clock, the timeouts, the last play, the remark history, plain data in `GameState.extras`, JSON for football.sav, `load_saved`), `clock.py` (NflPeriodClock: the H1 calls from seconds; the rules of time as functions), `timeouts.py` (units, counts, refusals, charges and the computer's six rules), `nfl_rules.py` (NflRules: the hooks of the clock and the timeouts), `remarks.py` (the remark pools of remarks.txt; the TO pool), `rules_menu.py` (F9's Rules item), `saved.py` (which rules a loaded saved game is played with), `nx_texts.py` (GENERATED from `spec/strings_nx.tsv` by the new `work/make_nx_texts.py`). (2) ENGINE SEAMS, each a no-op in Classic, the golden Classic transcript unchanged in both bitnesses: H2 `Rules.after_tackle(state)` in `field_play_end` after the tackle sentence and sound, before the crowd fade and the gain sentence (so the out-of-bounds sentence follows the tackle sentence, design 5.2; no original draw lies between, so the new draws keep design 15's order); H3 a `letter` per `MenuItem` ("" in Classic) and `Rules.menu_action(state, action)` for an item whose action is neither a play nor Save Game (`pag._menu`: done = the menu from the top, refused = the menu stays at the item); H1 `before_snap()` may return True (the pending runoff ran the period out) and the engine then goes to `period_checks`, the next-play step without counting a play (next_play is now `after_play` plus `period_checks`, the same order); H1 `warning_given()` after the warning's sentence and whistle; H4's kind "period over" sends `kick_off` to `next_play`; `Rules.for_game()` gives each game its own rules object (Classic: itself); `Game.say_clock` (sgc's ending: say5, clk, g = F1) and `gameclock.clock_sentence` (sgc's sentence) were factored out unchanged for the real clock's F4. (3) THE CLOCK as designed (section 5) with these as-built choices: L is drawn at the narration end point of every play (H2 `after_outcome`), scores, tries (0) and kick-offs included; R once per play in H10, guarded so the second H10 of the quarter statistics' down sentence draws nothing; the warning follows a play whose L crosses 2:00, and a runoff that would cross it stops the clock at exactly 2:00; the period ends at 0 at the next-play step, or at the snap when the pending runoff runs it out (the menu said " the clock is running with <n> seconds left. " first, C9); a score as time runs out ends the half or the game after the try with no kick-off in quarters 2 and 4, and in quarters 1 and 3 the try and the kick-off come at 0:00 and the quarter ends after the return (the NFL would kick off at the start of the next quarter: a simplification that keeps the original's period flow, on SX.9's playtest list); a hurry-up offense with at least 6 seconds left snaps with 1 second left; the computer reads plays_left = ceil(left / 38) and the last play as design 5.6, whose example "fewer than 7 plays when fewer than 266 seconds" is the rounded-down reading: with the design's own "rounded up" it is 228 seconds or less (the formula kept, the design corrected by a note); F4 speaks the time at the end of the last play with the original's sentence; F1 adds " the clock is running " or " the clock is stopped " and the timeouts. (4) THE TIMEOUTS as designed (6.3): the menus end with " 8. Time Out " and " 9. Save Game " (offense) or " 7. Time Out " and " 8. Save Game " (defense) when timeouts are on; T and S make their item current like a digit and Enter takes it, so no single key calls a timeout or saves (the key characters of F4 and F5 are "s" and "t" in this key model, B-W6, and the letters are compared only after every code the menu handles); the two-point try keeps the original's items 1 to 5 (the try is untimed); refusals " you have no time outs left. " and " you have already called a time out. " leave the menu at the item; a granted one plays foot11 through the original's PlayTackle(8), says " time out called by the <team>. " and the timeouts left with the log flag on, and a TO remark with chance 1 in 3 (no draw with comments off); u = 3 to 8 seconds of the pending runoff but never the period's last second; the timeouts are announced at the first kick-off of each half and overtime unit, before the kick-off set-up (where H4 is; the design said after it); rule 1's challenge clause waits for SX.6; the player's side is never decided by the computer. (5) CALIBRATION (design 5.7, NX1's done-when): `work/calibrate_clock_sx2.py` plays whole games with the real engine and both sides played by the computer (`clone/tests/nflrig.py` SimEngine and RecordingRules); 10,000 seeded games (seeds 1 to 10,000, `work/calibrate_clock_sx2_10000.txt`, the run on the final code): 34.03 counted plays per regulation quarter (band 29 to 36; quarters 30.86, 36.98, 31.03, 37.25), quarters 2 and 4 carry 6.17 more (band 2 to 7), 26.45 seconds per counted play (band 26 to 30), 0 property problems (no negative clock, every period ending at 0, one warning per warning period, the timeouts within their limits), the redraw fallbacks and the play-noise net 0, 336 games with overtime. Moves from the start values, each recorded in `tuning.py`: the normal runoff 27 to 37 became 30 to 40 (+3 seconds; the start values gave 25.07 seconds per counted play; 40 is the play clock); rule 2's pending runoff "more than 10" became "more than 12", rule 3's defense threshold 40 became 60 seconds per timeout held, rule 6's chance 1 in 80 became 1 in 140: 3.76 charged timeouts per team per game (target 3.85), quarters 1 and 3 0.20 each (0.2), quarter 2 1.78 and quarter 4 1.57 (about 1.7), overtime 0.015; accepted as close. Q-030's NQ3 is answered by this evidence. (6) SETTINGS AND F9: settings.txt `rules = classic` or `nfl` and `nx_timeouts = on` or `off` (defaults classic and on: NQ2 unchanged until SX.9); F9 has five items (Rules before About; its help says five); the Rules menu shows only the items whose feature exists (now 1 Rules, 2 Time outs, 3 Reset the rules to the defaults; SX.3 to SX.7 insert theirs before Reset), Enter or Space changes an item and says its new value and " the new rules apply from the next game. ", Left and Right change it, the digits go to an item, F1 says its help (new text NXM-36), a switch says " used with the N. F. L. rules " while Rules is Classic; every change is saved at once; `Game.apply_rules_setting` sets the program's rules for the next game and tells the customization whether the extras' files are in use; a game in progress keeps its rules (the added help of F5 to F7 reads the running game's rules). `SpokenMenu` got two options, `space_enters` and `digits_jump`, off by default (generic: for the kit refresh). (7) FOOTBALL.SAV (design 12.3): after D-027's Variant, a String Variant "NX-EXTRAS" (type 8, a two-byte length, the bytes), the Integer 1 and a String with the ExtrasRecord as UTF-8 JSON (`data/files/variant.py` `pack_string`, `next_string`, `peek_type`; `data/files/savedgame.py`); a Classic save is the S7.5 file byte for byte; a marker with another format, a record that is not UTF-8 or a cut tail keeps the marker and drops the record; anything else after the game is a damaged file as before; the record is used only when it is whole (every value in its range; a nested-bracket bomb is a ValueError, B-X33); a loaded game keeps the switch and the record it was saved with (C1); a Classic save played with the N. F. L. rules starts with full timeouts, the clock at the period's length minus 28 seconds per counted play (at least 1:00), no announcement, and the warning counted as given at 2:00 or less; a Classic program asks " this saved game was played with the N. F. L. rules. do you wish to play it with those rules? " once before such a game (yes first; Up, Down, Tab, y, n, Enter, F1; the setting stays Classic); when the record cannot be used and the N. F. L. rules play it, " the saved rules could not be read. the game goes on with fresh time outs and a fresh clock. " is said once. (8) REMARKS.TXT (CUSTOMIZABLE_CONTENT 13 and 14): one more list of `data/customlists.py` with the same healing, read, made, checked and reset only while the Rules are the N. F. L. rules (`Customization(extras=)`, `use_extras`; with Classic it is never opened); it holds the pools of the features that exist (TO01 to TO05 now; each later session adds its pool and a player's file without it uses its defaults); a line is banter markup plus the pool's placeholders `<team>`, `<coach>`, `<quarterback>` (an unknown one is spoken as written and counted); [name] draws a banter name only when the line has it; a draw is never one of the last three spoken in the game when more than three lines are on (the history is in the record); F8 silences the pool before any draw. (9) TEXTS: fourteen new rows of `spec/strings_nx.tsv` (NXH-01 to NXH-08: the F5, F6 and F7 additions of C6, kind help; NXM-36: the Rules menu's help; after the review NXT-11 to NXT-14 and NXC-05: the singular forms and the play-off overtime pair; NXH-01, NXH-02 and NXH-05 reworded), STRINGS_NX 209 texts, `work/make_strings_nx.py` accepts the kind help (`work/add_help_rows_sx2.py` and `work/fix_rows_sx2.py` changed the rows); every text of the extras comes from the generated module, none is typed. (10) TESTS (each new file first alone with a time limit): `test_nx_clock` 81, `test_nx_timeouts` 49 (the scripted end-of-half tests of NX1), `test_nx_engine` 22, `test_nx_games` 16 (the properties and bands of 60 simulated games, 7 games through the menus with the player's timeouts), `test_nx_save` 36, `test_nx_settings` 17, `test_nx_remarks` 35 (one for the example of the player's guide), `test_nx_review` 14, one in `test_app_startup` (a start with rules = nfl makes remarks.txt; the Classic start's folder listing already proves it is not made), `test_functions` changed for five F9 items: 271 new, 1,852 pass in 64-bit and 32-bit with numpy blocked; `test_app_startup.py` 30 times per bitness on the step-1 code (60 of 60) and again on the final code (60 of 60); `clone/CUSTOMIZE.md` got a section on remarks.txt. (11) THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW (a fresh Sonnet 5.5 subagent that read only the code and the contract after the tests were green, ran 38 whole simulated games and several thousand hostile inputs in a scratch folder; `work/review_sx2_findings.md`): 1 MAJOR, 5 MINOR and 4 notes, each checked against the code; all six findings fixed with a test each (`tests/test_nx_review.py`, 14 tests). MAJOR: a two-point try after a touchdown that used the last second was skipped (its snap met the clock's end-of-period check) and left the try flag set, so the next kick-off return counted as the try or the drive was played again; now the try is untimed at the snap too and the half or the game ends after it (B-X34). MINOR: a football.sav cut inside the 13 bytes of the marker was refused whole (now a cut tail: the original's values load); a computer's rule-6 timeout could come at 0:00 of quarter 1 or 3 (now nothing is granted at 0:00); F1 said " the clock is running " at the try's menu (the clock is now stopped at every narration end point until H10 decides) and F4 at 0:00 said no time (now " 0 seconds" with the original's words); remarks.txt held by another program after Rules went Classic and back lost the loaded lines and said they stayed (the switched-off list now keeps its values for that case); the saved-game question came after football.sav was read and deleted, so closing the window there lost the save (now it is written back). Notes taken: the help said "more in over time" (now the counts), F1's timeouts were named with the switch off (the help now names them only when on), no singular forms (four new rows: " they have 1 time out left. ", " you have 1 time out left. ", the F1 form, " the clock is running with 1 second left. "), a play-off overtime pair now says " for these two periods. "; noted, not changed: a play chosen when the runoff then ends the period at the snap still counts in the computer's repeat matching (harmless, Classic's own counters), Save Game moves to 9 and 8 in N. F. L. rules mode (the design; S stays), settings.txt gains two lines. Found by the author while waiting for the review: a game saved in overtime lost its overtime number (the original's file never held it); the extras record now restores it. The calibration was run again on the fixed code: 34.03 counted plays per quarter, 6.17 more in quarters 2 and 4, 26.45 seconds per play, 3.76 timeouts per team, 0 problems (the report `work/calibrate_clock_sx2_10000.txt`; the first run's is `_before_review.txt`). (12) Wrong if: the owner hears the clock run too fast or too slow, a timeout come a play too late, the kick-off at 0:00 of quarters 1 and 3 as wrong, or the new last items of the play menus as confusing (SX.9's playtest list); SX.3 or SX.6 finds that a penalty or a review needs the pending runoff or the period end at the snap to work otherwise; a later calibration with all features moves the bands (the values of `tuning.py` are then moved with a recorded run).
- **D-050 (2026-10-15, PX SX.3, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The penalties with accept or decline of the N. F. L. rules mode (NX7); the Penalties switch of the Rules menu; penalties.txt and the PN remarks.** Nothing needs the owner. GAME_SPEC stays 1.3 (it describes the original; only its checker's generated decision count was refreshed). (1) WHAT WAS BUILT, in `clone/src/nfl4/extras/`: `fouls_2026.py` (the foul table of design 9.2 as data: ID, the rulebook's code, the spoken name's default, offender, phases, yards, spot, start rate, citation; a foul either team commits is two entries with its share of the rate; P19 is two entries, the offense on a run and the receivers on a kick-off return), `penalties.py` (the enforcement as plain functions: half the distance, the spots, the down and the distance, the results of accepting and declining, the computer's choice, the spoken situations), `penalty_flow.py` (PenaltyFlow: the draws, the announcement, the prompt, the computer's decision, the enforcement in the game, the clock after a foul, the run off and the untimed down; an events list the tests and the calibration count), `penalty_list.py` (penalties.txt); the penalties group of `rules_2026.py`; the rates, factors, spot ranges, the second-foul chance and the remark chance in `tuning.py`; `nfl_rules.py` calls the flow from its hooks; ExtrasRecord got penalties_on, flags and flag_yards per side, foul_clock, foul_by_offense, was_running, extension, extension_snap and no_snap (checked whole on load). (2) ENGINE SEAMS, each never taken in Classic, the golden Classic transcript unchanged in both bitnesses: `snap` checks the clock's end of the period before the foul draw (Classic: both no-ops, so the swap changes nothing); H2 `before_snap` may answer FOUL_BEFORE_SNAP: `snap_count(until_first_hut=True)` says the huddle, the quarterback's line and " hut 1 ", then `Rules.foul_before_snap(state)` answers the next step; H2 `Rules.instead_of_sack(state)` right after the original's sack test: a sentence makes the engine's new step `thrown_away` end the play as an incomplete pass with it (SX.5's scramble draw will sit at the same place); every narration end point goes through `PlayEngine.end_point`, which follows `PlayOutcome.then` (new field, None in Classic) through `continuation` (game/rules.py: NEXT_DOWN, ON_DOWNS, SAFETY_BY_PENALTY with the new step `penalty_safety`, TRY_AGAIN, TRY_FAILS, KICK_AGAIN, PERIOD_END); ClassicRules got `foul_before_snap` (raises: never reached) and `instead_of_sack` (None). (3) WHEN FOULS ARE DRAWN (design 9.1, 15, as built): before or at the snap only on plays 1 to 5 and the two-point try (punts and field goals have no snap count of the quarterback); during the play by the play's kind; pass interference only on a pass not caught (3.75 per 100, the design's 1.5 per pass); after the play only when the play ends in the field of play and the offense keeps the ball; no personal and no after-the-play foul on a try; none on " is way to short"; grounding in place of a sack; P33 is drawn only by SX.4's onside kick. The draws and their order are in `penalty_flow.py`'s docstring and design 15 (as built). (4) ENFORCEMENT readings (design 9.4, Part 6, Part 1.5 defaults): half the distance rounded down (the ball stays on the 1, never on a goal line); a foul by the offense that leaves the ball exactly on the line to gain is not a first down and leaves 1 to go; fumbles and interceptions from the previous spot (no spot of the fumble in the clone); P24 offside on a kick-off gives the receivers 5 yards from the dead ball spot (14-4-7's option) instead of a kick made again (the kick-off has no kicking spot); a personal foul by the defense on a touchdown is not offered: the score stands and the yards go to the try (a two-point try from the 1, " the score stands, and the penalty will be enforced on the try. ", 14-2-3); a personal foul on a field goal or a safety is an ordinary choice (its yards are not carried to the kick-off); a kicking-team foul on a returned touchdown, a defense foul on a good two-point try, an offense foul on a failed one and a personal foul by the offense before a turnover are not offered and are said declined; grounding is a safety only with the passer in the end zone; fouls by both teams: 15 against a minor 5 enforces the 15 from the previous spot, both after the play let the play stand, a kick-off is kicked again, else the down is played again. (5) THE CHOICE (design 9.3, C9): the prompt (" <coach> if you accept the penalty, <situation>. ", " if you decline it, <situation>. ", then the answer) starts at the computer's choice; Up, Down or Tab change it, A accepts, D declines, Enter takes it, F1 says the flag and the prompt again, F2 to F4 as in a play menu; the keypad digits type letters in this key model and are only echoed (B-W6); a dead-ball foul before the snap is accepted without a prompt. The computer compares points, the ball, a new first down, the field position (within 3 yards counts as equal) and then the down and the distance; it says " the <team> accept the penalty. " or " decline ". NOT BUILT HERE: S at the prompt (Save Game with the decision pending, C10): the decision sits inside a play whose remaining steps a save cannot resume, so it is built with SX.6's challenge question in SX.9 (S is echoed like any other key). (6) THE CLOCK (design 5.2, 4-3-2 (e) and (g)): after a foul as if it had not happened, restarting on the ready signal; on the snap after the first half's warning, in the last 5:00 of the second half, in overtime and after an offense foul before the snap in the fourth quarter or overtime; after a penalty the runoff to the next snap is at most the play clock of 25 (30 after a run off); the ten-second run off (4-7-1) is offered for an offense foul before the snap with the clock running after the two-minute warning, delay of game excepted (three answers, the prompt saying the time left after the run off; the computer's defense takes the run off when it leads or is tied; when it takes the last seconds the period ends); grounding's run off is not built; the untimed down (4-8-2 (a)) follows an accepted foul by the defense during the play on which time ran out in the second or fourth quarter, and is always taken; a foul before the snap is not a counted play (design 5.7's unit). (7) CALIBRATION (NX7's done-when, N7.7): `work/calibrate_penalties_sx3.py`, 10,000 seeded games with both sides played by the computer (`work/calibrate_penalties_sx3_10000.txt`): 12.97 accepted fouls per game (target 12.8, band 12.0 to 13.6), 32.4 percent before the snap, 112.9 penalty yards per game (8.70 per foul), by quarter 22.0, 27.2, 22.3, 28.1 and 0.5 percent in overtime, 0.44 offsetting flags per game, no property problem, the clock's bands still met with penalties on (34.26 counted plays per quarter, quarters 2 and 4 carry 6.85 more, 26.27 seconds per counted play; 3.71 timeouts per team per game). Moves from the start values, each in `tuning.py` with its history: the rate scale 1.0 became 1.2 (1.0 gave 11.15 in 40 games, 1.24 gave 13.28 in 1,000); grounding 1 in 12 of sacks became 170 per 10,000 sacks (the original's engine sacks about 12 times a game: 1.03 groundings a game against the NFL's 0.21). The computer declines 8.1 percent of the choices (NFL 12 to 13 percent, a plausibility check of design 9.3, not a target): the per-type pattern is close for holding, illegal formation, too many men and illegal shift, lower for offside on the defense and ineligible receivers, and pass interference is never declined because it is drawn only on passes not caught; a tolerance of 5 yards was tried (9.9 percent in 1,000 games) and not kept because it accepts an offside after a 9-yard gain, which coaches decline. (8) SETTINGS, MENU, FILES, TEXTS: settings.txt `nx_penalties` (on by default; NflRules made by hand has penalties off, the program's rules come from the settings); the F9 Rules menu has 1 Rules, 2 Time outs, 3 Penalties, 4 Reset; penalties.txt (CUSTOMIZABLE_CONTENT 13 L14 and 14) read only with the N. F. L. rules; remarks.txt's PN pool; STRINGS_NX 227 texts (18 new rows: NXP-15 to NXP-21, NXP-S11 to NXP-S16, NXH-09 to NXH-13); the statistics add " Penalties" and " Penalty Yardage" (C7); F5, F6 and F7 add the penalty help when penalties are on (C6). (9) TESTS: `test_nx_enforcement.py` (43: every foul of the table, the research's seven worked examples, scores, turnovers, kicks, tries, offsetting, the half-distance property, the computer's choice, the situations), `test_nx_penalties.py` (the engine flows on a strict scripted source), `test_nx_fouls.py` (the table against the design and the rules), `test_nx_penalty_list.py` (penalties.txt and its 7.10 matrix), `test_nx_penalty_games.py` (40 simulated games' properties and five games through the menus with the player's choices), `test_nx_penalty_review.py` (the review's fixes); 152 new tests, 2004 pass in 64-bit and in 32-bit with numpy blocked (`work/suite_sx3_64.txt`, `_32.txt`), the real-thread file test_app_startup.py 30 of 30 runs in each bitness (`work/repeat_sx3/`); SX.2's through-the-menus timeout tests now read the game's own rules object (`RecordingRules.used`): they had read the program's object, which `for_game` replaces, so their timeout checks were empty. (10) INDEPENDENT REVIEW (a fresh Sonnet 5.5 subagent with the code and the contract, no conclusions, inputs run in a scratch folder: about 500 whole games, 12,000 forced-foul fuzz cases, 49,000 enforcement cases; `work/review_sx3_findings.md`): Classic confirmed unchanged, no exception anywhere; 1 MAJOR and 5 MINOR findings and 7 notes, all real; fixed with a test each in `tests/test_nx_penalty_review.py` except S at the prompt (C10, SX.9 by plan): the run-off prompt now says the time after the run off; an at-snap foul and grounding on one play are a double foul; a foul before a two-point try leaves nothing waiting for the next play; a defense foul on a good try and an offense foul on a failed try are not offered (11-3-3); off wins over a weight in penalties.txt; delay of game gets no run off (rules_2026.NO_RUNOFF_FOULS, Part 1.5 default); after a penalty the runoff is at most the play clock of 25 (30 after a run off; 4-6-2 (e), 4-6-3 (c)); a personal foul by the offense before a turnover is not offered and the turnover stands (14-4-3 (b); its yards are not carried, the turnover's spot is the engine's later draw); a foul not offered is said declined; on the last play of a half both answers say the half ends; grounding exactly on the goal line is not a safety; the module's docstring and the F6 help (NXH-11 now names the pass interference exception) corrected. The calibration was run again on the fixed code (item 7's numbers). Wrong if: a playtest finds a prompt too long or a flag too frequent for the game's pace (the scale and the remark chance are data); the NFL's 2026 season settles far from 12.8 (the target is data); SX.6's rollback or SX.4's onside kick needs a different take-over order at the end point (the hook moves, Classic never does).
- **D-051 (2026-10-15, PX SX.4, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The onside kicks of the N. F. L. rules mode (NX4); the Onside kicks and Onside question items of the Rules menu; the ON remarks.** Nothing needs the owner. GAME_SPEC stays 1.3 (it describes the original; only its checker's generated decision count was refreshed). (1) WHAT WAS BUILT: `clone/src/nfl4/extras/onside.py` (the rules as plain functions `question_due` and `computer_rule`; `OnsideFlow`: the player's question, the computer's declaration, the kick), the hooks in `nfl_rules.py`, `penalties.py` (the enforcement of P24 and P33 on the kick), `penalty_flow.py` (the kick made again and the safety kick set the record), `state.py` (ten new fields: `onside_on`, `onside_always`, `onside_tried`, `onside_recovered`, `kick_kind`, `kick_again`, `rekick`, `kick_back`, `kick_from`, `safety_kick`; a record saved before SX.4 loads with onside kicks off), `clock.py` (L = 3 to 5 seconds), `remarks.py` (pool ON), `rules_menu.py`, `rules_2026.py` (the kick-off spot 35, the safety kick spot 20, the five yards), `tuning.py` (the values with their history), `saved.py`; `data/settings.py` and `settingsfile.py` (`nx_onside` on, `nx_onside_question` sensible or always). (2) ENGINE SEAMS, each never taken in Classic (the golden Classic transcript is unchanged in both bitnesses): the kind ONSIDE_KICKOFF returned by `before_kickoff`, which `kick_off` maps, after the first-kick-off record and the replay and spot setup, to the new step `onside_kick`; that step asks `Rules.onside_kick` (ClassicRules raises: never reached) for the kick, which returns ONSIDE_KEPT, ONSIDE_LOST or ONSIDE_RUN, and ends the play like a kick-off: kept: the end point `ONSIDE_KICK` with `turnover` = the kicking team kept it, then the original's possession sentence (" it's your ball on ... "); lost: the end point, then " It's" and the position sentence as after a touch back; run: the state is set like the original's kick-off return (the yards, the tackle sentence and sound, the result kind, no pause before the tackle, to go 1) and the original's end of play runs the tackle, the gain, a touchdown with its countdown, the extra point or two-point choice and the kick-off, and the fouls of a return. `outcome.ONSIDE_KICK` is a new outcome kind; the clock's live time and the penalty flow's facts read it. (3) THE QUESTION (D-041, design 8.2): `question_due` (the player's team trails in the third or fourth quarter, or leads by 1 to 8 points with at most 5:00 left in the fourth quarter, or the setting is always; never in overtime unless always); answer no first; Y, N, Up, Down, Tab, Enter, F1 asks again, F2 to F4 as in a play menu, the keypad digits type letters and are only echoed (B-W6), any other key is echoed; no Save Game at the question (it sits at a kick-off, which has no menu to save from; SX.9 with the other pending decisions). (4) THE COMPUTER (design 8.4, calibrated, item 8): only the fourth quarter and only when behind, the first rule that fits decides with one draw `rnd(b) <= a`, no draw otherwise; the sentence " the <team> will try an onside kick. " begins the kick (after the engine cleared the replay list, so F2 replays it). (5) THE KICK (design 8.3 as built; the draws and their order are in design 15 as built): the crowd, " the <kicking team> line up for an onside kick. ", " the <receiving team> send in their hands team. ", an ON remark (one in three, comments on), the whistle (foot11), the crowd up, the kick (foot7), the play noise, " <kicker 1> bounces the ball along the ground. ", " there is a scramble for the ball. ", a rady pause, then the result with foot12 and the whistle; the distance 8 to 14 yards, the kicking team recovers with 8 percent (8 of 113 declared kicks of 2024 to 2026), else one draw of 100: 0 a touchdown (all the way home), 1 to 3 a run back of 5 to 44 yards (never to the goal line) with the original's two tacklers, else the receivers fall on the ball (the design's 1 in 25 returns and 1 in 100 touchdowns read as one draw: 4 in 100 return, 1 of them a touchdown). THE FOULS draw order moves: the design drew the foul before the recovery; the penalty flow draws every foul at the narration end point and the receivers' choice needs the result, so P24 and P33 are drawn there, after the result (B-P57). Where the kick is made from: the kicking team's 35, the 20 after a safety (the flag is set by a safety, by a safety by penalty, and cleared by the kick-off or a period over), five yards further back after an accepted offside; a kick made again keeps its kind, asks nothing and is not a new try. Enforcement: the receivers hold the ball: five more yards from the dead ball spot (the half distance applies); the kicking team recovered it: P24 accepted is the kick made again, P33 accepted gives the receivers the ball at the spot (6-2-5); the computer's choice is the penalty flow's `better_for`. (6) STATISTICS (C7): tried per kicking side when the kick is made (not again for a kick made again), recovered when the kicking team kept the ball and no penalty took it away; an onside recovery counts no first down (the original's Q19 quirk counts the receivers' return only); spoken after the time outs (NXS-06, NXS-07). (7) TEXTS, MENU, FILES: 232 texts (five new rows of the TSV: NXP-S17, NXH-14 to NXH-17); F5, F6 and F7 add the onside lines when the switch is on; the F9 Rules menu is 1 Rules, 2 Time outs, 3 Onside kicks, 4 Onside question, 5 Penalties, 6 Reset (the design's order; Reset now brings back every switch); remarks.txt gets pool ON (ON01 to ON03, F8 silences it, an older file without ON lines uses the defaults); `clone/CUSTOMIZE.md` says so. (8) CALIBRATION (design 8.4 start values, B-X39): `work/calibrate_onside_sx4.py`, 10,000 seeded games with both sides played by the computer (`work/calibrate_onside_sx4_10000.txt`): 0.174 onside kicks a game (band 0.12 to 0.27; the NFL 0.19 in 2024 and 2025 and 0.14 in 2026 to week 5), all in the fourth quarter by the team behind, 57.7, 30.2 and 12.0 percent with up to 2:00, 2:00 to 5:00 and 5:00 to 10:00 left, 8.2 percent kept (the NFL 6.0 and 9.6), 87 percent fell on and 4.4 percent run back; with onside kicks on the earlier bands hold (12.97 accepted fouls a game, 34.27 counted plays per quarter, quarters 2 and 4 carry 6.87 more, 26.26 seconds per counted play, 3.71 timeouts per team, no property problem). The start values gave 0.133 a game in 400 games; two moves from a count of the engine's own opportunities (0.72 trailing kick-offs a game in the fourth quarter, by time left and deficit): the first rule (up to 2:00 left, 9 in 10) has no upper limit on the deficit and the third (5:00 to 10:00 left, 1 in 4) starts at 9 down. The share of kicks by a team behind by 1 to 8 is 28 percent against the NFL's 42 (the engine's games are decided by more than a score more often): a plausibility gap, kept (B-P56). (9) TESTS: `test_nx_onside.py` (73: the two rules as plain functions on every boundary, the question and its keys, the computer's declaration and its draws, every result of the kick on a strict scripted source, the kick from the 20, the leftover flags, the keypad, the yard line's floor) and `test_nx_onside_fouls.py` (35: the four foul cases with the exact spoken order and draws, the kick made again, the statistics, the help, the remarks and their customization, settings, the record and its damage, 40 whole simulated games, four whole games through the menus), `nflrig.py` (RecordingRules keeps the onside kicks), the Rules-menu and remarks tests changed for the new items and pool; 2112 pass in 64-bit and in 32-bit with numpy blocked (108 new; `work/suite_sx4_final_64.txt`, `_32.txt`; the first full runs before the review's fixes passed 2097 in each: `suite_sx4_64.txt`, `_32.txt`) (`work/suite_sx4_64.txt`, `_32.txt`), the real-thread file test_app_startup.py ran 30 times per bitness on the final code, 30 of 30 passed in 64-bit and 30 of 30 in 32-bit (16 tests each, `work/repeat_sx4/`). (10) INDEPENDENT REVIEW (a fresh Sonnet 5.5 subagent with the code and the contract, no conclusions): Classic unchanged (whole-game hashes of 350 games identical to the commit before SX.4, every spoken text, sound, draw and score), no exception in about 3,700 simulated games over every switch combination and 360 games through the menus with random keys; 2 MINOR and 5 NOTE findings, all real, fixed with a test each except two notes kept: a recovered kick with an accepted offside at 0:00 of the second or fourth quarter promised a kick that was never made (a foul by the kicking team does not extend a period, 4-8-2 (a): not offered then, the recovery stands); the kick's yard line had no floor after repeated accepted offsides (it moves back at most half the distance to the goal line, the record allows 1 to 35); the keypad's dot typed an n and answered no (the whole keypad, 96 to 111, is only echoed); the computer's declaration was missing from F2's replay (it is said at the start of the kick); stale comments and a constants test. Kept as notes: the live time of a kick made again stays charged (3 to 5 seconds, as the normal kick-off's replay), the flagged result's second whistle (as a touch back with a flag since SX.3) and the answer kept across F1 to F4 re-asks (as the penalty prompt). `work/review_sx4_findings.md`.
