- **D-053 (2026-10-15, PX SX.6, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The replay challenges, the booth reviews and the ruling model of the N. F. L. rules mode (NX2), with the declined flags offered again after a reversal (N2.7); the Replay challenges item of the Rules menu; the CHS, CHC, RVR and RVC remarks.** Nothing needs the owner. GAME_SPEC stays 1.3 (it describes the original; only its checker's generated decision count was refreshed). (1) WHAT WAS BUILT: `clone/src/nfl4/extras/review.py` (the rules as plain functions `candidates`, `booth_reason`, `ruled_result`, `tally`, `moved`, `goal_distance`; the board of a play `capture` and `restore`; `ReviewFlow`: the ruling draw, the hint, the review, the reversal, the player's question, the computer's flag, the statistics), the hooks in `nfl_rules.py`, `penalty_flow.py` (`redo`), `timeouts.py` (`challenges_left`, `challenge_possible`), `state.py` (the record's `challenges_on`, `chal_used` and `chal_won`; a record saved before SX.6 loads with the challenges off), `remarks.py` (pools CHS, CHC, RVR and RVC), `rules_menu.py`, `tuning.py` (the rates, the chances and their history), `saved.py`; `data/settings.py` and `settingsfile.py` (`nx_challenges`, on). (2) THE RULING MODEL (design 6.1): the narrated result is the ruling on the field. After a play that ends in the field (a run, a catch, an incompletion, a touchdown run or catch, a fumble lost; never a kick, a two-point try or a play a penalty took over) `candidates` lists its reviewable aspects in the order goal line, line to gain, fumble, down by contact, catch, boundary, and ONE draw `below(1000)` decides over them: the wrong windows of the aspects first, then the close ones, then right and plain. A wrong ruling has a truth (`Truth`: a play in the field, a touchdown or a fumble lost; one draw of the play's own row for the yards of a catch or a runner who was down before the fumble) and is clear and obvious on the replay with 85 percent (15-2-1); a close ruling is never reversed. The announcer hints after a wrong ruling with 70 percent (pool CHS) and after a close one with 50 percent (pool CHC); the computer's coach hears the same hint and nothing else. (3) THE ORDER OF THE DRAWS differs from design 15 in one place, as built: the ruling is drawn after the fouls of the play were drawn, told and chosen (`settle`), not before the foul draw and the live time. Reason (N2.7, 15-7-3 Item 3): a flag the offended team declined has to be offered again after a reversal, so the flag's choice comes first and the ruling waits for it; a play a penalty took over is never ruled. The live time L is charged before the ruling and a reversal gives it back (4). The as-built list is in design 15. (4) THE REVERSAL (design 6.4, B-X43): the board of the narration end point is captured at the top of the end-point hook (the fields of `BOARD_FIELDS`, both sides' statistics, the quarterback counts); a reversal restores it, takes the ruled result's statistics back and counts the truth's with the one function `tally`, sets the corrected result in the state (the ball moved from the spot of the snap by the engine's own rule) and answers the name of an engine step: `gain_and_end` (the second half of `field_play_end`, split off for it), `touchdown_reached` (a score the ruling had denied) or `reviewed_fumble` (a fumble the ruling had called down); the corrected end point passes the hook once more without a ruling and without a foul draw, charges L again and, only when flags were declined, runs `settle` again over `PenaltyFlow.redo` (the flags against the corrected result, no new foul draw). The clock goes back to `left_before`, the time left before the live time, less the seconds that ran off after the ruled play (a time out's signal time), never more than the period's length (B-P59); the out-of-bounds fact of the play is kept unless the corrected play is a fumble, an incompletion or the boundary aspect itself (the review's finding 2); a time out charged in this dead-ball period keeps the clock stopped through the corrected play (finding 1). The time outs are never given back; a challenge that wins costs nothing. After the warning of the half the clock may run back over 2:00 and through it (the warning is given once, finding 12), and the 10-second runoff of 15-5-2 after a booth reversal in the last two minutes is NOT built (the corrected play gets the tempo's runoff; a plausibility gap, SX.9 may build it, finding 11). (5) THE BOOTH (design 6.2): a play of the replay official's own (`booth_reason`: a score or a turnover as ruled, a failed fourth down as ruled, a play that began after the two-minute warning, overtime; overtime before the warning before the booth, which is the order the menu says the reasons) is reviewed when its ruling is wrong, or when it is close with 14 percent: foot11, the stop sentence, the referee under the hood, a pause of 2.0 seconds and two `rady` pauses, then reversed (wrong and clear, with the corrected ruling said), stands (wrong, not clear) or confirmed (right). No time out is charged, and with comments on a remark follows with chance 1 in 2 (RVR after a reversal, RVC after the others; RVC03 only after a lost challenge). (6) THE COACH (design 6.4, N2.1 to N2.6): the item "Challenge the Ruling" (letter C) is in the offense and defense menus before Time Out only while the coach may challenge: a reviewable ruling on the last play that no review looked at, not a booth play, before the two-minute warning of the half, not overtime, a challenge left (two a game, a third once one won, never a fourth) and a time out in hand (also with Time outs switched off: the count of the game is kept either way). The letter C without the item says why not (NXR-10 to NXR-15) and the menu goes on. The question names the ruling, the challenges and time outs left and that a time out is lost if the ruling stands; the answer starts at no (Y, N, Up, Down, Tab, Enter, F1 to F4 as in the penalty prompt; the keypad digits type letters in this key model and are guarded, B-W6). A lost challenge (stands or confirmed) costs a time out and is the one charged time out of its dead-ball period (4-5-1 Item 3); the menu that starts again is made again, so the item is gone when no challenge is left (`PlayEngine._menu` takes a `rebuild`, never taken in Classic). Nobody challenges the play that used the last second of a period (a reversal would have to reopen the finished period; the replay official still reviews it). A pass that the passer threw away (the sentence of intentional grounding) has no catch to rule on. With the Time outs switch OFF the teams keep their time outs for the challenges (the switch removes the Time Out item and the computer's own calls; a challenge still needs and costs one: decided, finding 7). The sentences of a review are logged and waited like the play-by-play but are not kept for F2 (N2.6, finding 9). The hint is a fact of the play: F8 mutes only the announcer, the computer's coach hears it either way and no draw depends on F8 (finding 10). The computer's coach (side the ruling went against, `auto_sides`) throws the flag between plays when it heard a hint: 80 percent after a strong one, 30 after a close one, only when it may; the computer keeps its last time out for a challenge before the warning (`timeouts.challenge_possible`; design 6.3 rule 1). (7) ENGINE SEAMS, each never taken in Classic (the golden Classic transcript is unchanged in both bitnesses): `PlayEngine.continuation` maps the three new `then` names; `_menu` returns a step when `Rules.menu_action` answers one and calls `Rules.menu_key` for a letter no item has; `field_play_end` is split into `field_play_end` and `gain_and_end`; `down_sentence` asks `Rules.between_plays` for a continuation; the new step `reviewed_fumble`; `ClassicRules` answers `menu_action` False, `menu_key` False and draws nothing. (8) TEXTS, MENU, FILES: 8 new rows of the TSV (NXR-16 to NXR-19, NXH-23 to NXH-26, 245 texts in all), the help of F5, F6 and F7 (only with the challenges on), the statistics lines " Challenges Won" and " Challenges Lost"; the F9 Rules menu is 1 Rules, 2 Time outs, 3 Replay challenges, 4 Quarterback runs, 5 Onside kicks, 6 Onside question, 7 Penalties, 8 Reset (`nx_challenges`); remarks.txt gets pools CHS, CHC, RVR and RVC (an older file without them uses the defaults); `clone/CUSTOMIZE.md` says so. The ruling of the last play and its board are NOT saved in football.sav (a loaded game has nothing to challenge); the counts are. (9) CALIBRATION (design 6.1 start values, B-X39): `work/calibrate_review_sx6.py`, 10,000 seeded games with both sides played by the computer: the design's start values gave 0.198 coach challenges per team per game (NFL 0.24 to 0.28) that changed the ruling 53 percent of the time (NFL 38 to 52) and 0.32 replay official reviews a game (NFL about 0.65) that changed it 83 percent (NFL 74 to 78). Both targets share the rates, so two knobs moved, each from the counts of the engine's own plays: the plays of the replay official's own have their wrong windows doubled (`BOOTH_PLAY_WRONG_FACTOR` 2: the ball is on the line there and the booth sees every ruling) and the close rates are 1.7 times the design's (the rulings that were right but close are what the coaches lose on); the booth's chance on a close ruling is 14 percent (7) and the computer's flag after a strong hint 80 percent (70). Result: 0.270 coach challenges per team per game (band 0.20 to 0.32, NFL 0.24 to 0.28) that changed the ruling 51.0 percent of the time (NFL 38 to 52; by aspect 44 to 48 percent, a runner called down 83), team-games with 0, 1, 2 and 3 challenges 76.4, 20.4, 3.0 and 0.2 percent (NFL 77, 21, 2 and 0.3), 0.682 replay official reviews a game (NFL about 0.65) that changed the ruling 74.5 percent (NFL 74 to 78), 1.20 wrong and 2.05 close rulings a game of 95.6 ruled plays; the earlier bands still hold with challenges on (12.73 accepted fouls a game, 33.70 counted plays per quarter, quarters 2 and 4 carry 6.83 more, 26.71 seconds per counted play, 0.171 onside kicks a game, 1.349 designed quarterback runs per team per game, no property problem; `work/calibrate_review_sx6_10000.txt`, tag TESTED). The challenges cost the computer's time outs too: charged time outs per team per game fell from 3.76 to 3.65 (the target 3.85 stays a plausibility check of SX.2: a coach who keeps a time out in hand for a flag, and who lost one to a lost challenge, calls fewer for the clock). (10) TESTS: `test_nx_review_rules.py` (100: the rates, the windows and the aspects of every kind of play, the booth reasons, the hint, the tally, the refusal order), `test_nx_review_plays.py` (25: every ruling both ways through `scene`, the reversal's board, statistics, clock and next step, the corrected play's own sentences), `test_nx_review_flows.py` (11: the menus: the item, the letter C, Enter, the question's keys, a challenge won and lost, a booth review), `test_nx_review_refusals.py` (23: every reason and every limit), `test_nx_review_computer.py` (24: the computer's flag, the hint chances, its last time out), `test_nx_review_penalties.py` (5: a declined flag offered again after a reversal, a play a penalty took over never ruled, the try, a lost fourth down), `test_nx_review_extras.py` (13: the record, the settings, the Rules menu, the statistics, the remarks pools, the help), `test_nx_review_games.py` (16: whole simulated games and five games through the menus with a player who challenges), `test_nx_review_fixes.py` (13: the tests of the review's fixes, below), the older tests changed for the new item, pools and help; 2525 passed, 0 failed in 64-bit and in 32-bit with numpy blocked (`work/out_sx6/`), the real-thread file test_app_startup.py 30 of 30 passed in 64-bit and 30 of 30 in 32-bit (`work\repeat_sx6\`). (11) INDEPENDENT REVIEW (a fresh subagent with the code and the contract, no conclusions): Classic unchanged (500 whole Classic games through the menus with random keys, 400 N. F. L. games with the other switches and 200 with every switch off identical to the commit before SX.6: every text, sound, pause, clock and score) and no exception in 1,600 simulated games over every switch combination and 480 games through the menus; 16 findings, 2 MAJOR, 7 MINOR and 7 NOTE, all checked: (1) a won challenge cancelled a time out called before it and let the clock run, and (2) a reversal forgot that the play went out of bounds (B-P60); the rig dropped the computer's reversal (B-X44); a throw-away could be reversed into a catch; the Challenge item stayed after a lost challenge or the last time out (B-W8); a lost challenge was not the time out of its dead-ball period; the Time outs switch off; the last play of a quarter was challengeable by the computer only (B-P61); F2 replayed the review; the stale 70 percent, the plain pass's question, an impossible record, a missing space; 12 fixed (ten with a test each in `tests/test_nx_review_fixes.py` and the games and flows files, each of the two major ones failing with its fix switched off; the stale 70 percent and the missing space are text), 3 decided: with the Time outs switch off the teams keep their time outs for the challenges, F8 does not change what the computer hears, the clock may run back through 2:00 after the warning; and 1 not built: the 10-second runoff of 15-5-2 after a booth reversal (SX.9 may build it). `work/review_sx6_findings.md`. Wrong if: a playtest finds the rulings too often wrong or too often hinted (the rates are data in `tuning.py`), a reversal's corrected play sounds wrong after the ruling's sentences (the corrected play is told by the original's own steps, so its sentences are the original's), or the challenge item is hard to find (its place before Time Out is design 6.4); SX.8's Franchise inherits `Ruling`'s absence from football.sav (the last ruling is lost when the game is saved and loaded, by design).
- **D-054 (2026-10-15, PX SX.7, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The playbooks of the N. F. L. rules mode (NX5): a catalog of 82 plays with seven styles and the 32 teams' assignment, bounded modifiers on the original's rows, two-level menus, the computer's weighted choice, the folder `customize\playbooks`, the F9 items Offense playbook and Defense playbook.** Nothing needs the owner. GAME_SPEC stays 1.3. (1) NOT SPLIT: one session built the whole of SX.7 (the data, the modifiers, the menus, the computer, the files, the items, the tests and the balance run). (2) THE DEFAULT IS CLASSIC: NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES line 27 and N5.4 say Classic is the default and always first; design 12.1's `team` default is superseded. `team` (the player's team's style) is the second choice of the F9 items. The computer's teams play their own style whenever the Rules are the N. F. L. rules (a game made by hand has none); `team_styles.txt` can set any team to Classic. This is an accepted default: the owner may ask for Classic opponents by default (SX.9's playtest list names it). (3) THE MODEL: a play is a family of the original's tables plus a Mod (design 10.3), a style adds its tilts for its plays of a family (at most three bonus and three penalty entries of equal total size per side, checked by a test), the two plays called add and the sum is clamped to the bounds again; the sign of a Mod is the effect on the offense. The 52 offense and 30 defense plays have their own rules (81 rule sets, one shared by two plays: a strength and a weakness each, the quarterback runs' and the defense's too); the names use only the plain football words of research List A (NX-R6, a test checks the banned words); no play name has a comma (the names must pass the same limits as the player's). (4) THE COMPUTER: weights are the style's, scaled by `MASS` (the share of each of the five families, so a style's mix is data); the situation's percents (`tuning.PB_*`) count from the second down; kicks, tries and the original's fourth-down and last-play rules stay above the draw; the defense keeps the original's family choice and the repeated-play match and picks the play of the family. (5) FILES: `plays.txt` (names, `; off`), `styles.txt` (`S2O017 = weight 7`, `S2OB1 = families 3 4; vs 1 2 3; sack -2; comp 3`, `S6QB = 300`) and `team_styles.txt` (`T01 = Air Raid; defense Pro Style`) in the folder `playbooks`, three more lists of `data\customlists.py` read only with the N. F. L. rules; `ListSpec` got a `folder`; a number past its bound keeps the default and is a problem. (6) THE ASSIGNMENT: the research's table with its alternatives taken where EA states a different defense (Bengals and Chiefs: offense Air Raid, defense Pro Style; Steelers: offense Pro Style, defense Pressure), so Pressure has two offenses and three defenses; the weak assignments (Browns, Panthers, Lions, Redskins) stay as proposed. (7) CALIBRATION (`work\calibrate_playbooks_sx7.py`, `work\calibrate_playbooks_sx7_10000.txt`): each style (offense and defense) against Classic, the side alternating, 10,000 games each: Pro Style 51.3, West Coast 50.0, Air Raid 49.9, Power Run 52.3, Zone Run 50.8, Option 51.1 and Pressure 52.4 percent (Classic against Classic 50.0). The balance needed three moves, each from a measurement: the plays' archetypes were centered (the control group, Pro Style, won 53 to 55 percent before), the situation's distance counts from the second down (first and ten had cut the designed Line Plunge share of Power Run from 40 to 25 percent) and the family masses and tilts of five styles were reset. (8) SEAMS: `_menu` answers a chosen play for a tuple `menu_action` returns and calls `menu_back` on Backspace and Left, `offense_chosen` became `_defense_choice` plus `computer_defense`, `QbFlow.computer_offense` takes a percent, the extras record has `offense_book` and `defense_book`. (9) A fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer read the code (`work\review_sx7_findings.md`): 15 findings (3 MAJOR, 6 MINOR, 6 NOTE), 10 fixed (nine with a test), 4 decided, 1 not built. The MAJOR ones: a quarterback percent of 0 divided by zero, one badly written number dropped a whole styles.txt without a word, and the try was not left alone on the defense side. Decided: the letter B selects the Back item like every letter (the help says so); Pro Style is the control group of the balance test, read as "no style tilts" (design 10.3's "equal to Classic in numbers"), it wins 50 to 52 percent; equal tilt size is the rule, not equal reach. (10) NOT BUILT: editing a play's own strength and weakness in the files (the style tilts, the weights, the names and the switches are editable; the plays' own rules are data in `playbook_data.py`), the plays' descriptions beyond the F7 lines (NXH-31 to NXH-33 define a handful), a play-by-play sentence naming the called play (the original's sentences are unchanged), and the playbook choice per franchise season (SX.8).
- **D-056 (2026-10-15, PX SX.8a, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The Franchise built, first part: the league that lasts, the season loop and history (NX6 F0 and F4), `spec\NFL_FRANCHISE_DESIGN.md` 1.1 (the as-built notes in its history).** Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8). GAME_SPEC stays 1.3; the golden Classic transcript is unchanged. (1) H15: `Program.mode`; `game\season_mode.py` `ClassicSeason` is the Program's old season frame moved, not changed (set-up, new season, season end), `extras\franchise\mode.py` `FranchiseSeason` the league that lasts; `Program` calls `choose_at_start`, `season_setup`, `new_season(coach, ask)`, `before_menu` (inside `main_menu` after today's game, before the coach's select line), `before_game`, `after_week`, `season_over`, `switch_pending` (after each key of the main menu: true ends the wait, B-X48) and `switch_if_needed` (top of the week loop); `Program.switch_requested`, `request_switch()` and `rules_changed()`. `game_arguments()` is not built (the store swap already hands `play_game` the league's roster; SX.8b adds the opponent's names there). (2) H14: `ClassicSeasonHooks.game_played(stage, week, winner, loser, winner_score, loser_score)` with the stage a string, called once at every result (the player's game and the 15 others, every play-off game with and without the player, the bye week, finplay17, the conceded Super Bowl, and the player's Super Bowl once); the engine's private round methods took a `stage` parameter with a default. (3) THE SWITCH swaps the three stores, the hooks, the mode, what the one `SeasonState` holds (the classic season is parked as a deep copy and restored in place) and `saved_game`; the classic files are never written by the franchise. (4) THE QUESTION (design 3.2) is a `FranchiseQuestion` on `getkey` (hot keys, F1, F2, digits, paging of nine, bare waits) and a yes or no question that starts at no; a new franchise keeps the three newest old ones in `old\`. (5) FILES: `files.py` (DiskFiles with the three steps of a write, MemoryFiles for the tests), `store.py` (league.sav with league.previous.sav, the healing table of design 10.1, the franchise's football.sav with the league, season and week in the extras record, history.txt), `league.py` (the record, the creation draws of design 12 item 1, the strength sums, the whole-file check by stage). (6) DECIDED IN THE BUILD: the stages are `((1, review), (6, new season))` and the year's aging is part of stage 6 until SX.8c; the season stage with the week 0 and the week 1 without a schedule are valid saved states (B-X50: the engine itself writes the week 0 when the player loses a play-off game); a stage speaks, waits and then changes the record and saves in one block (B-X51); a file held by another program or saved by a newer version makes the franchise unavailable for the run; the roster in F9 speaks name, position and age with a new row NXL-32 until SX.8b speaks the ratings; the franchise settings menu has the offseason setting only; the record is saved only at whole states of the engine (`engine_consistent`) and a restart plays the rest of the play-offs for a player who is out or had a bye (`resume_playoffs`); the longest winning streak counts play-off wins; a failed copy to league.previous.sav does not stop a write; a new franchise that cannot move the old one keeps it and is not written over it. (7) CLASSIC CHANGED ONLY WHERE THE DESIGN NAMES IT: program.py (the calls above), season_engine.py (H14), flow.py (seams), functions.py (the Franchise item, built per opening), rules_menu.py (NXM-26 and NXF-04 only while the franchise is in use), state.py (`franchise` left out of the text when None), settings (four keys), data\files\store.py (`previous_player_names`, read only), app.py (the factory of the folder); the 2,742 earlier tests pass unchanged. (8) TEXTS: 95 rows in `spec\strings_nx.tsv` (NXF, NXE 01 to 03, 11 to 16, 20 to 29, 60, 61, NXL, NXG) and the checker reads the franchise design's IDs (the later sessions' IDs exempt by pattern); `nx_texts.text()` fills `<runner_up>` and `<runner up>`. (9) TESTS: 2899 passed, 0 failed in 64-bit and in 32-bit with numpy blocked (2,742 earlier tests unchanged plus 157 new in test_fr_*.py). (10) THE REVIEW: a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 2 MAJOR (a window closed in a play-off round left a league.sav the program could not resume; a new franchise could destroy the old one it promised to keep when the move to the old folder failed), 9 MINOR and 5 NOTE; all fixed or decided, none rejected (work\review_sx8a_findings.md); the property test that restarts the program from every league.sav written in the play-offs also found a bug of the author's own (a restart from week 0 ran the offseason before the season was loaded). Wrong if: a later session finds a state the engine writes that the check refuses, a switch that leaves a store of the old kind, or a Rules = Classic run that differs from the original.
- **D-055 (2026-10-15, PX SX.8, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The design of the Franchise (NX6): `spec\NFL_FRANCHISE_DESIGN.md` version 1.0.** Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8: every open point of NX6 was settled from the requirements, the research and the code; the choices the owner may change are the design's section 18). Design only: no code. GAME_SPEC stays 1.3. (1) THE MODE: a setting `nx_season_kind` (classic or franchise) says which kind of season is played; the franchise is in use only with the N. F. L. rules; the classic season and the franchise keep separate files (the original's season files in the data folder; `franchise\league.sav`, `league.previous.sav`, its own `football.sav` and `history.txt` in the franchise folder), so the player moves between them without losing either; a switch applies at the next return to the week loop, at once when asked from F9 at the main menu, never inside a game. The question at a new season (design 11.2) gets a third answer when a franchise exists (your franchise, a new franchise; an old franchise is kept as a copy); F9's Franchise menu can start a franchise in the middle of a classic season. Why: N6.1 asks for its own save file, and a franchise that replaced the classic files would end a classic season in progress. (2) THE DATA: 34 places per team (the original's slots and the quarterback), each with a position (the 17 defensive places as design 11.3), a player with a rating 40 to 99 (70 neutral), three potential levels, an age 21 to 40, seasons, peak, the draft; the team strength is a weighted mean of the 34 ratings by how often the engine reads each place; a new league draws a team level (-4 to +4) before its players so team strength spreads from season 1; the player's team takes footplay.txt's names when the file is complete. (3) THE RATINGS' EFFECT (N6.3, C3): in a played game, per play, through H8: the ball carrier's or target's (and the quarterback's) rating against the two tacklers', a step function of the difference (at most 1 yard, 3 points of sack and catch, 3 on the fumble denominator), added to the playbook Mod and clamped to design 10.3's bounds; the kicker's and punter's ratings move the field goal odds (65 to 75) and the shortest punt (22 to 28); scrambles get no ratings Mod (their row does not pass the playbook door, as SX.7 built it); in the computer's games a bounded shift of the drawn score-table index (the original's table and draws) and a play-off coin that leans through a new `GameRandom.chance_first`, which at 500 is exactly `rnd(2) == 1` for every number, both taken from the team's PLACE IN ITS LEAGUE (its exact weighted sum against the league's mean, scaled by the league's spread with a floor), not from its distance to 70: the review's simulation showed the rosters evening out (the spread of strengths 2.7 to about 1.5 in five seasons), which would fade a distance-to-70 shift back to the original's coin, and a league above 70 would inflate every score; all ratings 70, or all teams equal, give exactly the original (four tests N1 to N4). The weighted choice of the ball carrier that research D item 3 named is LATER: it needs a hook in the engine's `snap_draws`, which the core avoids; a play still reads the rating of the carrier the original drew. (4) AGING (N6.4): one year older at the start of the offseason, a change by position windows and potential plus a spread of -2 to +2 and the season's development points; retirement only from 30, its chance rising with age and falling with rating; a forced retirement at 40. (5) THE DRAFT (N6.5): a class of 260 prospects with ranges for ratings, the NFL's order (non-play-off teams by record, then by the round lost), seven rounds, every computer pick and the staff's list by one value rule without draws (one code path), the player's pick from the staff's five with paging and the staff's options, a setting for which picks are told; rookies named from the surname pool as `customize\surnames.txt` gives it, a duplicate of an active player's name only when the pool is used up (N6.5's open point); then the roster cut back to the 34 places (the player may choose) and a fill of empty places by undrafted rookies (45 to 55, and 50 to 60 in the groups of one or two places, where one weak player is the whole group), so every place is filled after every offseason. (6) THE TO-DO LIST AND NEWS (design 11.1): before each game 0 to 3 items with the staff's recommendation first and "later"; two kinds in the core (extra work for a young player in four weeks of the regular season: a development point, every computer team giving its own in the same weeks; the game plan: playbooks for this game from a table worked out once with the yardstick); the play-off weeks have no items; a setting lets the staff decide; with it off nothing is ever decided for the player (research G); the weekly news by rules (the player's streak, the coach's milestones, the biggest win, an upset by strength, a long streak, the closest game), at most four lines, from twelve customizable pools in `customize\news.txt` (L16); F8 does not silence the news. (7) HISTORY (N6.9): champions, the coach's record, season by season, league records, retired stars; a history menu in F9 and `history.txt` rewritten after every season. (8) THE OFFSEASON: six stages in a data list (the season in review, retirements, player development, the draft, the roster cut, the new season), league.sav written after every stage and every pick so a closed window resumes there; a short setting. The program's own prompts of the franchise (the question, the stage waits, the to-do list, the draft, the cut) are a new class `FranchiseQuestion` on the original's key wait (`getkey`: the global keys and F9 work, the lines go to the replay list and the log), because the F9 menu classes read keys without the hot keys; the F9 Franchise menus stay those classes. (9) FILES: league.sav is UTF-8 JSON, written whole through a temporary file with the previous one kept as league.previous.sav, checked whole at load with the checks of its stage (in the offseason places may be empty and a group may hold its rookies); a damaged file falls back to the previous one, both damaged sets the franchise aside with a notice and a classic season (never silently a new franchise); a newer format is left alone; a franchise game's football.sav carries the league ID, season and week in the extras record. (10) HOOKS, each a no-op or an identity in Classic, built in SX.8a and SX.8b: H14 in the season engine (`computer_index`, `playoff_first_wins`, `game_played` at every result, the conceded Super Bowl and the player's Super Bowl once), H15 the program's season frame as one mode object (Classic is today's code moved; `before_menu` inside the main menu after today's game, `before_game`, `switch_pending` so a switch asked from F9 at the main menu is heard at once), H7 extended (`adopt_roster` keeps ratings and the quarterback; `opponent_names` through `play_game` and `PlayEngine`), `GameRandom.chance_first`; no hook in the play itself; F9's item list built per opening. (11) THE SESSIONS: SX.8a the league, the season loop, history, the question class and the hooks H15 and H14's results; SX.8b ratings, strength and their calibration (C-F1 over seasons 1 to 20, C-F2 one target with drawn rosters, C-F3 also with the computer's styles); SX.8c aging; SX.8d the draft and the cut (may take two); SX.8e the to-do list and the news; C-F4 (no drift of the mean rating, the mean age and every group's mean) is in the done-when of SX.8c, SX.8d and SX.8e, measured with the whole loop; SX.8f the 20-season simulation with the invariants I1 to I12 and I8b and the knobs it may move; Sonnet 5.5 at high. Start values are marked as such in the design and join Q-030's accepted defaults. (12) THE REVIEW: a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer read the design against the contract and the code and simulated the league (`work\review_sx8_findings.md`): 5 MAJOR (the league file's whole check rejected every offseason save; the menu class named for the flow runs no hot key and has no replay; the computer teams' weekly point lifted the mean rating to 74.6; the strength spread collapsed so the computer's results fell back to the original's coin and a weak team stayed in the bottom three 8 to 11 seasons; the small groups fell under 50 at the fill), 24 MINOR and 9 NOTE; all 38 taken into the design before the commit, none rejected. Wrong if: a build session cannot build a seam as a Classic no-op (then that session records the change), a calibration cannot reach its band with the named knobs, or the owner's playtest (SX.9) finds the offseason too long or the to-do list empty of meaning.
- **D-057 (2026-10-16, PX SX.8b, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The Franchise built, second part: ratings with a bounded effect (NX6 F1), `spec\NFL_FRANCHISE_DESIGN.md` 1.2 (the as-built notes in its history).** Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8). GAME_SPEC stays 1.3; the golden Classic transcript is unchanged. (1) IN A GAME THE PLAYER PLAYS (design 5.1): `extras\franchise\ratings.py` `FranchiseModifiers`, the playbooks' H8 object (`PlaybookModifiers`, now with `mod_for(kind)` and `bind(state)`) that reads the ratings of the players of the play from the game's state at every table read (a run: the carrier; a pass, a completion, an incompletion or a sack: two thirds quarterback and one third target; a designed quarterback run: the quarterback; the defense: the two tacklers' mean; a scramble gets none), adds the Mod of the gap to the playbook's Mod and clamps the sum to the playbooks' bounds; the field goal follows the kicker and the punt the punter. It is the playbooks' object exactly while a roster has no ratings, which is every game that is not a Franchise game, and `NflRules` always uses it (`PlaybookFlow.modifiers`, bound in `period_clock`). (2) IN THE COMPUTER'S GAMES (design 5.2): `extras\franchise\strength.py` `LeaguePlace` (the league's 32 exact sums kept at the start of the season; the shift and the play-off lean in whole-number arithmetic with one integer square root, sign-symmetric, checked against a 60-digit decimal calculation in 7,000 cases and by mirrored leagues), `FranchiseHooks` (H14 `computer_index` shifts each team's score index in `SeasonEngine._regular_week` before the redraw's test and scan, `playoff_first_wins` replaces `rnd(2) == 1` in `playoffs.computer_game`), `GameRandom.chance_first` (one draw traced as size 2, equal to `rnd(2) == 1` at 500 for every number: tested on 100,000 numbers and the edges). `ClassicSeasonHooks` are the identity and `rnd(2) == 1`. (3) THE GAME'S ROSTERS (design 13, H7 extended): `adopt_roster` keeps the ratings and the quarterback, `adopt_opponent` takes the league's team without a draw or the pool, `new_game_setup`, `Game.play_game` and `PlayEngine` take `opponent_names`, H15's `game_arguments()` (Classic `{}`; the Franchise the opponent's roster and a `quarterback_names` of the two league quarterbacks), so Getqbs asks nothing and no pool name is drawn. (4) SPEECH: the roster says NXL-31 (rating, age and potential; NXK-20 to NXK-22 added), F9 Franchise has the item team ratings (NXF-15: NXL-41 and 32 lines NXL-40), NXF-09 is said after NXF-07 at a new league and after NXE-60 at every new season. (5) CALIBRATION, the finding of the session: the design's start values do not meet C-F1 (the win spread of the 32 teams) and C-F2 (the same lean in played and computer games) at once. At the design's values the fast run gave a MEAN win spread over 500 leagues of 2.36 to 2.41 in each of 20 seasons (10,000 seasons; a single league's season varies from about 1.3 to 3.5; target 2.6 to 3.4, the NFL's average since 1978 is 3.04 and 3.2 in 2009 and 2017, Bleacher Report and Harvard Sports Analysis, SRC-SECONDARY) because the lean of the computer's games is the whole of the spread (z has a spread of 1 by construction: the variance of the wins is 4 + 256 c^2 for a lean c of the win share per standard deviation), and a played game gave the stronger team 0.51 to 0.53 at gaps of 1.3 to 3.6 rating points where the regular week gives 0.62 to 0.77 (`work\calibrate_ratings_sx8b_*_design_values.txt`). Moved, each recorded with its history in `extras\tuning.py`: FR_SHIFT_PER_SD 3 to 5 and FR_SHIFT_MAX 6 to 10 (mean win spread 2.82 to 2.88 in every season), FR_PLAYOFF_PER_SD 60 to 150 (the play-off coin leans as the regular week does: 0.65 and 0.75 at one and two standard deviations against 0.67 and 0.77), the steps of the ratings Mod finer with the design's caps kept (yards (12,1) to (4,1), sack (6,3) to (2,3), catch (6,3) to (2,3), fumble (8,3) to (3,3); kick and punt as the design), FR_TEAM_LEVEL -4..4 to -2..2 (the first season's spread of the strengths 2.8 points a standard deviation against 1.4 once the rosters have evened out, so the first season did not play as the later ones; now 1.8 falling to 1.4 and 1.6), and ONE NEW VALUE, FR_TEAM_EDGE (5, 2): every play's gap d counts 2.5 times the two teams' strength gap (in rating points of the weighted mean) as well. Why the new value: the bounded Mod saturates on the noise of the individual players (the gap of two players of one play has a spread of about 7 points), so no steps within the caps give a lean per team point above about 0.5; a shift of the whole distribution by the team's gap is not saturated by the noise, so the edge amplifies the signal and not the noise; with it the stronger team's share in played games is 0.666, 0.672 and 0.701 at one standard deviation of the league (the spreads of seasons 5, 10 and 1: gaps of 1.3, 1.4 and 1.8 rating points) against the regular week's 0.672, and 0.760, 0.769 and 0.792 at two (2.7, 2.8 and 3.6 points) against 0.769, in 10,000 games a gap. (6) THE BANDS over 10,000 games or seasons: C-F1 the mean win spread over 500 leagues is 2.82 to 2.88 in every one of the 20 seasons (`work\calibrate_ratings_sx8b_fast_500x20.txt`; the design's start values 2.36 to 2.41, `..._design_values.txt`); C-F2 within 0.03 of the regular week's share at one and two standard deviations in the spreads of seasons 1, 5 and 10 (`work\calibrate_ratings_sx8b_played_10000.txt`; the design's start values 0.51 to 0.53, `..._played_2000_design_values.txt`; the exact regular-week shares are `work\regular_week_share_sx8b_5_10.txt`); C-F3 (B-P62) against a league of 70s, 10,000 games a line, points a game +0.87 percent with the Classic playbooks and +0.94 percent with the styles, yards a play -0.01 and +0.19 percent (`work\calibrate_ratings_sx8b_centering_10000.txt`; the band is 2 percent). (7) DECIDED IN THE BUILD: the team edge is a design addition (design 5.1 says the ratings of the players in the play; the edge is derived from the same ratings and stays inside the bounds, at most 1 yard and 3 points from the ratings alone, and is zero for equal teams, so neutral equals Classic is untouched: N1 to N3 pass, also with every strength equal at any value); `NXL-32` is no longer used (the table is append-only); neither team of a franchise game blanks a name of the pool (the review's finding 14); a franchise game's quarterbacks are players of the league; THE MEASURED COST of the lean: the clamp of the shifted index piles a strong team's draws on 32 (51 points) and a weak team's on 1 (0 points), so 51 points are 7.7 percent of the computer's team scores (original 3.1, design start values 5.4) and 0 points 9.6 percent (original 6.3, start values 7.6), the mean points unchanged: kept (the table and its clamp are the design's; a lower FR_SHIFT_MAX would trade the win spread), named for the owner's playtest (SX.9: listen for 51-point and shutout results) and for SX.8f; stage 6 saves before its wait (SX.8a's order), so a window closed at that wait does not hear NXE-60 and NXF-09 of that season (NXF-08 and F9's team ratings give them); C-F1's band is a property of the MEAN over leagues of a season (a single league-season varies from 2.1 to 3.8), so SX.8f states it so; a league made by the SX.8a code (levels -4 to +4) plays its first season outside C-F2 (only development leagues exist). (8) THE TESTS: 2994 passed and 2 deselected (the two real-SAPI tests, which fail on this machine at the committed SX.8a state too, fixesforgames B-S25) in 64-bit and in 32-bit with numpy blocked (`work\suite_sx8b_64.txt`, `_32.txt`): the 2,899 earlier tests unchanged except the F9 key sequences of seven franchise tests (the menu has a fifth item), the draw sizes of one league test, one recording hooks object that now subclasses the identity hooks, and the loaded-game checks added to one, plus 97 new tests in `test_fr_ratings.py` (the Mod by gap, the state at each read, the bounds as a property over every carrier, tackler pair, playbook corner and style for the design's and the calibrated values, the kicks, the team edge), `test_fr_strength.py` (hand-worked leagues, the floor, the caps, 7,000 cases against a 60-digit decimal calculation, mirrored leagues, `chance_first`, the engine's hooks on strict scripts), `test_fr_neutral.py` (N1 to N3) and `test_fr_games.py` (H7, the game arguments, NXF-09, the roster and the team ratings in F9, whole games). (9) THE REVIEW: a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 0 MAJOR, 9 MINOR and 11 NOTE (`work\review_sx8b_findings.md`): 9 fixed (an unbound read that raised, wrong words, tests that could not fail, a missing loaded-game test and a pool blanked by the league's names), 4 fixed in words (the band is for the mean over leagues, the 5.1 rationale), 5 decided (the pile-up, stage 6's order, the dead row NXL-32, the widened hook protocol, the draw-order test's span) and 2 noted; the reviewer's own checks held: Classic unchanged line by line, `chance_first(500)` for every double, 75,542 integer-arithmetic checks per value set against an exact calculation, 57,543 reads of the H8 door in 250 games with the right state, N2 in 30 of 30 games with every extra on. Wrong if: a later session finds a Rules = Classic run that differs from the original, a franchise whose computer teams' results drift from the played games' lean (SX.8f's 20-season run with the real offseason checks C-F1 again), or an owner who hears the team edge as too strong (a data change: FR_TEAM_EDGE).
- **D-058 (2026-10-16, PX SX.8c, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The Franchise built, third part: progression, regression and retirement (NX6 F2), `spec\NFL_FRANCHISE_DESIGN.md` 1.3 (the as-built notes in its history).** Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8). GAME_SPEC stays 1.3; the golden Classic transcript is unchanged. (1) THE RULES (design 6, 8.2): `extras\franchise\aging.py` (plain functions over the league record; `plan_*` makes the draws and changes nothing, `apply_*` changes) and the stages 2 and 3 of `offseason.py` (`stage_retirements`, `stage_development`); `data.STAGES` is 1, 2, 3 and 6, so the spoken stage is "n of 4"; the year's aging and the points' reset moved out of stage 6 into stages 2 and 3; stage 6 drops the retired and released players history does not need. Texts: NXE-30 to NXE-33, NXE-35, NXE-40 to NXE-43, NXE-71 and NXE-72 (rows added by `work\add_rows_sx8c.py`, the generated files PASS, 444 texts). (2) DECIDED IN THE BUILD: a stage that changes the league (2 and 3) plans, works its lines out, applies, saves, THEN speaks and waits, so F9's roster and team ratings at the wait are what was just said (review finding 3) and a window closed at the wait resumes at the next stage; ties the design left open (stars: highest peak, equal peaks in league order, the player's own team not named twice; best and worst: the first of equals in league order; the league count includes the player's team); a retired player keeps his team and loses his points; seasons are capped at 99. (3) THE VALUES THAT MOVED (history in `extras\tuning.py`): `FR_GROWTH` (5, 3, 1) to (4, 2, 1); `FR_PEAK` quarterbacks 26 to 32 to 24 to 32, running backs and returners 24 to 27 to 24 to 28. This changes design 6.2's windows and goes beyond the knob list of design 16 (SX.8f's list does not name `FR_PEAK`); decided because the growth alone left the quarterbacks at a mean of 74.7 with 34 percent of the league-seasons over 76 (a position whose window starts later grows for more years before its peak: four years for a quarterback against two for a running back, nobody develops before 22); the window's END (32) is kept, so a quarterback in his early thirties holds as research B6 says; the retirement values (110, 6), the decline table, the in-window changes and the spread are the design's, tried in a sweep (`work\calibrate_aging_sx8c_sweeps\`) and left (B-P56). (4) C-F4 AS MEASURED, a CHANGE OF THE DONE-WHEN (as SX.8b did for C-F1): over 10,000 league-seasons of the fast run (`work\calibrate_aging_sx8c_fast_500x20.txt`; the design's values `..._design_values.txt`) the MEAN over the leagues is inside its band in every one of the 20 seasons: mean rating 69.1 to 70.8 (band 68 to 72), mean age 25.8 to 26.8 (25 to 28), every group's mean 67.0 to 73.8 (64 to 76), win spread 2.81 to 2.88 (C-F1); no single league-season has a mean rating or a mean age outside its band; 11.2 percent of the league-seasons have one group mean outside 64 to 76 (kickers 5.4, punters 4.4, quarterbacks 2.0, returners 0.2 percent). The done-when's "every season" is therefore stated for the mean over the leagues; the tails of the single-place groups are a cohort effect and the stand-in cut's ratchet, and are left to SX.8d (the real cut) and SX.8f (I8b per run, knobs `FR_KEEP_YOUTH`, `FR_DRAFT_WEAK`, `FR_DRAFT_VALUE`, `FR_GROWTH`, `FR_DECLINE`). The retirement rate by age and rating band rises with age and falls with rating over the simulated leagues (the report's table, I2). C-F2 holds at the new strength spreads (the sd settles near 1.2): 0.655 at a gap of 1.2 rating points and 0.755 at 2.4 (6,000 games each) against the regular week's 0.672 and 0.769. (5) THE BUILD ALONE DOES NOT KEEP THE BANDS: until SX.8d builds the draft, the roster cut and the real fill, stage 3 fills every retiree's place with an undrafted rookie rated 45 to 60 (`aging.fill_places`, the design's fill rule; it gives way by itself once `data.STAGES` has the draft stage) and nobody is drafted, so a franchise played now loses rating season by season (the reviewer measured the mean falling from 69 to 55 in ten seasons); the calibration runs the stand-in draft and cut of `work\sim_league_sx8c.py`. No franchise is released before SX.9 and the release gate waits for SX.8d to SX.8f, so this is a development-build state: SX.8d must replace the stand-in and re-run C-F4. (6) NOT BUILT, by plan: the news lines NWR and NWP (their pools and `customize\news.txt` are SX.8e's; the plans of the two stages hold what they need, and the draws design 12 item 6 names for them come after the stage's own draws); the weekly item that gives development points (SX.8e; a test and the calibration's stand-in set the points). A record written by SX.8a that waits at stage 6 does not get the year's aging (only development leagues exist). (7) THE TESTS: 3044 passed and 2 deselected (the two real-SAPI tests, which fail on this machine while NVDA holds the voice, fixesforgames B-S25) in 64-bit and in 32-bit with numpy blocked (`work\suite_sx8c_64.txt`, `_32.txt`): the 2,994 earlier tests (changed: the stage counts and ages in `test_fr_mode.py`, one stage number in `test_fr_league.py`) plus 50 new, 49 in `test_fr_aging.py` (the two rules as numbers for every position and age with the design's values through a fixture and the shipped values pinned by a test of their own, the draws on strict scripts including a whole stage 3 with its fill on the program rig, the spoken stages and the short offseason, the stars and their ties, the file at each stage, a window closed at the wait of stage 2 and of stage 3, F9 play classic seasons at a wait, the roster and ratings at the wait, 20 offseasons as a property, the stand-in giving way to a draft stage) and one in `test_fr_league.py` (the kept sums) (8) THE REVIEW: a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 0 MAJOR, 11 MINOR and 7 NOTE (`work\review_sx8c_findings.md`): 11 MINOR and 6 NOTE listed, no MAJOR: fixed - the stages save before they speak (3), F9's group counts and the kept sums while places are empty (4), the calibration script's promised I2 table (7), the numbers of growth years (8), the loose or missing tests (9, 10, 11, 13), the report's column (16), the design's three inconsistent sentences (14); decided - the quarterbacks' window (1: the end 32 restored, the start 24 kept, recorded above, shipped windows tested), the record of SX.8a at stage 6 (2), the build alone degrading the league (5: recorded in capitals), the done-when for the mean (6); the rules, the draws, the speech, the files and the stand-in fill held on every point the reviewer tested and Classic is unchanged line by line Wrong if: a later session finds a retirement before 30, a rating outside 40 to 99, a franchise whose league mean leaves 68 to 72 with the real draft and cut (SX.8d re-runs `work\calibrate_aging_sx8c.py`), or an owner who hears the retirements or the development as too many or too few (data: `FR_RETIRE_PER_YEAR`, `FR_GROWTH`, `FR_PEAK`).
- **D-059 (2026-10-16, PX SX.8d, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The Franchise built, fourth part: the draft, the roster cut and the fill (NX6 F3), `spec\NFL_FRANCHISE_DESIGN.md` 1.4 (the as-built notes in its history).** Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8). GAME_SPEC stays 1.3; the golden Classic transcript is unchanged. (1) WHAT WAS BUILT, both parts of the session in one: `extras\franchise\draft.py` (the class, the order, what a pick is worth, the picks, the cut, the check of the draft record), `draft_flow.py` (stage 4 with the player's pick question and the told picks, stage 5 with the cut question and the fill), the six-stage `data.STAGES`, F9's roster with the waiting rookies and the picks-told item of the franchise settings, texts NXK-01 to NXK-13 and NXE-50 to NXE-58 (NXE-58 is new). SX.8c's stand-in fill at the end of stage 3 is removed. (2) DECIDED: the draft in progress is a plain dict in the league record (stage `offseason`, `stage_no` 4 then 5, no `draft` stage name); a drafted rookie is an active player without a place until the cut; the class is drawn once and written at once; every pick is written before it is told; `staff_all` is kept in the record. Readings of open sentences of design 7: the +3 bonus compares with the weakest player who would hold a place (not the weakest known, which drafted four quarterbacks in a smoke run), a group of one place is open when its best player is under 60, the order's strength tie uses the strengths kept for the season, a round is announced before the first spoken line of it and not before the player's own prompt, the staff's pick for the player says NXK-12, Escape leaves the long list of prospects. (3) BEYOND THE DESIGN, recorded: the cut's floor `FR_CUT_FLOOR` (50) for groups of one or two places (the first calibration run found a team group under 50 in 6.5 percent of the league-seasons: the fill only fills EMPTY places); in the player's own choice the floor applies only to the groups he was not asked about (the review's MAJOR finding: it had taken back a player he chose to keep). No value of the draft moved; `FR_CUT_FLOOR` is new. (4) RESULTS over 10,000 league-seasons of the fast run with the real draft, cut and fill (`work\calibrate_draft_sx8d_fast_500x20.txt`, the extra work SX.8e builds is the only stand-in): C-F4 as the MEAN over the leagues in every one of 20 seasons (rating 69.2 to 70.8, age 25.8 to 26.7, every group 67.2 to 73.9), win spread 2.81 to 2.87 (C-F1), no team group under 50 (I5), a player who left had played 4.91 seasons (C-F5), the round 1 picks are 76.3 and the round 7 picks 65.0 at 25 (C-F6); one group's mean is outside 64 to 76 in 10.3 percent of the single league-seasons (kickers 4.3, punters 5.0, quarterbacks 1.7: the cohort effect of B-P74, left to SX.8f's per-run I8b), and I11 is measured (207 of 500 leagues keep a team in the bottom three more than 5 seasons in a row, the longest 10: SX.8f's knobs). THE BUILD ALONE (no development points until SX.8e) is inside the same bands (`..._build_alone.txt`; the whole program on the rig for 5 seeds x 20 seasons, `work\build_alone_sx8d_5x20.txt`: rating 68.4 to 69.8, every place filled after every offseason), so the finding of SX.8c (B-P75) is closed: nothing stands in in the offseason. (5) THE TESTS: the suite passes in 64-bit and in 32-bit with numpy blocked (`work\suite_sx8d_64.txt`, `_32.txt`; the two real-SAPI tests deselected, B-S25): the 3,044 earlier tests (changed: the stage counts and numbers in `test_fr_aging.py` and `test_fr_mode.py`, the unknown stage in `test_fr_league.py`; the stand-in test of SX.8c is replaced) plus the new files `test_fr_draft.py` (the class by strict scripts, the order on hand-worked standings, the values, the picks against a brute force at every pick, the record's damage table), `test_fr_cut.py` (the keep value, the cut, the floor, the fill, 20 offseasons as a property), `test_fr_draft_flow.py` (the stage on the program rig: every key of the question, the three settings, a draft closed in the middle, I7 through the menu, F9), `test_fr_cut_flow.py` (the questions, the player's choices, the fill, the short offseason, a window closed at the question) with the rig `drrig.py`, and a mutation check of 21 deliberate breaks, all killed (`work\mutation_check_sx8d.py`). (6) THE REVIEW: a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 1 MAJOR, 6 MINOR and 4 NOTE (`work\review_sx8d_findings.md`), all fixed or decided; the draft's value not knowing the floor is left and named for the owner's playtest. Wrong if: a later session finds a franchise whose league mean leaves 68 to 72 once SX.8e's extra work is in (it adds rating: SX.8e re-runs `work\calibrate_draft_sx8d.py`), a team with an empty or weak place after the cut, a rookie who vanishes between the draft and the cut, or an owner who hears late-round kickers and punters drafted and released at once (data: `FR_DRAFT_VALUE`, `FR_DRAFT_WEAK`, `FR_CUT_FLOOR`).
- **D-060 (2026-10-16, PX SX.8e, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The Franchise built, fifth part: the to-do list and the news (NX6 F5), `spec\NFL_FRANCHISE_DESIGN.md` 1.5 (the as-built notes in its history).** Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8). GAME_SPEC stays 1.3; the golden Classic transcript is unchanged. (1) WHAT WAS BUILT: `extras\franchise\todo.py` (the items, `computer_extra_work`, the game plan item, the check of the list), `news.py` (the week's events by the rules of 8.3, the offseason's events, the lines kept in the record, the check of the news), `news_pools.py` (the twelve pools of `customize\news.txt`, built on the remarks' draw), `weekly.py` (the preparation of a week, the news and the questions in the program's week loop, the kick-off's deadline, the staff's setting, the game plan's rules), the F9 items to do list and news and the staff setting, the help NXH-34 to NXH-38 through the H12 hook, the news of the offseason's stages, 75 text rows (NXD, NXN, NXH-34 to 38, the 48 news lines; NXN-05 is new), `work\make_game_plan_sx8e.py` (the staff's table, frozen as `FR_GAME_PLAN`), the player's guide's news section. (2) DECIDED: the league record gets a `prepared` mark (an older league.sav has none and its week is prepared at the next start); the preparation is one save before anything is spoken, the news lines carry a `told` flag and the items a `state`; the staff's setting is a step of its own and also applies at the kick-off; at the deadline "later" is still offered and what is left is closed with NXD-30; an item answered in F9 while the flow's question is open is not answered again; the taken plan applies to the sides the player plays a playbook on when the game is made; a filled news line is kept as plain markup, brackets in names become parentheses, a line over the limit is replaced by the pool's first default line; F9's earlier news has a heading per week; the offseason's news lines are said in the full offseason as words and kept in both. (3) THE ONE VALUE THAT MOVED, and a change of the design: `FR_UPSET_GAP` 4 to 3 rating points (8.3 gave 4 as a fixed number; over 10,000 league-seasons an upset is told in 8.0 percent of the weeks at 4, 26.0 at 3, 2.0 at 5; the strengths' standard deviation settles near 1.3). The staff's table (`work\make_game_plan_sx8e.txt`): West Coast on offense against every defense (the styles' tilts add, they do not interact), Power, Option or Pressure on defense by the opponent's offense; the yardstick ranks West Coast first although whole games put it last (50.0 percent against Classic), because it ignores downs and field position: named for the owner's playtest (is the item useful?). (4) RESULTS: C-F4 with the real extra-work item over 10,000 league-seasons (`work\calibrate_todo_sx8e_fast_500x20.txt`) is the SX.8d report season by season (the item is the stand-in's rule): the mean over the leagues inside every band in every one of 20 seasons (rating 69.2 to 70.8, age 25.8 to 26.7, every group 67.2 to 73.9, win spread 2.81 to 2.87), 127.5 of 128 points a season given; the whole program, 5 seeds x 20 seasons, the player's team by the staff's first answer (`work\build_whole_sx8e_5x20.txt`): rating 69.0 to 70.9, every place filled after every offseason, the lowest team group 50.0. The news: 3.27 lines a week, the cut at four takes lines away in 6.9 percent of the weeks, every kind's share of the weeks is in the report. Still open for SX.8f: one group's mean outside 64 to 76 in 10.3 percent of the single league-seasons and 207 of 500 leagues with a team in the bottom three more than 5 seasons in a row (unchanged by SX.8e). (5) THE TESTS: the suite passes in 64-bit (3366 passed) and in 32-bit with numpy blocked (3366 passed; the two real-SAPI tests deselected, B-S25): the earlier tests (changed: the key scripts of nine files for the two new items at the top of F9's Franchise menu, the order of the customize lists) plus `test_fr_todo.py`, `test_fr_news.py`, `test_fr_news_list.py` and `test_fr_week.py` (the week on the program rig: the order, once, a restart, the staff's setting, the game plan, F9, the help, a whole season and an offseason), and a mutation check of 37 deliberate breaks (`work\mutation_check_sx8e.py`). (6) THE REVIEW: a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 2 MAJOR, 10 MINOR and 9 NOTE (`work\review_sx8e_findings.md`); the 2 MAJOR and the 10 MINOR are fixed (the MINOR about the design's fixed number is this decision), the NOTEs are in the design's entry 1.5 or documented (`[faster]` is not used in the news). Wrong if: a later session finds a franchise whose league mean leaves 68 to 72 (the extra work adds about one rating point), a player who is never asked about the extra work or a game plan that sticks to the wrong game, an owner who hears an upset every week or never (data: `FR_UPSET_GAP`), news lines that repeat too often (data: `FR_STREAK_LEAGUE`, the pools' sizes) or a game plan that is no use (data: `FR_GAME_PLAN`, or a better yardstick).
- **D-061 (2026-10-16, PX SX.8f, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) The core's 20-season simulation: three windows moved, four bands restated for the core, the whole thing re-measured by SX.8p (D-062).** `spec\NFL_FRANCHISE_DESIGN.md` 1.6. Decided automatically (rule 0.8). Reports in `work\`: `simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_100x20.txt`, `_fast_500x20.txt`, `_full_5x20.txt`, `_selftest.txt`, `_neutral.txt`; scripts `simulate_franchise_sx8f.py` (the fast run, the shared checks and verdicts, `selftest`, `neutral`), `full_sx8f.py` (the whole program), `sweep_sx8f.py` with `sweep_sx8f_log.txt`, `analyze_persistence_sx8f.py`, `probe_draftmix_sx8f.py`, `probe_sides_sx8f.py`, `mutation_check_sx8f.py`, `review_sx8f_brief.md`, `review_sx8f_findings.md`. (1) THE RUNS. FAST: 100 and 500 leagues x 20 seasons, the player's games decided like the computer's, every stage the real code of `extras\franchise\`, I1, I3, I4, I5, I9 (the bracket), I10 (the league record's whole-file check and a round trip through its text), I12 (8 seeds twice, a digest of the league and the results after every season and of the league after the last offseason) in every run-season, the league after the last offseason judged too. FULL: 5 seeds x 20 seasons through the whole program on the rig (the real season engine, the weekly preparation, news and to-do list, the six stages with the draft and the cut asking, the key Enter at every question so the staff decides everything, league.sav written by the real store), the player's games played by the real engine with both sides by the computer (`nflrig.SimEngine`, the league's rosters and ratings handed over through `game_arguments`). The full run adds: I7 (each of the 100 drafts replayed by `best_for` alone from the league at the start of stage 4 AND the drafts of seasons 1, 8 and 15 of every run made again from the saved league and the same seed through the menu with the keys of the staff's pick (item 7), "the staff makes your other picks" (item 8) and the picks told "mine" and "all": 60 drafts, the leagues equal the one the first item gives), I9 (20 seasons, one champion and one runner-up each, the coach's wins and losses equal the games played; every turned game plays the player's roster as side 2), I10 (league.sav loaded after each of 600 stage ends equals the league in memory; 900 cut-write scenarios: the REAL `FranchiseStore.save` stopped at each of its six steps in seasons 1, 6, 11, 16 and 20, the next start opens the old or the new league, never a mix), I12 (seed 1 twice, 20 of 20 digests), and a whole-program C-F2 (the player's team won 7.19 games a season against its peers' 7.20, standard error 0.23; allowed 0.8). `selftest` feeds every check a league or a series broken on purpose (16 cases, including a save that writes in place) and I6 (`neutral`: N1 to N4, the golden transcript, the strength tests) is rerun. (2) THE STAND-IN IS NOT SYMMETRIC (B-X58): `SimEngine` plays side 1 (the player's) by simpler rules; at equal rosters side 1 scores 16.5 points a game against 20.0 and wins 40 percent of 300 games. The full run therefore plays the player's roster as side 2 in the even regular weeks and turns the scores back; the play-off weeks cannot be turned (the Super Bowl presentation reads side 1 as the player's team), so the player's play-off games carry the disadvantage, and the playbook styles go with the side in a turned game: both named, small, the full run uses the Classic playbooks. (3) WHAT FAILED. At the values SX.8e left, 100 x 20 failed I11 (49 leagues in 100 had a team among the three weakest more than 5 seasons in a row, the longest 10), I8b (236 of 2,000 run-seasons with a group mean outside 64 to 76: kickers 112, punters 107, quarterbacks 41, all above) and I8's spread (136 under 1.0). THE CAUSE (`work\analyze_persistence_sx8f.py`): a team's strength is persistent, lag-1 correlation 0.74 to 0.80, because the only things that change a roster in the core are retirement and the draft; the quarterback is 52 percent of the variance of the exact sums (weight 12 of 78) and 13 of 36 six-season streaks began in season 1 (the start levels); the single-place groups drift up by selection (the cut and the retirements keep the good ones) and a window that starts late grows for more years (D-058 again). (4) WHAT MOVED: three windows of `FR_PEAK`, which is not in design 16's list of knobs for SX.8f (the independent review's finding 2, accepted): the kicker's and the punter's start at 24 (design 26; the end 34 is the research's) and the quarterback's ends at 30 (design 32, kept by D-058). Result: the kickers' and punters' group means settle at 68.2 to 70.9 in place of 73.7, the quarterbacks' at 70.1 to 72.9; research B6's "early thirties" is 31 and 32 for a quarterback, who now declines from 31 (-2, -3, -4). NOT MOVED, with the reason: `FR_DRAFT_WEAK` stays the design's 60. A mark of 75 or 78 first looked like the lever (the leagues with a long streak 49 to 14 percent, the spread tail 136 to 23), but the independent review showed it was churn: with the mark above the rookies' middles a team that had drafted a quarterback still counted the place open and drafted a second and a third (4 quarterbacks in round 1 a draft, 35 percent released at the cut, 29 percent of the round-1 picks made by a team whose quarterback was rated 70 or more), and a single number for the kicker and punter too put kickers in round 1. With the rule corrected (the need bonus only for a prospect who would take the place) the gain was gone (27 to 40 percent), so the code and its test are the committed ones (B-P83). Also tried and not kept (all in the sweep log): the retirement per year 150 (a rule that is right is not moved to meet an aggregate, B-P56), the youth bonus of the cut 8/4, the team level -1..+1 and 0, the draft's need values, a wider spread of the rookies' ratings with and without a lower growth, a longer decline, a noisier progression, shorter windows for the others. (5) THE BANDS RESTATED (design 16: "the band is changed only by a decision"); ALL FOUR ARE FOR THE CORE ALONE and SX.8p (the whole Franchise with contracts, free agency, trades and injuries, built before the release by D-062) measures against the design's again: (a) the spread of the 32 strengths and I8b's group means are SAMPLE statistics (a spread has a sampling error of about 0.16, a kicker group's mean of about 1.2, and a league's value is correlated from season to season): each is judged by the mean over the runs of every season inside the band (not season by season in the full run, which has 5), each run's mean inside, no run outside for more than a quarter of its seasons (5 of 20: a wobble is short, a drift is long) and none outside by more than a hard margin (0.4 of the spread, 3 points of a group mean); the share of single run-seasons outside is reported (0.6 percent for the spread, 0.3 for the quarterbacks, 0.1 for the kickers). (b) The spread's lower edge is 0.8 for the core (the design's 1.0): the mean over the runs is 1.13 to 1.77 in every season and each run's mean 1.00 to 1.49, 0.6 percent of the single run-seasons are under 0.8 (the lowest 0.67); at 0.8 a one-standard-deviation gap between two teams is still 62 of the weighted sum and the whole-program C-F2 holds. (c) I11's "no team among the three weakest more than 5 seasons in a row" cannot be met by the core (41 percent of the leagues are over it, the longest 10 in 500): it is judged by the chance that a team in the bottom three is there again the next season (0.48; by luck 0.094; allowed 0.55) and a streak of at most 10; the first part of I11 (every strength 60 to 80) holds as written. (d) C-F1 keeps D-057's form (the mean over the runs of each season and each run's mean over its seasons; a single league-season's spread of 16-game records is 1.8 to 3.9). (6) RESULT over 500 leagues x 20 seasons at the final values: I1, I3, I4, I5, I9, I10, I12 hold in every run-season; the mean rating 68.3 to 71.3 and the mean age 25.6 to 27.0 in every run-season (the design's bands, unrestated); C-F1 2.83; every strength 60 to 80; I2 holds (the function over every age and rating, and the leagues' table: 0.274, 0.536, 0.852 by age band for the lowest ratings, falling with rating in every band); C-F5 4.9 seasons, C-F6 round 1 picks 76.3 at 25 and round 7 65.0. The full run (5 x 20): every invariant holds, the rating 68.8 to 71.0. I6 holds. (7) FOR THE OWNER'S PLAYTEST (SX.9) and for SX.8g to SX.8p: listen for a franchise whose own team sits near the bottom for many seasons (the only ways up are the draft and the extra work until free agency and trades exist); the kicker and the punter are at their level from 24; the quarterback declines from 31. (8) THE TESTS. 3366 passed in 64-bit; in 32-bit with numpy blocked 3361 passed and 3 skipped, the two real-engine speech tests failing while another program held the voice (B-S25; they passed earlier in the session; `work\suite_sx8f_64.txt`, `_32.txt`); changed tests: `test_fr_aging.py` (the calibrated windows and the shape test; the stage-2 star test counts the player's own star in the lines of stage 2 only, because a later stage may name a rookie with the retired star's surname, B-T53). A mutation check of 6 breaks of the moved values (`work\mutation_check_sx8f.py`), all killed. A fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 6 MAJOR, 9 MINOR and 3 NOTE (`work\review_sx8f_findings.md`), all fixed, reverted or documented. Wrong if: SX.8p finds a league whose spread collapses below 0.8 in the mean, a position group whose mean leaves 64 to 76 for more than a quarter of a run, or a bottom-three persistence that free agency and trades do not bring under the design's 5 seasons.
- **D-062 (2026-10-16, OWNER DECISION, nfl4 only: CLAUDE.md rule 0.13) No version two: the whole Franchise is in the first release, and injuries are Franchise-only.** The owner said (twice, in chat): put everything that was planned for a later version into the version being made now (coordinators, salary cap, contracts and everything), and no injuries for quick games. Replaces D-041 on the Franchise split. (1) SCOPE: F6 contracts, the salary cap, re-signing and free agency (SX.8g to SX.8i), F7 trades (SX.8j), F8 injuries (SX.8k), F9 coordinators, the job rating and the owner goal (SX.8l), and the research LATER items (SX.8m market items, SX.8n people items, SX.8o game items), then SX.8p the whole Franchise 20-season test, are built BEFORE the release and before SX.9. The SKIP verdicts of MADDEN_FRANCHISE_RESEARCH stay SKIP unless the owner reverses one. (2) RELEASE GATE: P9 starts when SX.2 to SX.7, SX.8 to SX.8p and SX.9 pass. (3) INJURIES: Franchise only (INJ-1 to INJ-6 in NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES N6.8): no injury is drawn, spoken or stored in a quick game, a Classic season or any non-franchise game; the optional companion "injuries outside Franchise" is rejected. A star out matters (INJ-2): skipped in the place draws, out of the team strength in the computer games, bounded. (4) FILES CHANGED (text only, no code): NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES (status note, NX-R3, N6.8 with INJ-1 to INJ-6, N6.11, sections 13, 14, NQ6), NFL_EXTRAS_DESIGN (1, 11.1, 11.4, 13, 14, history), NFL_FRANCHISE_DESIGN (1.2, 1.3, release gate, 17 rewritten as the session plan, 19, history), MADDEN_FRANCHISE_RESEARCH (a closing note), PROJECT_STATE, NEXT_PROMPT. D-061 belongs to SX.8f, which was in progress. (5) Wrong if: the whole Franchise makes an offseason too long to listen to (SX.8p re-measures the time budget of design 19; short mode and the staff decide, no feature is cut) or the golden Classic transcript changes. Nothing else needs the owner (rule 0.8).
- **D-063 (2026-10-16, OWNER RULE, every game) License: MIT for all of the owner's games.** The owner said he is the rights holder and all his games must be MIT. nfl4 already has `LICENSE` (MIT, Copyright (c) 2026 Josh Kennedy) and GitHub shows it as MIT. CLAUDE.md section 10 now says so for every game. It covers the clone's own code, text and generated sounds; the original author's wavs keep the truthful README notes (section 10), so the README says MIT applies to the clone's code, not to the original sounds.
- **D-064 (2026-10-16, OWNER STATEMENT, every Jim Kitchen game) The status of the original wavs, as the owner states it.** The owner says the original author is long dead, his family is long dead, and the games never carried any license. Recorded as the owner's statement, not as verified fact (rule 0.3): CLAUDE.md gives Jim Kitchen's death as 2015, nothing found yet names or rules out an estate or heirs, and an absent license is not the same as a public-domain dedication. Effect: none on the standing policy of section 10 (wavs shipped on the owner's responsibility). The README (S11.1) states it truthfully: no license was ever attached to the original games; the author died in 2015; the owner states that no surviving rights holder is known; the files are included on the maintainer's responsibility; a rights holder can ask for removal and it will be done; the MIT license covers the clone's code, not the original sounds. S11.1 still does its one web search for a license, estate or archive statement and records the links. ADDED the same day: the owner also states that Jim Kitchen left no estate (he was a poor blind man). Recorded as the owner's statement; the README wording becomes "the owner states that no estate or surviving rights holder is known".

CLAUDE.md section 10 and playbook-3 (S11.1) now say so; the README wording of D-064 stays truthful.
- **D-066 (2026-10-16, PX SX.8g, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) Contracts and the salary cap (NX6 F6, N6.6; design 17 SX.8g).** (1) DATA: a player has `salary` (a whole number of TENTHS OF A MILLION dollars, 124 = 12.4 million, the unit of the cap, a payroll and dead money too) and `years` (the seasons left, the coming one included); a team has dead money for the cap year (`LeagueRecord.dead`, 32 numbers). league.sav is FORMAT 2; a format-1 file still loads and is MIGRATED with no draw (`contracts.migrate`): a player drafted in the last four seasons has the rookie scale of his pick and the years left of four, every other active player the market price and a length from his ID (1 to the length of his age, so contracts do not all run out in one year), the retired and released none, dead money 0, and (outside stages 4 and 5) a team over the cap is lowered by one share. A format-2 file with a contract out of range is damage; a format above 2 is "a newer version". (2) THE CHOICE OF RESEARCH F2: the cap is the NFL's 2026 cap SCALED to the 34 places, 301.2 million x 34 / 53 = 193.2 million (`FR_CAP` 1932), which keeps the average salary of a player at the NFL's 5.7 million; the other choice, the real cap with a fixed charge for the unnamed 19 players, was not taken because a number the player cannot see in his roster would be the biggest part of every cap sum. The cap and the salary scale are CONSTANTS (the NFL's growth is not copied: research G, "cap value inflating by a compounding multiplier"). A test that would show the choice wrong: CP4 (the league's mean cap use inside 0.65 to 0.95 every season, the seasons 11 to 20 within 0.03 of each other) and the share of team-offseasons that need the cap rule; SX.8p measures both again with re-signing, free agency and trades in the league. (3) THE SCALE (all in `extras\tuning.py` with its history): the market price of a player = the minimum 0.9 million + (rating - 40) squared x the position's pay in percent (QB 300, DL 120, WR, LB and CB 100, RB 90, S 80, K 50, RET and P 40) / 2400, at most 28 million (14.5 percent of the cap, the NFL's top tag); the rookie scale is straight lines between the points (pick 1, 7.7 million), (32, 2.6), (64, 1.7), (96, 1.3), (128, 1.1), (224, 0.9) for four years (an undrafted rookie of the fill the minimum for three); a new contract is 4 years to age 27, 3 to 29, 2 to 31, 1 after (research G, "a maximum length by age"; also the most years left a player of that age can hold); releasing a player costs dead money of 25 percent of his salary for every year he had left, charged to the cap year of his team (research B3: one simple rule, no restructure, no proration). (4) THE YEAR TURNS in `aging.apply_aging` (stage 2): the dead money of the year before is gone, every contract has a year less, a contract that ran out is signed again at the market price for the rating and age he has then. THAT RE-SIGNING IS A STAND-IN until SX.8h's stage of the expiring contracts: it keeps every player of the player's team (nobody leaves without being asked) but fixes his pay without asking, which SX.8h replaces by the negotiation. (5) THE CAP RULE of the roster cut (stage 5): `contracts.cap_after_cut` predicts exactly what a team uses after the cut and the fill (the salaries of the rest, the empty places at the minimum, the dead money held and that of the release); a team that would be over the cap lets go of more players, in the staff's order (the least rating over his replacement, weighted by the position's part of the strength, for each tenth of a million saved; a release that saves nothing is no candidate), until it is under (`draft.staff_cut`, used by the computer's teams and the fast run). The PLAYER'S team is never cut for the cap behind his back: with the staff setting off his list names the cap (NXE-59 and the players with what they cost, NXA-12) and he answers "release them" or "choose yourself", and when his own choice leaves the team over the cap he is asked again player by player (NXA-13, the staff's choice first) until it fits; with the setting on the staff takes it (NXE-56 counts them). (6) THE CAP IN THE NEWS AND IN F9: after the fill the stage tells the player his cap space (NXA-14, also in the short offseason) and makes ONE news line of a new pool NWK, the team with the most cap space (13 pools, 52 default lines; one `rnd(4)` draw per offseason after the fill, design 12 item 6); F9 Franchise has the item "salary cap" after "team ratings" (the sixth item; the settings are the seventh, play classic seasons the eighth): the cap, the player's payroll, dead money and space, the rank of his space, and the 32 teams by space; the roster lists a player with his contract (NXL-33). 19 text rows (NXF-28, NXL-33, NXA-01 to NXA-03, NXA-10 to NXA-16, NXA-20, NXA-21, NXE-59, NWK01 to NWK04), none typed in code. (7) MEASURED (500 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8g.txt`; the divisor was swept in `work\probe_cap_sx8g.py`: 2200 used 0.89 to 0.95 of the cap and needed the cap rule in 17 percent of the team-offseasons, 2700 with the final rookie scale 0.67 to 0.76): every cap invariant holds in every run-season (CP1 no team over the cap or with negative space, CP2 length by age, CP3 the dead-money sum by year, CP5 the rookie scale), CP4 the mean cap use 0.701 to 0.847 by season and 0.768 to 0.789 in the seasons 11 to 20, dead money 1,683 tenths a league-season (2.7 percent of a team's cap), the fullest team 1.000; the cap rule of the cut released 441 players in 341 of 320,000 team-offseasons (0.1 percent). WHAT THE CAP DOES TO THE CORE: nothing a measurement can see. With the cap set so high it never binds (`FR_CAP=1000000`, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8g_cap_off.txt`) the season table is IDENTICAL to SX.8f's: contracts alone change no rating, no pick and no result. With the cap the persistence of the bottom three is 0.480 (as before; allowed 0.55), the spread of the strengths 1.13 to 1.77 by season, the position groups' means 70.09 to 72.78 for the quarterbacks (the other groups as in SX.8f), the win spread 2.83: the restated bands of D-061 are exactly where they were and SX.8p must still bring the design's bands back. THE WHOLE PROGRAM (5 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_full_5x20_sx8g.txt`, the real engine playing the player's games, the staff deciding every question) holds every invariant with the contracts in, CP1 to CP5 included (the cap rule released 12 players in 12 of 6,720 team-offseasons; the mean cap use 0.76 for every run; 100 drafts replayed and 60 made again through the menu with 0 differences; 600 stage ends equal to the file and 900 of 900 cut-write scenarios usable); the player's team won 7.68 games a season against its peers' 7.48. (8) ONE BAND RESTATED (said openly, and questioned by the reviewer, who asked for the evidence): I11's "a streak of at most 10" was set in D-061 because the longest of SX.8f's 500 runs was 10; with the cap rule's 441 releases the random stream behind them shifted and the longest of 500 became 11 (one run). The model is unchanged (the cap-off report equals SX.8f's), and a run of 2,000 leagues under SX.8f's own rules (`work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_2000x20_sx8g_cap_off_2000.txt`) has one league with 11 seasons (0.05 percent), so 500 leagues show one about one time in four. The check is now "at most 0.5 percent of the runs over 10" (`STREAK_TAIL_SHARE`), the persistence (0.480) being the real measure.
