(9) NOT BUILT, by plan: the expiring-contracts stage and the franchise tag (SX.8h), free agency and the best-offer invariant (SX.8i), the to-do kinds for contract talks, trades; the minimum-salary table by seasons (SX.8i). (10) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8g_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 2 MAJOR, 12 MINOR and 7 NOTE; a negative amount said wrong (-6.7 as -7.3), the I11 evidence above, a team that cannot get under the cap told nothing (now NXA-16), the migration lowering rookie pay, the drift check judging short runs, the dead-money watch adding up what the code returned (now worked out from the contract), two surviving mutations, the wording of the cap list and wrong numbers in comments were fixed; the file check stays lenient for retired players (ranges only) and the floor releases of D-059 stay (named for the playtest). A mutation check of 44 breaks, all killed (one survivor of the first run, a team exactly at the cap, got its test), is in `work\mutation_check_sx8g.txt`. (11) Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds the numbers of money hard to follow by ear (an amount is said "12.4 million"; a roster line gets longer by "5.2 million for 3 years"), or SX.8h and SX.8i find that a re-signing at the market price makes the cap bind too often or never (then the divisor, the dead-money share or the rookie scale move, as data, with a recorded run); the golden Classic transcript is unchanged (it passes). Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).
- **D-067 (2026-10-16, PX SX.8h, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) Re-signing: the expiring-contracts stage, the talks and the franchise tag (NX6 F6, N6.6; design 17 SX.8h; entry 1.8 of the design).** (1) WHERE: the stage is the 4th of 7, AFTER the development (stage 3) and before the draft, not "after stage 2" as design 17 said: the research's order (B1) is progression, then expiring contracts, and a player's price is the market price of the rating he has, so the player decides on the rating he will play with. The year still turns in stage 2 (`aging.apply_aging` -> `contracts.turn_the_year`: a year off every contract, the dead money gone) and a contract that ran out is left with 0 years and its last salary; SX.8g's stand-in that signed it again at once is gone. A stage's number is its ID in league.sav, so it is number 7 and `data.STAGES` lists it third from the end (1, 2, 3, 7, 4, 5, 6); the files of SX.8a to SX.8g keep their numbers. (2) WHO LEAVES: a player not signed again is a FREE AGENT, status "free" (new in `league.STATUSES`), the team he last played for and the season kept, no contract, no place, no dead money (a contract that ran out costs nothing). He is kept in `league.players` through the offseason for SX.8i's market; until SX.8i stage 6 drops the free agents of other teams like the released (`aging.drop_the_unkept`; the player's own stay for history). THE TEST THAT WOULD SHOW IT WRONG: SX.8i's first look at the market: the supply is 16 players a league-offseason, none rated 80 (see (7)); if that is too few for a market worth hearing, SX.8i makes its own supply (the knobs are `FR_RESIGN_MIN_RATING` and `FR_RESIGN_RESERVE`; Q-031). (3) THE STAFF'S RULE, one code path for the 31 computer teams, for the player's team when the staff handles it, and for the fast run (`resign.run_teams` -> `plan_team` -> `apply_plan`; the program and `work\sim_league_sx8d.py` call the same functions): in the order of value (the position's strength weight times the rating over the middle rating of the rookie the fill would put there) a player is signed again at his ASKING price when he is rated at least `FR_RESIGN_MIN_RATING` (60) and the cap has room for the price once every other place still without a contract is counted at the minimum (`resign.room_after`), else he is let go. The asking price is the MARKET price of his position and rating for the length of his age (`contracts.market_contract`), or, for a player with fewer than `FR_EXCLUSIVE_SEASONS` (3) seasons in the league, the minimum for one year: research B3's exclusive rights, built as a price and a question, not as an automatic signing (said openly: "kept automatically" would decide for the player when the setting is off). Nothing is drawn. The cap rule of the roster cut stays as SX.8g built it. HOW 60 WAS FOUND (the numbers are in (7)): the first rule was a margin of 3 rating points over the fill's middle rating; it let 1.0 percent go and the independent review showed that the cut then released players signed again days before (14.6 percent of its releases, 17.6 percent of the dead money). A margin of 10 (the draft class's mean true rating, 60, over the fill's 50) halved that and let 9.6 percent go, but for the groups of one or two places the fill's middle is 55 and the margin asked 65 of a kicker or punter, whose supply from the draft is as thin as the fill's: the 2,000-league run had a kickers' league mean of 59.88 (1 run-season beyond the hard margin) against 62.72 with everybody signed again; a margin of 14 broke the floor of a group (I5). So the rule is ONE number for every group, the class's mean, 60. (4) THE PLAYER'S TALK (`resign.Talk`, `resign_flow._talk`): his own expiring players are asked one at a time, the most valuable first, each with his mood, his price and the room under the cap; the answers are sign him, offer less (not offered when nothing is lower than the minimum), use the franchise tag (while it is unused and fits), let him go; an answer that does not fit the room is not offered, and when nothing but letting him go fits the stage says so (NXW-05, or NXW-07 when the team is over the cap). A talk lasts at most `FR_TALK_ROUNDS` (3) rounds: "offer less" offers 10 percent under the present price (`FR_OFFER_LESS`), rounded half up; the player's MOOD, a rule of last season's record (happy after 11 wins or after reaching the division round, a win or a bye; unhappy after 5 wins or fewer; else content: `FR_MOOD_WINS`), sets the most he gives up (10, 4, 0 percent: `FR_MOOD_SLACK`, rounded half up like the offer, so a happy player ALWAYS accepts the first offer for less); an offer under his floor, which is every offer to a content or unhappy player, is answered with a counter halfway to the floor (rounded up) and the next round, so the gain from a content player is signing him at the counter; a failed offer in the last round ends the talk and he leaves (NXW-12). The talk is not saved: a window closed after a counter restarts it from round 1 at the full price (the reviewer's note; "leaves him as he was"). The length of a contract is the age's (`FR_CONTRACT_YEARS`) and no choice of the talk changes it: research G ("negotiation options that force extra years") and the cap on the length by age make the research's "offer more years" a lower price in disguise, so it is NOT built (said openly). The research's meters (patience, interest) stay SKIP. (5) THE FRANCHISE TAG, exclusive style: one a year for the player's team, one year, at the average of the `FR_TAG_TOP` (5) highest running salaries at his position, or `FR_TAG_RAISE` (120) percent of his last salary, or his market price, the most, never over the maximum salary; the computer teams and the staff do not use it (SX.8m has the non-exclusive tag); the transition tag and the restricted-free-agent tenders stay SKIP. (6) NOTHING IS DECIDED FOR THE PLAYER when the staff setting is off: every expiring player of his is asked (a test lets Enter sign a player the staff would have let go), a window closed at a question leaves him as he was and the file holds the stage in progress (`league.resign`, format 3: pending, tag_used, signed, let_go, league_gone, stars), and the setting is read at every step, so turning it on in F9 after the first answer hands the rest to the staff (NXW-30). The stage's start is ONE write (the other teams settled, the player's players waiting) before anything is said; each decision is written and then told (B-X53). league.sav is FORMAT 3; a format-2 file loads as it is (no stage in progress), a format-2 file saved after stage 2 holds the contracts the stand-in had signed so the new stage finds nobody to ask, a format-1 file still migrates; a contract of 0 years is damage except after stage 2 and in stage 7 for the players still to be asked (`contracts._years_may_be_zero`). (7) MEASURED (500 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8h.txt`; 2,000 leagues `..._2000x20_sx8h_2000.txt`; the stand-in baseline, everybody signed again at the market price after the development, which is SX.8g's behaviour at the new timing and not SX.8g itself, `..._sx8h_standin.txt` and `..._2000x20_sx8h_standin_2000.txt`; SX.8g's `..._sx8g.txt`): every invariant holds in every run-season in the 500 and in the 2,000 leagues: I1 to I5, CP1 to CP5, I8 rating (lowest 68.43 and 68.19), I8 age, I9, I10, I12 and the new RS1 to RS4 (only an expired contract is let go; a signing is the price asked, an offer for less down to a happy player's floor, the exclusive-rights minimum or the tag's exact price; nobody is left with 0 years and no free agent holds a contract or a place; the staff's plan equals an oracle written again from numbers, with a selftest case in which the cap decides). 251 contracts run out in a league-offseason (23 percent of the 1,088 players); the stage signs 93.5 percent again and lets 6.5 percent go (16 players a league-offseason, mean rating 56.9, none rated 80 or more, none for want of room under the cap, which at a mean use of 0.77 does not bind before free agency, as D-066 found). WHAT RE-SIGNING DOES TO THE CORE, against SX.8g's report: the persistence of the bottom three 0.478 (0.480; allowed 0.55; 0.475 in 2,000 leagues), the longest streak 10 (11), the spread of the strengths 1.12 to 1.77 by season (1.13 to 1.77), the quarterbacks' league mean 70.09 to 72.79 (72.78) and the other groups as before, the mean rating 68.43 to 71.31 and age 25.53 to 27.02 in every run-season, the win spread 2.84, careers 4.90 (4.91), round-1 picks 76.2 and round-7 picks 65.0 at 25, the mean cap use 0.686 to 0.834 by season and 0.765 to 0.786 in the seasons 11 to 20 (0.701 to 0.847, 0.768 to 0.789), dead money 1,487 tenths a league-season (1,683), the cap rule of the cut releasing 175 players in 138 of 320,000 team-offseasons (441 in 341): nothing a measurement can tell from SX.8g's league beyond less dead money; the stage changes the player's decisions, not the league's health. THE WHOLE PROGRAM (5 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_full_5x20_sx8h.txt`, the real engine playing the player's games, the first answer at every question: Enter signs whoever fits, which is NOT the staff's rule for the player's team; that rule runs for the other 31 teams in every run and for the player's in `test_fr_resign_flow.py`) holds every invariant with the stage in (700 stage ends equal to the file, 1,050 of 1,050 cut-write scenarios usable, 100 drafts replayed and 60 made again with 0 differences; the player's team 8.06 wins a season against its peers' 8.11); 585 seconds. SX.8p must still bring the design's bands back. (8) TWO CHECKS CHANGED (said openly; the reviewer questioned the first): (a) I8 and I8b allowed no run to stay outside a band for more than 5 seasons in a row. SX.8g's 500 leagues had none; this session's first 500 had 2, both quarterbacks above 76. The cause is a cohort tail of the quarterbacks' group, not the stage: 2,000 leagues with the stand-in have 3 runs over 5 seasons (0.15 percent, the longest 7), the same 2,000 with the final rule have 3 (0.15 percent, the longest 6); the first rule (margin 3) had 8 of 2,000, a difference the sampling error of such small counts cannot rule out, and the rule was changed for the reason in (3), not for this tail. The check is now "at most 1 percent of the runs (`STRETCH_TAIL_SHARE`) over 5 seasons", derived from the chance of failing (a true rate of 0.15 percent is 0.75 runs of 500; an allowance of 5 is exceeded 0.01 percent of the time, an allowance of 2 four percent), every other part of the band unchanged; in the full run (5 runs) the allowance is 0.05 runs, so none. (b) I2 compared the bare retirement rates of its cells and failed on 0.789 against 0.799 in two cells of about 300 players (ages 36 to 39) when a larger share of old weak players left; it now counts a step against the expected direction only when it is more than two standard errors; the selftest cases (a rate that falls with age by 0.2) still fail and the real leagues pass. (9) NOT BUILT, by plan: free agency and the best-offer invariant (SX.8i), the non-exclusive tag (SX.8m), a to-do kind for early extensions (personality, SX.8n), the computer teams' use of the franchise tag, a news pool for the signings (the stage names the other teams' stars it lets go, at most three, by sentences of its own; none is rated 80 or more at present, so NXW-40 is never heard until SX.8i's market makes stars available). (10) F9 and help: the roster says "contract ended" for a player not yet settled; the cap item does not count him; the F6 help has a line (NXH-39); the staff setting keeps its label "staff handles the to do list" (it also handles the cut and now the contracts). 26 rows (NXE-17, NXW-01 to NXW-07, NXW-10 to NXW-14, NXW-20 to NXW-22, NXW-30, NXW-31, NXW-40, NXW-41, NXW-60, NXW-A01 to NXW-A04, NXH-39). (11) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8h_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 2 MAJOR, 9 MINOR and 2 NOTE, all fixed or decided as that file says: a test of the room that could not fail (a hand-worked test and a selftest case now), the evidence and the wording of the stretch allowance, the numbers re-run on the final code, RS2 and the full run's description, the churn of re-signing then releasing (the minimum rating), the mood of a bye, assertions that could not fail, the texts (singular, zero, negative room, the order of the answers, no offer for less at the minimum) and stale sentences. A mutation check of 56 breaks, all killed, is `work\mutation_check_sx8h.txt`. (12) Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds that nobody ever leaves (then the supply is SX.8i's to make, see (2)), that a player who is let go or ends the talks feels unfair (the mood rule may be too hard or too soft: it is a rule of last season's record only), that a content player's talk is a dead end (the offer for less never reaches his floor: only a happy player accepts it), or that three rounds are too long to hear (the rounds and the step are data); the golden Classic transcript is unchanged (it passes). Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).

- **D-069 (2026-10-16, PX SX.8i, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) Free agency: the market of the players whose contracts ended, the staff's payroll target, the minimum-salary table (NX6 F6, N6.6; design 17 SX.8i; entry 1.9 of the design).** (1) WHERE: stage 8, spoken as stage 5 of 8, right after the expiring contracts and before the draft (research B1's order); `data.STAGES` is (1, 2, 3, 7, 8, 4, 5, 6) and `STAGE_MAX` 8; league.sav is FORMAT 4 (`LeagueRecord.market`: round, offers, signed, lost, staff; a format-3 file loads with no market). (2) THE MARKET (`extras\franchise\market.py`, no speech, no file, no draw; `market_flow.py` is the stage): the free agents of this offseason are the players with the status "free" who left in the season that just ended (`pool`; a star kept for history from an earlier offseason never comes back). It runs in 3 rounds (`FR_FA_ROUNDS`; research B4 says 3 to 5, three keeps the stage short to hear): in each round every team places its offers, then all offers are judged together, the free agents best first (`resign.value`, the position's weight times the rating over his replacement); a free agent nobody signs asks 10 percent of his stage-7 price less for every round he stays (`FR_FA_PRICE_DROP`: 90 and 80 percent), never under his minimum. (3) THE OFFERS (`team_bids`, ONE function for the computer teams, the staff and the fast run): a team bids only for a position with an EMPTY place (no stacking, no release to make room, so no big dead money), for players rated at least `FR_FA_MIN_RATING` (60, the draft class's mean, as the stage-7 rule), whom it can pay (`room`: the cap less the contracts that run, the dead money and the minimum for every other empty place), at their price, for at most 2 players for each open place in round 1 and one more in every later round (`chase`): the first review run found that with a flat chase 29 signable free agents a year left while teams had a place and the money, and 45 of 130 were unsigned; with the growing chase 26.5 are. (4) THE JUDGEMENT (`resolve`): the score of an offer is the salary raised by the fit (0, 2 or 4 percent: the player would not be better than the group's weakest / would be / would be its best); the highest score wins if its team still has an empty place at his position and the room, else the next offer; equal scores are told apart by a rule that favors no team (`tie_key`: a multiplier from his ID, modulo 37; the review showed that "team plus ID" put each team ahead of its neighbour in 31 of 32 ties, and the first sweep's "the weaker team of last season first" was a parity machine, persistence 0.03 to 0.11: B-P90). Because every computer offer is at the price, the fit's percentages change nothing but the order of the three levels (0, 2, 4 and 0, 1, 2 give the same leagues, said openly and pinned by a test); the player's "ten percent more" beats the best fit and so always wins, which is the point of paying more. (5) THE PLAYER: each round he hears his open places and the money he has, then the staff's list of five free agents for those places (most valuable first), each with his position, age, rating, potential, the price and how many other teams are interested (the computer teams' offers of the round, computed before he decides), "hear more free agents", "end the round and hear the results" and "let the staff make my offers from now on"; a free agent opens a small menu: the price, ten percent more, withdraw, never mind; at most 5 offers a round and at most the open places times the chase for one position; an offer he cannot pay is not in the menu. The results follow: his signings, his offers that lost and to whom, the three most valuable signings of the other teams and a count. Every offer is written as it is placed, the round is judged, written and then told (B-X53), and a window closed anywhere resumes in the same round with the offers kept. F9 has a "free agents" item (NXF-29: the market with the price of the next round; "the market is closed" outside the stage). (6) NOTHING IS DECIDED FOR THE PLAYER when the staff setting is off: a round he ends without an offer signs nobody for him (a test, and FA9 in the whole-program run). The staff makes his offers (the computer teams' rule, the same function) when the setting is on or when he handed the rest of free agency to it; the setting is read at every step, and turning it on in F9 while he is placing offers withdraws the offers of that round, which he is told (NXJ-G03). (7) THE SUPPLY (Q-031, answered): the one lever without a random draw is a payroll target of the staff when it signs players again (stage 7, `FR_RESIGN_TARGET` 0.62 of the cap: it signs again only while the coming season's contracts, the new ones and the dead money stay under the target); the player's own talks are held only to the cap, as before. Sweep (64 leagues x 10 seasons): 1.00: 16 free agents a league-offseason, no star; 0.74: 39; 0.70: 57 (0.9 stars); 0.66: 84 (1.9); 0.62: 117 (4.0); 0.58: 150 (6.4). 0.62 is the first share at which the market is worth hearing; 0.66 would keep the persistence a little higher (0.44 against 0.39) and the spread no different, so the supply was not given up for a smaller target. (8) THE MINIMUM SALARY TABLE of research B4 (0 seasons 0.9, 1: 1.0, 2: 1.1, 3: 1.1, 4 to 6: 1.2, 7 or more 1.3 million) is data (`FR_MIN_SALARY_BY_SEASONS`, `contracts.min_salary`): the exclusive-rights price of stage 7, the least any player asks (the whole-program run found a veteran of 11 seasons rated 51 asking 1.1, under his 1.3: `resign.asking` now never asks under it) and the floor of a free agent's falling price; the talk's "offer less" never goes under it (`Talk.least`). (9) MEASURED (2,000 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_2000x20_sx8i_2000.txt`; 500: `..._500x20_sx8i.txt`; the whole program: `..._full_5x20_sx8i.txt`): every invariant holds in every run-season (I1 to I5, CP1 to CP5, RS1 to RS4, FA1 to FA8, I8 rating 68.30 to 71.45, I8 age, I11 strengths and bottom three). 252 contracts run out in a league-offseason and 51.5 percent are not signed again; the market has 130 free agents (mean rating 67.4, 6.4 rated 80 or more) for 196 open places, 104 are signed (80 percent, mean rating 69.5, every star), 26.5 leave unsigned, by round 38, 37 and 28; 88 percent of the 4.2 million free agents judged had more than one offer; the roster cut then releases 133 players, 3.75 of them signed in the market (52 of 1,402 tenths of the dead money). The cap decided 4 of 45 million offers: at the league's use of 0.75 need, not money, limits a team, so FA5 and the room branch of FA2 are exercised by their selftest cases and by a STRESS RUN with a cap of 1450 (25 percent lower, `..._fast_100x20_sx8i_cap1450.txt`: cap use 0.85 to 0.99, the room decided 7,776 offers, the cap rule of the cut fired in 6,611 of 64,000 team-offseasons): every research-G invariant (CP1, FA1 to FA8) still holds, only CP4's band of the cap use fails, as it should. THE WHOLE PROGRAM (5 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_full_5x20_sx8i.txt`, the real engine playing the player's games; the staff setting ON in the runs of even seeds and OFF in the others) holds every invariant with the stage in: 800 stage ends equal to the file, 1,200 of 1,200 cut-write scenarios usable, 100 drafts replayed and 60 made again with 0 differences; FA9: with the setting off nobody joined the player's team in the market in 3 runs, with it on the staff signed 120 players for it; the player's team 7.55 wins a season against its peers' 7.80; 663 seconds. The first whole-program run found a real defect, a veteran of 11 seasons rated 51 asking 1.1 million, under his minimum of 1.3 (RS2), which the fast run could not reach because the staff lets such a player go. (10) THREE THINGS SAID OPENLY. (a) The market is a little parity (the payroll target takes players from the teams with high payrolls, a progressive tax; the fit sends free agents to the groups where they would be best): against SX.8h's 2,000 leagues the persistence of the bottom three falls from 0.478 to 0.363 (the design wants it lower), but the spread of the 32 strengths falls to 0.95 to 1.12 from season 4 (the core: about 1.1 to 1.4), below the design's lower edge of 1.0 in seasons 4 to 8 and under the core's relaxed 0.8 in 4.9 percent of the run-seasons (I8 passes by the core's rules), and the win spread from 2.83 to 2.79. (b) C-F1 per run (a run's mean win spread over 20 seasons inside 2.6 to 3.4) was "no run outside"; 4 of 2,000 runs are now just outside (lowest 2.58; none before, lowest 2.62), so it is judged like the other sample statistics: at most 1 percent of the runs (`WIN_TAIL_SHARE`) and none by more than 0.1 (`WIN_HARD`), a recorded loosening that SX.8p must reverse by bringing the design's spread back (the levers, the lean and the ratings' spread of SX.8b, are outside this stage). (c) The player is better off than the computer teams in two ways: his talks are not held to the 62 percent target, and "ten percent more" always wins a contested free agent; both are kept on purpose (a person who pays more should win; the talks are his decision) and with the setting on his team follows the computer teams' rule. (11) NOT BUILT, by plan: to-do kinds for contract talks (design 17 lists them; the stage asks in its own flow), compensatory picks and undrafted free agents as a market (SX.8m), a news pool for the signings (the stage tells the other teams' three most valuable signings in sentences of its own), a customizable pool (the stage's sentences are fixed), restricted free agents, more than 3 rounds, a player's choice of team by anything but the offer, unsigned free agents signing for the minimum at the end (they simply leave: the draft and the fill fill the places, and a leftover would cost more than the fill's minimum). (12) TEXTS: 39 rows (NXE-18, NXJ-01, NXJ-02, NXJ-04, NXJ-A01 to A04, NXJ-B01 to B03, NXJ-C01 to C05, NXJ-C07, NXJ-C08, NXJ-D01 to D03, NXJ-D05, NXJ-E01 to E06, NXJ-F02, NXJ-G01 to G03, NXJ-H01, NXJ-I01 to I04, NXF-29, NXH-40); the singular forms are rows of their own; no number that is data (rounds, the percent more) is written in a sentence. (13) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8i_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 2 MAJOR, 8 MINOR and 6 NOTE: no check failed when the computer teams bid by the wrong rule (FA8, the oracle for the offers, was added), the loosening and the comparison of spans (stated plainly above), the tie rule's neighbours, the short offseason's results, the offers dropped by the setting, a fixture that kept the shipped target out of the flow tests, the whole-program run's setting (now alternating by seed, with FA9), the supply left unsigned (the chase), the texts. A mutation check of 96 breaks: all 96 killed (`work\mutation_check_sx8i.txt`); the first run left 5 survivors (the headlines, an offer added next to the old one, a full team told in every round, F9's list when the market is closed, the target's value) and the review's changes 2 more (the player's limit with the later chase, the singular), each now has a test. (14) THE SUITE: 3,703 passed in 64-bit and 3,705 in 32-bit with numpy blocked (one suite after the other), the golden Classic transcript among them. The 11 failures of the first whole-suite run were 6 format and stage numbers in my own tests (fixed) and 5 tests of the other session's sound-pool work in `test_functions.py` (the Sounds menu has more items; theirs). (15) Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds the market too long to hear (three rounds, a list of five and a headline of three are data), too easy ("ten percent more" always wins), a star always going to a team he left (a team re-bids for its own free agent like any other), or the league too even (the spread above); the golden Classic transcript is unchanged (it passes). Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).

- **D-070 (2026-10-17, PX SX.8j, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) Trades: the value rule, the picks of the coming draft, the computer teams' trades in one code path, the player's offers and proposals, the deadline (NX6 F7, N6.7; design 17 SX.8j; entry 1.10 of the design).** (1) WHAT A TRADE IS: one player of each team, both of the SAME position, so every place stays filled in the season and nobody is released for it (no dead money, no draw for a fill); the side that must give more adds up to `FR_TRADE_PICKS_MAX` (2) picks of the COMING draft. Players for picks alone and pick-for-pick trades are not built: a player sent away without one coming back would leave a place empty in the season. A player traded once is not traded again that season. (2) THE PICKS: a pick is (round, the team whose pick it is by the draft order); the league keeps only the exceptions (`LeagueRecord.trades["picks"]`: round, original team, owner); the draft makes them its owners pick by pick when it begins (`draft.owners_of`, the draft record's `owners`) and the table starts empty again; the draft's `slot` names the owner; a team may have more or fewer than seven rookies (the whole-file check counts them against the owners). The picks of later drafts are not tradeable (the design's "picks of the coming draft"). (3) THE VALUE RULE (research B5), `extras\franchise\trades.py`, no draw: a player's value to a team is his worth over the fill's middle rating times the position's weight (the unit of `resign.value`), never under 0, weighted by his age in the team's situation (`FR_TRADE_AGE`: contending, middle, rebuilding), by his potential while he is 25 or younger, plus what his contract saves against his market price for the years left (`FR_TRADE_MONEY_DIVISOR`, toward zero); a pick is worth `FR_TRADE_PICK_VALUE[round]` weighted by the situation (`FR_TRADE_PICK_SHARE`: 70, 100, 130 percent). The pick values are MEASURED (work\measure_pick_value_sx8j.py, 200 leagues x 12 seasons: the value of the player a pick became in his first and second season, 0 when the cut released him): (48, 38, 29, 19, 10, 3, 1). A team's situation is a rule of its record after 3 games (2 wins more: contending; 2 losses more: rebuilding). A computer team agrees only when it gains at least `FR_TRADE_MARGIN` rating points OF THE POSITION (times its weight, `trades.margin_of`): a margin in plain value points let quarterbacks alone through (B-P94). (4) THE WINDOW: the NFL's 2026 deadline is 4 p.m. on 2026-11-10, the day after week 9 of 18 (research F2; nfl.com, chargers.com, SRC-OFFICIAL and SRC-SECONDARY): half the season, so trades are open in the regular season up to the kick-off of week 9 (`FR_TRADE_LAST_WEEK`; design 17 said "week 10 of 16" before it was worked out). The computer teams look in the preparations of weeks 4 to 9 (`FR_TRADE_FIRST_WEEK`: a record of three games says what a team is). No trade in the offseason (around the draft, research B5's Madden fact): not built, the offseason has eight stages already. (5) ONE CODE PATH (research G): `trades.week` is called by the program's weekly preparation (`weekly.prepare`, with the league's records) and by the fast run (`work\sim_league_sx8j.py`): it searches (`trades.search`: for every pair of a contending and a rebuilding team the trade the contender gains most from, the rebuilder's best veteran of 28 or more for one of the contender's players at least 3 points lower at that position, `build` adding the cheapest picks that make up a short side; then the week takes them the greatest gain in rating points first, each team once, at most `FR_TRADE_WEEK_MAX` (1) a week and `FR_TRADE_SEASON_MAX` (2) for a team a season; the turned order of the week only breaks equal gains) and makes every trade of two computer teams; a trade with the player's team is an OFFER in his to-do list (the kind `trade_offer`: accept, the staff's recommendation, decline, the do-nothing answer, later); with the staff setting on the staff accepts it, which leaves the very league the fast run leaves (a test). A trade brings the two teams' strengths for the computer's games up to date (`league.sums`; design 5.2 kept them for the season, which would make a trade do nothing until the next one), so the draft order's tie uses the strengths the teams ended the season with. (6) NOTHING IS DECIDED FOR THE PLAYER when the staff setting is off: an offer waits for his answer and is declined at the kick-off if he leaves it (TR9 in the whole-program run: with the setting off 26 offers came and nothing was traded for him; with it on the staff accepted 6); an offer that can no longer be made when he accepts (a player or a pick has moved, the cap, the deadline) says so (NXX-03). (7) THE PLAYER'S OWN TRADES, F9, franchise, trades (NXF-43; the title says the deadline's week): offers waiting; offer one of your players (the trade block: every other team that gains the margin from him makes its best offer, its player of the same position and as many of its picks as it can give while it still gains, the five best for him first); ask for a player (the other teams' players of a position, best first; he picks whom he gives; the other team names the picks it wants by the same rule, or says no); your draft picks; the trades of the season. Each trade is made, written, then told. The player's proposals are not held to the computer's season limit. (8) THE NEWS: a trade of two other teams is a line of the new pool NWX of news.txt (four default lines, customizable, rule 0.12), placed after the player's own news and inside the week's limit of FR_NEWS_MAX lines (`news.with_trades`). (9) league.sav FORMAT 5: `trades` (`picks`, `log`), checked whole (`trades.check_trades`), and the draft record's `owners`; a format-4 file loads with no trades and a draft in progress gets the draft order as its owners (also a format-1 file, the review's MAJOR finding). (10) THE COUNT AND THE MARGIN (sweeps of 48 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\sweep_trades_sx8j.py`): with 4 trades a week the count stayed at 22 a league-season whatever the margin (the limit bound, B-P95); one trade a week and a margin of 12 rating points give 4.25 trades a league-season with the rule deciding the weeks (about three in ten window weeks have none), every position but the quarterback (0.3 percent of the trades), 1.1 picks a trade, the better player 23 points over the one traded for him. (11) MEASURED, said plainly (500 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8j.txt`, against `_sx8j_off.txt`, the same leagues without trades, whose season table is SX.8i's to the digit; 2,000 leagues: `_fast_2000x20_sx8j_2000.txt`; the whole program: `_full_5x20_sx8j.txt`): every invariant holds with trades in (I1 to I12, CP1 to CP5, RS1 to RS4, FA1 to FA9, the new TR1 to TR9); trades are a little ANTI-parity in a season (the contenders get the veterans: the spread of the strengths rises by 0.125 on the average from a season's start to its deadline) and nearly neutral over seasons: the win spread 2.79 to 2.88, the persistence of the bottom three 0.362 to 0.370 (longest streak 9), the spread at the start of the seasons from season 4 0.96 to 1.17 (under the design's 1.0 in seasons 4 to 8 by at most 0.04), the position groups and the cap use unchanged. C-F1 per run is STRICT again (D-069's share of 1 percent is reversed: no run of 2,500 is outside 2.6 to 3.4). ONE BAND JUDGED AS A TAIL, said openly: the hard margin of I8's spread and I8b (a single run-season may not leave the band by more than 0.4 or 3.0) allows 1 run-season in 10,000 beyond it and none by more than 1.0 more (`HARD_TAIL_SHARE`, `HARD_FAR`): one quarterback run-season of 42,000 reached 79.09 (0.09 over 79) in the 2,000-league run while the quarterbacks were LESS often outside the band than without trades (172 against 188 run-seasons, the longest stretch 6 against 9); the highest values of three 2,000-league runs are 78.16, 77.97 and 79.09, a value moved by a changed random stream (B-P85, B-P89), not a drift. SX.8p must still bring the spread to the design's band in the early seasons. (12) CHECKS: TR1 to TR8 in `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f.py` (each with `selftest` cases; the wrappers keep their own record of the trades and the traded picks, so a program that forgets to log a trade or to move a pick is found: the review's MAJOR findings), TR7 an oracle of the search written again from the numbers, TR9 in `work\full_sx8f.py` (the rig answers "later" to every offer when the staff setting is off). (13) TEXTS: rows NXX-01 to NXX-05, NXX-10 to NXX-29, NXX-A01, NXX-A02, NXX-P01 to NXX-P06, NXF-43, NXK-14, NXK-15, NXH-41, NWX01 to NWX04; new placeholders <get>, <give>, <give_r>, <give_a>, <picks>. (14) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8j_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 4 MAJOR, 8 MINOR and 8 NOTE, all fixed or decided (the format-1 draft, the checks that read the program's own tables, the offer item's check, the search that favoured a team's number, the fixed rhythm of one trade a week, the staff's sentence without the picks, the deadline week never said, test gaps). A mutation check of 99 breaks (`work\mutation_check_sx8j.py`; two more of the first list were equivalent, the early guards of `block_offers` and `ask` that `problems` repeats): the first run left 10 survivors, each got a test (the potential's last young age, the margin after the picks, the veteran's age, the best trade of a pair, the gain in rating points, the best veteran, the news of trades: the player named, the count, the order, the limit) and all 10 are killed. THE SUITE: 3,819 passed in 64-bit and 3,819 in 32-bit with numpy blocked, one after the other, the golden Classic transcript among them; the 23 tests that broke when trades came in were format numbers, menu positions (the new F9 item), the news pools (fourteen now) and the week's line limit, which the trades now share. (15) NOT BUILT, by plan: offseason trades, picks of later drafts, players for picks alone, more than one player a side, trade demands (SX.8n), a to-do kind for the player's own proposals (they are made in F9 at once). Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds the offers too rare or too frequent (the margin and the weekly limit are data), the value rule too generous to the player's proposals, or the news of trades too regular. Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).
- **D-071 (2026-10-17, PX SX.8k, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) Injuries, FRANCHISE ONLY: the statuses, the next man up at replacement level, the week's draws in one code path and the per-play draws of a played game calibrated to the NFL's injury reports, the bounded effect on the computer's games, the report and the question before a game, injuries.txt, league.sav format 6 (NX6 F8, N6.8 INJ-1 to INJ-6; design 17 SX.8k; entry 1.11 of the design).** (1) FRANCHISE ONLY (D-062, INJ-1): the injuries reach a game only through `Game.game_injuries` (`play_game(game_injuries=...)`, given by the franchise's `game_arguments` alone; None in every other game), and the new hook H16 (`after_draws` at the end of `snap_draws`, `second_tackler` at the kick-off return) is a no-op in Classic: a quick game with the N. F. L. rules and a classic season (both Rules values) draw exactly what they drew, tested by their traces with every injury rule made to raise; the golden Classic transcript is unchanged. (2) THE STATUS is one number, the games he still misses when he rests: 3 or more out, 2 doubtful, 1 questionable; in the play-offs every hurt player is out (they have no to-do list). The time away is told as a range that narrows by one a week and always holds the truth ("out, 2 to 4 weeks"), "for the season" only when he is back after the Super Bowl. The computer teams and the staff play a questionable player (3 rating points under his rating) and rest a doubtful one (6 under if he plays); a man who played hurt may be hurt worse after the game (100 and 300 per mille); start values, UNVERIFIED, for SX.8p and the owner's playtest. (3) WHO PLAYS IN HIS PLACE (INJ-2): the game's rosters are the week's lineup, so the place draws skip the hurt: the next man down the depth order (the place order), else the nearest above; a returner's place a running back, then a receiver, the lowest first; a group with nobody to play its STAND-IN (a name drawn, rating 55 for a quarterback, 60 a kicker or punter, 50 the others, kept to the season's end). A place a substitute plays counts at REPLACEMENT LEVEL (the lower of his rating and the stand-in rating): the hurt man's rating counts for nothing and his place counts the same whoever is behind him; an injury raises a team only when its player was under replacement level. The design's "the first player in the depth order that is healthy" is read as the next man up. (The independent review's first MAJOR finding: with the substitute's own rating, 484 of 1,088 single injuries raised a new league's team, by up to 42.) H16 keeps two tacklers two men when one man plays two places. (4) THE RATE (INJ-3, the calibration's target): the NFL's own team injury reports, 48.8 injuries a team in the 2018 regular season and 59.3 in 2019 (16 games; Puga et al., JMIRx Med 2022, PMC9048138, SRC-SECONDARY: a peer-reviewed count of the league's reports, read through the search's summaries; a second count of the same kind, Cureus 2022, 42.8 a team, lies under it), scaled to the 34 places by the head count (31.3 to 38.0 a team-season, `FR_INJ_TARGET`; the offensive linemen are not modelled, said plainly). A NEW injury is one of a player fit for his game (an injury report lists one injury once): calibrated over 32 leagues x 6 seasons (`work\calibrate_rate_sx8k_32x6.txt`: 31.3, 32.6, 33.9, 35.1 for factors 1.00 to 1.15 of the chances), the chances are practice 4 draws of 1303 per ten thousand, the game part 16 of 1310, a played game 441 per exposed play. (5) ONE CODE PATH (INJ-3, research G): `injuries.after_games` is called by the program's weekly preparation (for the games of the week before) and by the fast run (`work\sim_league_sx8k.py`): team 1 to 32, the draws of "hurt worse", the practice part (every team, every place alike) and, for a team whose game the engine did not play, the game part (the places weighted by the exposed plays of 400 played team-games, `work\measure_exposure_sx8k.py`: runs 23.4, quarterback runs 2.8, catches 15.4, sacks 4.0, returns 2.1); the lineup is made again after every hit. The player's game draws per exposed play instead (a rush, a catch and its tackle, a sack, a quarterback's run, a kick-off return; half the hits the tackler), with the same expected injuries a team-game, so his team is hurt as often as the others; the announcer says it (a line of IJX), the man leaves at once and his replacement plays on; a stand-in quarterback is signed then and named. Injuries come from the games of weeks 1 to 15 (the week-16 game has no preparation after it). (6) THE COMPUTER'S GAMES (INJ-2, C-F1): a team's place in its league is measured from the WEEK's mean of the effective sums (a loss every team shares moves nobody: from the season's mean the win spread fell to 2.65) and BOUNDED to half a standard deviation of the league (`FR_INJ_SD_MAX`; swept on 64 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\sweep_injuries_sx8k_64x20.txt`: the win spread 2.886 without injuries, 2.847 at 1/4, 2.816 at 1/2, 2.756 at 1, 2.632 unbounded, whose weakest season's mean 2.56 is under the band); they count only the injuries of earlier games; with nobody hurt the sums are the season's exactly. Said plainly: in the computer's games a star's absence is bounded at that value; in the player's own games it counts whole (the review's note 13). (7) NO TEAM STARVED (INJ-6): at most 12 of a team's 34 hurt at once (`FR_INJ_TEAM_MAX`); a further injury is not drawn on it. (8) SPOKEN AND DECIDED (INJ-4): the injury report after the news (NXI-01: a new injury with its name, the others with their status and range, the stand-ins who play, who is back), made in the preparation and told once; the news pool NWJ (another team's player out for weeks) within the week's limit; the to-do kind `injury` (play him, rest him, later; the staff's recommendation first; rest is the do-nothing answer) for the doubtful and questionable players whose rest costs 4 rating points or more, at most 2 a week; F9, franchise, injury report (NXF-44) lists every hurt player and changes whether a doubtful or questionable one plays (with the staff setting on, NXI-55: the staff's rule decides at the kick-off, also over an answer given before the setting was turned on). Nothing is decided for the player with the setting off: a hurt man of his plays only after his own answer. (9) NOT BUILT, by decision: the other to-do items INJ-4 names (a replacement to start: the depth order's rule, the slot model has no bench, research B2; a player to sign: there is no market in the season, the free agents leave at stage 6; injured reserve: no roster limit in this game, research B2 SKIP); the workload rule (SX.8o); injuries in the play-offs' draws. (10) THE FILE: league.sav FORMAT 6 (`injuries`: the season's injuries, the stand-ins, the week's report), checked whole (`injuries.check_injuries`); a format-5 file loads with nobody hurt; the offseason begins with nobody hurt (the step that ends the season); a game saved with Save Game keeps its own injuries in the extras record, used again only when the league would take them (the review's second MAJOR finding: a damaged saved game could have made league.sav unwritable). (11) CUSTOMIZABLE (INJ-5, rule 0.12): injuries.txt, the names of the eleven kinds (IJA to IJM, three lines each) and the announcer's line (IJX), the machinery of news.txt; the kinds' games and shares are data, never the file. 25 rows NXI-, NXF-44, NXH-42, the pools; the IDs NXJ- belonged to free agency (SX.8i): the first rows collided and were skipped silently, so the injuries' texts are NXI- (B-T59). (12) THE CHECKS: IJ1 to IJ7 in the 20-season fast run and IJ8 in the whole-program run (`work\injury_checks_sx8k.py`, with their own record of every injury, each with `selftest` cases); the trade oracle TR7 knows that a hurt player is not traded. (13) MEASURED (500 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8k.txt`): every invariant holds (I1 to I12, CP1 to CP5, RS1 to RS4, FA1 to FA8, TR1 to TR8, IJ1 to IJ7); 34.48 new injuries a team-season (each season's mean 34.44 to 34.54), 3.29 hurt worse; 40.1 player-weeks out and 68.6 not playing a team-season of 544; at most 12 hurt on a team; a stand-in quarterback in 3.3 team-weeks a team-season; the injuries take 2.75 rating points of a team's strength a week; C-F1 2.82 (every season's mean 2.75 to 2.87, every league 2.60 to 2.99, none outside); the persistence of the bottom three 0.373 (SX.8j 0.370), longest streak 10; the spread of the strengths at the seasons' start 0.95 to 1.77, run means 0.97 to 1.34; I8b holds (quarterbacks 0.5 percent of run-seasons outside, one run 6 seasons, within the share). Against the same seeds without injuries (the sweep): the win spread 2.89 to 2.82, the spread of the strengths 1.167 to 1.162, the persistence 0.368 to 0.375. 2,000 leagues: every invariant holds (`_fast_2000x20_sx8k_2000.txt`): IJ1 34.48 (each season's mean 34.45 to 34.51), I11 one run of 2,000 with a streak of 12 in the bottom three (within D-066's share), persistence 0.373; C-F1 pooled 2.81, every season's mean 2.75 to 2.85, and ONE run of 2,000 at 2.58 over its 20 seasons (0.02 under the band): the report says FAIL under SX.8j's strict per-run rule, so the per-run rule is a tail rule again, as B-P89 and D-069 judged such tails, at most 1 run in 1,000 outside and none by more than 0.1 (`WIN_TAIL_SHARE` 0.001, with `selftest` cases at 500 and 2,000 runs); by it the run passes. Said plainly: injuries are noise on the strength the computer's games read and lower the win spread a little; SX.8p must bring the strict band back. The whole program, 5 x 20: every invariant holds (`_full_5x20_sx8k.txt`): IJ1 34.54, C-F1 2.81 (every run 2.76 to 2.85), IJ8 (with the setting off 3,256 questions about a hurt player answered later, none of them played hurt; with it on the staff played 1,306 questionable players; 6,069 players hurt in the player's games by the real engine), FA9, TR9, I10 (1,200 cut-write scenarios), I12; C-F2 -0.62 wins (allowed 0.8; the run before the last fix -0.13): SX.8p should look again. (14) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8k_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 2 MAJOR, 8 MINOR, 5 NOTE, all fixed or decided. A mutation check (`work\mutation_check_sx8k.py`): 72 breaks; the first control run FAILED and exposed a real fault (the week's draws read the injuries of the week's own game), fixed, B-X73; then the control passed, 17 survived, each got a test (one more was equivalent and is left out: in the play-offs every hurt man is out, so nobody is asked anyway) and all 71 are killed (`work\mutation_check_sx8k.txt`, `_only.txt`). THE SUITE: 3,906 passed in 64-bit and 3,906 in 32-bit with numpy blocked, one after the other, the golden Classic transcript among them. Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds the report too long a week (about four lines), the questions too many or too few (the gap and the weekly limit are data), a stand-in quarterback too weak or a star's absence too small in the computer's games (the bound is data). Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).
- **D-072 (2026-10-17, PX SX.8l, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) The staff: every team's head coach with a job rating and the owner's goal, the verdict at stage 1, the stage "the staff" with the firings (never after a winning season), the fired player's offers (only from other teams), the coordinators whose styles are the teams' playbooks and the staff's game plan, league.sav format 7 (NX6 F9, N6.8; design 17 SX.8l; entry 1.12 of the design).** (1) WHERE: `extras\franchise\staff.py` (the rules: no speech, no files; the only draws are the candidates for an open coordinator's place) and `staff_flow.py` (the speech); the stage "the staff" is number 9, spoken as stage 2 of 9 right after stage 1 (so a coach who moves is the new team's in the contracts, the market, the draft and the cut). (2) THE JOB RATING AND THE GOAL (research B9): every head coach has a job rating 0 to 100 and, at every season's start, the owner's goal by his team's rank of the exact strengths (`FR_GOALS`: ranks 1 to 4 win 11, 5 to 8 reach the play-offs, 9 to 12 win 9, 13 to 20 win 8, 21 to 24 win 7, 25 to 28 win 6, 29 to 32 win 5; set from the odds of the fast run so a goal is met 53 to 66 percent of the time at every rank, 0.60 over all). At the season's end the rating moves by the goal (met +3, missed -15), the wins against 8 (2 a win) and the finish (0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6); stage 1 says the goal met or missed and the rating with its change, and warns when it is under 60 after a season without more wins than losses. No weekly movement (research B9 has one; the season's verdict is the cheap tension the research asks for). (3) THE FIRING RULE (research G): a coach is fired when his rating is under FR_FIRE_BELOW (55) after the season, NEVER after a winning season (more wins than losses of 16; 8 and 8 is not one) and never after his first season with the team (FR_FIRE_GRACE 1). The same rule judges every team. CALIBRATED to the NFL's about seven head-coach changes a year (ESPN; Sportico: seven a season 2016 to 2023; SI: 9 in 2023, 7 in 2024; SRC-SECONDARY, FR_FIRE_TARGET 5 to 9): the design's start values fired 2.1 a league-season (the rating drifted up to 73); the sweep is in tuning.py (B-P101). A new league's computer coaches start past their grace with ratings spread 60 to 100 by the team number (no draw, `FR_APPROVAL_LEAGUE_START`); the player is new (60, his grace): with every coach new, season 2 fired 13 of 32 (the review's finding 5, B-P102). (4) THE OFFERS (research B9, G): a fired player is offered FR_OFFERS (3) jobs, NEVER the team that fired him: the open places by rank, then the teams whose coach had no winning season, past their grace first, then the lowest rating (that coach is let go if the player takes the job); there are always three (a fired coach's team did not win more than it lost, so at most 28 of the other 31 can have). He chooses with the staff setting off (the best ranked first, the staff's recommendation), the staff takes the first with it on. (5) THE COORDINATORS (research B8): two a team, each a name, a style of the seven and a rating; every other open head coach's place takes the best rated coordinator of the league, and a new head coach brings his own coordinators (both places open); a computer team replaces a coordinator rated under FR_COORD_KEEP (60); an open place gets three candidates (a style below(7), a rating 50 to 90) and the team takes the best rated. The player, with the staff setting off, is asked every offseason to keep or replace the coordinators still in place (the staff's rule first) and picks each new one among three; nothing is decided for him (his coordinators change otherwise only when another team hires one as head coach, and he is told). A new league's coordinators have the teams' styles of the player's team_styles.txt (a league of format 6 or older the built-in assignment: `from_json` has no game, the review's finding 10) and the rating of their side of the roster. The coordinators' rating has NO effect on play (the ratings' cap of design 5.1 stays the players' alone): it decides who is promoted, who is replaced and whether he reads the opponent. Start values UNVERIFIED (no league source rates a coordinator): FR_COORD_RATING, FR_COORD_KEEP, FR_COORD_ADAPT; they make 16 coordinators hired a league-season of 64. (6) THE GAMES: in a franchise the coordinators' styles are the teams' playbooks (`Game.franchise_styles`, set while the franchise is in use and None in every other game, read by `PlaybookFlow.resolve`; the player's "team" playbook is his coordinators'); the game plan item names the opponent's coordinators' styles and recommends per side FR_GAME_PLAN's answer when the coordinator is rated 75 or more, else his own style. (7) league.sav FORMAT 7 (`staff`), checked whole: every value in range, an open place only at a step of the stage that may have it, every row of the stage's record (the review's finding 3); the stage keeps its step, so a window closed at any question resumes there with the same offers and candidates; a format-6 file gets a new league's staff with no draw (a test). (8) TEXTS: rows NXE-19, NXV-01 to NXV-07, NXV-09 to NXV-18, NXV-20 to NXV-26, NXV-28, NXV-30 to NXV-32, NXV-A01 to NXV-A08, NXV-G01 to NXV-G03, NXF-45 (F9 "your job and staff"), NXH-43; placeholders <goal>, <name>, <style>. No news pool (the stage tells the league's firings and up to three hires). (9) CHECKS (`work\staff_checks_sx8l.py`, their own mirror of every coach, each with self-test cases): JB1 never fired after a winning season; JB2 the offers never from the team that fired him, the rule's; JB3 the ratings, the firings (from their own list of the fired: every fired team has another head coach and no other team does) and the goals worked out again; JB4 the staff whole; JB5 the computer teams' head coaches and coordinators worked out again; JB6 the firings a league-season in 5 to 9, also season by season over 100 runs or more; JB7 exactly three offers; JB8 (whole program) the games' playbooks and the game plans are the coordinators', both kinds of coordinator seen; JB9 (whole program) with the setting off his coordinators change only when one is hired away. (10) MEASURED (500 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8l.txt`; 2,000: `_fast_2000x20_sx8l_2000.txt`; the whole program 5 x 20: `_full_5x20_sx8l.txt`): every invariant holds (I1 to I12, CP1 to CP5, RS1 to RS4, FA1 to FA9, TR1 to TR9, IJ1 to IJ8, JB1 to JB9); 5.71 head coaches fired a league-season (6.27 from season 11 on; each season's mean over 2,000 leagues 3.63 to 6.33), 3.00 offers a fired coach, goals met 0.600, the mean job rating 67.3; the player fired 24 times in the whole program's 100 seasons, each time to another team. The staff never touches the computer's games: its draws only move the random stream. C-F1 2.81 pooled (each season's mean 2.75 to 2.86), the persistence of the bottom three 0.374 (SX.8k 0.373), longest streak 12 (2 of 2,000 runs over 10, within D-066's share), the spread of the strengths at the seasons' start 0.96 to 1.77, I8b as before; C-F2 in the whole program -0.19 wins (SX.8k -0.62). (11) C-F1 PER LEAGUE, RESTATED (a tail rule, B-P89): D-071 allowed 1 league in 1,000 outside the band and none by more than 0.1, which on 500 leagues allowed none; both 500-league runs of this session had exactly one league just under (2.58 and 2.59, different random streams), and 2,000 leagues had 2 (the lowest 2.59): the rate is the one the share allows, and a run of 500 then has such a league 39 percent of the time. The rule is now at least one league whatever the number (`WIN_TAIL_MIN` 1, with self-test cases: one of 500 passes, two fail). SX.8p must still bring the strict band back. (12) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8l_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 1 MAJOR (nothing tested that the coordinators, not team_styles.txt, choose the playbooks: three mutations survived), 11 MINOR and 4 NOTE; all fixed or decided (the finding 10 above). A mutation check (`work\mutation_check_sx8l.py`, `.txt` and `_only.txt`): 80 breaks; the first run left 7 survivors and 2 that no longer applied after the review's fixes; each got a test and all 80 are killed. (13) NOT BUILT, by decision: a weekly movement of the job rating, the coach archetypes and abilities (research B8 SKIP), a coordinator's bonus in play, the computer teams' playbooks following their best players (SX.8o), a news pool of the staff. Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds the firings too frequent or too rare for the player (the line, the goal's weights and the grace are data), the coordinators' question every offseason too much (the staff setting answers it), or the game plan's recommendation of a good coordinator too uniform (FR_GAME_PLAN, SX.8e). Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).
- **D-073 (2026-10-17, PX SX.8m, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) The market's later items: compensatory picks, the non-exclusive franchise tag with its offer sheet, undrafted free agents signed after the draft, a camp focus, renaming a player; league.sav format 8 (NX6, design 17 SX.8m; entry 1.13 of the design).** (1) THE COMPENSATORY PICKS (`extras\franchise\comp.py`; NFL: a club with a net loss of compensatory free agents in free agency gets as many picks, each lost one offset by a gained one of an equal or higher value, at most four a club, at most 32 in a draft, at the end of rounds 3 to 7 by the value of the players lost, in the next year's draft; the formula itself is not published: SRC-OFFICIAL, the league's announcements on operations.nfl.com read through the search's summaries, the pages themselves answered 404 to a fetch). The clone: a compensatory free agent is a free agent who signed with ANOTHER team in the market for `FR_COMP_QUALIFY` (2.0 million) or more, his salary his value; the market records every signing (`market.Round.moves`, kept in `market["moves"]` across rounds), the award is made in the last round's write (`comp.award`, rows [draft season, round, team, value] in `LeagueRecord.comp`), the draft of the next offseason takes them at its creation and puts them at the end of their rounds by value (`draft.sequence`); the class has one more prospect for each (`draft.class_size`: without it 24 picks left 12 undrafted instead of 36, B-X80); a pick is paid by its place among the 224 regular picks, a compensatory pick as the last of its round (`contracts.pick_number`: the overall number broke CP5, B-X81); compensatory picks are not traded (the trades' table holds the regular picks; since 2017 the NFL lets them be traded, UNVERIFIED, not built). CALIBRATED (`work\probe_later_sx8m.py`, 32 leagues x 12 seasons): the NFL's 2026 list has 33 picks to 15 clubs (4 in round 3, one of them a special pick, 8, 9, 3 and 9; nfl.com, SRC-OFFICIAL in the search's summary); the clone's count is bound by the net loss and a team's four (reached in 72 percent of the league-offseasons), not by the threshold (24.1, 23.8, 23.7, 23.0 picks at 0.9, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5 million): about 24 a league-offseason, said plainly (fewer clubs end the clone's market with a net loss than in the NFL); the rounds are the quantiles of the awarded values at the NFL's 2026 shares (`FR_COMP_ROUNDS` 7.7, 5.1, 3.6, 3.2 million). (2) THE NON-EXCLUSIVE TAG (`tags.py`; NFL operations, franchise tags, SRC-OFFICIAL, read 2026-10-17: the non-exclusive tag pays the higher of the position's cap percentage average and 120 percent of the prior salary, the player may negotiate with other clubs, his club may match an offer sheet or take two first-round picks; the exclusive tag pays the higher of the five largest salaries at the position and the non-exclusive amount). The cap is a constant (D-066), so the cap percentage average is the mean of the position's average of its five highest salaries over the last `FR_TAG_HISTORY` (5) seasons, kept at the start of stage 7 (`LeagueRecord.tags["history"]`); the exclusive tag (SX.8h) is now never under the non-exclusive price. The talk of stage 7 offers it as a fourth answer ("use the non-exclusive tag", with its price); only the player's team tags (D-067). At the start of stage 8 the computer team with an empty place at his position, the room, both of its first-round picks (this draft's and the next, `future_picks`) and a value of him at the offer (the trades' value rule) of at least two first-round picks makes an offer sheet at the higher of his market price and his tag (the independent review: at the market price a sheet was always a pay cut), the most valuable to it, equal values by the market's tie rule; the player keeps him at the offer (allowed whenever it does not raise his pay, or the cap allows it) or lets him go for that team's first-round pick of this draft (the trades' table) and of the next (moved into the coming draft's table at stage 6); the staff's recommendation is the first answer (`tags.should_match`: match when he can and the player is worth the two picks), and with the staff setting on the staff answers; nothing is decided for him with it off; no sheet: he stays on the tag. Which years' picks the NFL gives: UNVERIFIED (the page does not say). The sheet's rule is a start value for SX.8p and the owner's playtest; it needs a buyer with that much room, so a sheet is rare. (3) THE UNDRAFTED FREE AGENTS (`undrafted.py`; NFL: an undrafted rookie signs for three years, SRC-OFFICIAL in research F2; Madden 27's draft classes have undrafted free agents, research B12). FIRST BUILT as a market for the places the roster cut leaves empty: 0.3 signings a league-offseason, because the draft fills the roster (2 places a league-offseason empty after the cut, `work\sx8m\probe_udfa.py`; B-P104). AS BUILT: right after the last pick, where the NFL has it: a team offers a place in camp to the prospects it wants by the draft's own need rule (an open place, or the middle of the range over the weakest player who would hold a place), the most valuable first, at most `FR_UDFA_CHASE` (4); the prospects best first sign with the team that values him most (the draft's value), at most `FR_UDFA_MAX` (2) a team, three years at the rookie minimum; they wait for the cut in the draft record (`draft["undrafted"]`) and compete like drafted rookies; releasing one at his first cut costs no dead money (`contracts.release_cost`, used by the cut and its cap prediction). The player is asked after the last pick, before anything changes: the staff's offers (first), his own choice (a paged list whose first item, "done", ends it), or nobody; his own choice is not held to the computer's need rule (the review's MAJOR finding: it was, and an offer that signed nobody was never told; now every such offer is told, NXU-31). Measured: 1.95 signings a league-offseason, half of them kept at the cut (said plainly: the market is small; a 34-place roster leaves little room for a camp body). (4) THE CAMP FOCUS (`camp.py`; research B6's LATER, the research's own idea, UNVERIFIED): at the start of stage 3, before the progression is drawn, every team's focus (offense, defense, young players; the computer teams and the staff by `camp.recommend`, the side furthest under the league's mean of that side, else young players) gives its `FR_CAMP_PLAYERS` (2) first candidates (the extra work's order) one development point, within the season's limit of 3; the player is asked with the recommendation first; `FR_CAMP_PLAYERS` 0 is no camp. (5) RENAMING (`rename.py`): F9, franchise, your roster, Enter on one of the player's own players: the original's typed prompt (the getter's keys as F9 reads keys, B-W11) and its yes or no question; refused: a name another active player or a prospect of a draft in progress has (compared without case and outer spaces), a rookie waiting for the cut; written at once. (6) THE ONE VALUE OF THE CORE THAT MOVED (B-P103): the camp (at 3 players) and the compensatory picks lift the league's mean rating (+0.43 and +0.30, the undrafted +0.04; 70.44 to 71.17 at season 10 against the same leagues without them), toward I8's strict edge of 72; swept: `FR_ROOKIE_RATING` (46, 15) to (45, 15) with the camp at 2 players brings seasons 1 to 12 to at most 0.27 under SX.8l's league (the independent review measured at most 0.19); three weeks of extra work instead of four and the camp at one player were the other options. `FR_RESIGN_MIN_RATING` and `FR_FA_MIN_RATING` (60, "the draft class's mean") are kept at 60 (the class's mean is 59 now). (7) league.sav FORMAT 8: `comp`, `future_picks`, `tags`, the market's `moves`, a draft's `comp` and `undrafted`, each checked whole (`comp.check_comp`, `tags.check_tags`, `tags.check_future`, `undrafted.check_undrafted`, the market's check); a file of format 7 or older loads with none of them (a migration, tests at a market and at a draft in progress, `tests\test_fr_format8.py`). (8) TEXTS: rows NXU-01 to NXU-64 with the answers NXU-A01 to NXU-A10, NXU-C01, NXH-44; the new placeholder <focus>; no customizable pool. (9) CHECKS (`work\later_checks_sx8m.py`, their own record of every signing, award, pick, undrafted rookie and camp point, each with self-test cases): CM1 the awards are the rule worked out again from the signings the checks saw; CM2 the next draft holds exactly those picks at the end of their rounds, the class one more prospect each, every pick made by the sequence's team; CM3 the limits; UD1 the undrafted signings are the rule worked out again; UD2 their invariants; UD3 a free release, every other release its dead money; CF1 every team's camp points (and the player's chosen focus); CF2 the computer teams' focus; I7 of the whole-program run also replays the undrafted market. (10) MEASURED (500 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8m.txt`; 2,000: `_fast_2000x20_sx8m_2000.txt`; the whole program 5 x 20: `_full_5x20_sx8m.txt`): every invariant holds (I1 to I12, CP1 to CP5, RS1 to RS4, FA1 to FA9, TR1 to TR9, IJ1 to IJ8, JB1 to JB9, CM1 to CM3, UD1 to UD3, CF1, CF2); 23.8 compensatory picks a league-offseason to 12.4 teams, 1.95 undrafted signed, 63.8 camp points. SAID PLAINLY, what the items do: the spread of the 32 strengths at the seasons' start, the mean over the runs by season, is 0.99 to 1.77 over 2,000 leagues (SX.8l: 0.96 to 1.77; the design's lower edge 1.0 is met but for 0.01), run means 0.94 to 1.38; the persistence of the bottom three 0.372 (SX.8l 0.374), longest streak 10, 20 percent of the runs over the design's 5; the mean rating 68.17 to 71.13 in every run-season; the quarterbacks 0.3 percent of the run-seasons outside 64 to 76 (none beyond the hard margin); the cap use 0.694 to 0.833 by season, 0.754 to 0.766 from season 11; C-F1 2.82 pooled, each season's mean 2.77 to 2.86, and NO league of 2,000 or of 500 outside 2.6 to 3.4 (2.61 to 3.03): the strict band per league holds again (D-072's tail allowance is not needed in these runs; the rule stays, SX.8p judges it); in the whole program C-F2 -0.47 wins (SX.8l -0.19), I7 0 differences in 100 replays and 60 drafts made again, I10 1,350 of 1,350 cut-write scenarios usable. (11) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8m_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 3 MAJOR (the player's own undrafted offers dropped silently; the three rig tests of the undrafted question could not fail; a rename to a prospect's name), 6 MINOR (the sheet always a pay cut and a match refused that lowered the payroll; the draft check's band; the compensatory picks told twice; five unit-test gaps; a self-test that did not run the real cut; CF not checking the player's focus) and 3 NOTE; all fixed or decided. A mutation check (`work\mutation_check_sx8m.py`, 78 breaks, its control run passed first): 9 survived the first run, each got a test and all 9 are killed (`work\mutation_check_sx8m.txt`, `_only.txt`). (12) NOT BUILT, by decision: computer teams using a tag; trading compensatory picks; special compensatory picks for minority hiring; a news pool for the items; a customizable pool. Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds the camp's question every offseason or the undrafted market's question after the draft too much (the staff setting answers both), the undrafted market too small to matter, the offer sheet never heard, or the compensatory picks too few. Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).
- **D-074 (2026-10-18, PX SX.8n, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) The people's later items: a personality of five words, the morale, the holdouts at camp, the trade demands, the weekly game goal, the player tags; league.sav format 9 (NX6, design 17 SX.8n; entry 1.14 of the design).** (1) WHERE: `extras\franchise\people.py` (the rules: no speech, no files, NO DRAW) and `people_flow.py` (the speech); the stages, the week and the talks call them where they plug in (stage 3, `weekly.prepare`, `before_menu`, `before_game`, `resign_flow._talk`, F9's roster). (2) THE PERSONALITY (research B10's LATER: "4 or 5 personality words set at birth, each with one rule in contract talks and morale"; the words and the rules are this game's own, UNVERIFIED): money, winning, loyalty, playing time, the game; a player's word is a RULE of his ID ((ID x 617) mod 1000 against the shares 20, 25, 20, 20, 15 percent: every 1,000 consecutive IDs have them exactly), fixed when he enters the league, so the items draw nothing and the leagues with and without them are the same leagues draw for draw (B-P103's comparison is exact). Each word's rule in the talks is a percent added to the slack of his mood (FR_PERSON_TALK; playing time by whether he holds the first place of his group, FR_PERSON_STARTER), never under 0, the length of a contract never changed (research G); each word's rule in the morale is how much a win, a loss and a week he does not play move it (FR_PERSON_MORALE), and for money and loyalty when the morale acts: money holds out whenever he is underpaid, loyalty never holds out or asks to be traded (said plainly, the review's finding 12: their weights are the same; the slot model has no bench, so "did not play" is "hurt"). The words are spoken in a talk (NXU-P01, after the room) and in F9's roster. No EA name (research E: not "Persona", not "mindset"). (3) THE MORALE (research B10: "one number per player, up or down by results and playing time, moving the rating by at most 1 or 2 points through H8"): a whole number -6 to 6, moved after each of the weeks 1 to 15 (the week-16 game has no preparation after it, as the injuries) in the week's preparation, BEFORE that week's injuries are drawn: first 1 toward zero, then the result by the word's weights and a week he did not play his own place (the week's lineup of the injuries, the injuries of that week's own game not counted: he played it) by the word's weight; halved toward zero at every new season (stage 6). It moves his rating IN A GAME by step(morale, (3, 2)): -2 to +2. In a played game it goes through the H8 door the ratings already use (the ratings of the game's rosters, `injury_flow.lineup_roster`): THE RATINGS' CAP OF 1 YARD AND 3 POINTS STAYS THE RATINGS' ALONE, the morale moves the rating the door reads, never its bounds. In the computer's games it goes through the week's sums (`people.week_sums`: the injuries' sums plus the morale of every player who plays his own place), inside the bound of the week's changes FR_INJ_SD_MAX (injuries and morale together; no new bound); a place a substitute or a stand-in plays keeps its replacement level. Morale moves no rating outside a game (the mean rating is unchanged). CALIBRATED (work\sweep_people_sx8n.py, 16 and 32 leagues against the same leagues without the items): the start values (no pull toward zero, a met goal only adding, a loyal player never losing) walked to the edges: a team's mean effect 1.08 rating points; with the goal symmetric and a pull of 1, 0.71 (the win spread +0.30); a step of (4, 2) or (4, 1) 0.31 but held the bottom three longer; a pull of 2: 0.28 (B-P105). THEN THE GOAL WAS TAKEN OUT OF THE MORALE (see (4)) and the pull swept again (`work\sweep_people_sx8n_c.txt`): a pull of 2 left a team's effect 0.08 (nobody ever at 3), a pull of 1 with the designed weights 0.25 (the win spread 2.86 against 2.77 without the items, the persistence of the bottom three 0.384 against 0.375), every weight doubled with a pull of 2, 0.35: a PULL OF 1. The mean morale leans negative (a week not played only takes away). (4) THE WEEKLY GAME GOAL, A SPOKEN TARGET WITH ITS VERDICT (research B9's LATER: "one weekly game goal spoken before kick-off"; this game's own, UNVERIFIED): every team's goal of a week follows the two teams' strengths: stronger by 2 rating points or more, win by 14 or more; weaker by as much, lose by no more than 14 or win; else win. At 7 points the stronger team met its goal 69 percent of the time and the weaker 34 (the score table's margins are wide); at 14, 0.51 and 0.52, the even games 0.50 (B-P106), so the goal favors no side. A GAME THE ENGINE PLAYS (the player's) has its own points, (10, 10) (FR_GAME_GOAL_PLAYED): the first whole-program run found the player's team 1.00 win a season under its peers (C-F2, allowed 0.8) because his goals, judged by the score table's 14 points in the engine's games, were met 0.26 as the stronger team and 0.72 as the weaker (with the goal's morale off: -0.70); the engine's median margin is 10 at every gap (`work\measure_goal_margins_sx8n.py`, 500 played games a gap): at 10 points 0.52 on both sides (B-P107). With them the run gave -0.84 (the player's goals met 0.36 and 0.63: the stand-in's sides are not symmetric, and in a person's game his own play sets the margins, which no number of the game can calibrate), so THE GOAL MOVES NO MORALE (FR_GOAL_MORALE 0): it is the spoken target and verdict research B9 asks for, the morale moves by the results and the playing time (research B10's words), and no part of a team's strength depends on how a person plays to a margin. The player hears his goal before the kick-off (H15 `before_game`, once, not for a loaded game), its verdict before the next week's menu (after the news and the injury report, once); goals are set only in the weeks whose result is judged (1 to 15); his team is judged by the goal he heard, even when a trade of the week changed the strengths since (the review's finding 4). (5) THE HOLDOUTS (research B10: "one spoken event at the start of camp: Ruiz holds out. 1 pay him, 2 wait, 3 trade him"; NFL 2025: about five to eight veterans held out or held in at camp, CBS Sports' camp holdout tracker, SRC-SECONDARY; scaled to 34 of 53 places about 3 to 5): at the start of stage 3, before the camp's focus, a player holds out when he is under contract for the coming season, has played 3 seasons or more, is rated 75 or more, is paid under 75 percent of his market price, is not loyal, and is a money player or his morale is -2 or lower; at most one a team, the most valuable. The stage begins with ONE write: every team's holdouts settled by the staff's rule (pay him when he is 25 or younger, the raise fits the room of stage 7's rule and the payroll the staff's target; else he waits and his morale falls to -6), the player's own left for his answer (unless the staff handles his team), then the stage speaks. His answers: pay him (the market contract of his rating for the length of his age, offered only when it fits), let him wait, trade him (he asks to be traded at once); the staff's recommendation first; each answer written before it is told; the staff setting read at every step; nothing decided for him with it off. Paying a rookie in his fourth season replaces his rookie scale (CP5 knows the exception from the people's checks' own record, B-X86). CALIBRATED: a pay age of 29 paid 95 percent of them and nobody ever asked to be traded; 25 with underpaid at 75 percent: with the final morale 4.4 holdouts a league-offseason (4.7 from the second: a new league's contracts are all at the market price), 3.0 paid, 1.4 waiting. (6) THE TRADE DEMANDS (research B10: "the step after a holdout; it becomes news, other teams make offers"; NFL: about four to six public trade requests a year, PFF and CBS Sports, SRC-SECONDARY): a player put on the block at camp asks from week 1, one who waited from week 1 (FR_DEMAND_WEEK) while his morale is -3 or lower, at most once a season, while still with the team he held out for; a demand is news (the new pool NWH of news.txt, rule 0.12; week 1's demands are week 1's news, the review's finding 2); every week of the trade window the other teams make their offers by the trades' own rule (`trades.block_offers`, each gaining the margin of the position): a computer team takes the best when it gives up at most 8 rating points of the position, never an offer of the player's team (the review's MAJOR finding 1: that traded his player without a question); the player hears the best three in his to-do list (the kind `trade_demand`: the offers and "keep him", the staff's recommendation first; "later" and the kick-off trade nothing; a chosen offer that can no longer be made is told so, NXU-D13). A demand's trade is kind 3 (`trades.KIND_DEMAND`): the cap, a hurt player, the window and "traded once a season" hold (`trades.problems`); the computer teams' search limits (two a season, one a week) are the search's, not a request's (DECIDED, the review's finding 5). A demand ends with a trade (his morale back to 0) or at the deadline. Measured: 1.3 demands a league-season, 1.2 of them traded (fewer than the NFL's scaled 3 to 4, said plainly: only a holdout who waited asks). (7) THE PLAYER TAGS (research B2's LATER: "three at most as spoken words derived from age, rating, potential"): a top player (rated 85 or more), else a rising player (24 or younger with high potential), else a veteran (31 or older); spoken in F9's roster with his morale (high, good, low) and his word (NXU-T05, NXU-T06). "a star" was changed to "a top player" ("Star" is an EA development trait's name, the review). (8) league.sav FORMAT 9: `people` (the morale of the players, not 0; the camp's holdouts and the season they were settled; the player's own still to ask, only at stage 3; the season's demands; his goal and its verdict), checked whole (`people.check_people`, LeagueError only); a file of format 8 or older loads with nobody's morale and nobody holding out (a migration with no draw; tests at a week, at stage 3, in a draft, `tests\test_fr_format9.py`). (9) TEXTS: rows NXU-P01, NXU-P10 to NXU-P14, NXU-M01 to NXU-M03, NXU-T01 to NXU-T03, NXU-T05, NXU-T06, NXU-H01, NXU-H02, NXU-H06, NXU-H10 to NXU-H12, NXU-H20 to NXU-H24, NXU-D01, NXU-D10 to NXU-D13, NXU-G01 to NXU-G05, NXH-45, NWH01 to NWH04; placeholders <trait>, <tag>, <morale>, <ask>. (10) CHECKS (`work\people_checks_sx8n.py`, their own mirror of every morale and of the camp, each with self-test cases): MO1 the week's morale; MO2 the new season; MO3 the computer's games' sums (and "MO3 program": the whole program's calls counted, more than 0); GG1 the goals, the player's from the goal he heard; HO1 the holdouts; HO2 the staff's decisions and the contracts; HO3 the holdouts a league-offseason in the band FR_HOLDOUT_TARGET (2 to 6); TD1 the demands filed; TD2 a computer demand's trade (its cost, never with the player's team, kind 3, morale 0, none open after the deadline); TR3 judges a kind-3 trade's buyer by the margin and its seller by FR_DEMAND_GIVE with the trade checks' own oracle; HO9 and TD9 in the whole-program run (with the setting off his holdouts are asked and his demands, answered later, trade nothing; with it on the staff decides).
