(11) MEASURED (500 leagues x 20 seasons, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8n.txt`; 2,000: `_fast_2000x20_sx8n_2000.txt`; the whole program 5 x 20: `_full_5x20_sx8n.txt`): every invariant holds; SAID PLAINLY what the items do: C-F1 2.88 pooled (SX.8m 2.82), each season's mean 2.84 to 2.92, every league's mean 2.73 to 3.07 (the morale lifts a team on a winning run and the week not played falls on the hurt: a little more spread); the persistence of the bottom three 0.372 (SX.8m 0.372), longest streak 11; the spread of the strengths at the seasons' start 0.99 to 1.77 (unchanged); the mean rating 68.28 to 71.00 in every run-season; the quarterbacks 0.6 percent of the run-seasons outside 64 to 76 (two runs with a stretch of 6 or 7 seasons, within the share); the cap use 0.695 to 0.834 by season; the firings 5.81 a league-season; the injuries 34.49 a team-season; the goals met 0.51 to 0.52 in the computer's games; the whole program: every invariant (HO9: 12 holdouts asked with the setting off, 6 decided by the staff with it on; TD9: 58 demand questions answered later, none traded; "MO3 program" 74,637 checked weeks), C-F2 -0.35 wins (SX.8m -0.47), the player's goals met 0.34, 0.46 and 0.63 (the stand-in's sides; the goal moves no morale). (12) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8n_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 3 MAJOR (a computer demand trading the player's player; no demand ever news; MO3 unable to fail for the program's path), 9 MINOR and 2 NOTE; all fixed or decided. A mutation check (`work\mutation_check_sx8n.py`, 88 breaks, its control run passed first): 7 survived the first run, each got a test, all 88 are killed (`work\mutation_check_sx8n.txt`, `_only.txt`). (13) NOT BUILT, by decision: a weekly movement of the job rating by the game goal (the firings are calibrated, D-072), a morale from per-player performance (the computer's games have no player statistics, research C), a depth chart for playing time (research B2 SKIP), trade requests without a holdout, offseason trades for a player put on the block (he asks from week 1), a customizable pool for the stage's sentences (they are fixed sentences of the table, as SX.8h to SX.8m's). Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds the holdout question too frequent (5 a league-offseason, about one in six of his offseasons), the demands too rare to notice, the goal or its verdict tiresome every week, or the morale's effect too strong or too weak for a team on a streak (the pull and the step are data). Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).
- **D-075 (2026-10-18, owner rules 0.15 and 0.16, for nfl4 as for every game; text only, no code yet) A detailed debug log and the spoken welcome.** (1) THE OWNER'S WORDS: every game, "including the nfl game currently being worked on", must have a detailed debug log "so that Claude can easily debug games that I make, especially since everyone's computer setup is different from mine"; and every game names itself, for example "welcome to Josh Kennedy's nfl football, loosely inspired by Jim Kitchen's original games." (2) WHERE THE RULES ARE: CLAUDE.md 0.15 and 0.16, playbook-2 7.11 and 7.12, fixesforgames A67, B-S26, B-R8 (all copies synced). Both are clone-level pieces (`data\logsetup.py`, `app.py`, `game\texts.py`, `game\program.py`, tools, tests): nothing in the classic engine or in the extras changes, and nothing is added to `spec\NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES.md` (rule 0.13: they are not nfl4-only). (3) WHAT NFL4 HAS TODAY: `data\logsetup.py` (one rotating file `nfl4.log`, 512 KB with 2 backups, INFO), `install_exception_logging`, and log calls in `app.py`, `lifecycle`, `healing` and the stores. NOT YET: the startup report, one file per launch with the caps, DEBUG events (keys, menu steps, speech with its backend result, sounds with the file chosen, file reads and writes, settings, slow steps, heartbeat, the seed of every game, season and league), the crash log with the hang watchdog, the one `redact()`, the queue writer, the level setting, the menu item "Save a problem report" and `--problem-report`, `clone\tools\read_debug_log.py`, the README section "If something goes wrong", the issue template, and the tests of 7.11. (4) THE WELCOME: `texts.TITLE_SPOKEN` (used at `game\program.py` lines 116 and 125) becomes "Welcome to Josh Kennedy's N. F. L. football, loosely inspired by Jim Kitchen's original games." (default: the owner's example, the letters of NFL spaced for the voices, no version number; wrong if the owner wants "version 4" spoken or the plain "NFL"). `texts.CAPTION` (the window title and the first line of footballlog.txt) stays "NFL Football 4 by Josh Kennedy, inspired by Jim Kitchen" (0.5.1). The original speaks P.WELCOME_TO ("Welcome to", 0x487c20, program.py line 112) before the title on the first start, so the clone must not say welcome twice: the session decides between dropping that line on the first start (the sentence spoken whole) and keeping it with the rest of the sentence after it, records the choice in STRINGS.md and GAP_REGISTER G-F1, and checks the order in the rig. Tests to change: `test_window.py` (the caption stays), the conformance tests that pin `TITLE_SPOKEN`, `test_program_start.py`, `test_app_startup.py`, `tools\exe_smoke.py` (it looks for "by Josh Kennedy, inspired by Jim Kitchen" in the speech), and the golden Classic transcript (one commit, only the first speech event changes). (5) WHEN: a session of its own, SX.L "debug log and welcome", RECOMMENDED BEFORE SX.8o and in any case before the owner's first playtest on other PCs (P8 S8.2) and before P9; Sonnet 5.5 at high effort (S7.x class), with an independent review of the log's privacy and size limits. (6) THE FRANCHISE AND THE LOG: league.sav, the saved game and the player's own text files are never copied into a log or a report; the Franchise's stages, drafts, trades, injuries and holdouts log one DEBUG line each in the 7.11 format, with the seed of each league and season at its start. Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8); the one open point is the wording in (4).
- **D-076 (2026-10-18, PX SX.8o, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) The game's later items: the awards of a season and a hall of fame (by one rule over what every team has, no invented statistics), the weighted draw of the ball carrier (H17), the returners' rating in the kick-off return (H8), the injury workload rule (INJ-3), the playbooks that follow every team's best players; league.sav format 10 (NX6, design 17 SX.8o; entry 1.15 of the design).** (1) WHERE: `extras\franchise\workload.py` (the carry weights and their draw, a game's expected carries and the game part's places, the season's games and carries, the workload rule; no speech, no files; its draws are the carrier's `below(total)` and the workload's `below(10000)`), `honors.py` (the awards and the hall: rules, no speech, no files, NO DRAW), `honors_flow.py` (their words), `staff.lean`, `fit`, `fitted_styles`; the engine's seams H17 `Rules.draw_carrier(state, rnd)` in `snap_draws` (Classic `rnd(6)`) and H8 `Modifiers.return_shortest(side, returner)` at the kick-off return (Classic RETURN_SHORTEST, 12). Nothing of them is reached outside a franchise: a game without ratings draws `rnd(6)` before anything of the feature is asked, and a quick game and a classic season (both Rules values) draw and say what they did with every new rule made to raise (`tests\test_fr_fit.py`); the golden Classic transcript is unchanged. (2) THE WEIGHTED BALL CARRIER (research B2 and C: "ratings can weight the draw through H8"; this game's weights, UNVERIFIED): a run's six backs and a pass's six receivers weigh 4 + step(rating - 70, 4, 1), 3 to 5 (FR_CARRY_WEIGHT), of the ratings the game's roster gives them (the lineup with the morale); equal weights are the original's `rnd(6)` itself (a branch, not a remap: N2, B-P108); a kick and a quarterback's run keep `rnd(6)`. The weights change who the ratings' Mod reads, never its bounds (the ratings' cap of 1 yard and 3 points stays the ratings' alone). CALIBRATED against C-F3 on the same played games without the items (`work\sx8o\played_sx8o_centering_*.txt`): the start value (4, 4, 2) lifted points 2.48 percent with the Classic playbooks (band 2); (4, 4, 1) on the very 10,000 games of SX.8b's centering run: points +1.50 and +1.44 (SX.8b +0.87 and +0.94), yards a play +0.98 and +0.93 (-0.01, +0.19): about 0.6 percent of points and 1 percent of yards a play, inside; C-F2's stronger team's share unchanged within the error (0.628 at a gap of 1, 0.673 at 1.5). A team's top back carries 22.7 percent of its runs. (3) THE RETURNERS' RATING (design 5.1's open item): the shortest kick-off return is 12 + step(returner's rating - 70, 4, 3), -3 to +3 yards (FR_RETURN_STEP, UNVERIFIED), as the punter's rating moves the shortest punt; the draws are the original's. (4) THE WORKLOAD RULE (INJ-3, research B7's LATER: "a running back who carries a lot gets hurt more"; per-hit wear stays SKIP; UNVERIFIED as a rule): a back's carries of the season are counted in tenths, every rush of a game the engine plays (the game's record, kept by Save Game and checked on load) and his share by the carry weights of the team's 23.4 running plays (FR_WORKLOAD_RUNS, measured in SX.8k) in a game it does not play; past an even share (FR_WORKLOAD_FREE 60 carries) his chance as the ball carrier rises 20 percent every 5 carries, at most double: one more `below(10000)` a carry in a played game after a play that hurt nobody, one more a game in the week's draws (the chance is his expected carries times one carry's: the same expected injuries, B-P107). The week's game part weighs the backs' and the receivers' places by their carry shares (equal weights: the table's own numbers). CALIBRATED (B-P110): the start values hurt 1.1 backs a league-season; a free count of 40 reached 77 percent of the backs; the final 3.2 a league-season (the fast run), a third of the backs past the free count by the season's end, the running backs' new injuries 260.7 against 260 without it, the league's 34.6 a team-season (band 31.3 to 38.0). (5) THE AWARDS (research B11's LATER; the NFL's AP awards at NFL Honors, SRC-OFFICIAL: nfl.com's lists): NO STATISTICS MODEL: 15 of a week's 16 games are the score table with no plays (research C); an invented box score would be fiction and the player's own game would have real numbers where the others have none (B-P109). One rule over what every team has: a player's value is WEIGHT x (rating - 60) x games / 16 + 2 a win of his team (FR_AWARD_BASE, FR_AWARD_WIN), at least 10 games (FR_AWARD_MIN_GAMES), the games he played his own place counted from the lineups every week for every team (`workload.count_games`: weeks 1 to 15 in `weekly.prepare`, week 16 when the season ends in `offseason.run`); the most valuable player by the position's weight among the play-off teams; the offensive (backs and receivers) and defensive player of the year and the rookie of the year (first season, no kicker or punter) every position alike (with the weight a rookie quarterback won every rookie award); the coach of the year the most wins over his rank's expectation (FR_COACH_EXPECT, measured: 11.2 to 5.1 wins by bands of four ranks; the rank of the league's sums, which a trade brings up to date: said as it is, the review's finding 2); ties by the rating and the player ID. A season counted from a later week (a league migrated from format 9 in the middle of a season) scales the 16 and the 10 to the weeks counted (the review's finding 6). Said at stage 1 before its wait (NXG-B01 to NXG-B05; the short offseason: the most valuable player and the hall's count, NXG-B08 and NXG-B09) and kept after it, once. Measured: the most valuable player a quarterback in all but a few in ten thousand league-seasons (said plainly: the position's weight 12 of 78; a kicker in a few), the offensive award a receiver about four in five, the defensive a lineman or linebacker about seven in ten. (6) THE HALL OF FAME (research B11's LATER; eligible after five full seasons retired, a class of at most five modern-era players: nfl.com's coverage of the classes of 2024 to 2026, SRC-SECONDARY): at every stage 1 a kept former player (a star of a peak of 85 or more, or an award winner) who left five seasons before or more is a candidate; his hall value is his peak + his seasons + his award points (FR_HOF_AWARD_POINTS); those at FR_HOF_BAR 105 or more enter, the highest first, at most FR_HOF_CLASS 3 a season (five scaled by 34 of 53). SWEPT (64 leagues x 30 seasons): the bar 100 filled every class from season 19 (the size alone decided), 110 took 1.4 a season at most, 105 2.1 from season 11 and 2.8 to 3.0 from season 22. Award winners and members are kept for good (`aging.drop_the_unkept`). History: F9's league history has "awards by season" and "hall of fame" (NXG-06, NXG-07, lines NXG-20 to NXG-22), history.txt the parts Awards and Hall of fame. (7) THE PLAYBOOKS THAT FOLLOW THE BEST PLAYERS (research B8's LATER): in a franchise game a team's coordinators play their own styles unless the week's lineup (the ratings the game reads) leans by FR_FIT_GAP 5 rating points: the passing rating ((2 x the quarterback + the receivers' carry-weighted mean) / 3) over the backs' carry-weighted mean plays Air Raid, under it Power Run; the line and linebackers' mean over the cornerbacks and safeties' Pressure, under it West Coast (FR_FIT_STYLES; whole numbers). EVERY TEAM, THE PLAYER'S "TEAM" PLAYBOOK TOO (MEASURED, B-P111): with the computer teams alone fitted the whole-program run's C-F2 was -1.07 wins (allowed 0.8); the same seeds without the fit -0.21, without the fit and the other played-game items -0.53: the fit helped only the player's opponents, in his games, and no game of the score table has playbooks. So his coordinators follow his best players as every team's do; the staff's game plan of a coordinator who keeps his own style recommends the style his lineup fits; a playbook he names in F9 is his and never changes (nothing is decided for him he did not leave to his staff). The run fit works through Power Run's tilt (a yard on its inside runs), not its mix (every style passes more than it runs: said plainly, the review's finding 3). JB8 is restated with its own oracle of the fit. (8) league.sav FORMAT 10: `honors` (the awards rows [season, award, player id or 0, name, position or "", team] and the hall [player id, season]) and `usage` (the season's games and carries), checked whole (`honors.check_honors`, `workload.check_usage`); a format-9 file loads with no award, nobody in the hall and nothing counted (a migration with no draw; tests at a week, at stage 1, in a draft); a saved game's injuries record may hold its carries (`state.check_game_injuries`). (9) TEXTS: rows NXG-06, NXG-07, NXG-A01 to NXG-A05, NXG-B01 to NXG-B09, NXG-20 to NXG-22, NXH-46; the new placeholder <award>; no customizable pool (the stage's sentences are fixed, as SX.8h to SX.8n's). (10) CHECKS (`work\game_checks_sx8o.py`, their own record of games, carries, awards and the hall, each with self-test cases that must fail): AW1 the awards worked again from the checks' own games; AW2 the games after every count; AW3 the checked calls more than 0 (B-X91); HF1 the hall's class worked again and its members kept; WL1 the carries after the week's draws and the workload's hits only on backs past their free count; WL2 (the whole program) a played game's carries equal its backs' rushes, with the program's weighted draws and moved returns counted. (11) MEASURED: 500 leagues x 20 seasons (`work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20__sx8o.txt`): every invariant holds; C-F1 2.89 pooled (SX.8n 2.88), every season's mean 2.83 to 2.93, every league 2.70 to 3.11; the persistence of the bottom three 0.375 (0.372), longest streak 9; the spread of the strengths 0.99 to 1.77 by season (unchanged); the mean rating 68.19 to 71.11 in every run-season; the quarterbacks 0.4 percent of the run-seasons outside 64 to 76; the cap use 0.696 to 0.834; the firings 5.80; the injuries 34.64 a team-season; the holdouts 4.71; the workload's hits 3.20 a league-season. 2,000 leagues x 20 seasons on the final code (`work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_2000x20_sx8o_2000_final.txt`, 7,606 seconds): all 76 verdicts pass; C-F1 2.88 pooled, every season 2.82 to 2.91, the runs 2.65 to 3.11; the persistence of the bottom three 0.374, 3 of 2,000 runs with a streak over 10 (longest 13); the spread of the strengths 0.98 to 1.77 by season; the quarterbacks 0.4 percent of the run-seasons outside 64 to 76; the injuries 34.64 a team-season; the firings 5.80; the holdouts 4.71; the workload's hits 3.20 a league-season; I12 8 of 8 runs repeated draw for draw. I8 RATING TAKES THE TAIL RULE OF THE OTHER BANDS (B-P112): the first 2,000-league run on this code had ONE run-season of 42,000 at 67.87 (band 68 to 72; I8 rating was the last band judged with no run-season outside) while the mean of every season was SX.8n's to 0.01 on the same leagues and the earlier 2,000-league lows were 68.07 to 68.35: a value moved by a changed random stream, not a drift. Now at most HARD_TAIL_SHARE (1 in 10,000) of the run-seasons outside, none by more than RATING_FAR 0.5, the mean of every season (68.99 to 70.41) and of every run (69.37 to 70.33) inside (`rating_band` in `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f.py`, with the self-test "I8 rating tail": one in 10,000 passes; two, one far, one in 100, or a run's mean under the band fail; the self-test 234 of 234). The suites: 64-bit 4,291 passed (the first run had one failure, the startup test reading an empty launch log under the load of the long run: its flush waited 2 seconds against the log's hold of 8 for the startup report; fixed in the test, B-X97), then 32-bit with numpy blocked 4,291 passed, one after the other. The whole program, 5 x 20 (`work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_full_5x20__sx8o.txt`): every invariant holds; C-F2 -0.60 wins (SX.8n -0.35; the same seeds without the played-game items -0.53), C-F1 2.89, the persistence 0.354, WL2 1,680 played games with 195,080 weighted draws and 5,558 moved returns, JB8 with the fitted styles, I12. Said plainly: the items leave the league's health as SX.8n left it; SX.8p must still bring the design's bands back. (12) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8o_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 1 MAJOR (the in-game refresh of the rosters dropped the morale: SX.8n's code, so the morale had never reached a played game after the kick-off; fixed with a test), 8 MINOR and 5 NOTE; all fixed or decided. A mutation check (`work\mutation_check_sx8o.py`, its control run passed first): 72 breaks, 62 killed on the first run, 3 equivalent to the real code and left out with a note (the step's integer division, a quarterback's run never at a back's place, the offensive award's equal weights), 7 survived (the kick-off return's H8 call and its side, the game part's carry-weighted places, the rookie in his second season, the player's own name as coach, the hall's class size, the player's plan with his lineup's fit): each got a test and all are killed (`work\mutation_check_sx8o.txt`, `_only.txt`). (13) NOT BUILT, by decision: a statistics model, comeback player and assistant coach awards, separate offensive and defensive rookie awards, an all-pro team, a seniors' route to the hall (a 34-place league keeps only stars and award winners), per-hit wear (research SKIP), a fit for the player's named playbooks. Wrong if: the owner's playtest finds the best back named too often or too rarely (the weights are data), the awards' stage too long, a quarterback as the most valuable player every season tiresome (the weights are design 4.4's), the hall too full or too empty. Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8).


## D-068 (2026-10-16): numbered sound pools the player can extend and optional commercials (owner rule 0.14; the clone, nothing in the original changes)

Owner request in chat: "Jim's games have sound files that are numbered, such as foot0, foot1, foot2. I want the user of all my games to be able to add more sounds if they wish, especially for comments in various sports games, and for optional commercials." Written as CLAUDE.md rule 0.14 and playbook 7.7.1 first, then applied to nfl4 in the same day. Decisions made automatically (rule 0.8):
1. Which names are pools: decided from the code, not the names (WAV_EVENT_MAP 8). Two pools: `ambient` (foot8 to foot10, PlayRan's `Int(Rnd * 3)`) and `celebration` (foot15 to foot24, `Int(Rnd * 10)`). Every other foot<N> is a different sound for a different event. The game's commentary is text (banter), so "comments" are covered by banter.txt with `[sound file]` (L12), and no numbered commentary wav exists to extend.
2. Names of added files: the pool's plain-word name plus a number (`celebration3.wav`), because all 26 originals share the base `foot` and a number alone says nothing about the event. Scanned from the folder listing (never probed), case-insensitive, wav, ogg, flac or mp3, number order, gaps allowed, no cap; two files with one number: the first by name is used, the other is logged.
3. The draw: `Int(Rnd * member count)` in the same place as the original's draw, so with nothing added every draw, the golden transcript and the conformance tests are unchanged (`test_pag_golden.py` passes unchanged; `tests\test_sound_pools.py` proves the draw sizes). A rule 0.14 change of behavior, recorded: an original member switched off in sounds.txt now LEAVES the draw (before, it stayed and played silence, G-A3); the one test that said so (`test_a_waited_sound_switched_off_takes_no_time...`) keeps G-A3 for the waited sounds and the pool case moved to the new test file. An original member with no usable file at all stays in (a broken install must not move the draws). An added file that cannot be read is left out and logged; one spoken notice per start (G-A6).
4. Levels of an added member (the original has no rule for a new file): celebration -300 + wavol with the original's pitch draw around the file's own rate; ambient -1200 + wavol (foot9's), centered. Wrong if the owner finds added ambient sounds too loud or too quiet at the playtest (P8 S8.2): then change one constant (`ADDED_AMBIENT_LEVEL`).
5. Commercials: four breaks (quarter, two-minute-warning, halftime, final), where each sits is in CUSTOMIZABLE_CONTENT 15. Nothing ships; setting off by default (also with files); every, second, third; spoken cue "Commercial. Any key skips it."; the key that skips is swallowed; own random generator (a shuffle bag), nothing in saves, log or scores; the sound layer's volume wavol applies. Break order and "final" after the player's key: after the final score the original waits for a key, so the commercial follows it. Wrong if the owner finds the final break awkward (then move it after the statistics: one line in `pag_breaks.game_end`).
6. NOT built (recorded, with what closes them): (a) the typed-path command "Add a sound to a pool" (the original's typed prompts are name prompts, 30 characters; a general text prompt does not exist yet; the folder and the file name do the same job), (b) text commercials (`commercials.txt` with the banter markup), (c) a break for the time outs of the N. F. L. rules (the extras are a separate piece of work: add `timeout` to `commercials.BREAKS` and one call), (d) per-file trim for added pool members. None changes the classic game.
7. Files: new `clone\src\nfl4\audio\pools.py`, `clone\src\nfl4\game\commercials.py`, tests `test_sound_pools.py` (18) and `test_commercials.py` (17); changed `audio\library.py` (pool_members, pool_summary, pick_for, the scan at refresh), `audio\engine.py` (play_member, member_rate), `game\sounds.py` (celebration_sound, play_ran, commercial), `game\pag_breaks.py` (four calls), `game\flow.py` (the cue is spoken by the Game, not logged), `game\functions.py` and `game\extra_texts.py` (Sounds menu: pools, Commercials, the commercials folder), `data\settings.py`, `data\settingsfile.py` and `data\paths.py` (the `commercials` setting and folder), `app.py` (the folder object). Texts in `extra_texts.py` are written there, not in `strings_nx.tsv` (they are not N. F. L. rules texts; the generated NX files were not touched, a separate session has uncommitted edits there).
- **D-077 (2026-10-18, SX.L, owner rules 0.15 and 0.16; D-075 BUILT) The detailed debug log, the problem report and the spoken welcome in nfl4.** Nothing needs the owner. Clone-level pieces only: nothing of the classic engine or of the extras (rule 0.13) changed except log calls; the golden Classic transcript is unchanged (it records one game, not the start-up). (1) THE LOG (`data\debuglog.py`, `data\logsetup.py`, `data\sysreport.py`, `data\problem_report.py`): one file per launch `nfl4-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.log` in the logs folder, newest 10 launches kept, a ring of two halves of 5 MB (the report at the top of each), `nfl4-crash.log` (1 MB, faulthandler plus a 10 s hang watchdog, one dump per hang; a wait for speech may take 90 s), a state file for `log_errors_last_launch`, a queue and a writer thread (14 to 16 microseconds a call on the game thread; redact() 13 microseconds a line), levels detailed (default), trace, normal, off, from `--debug-log=` or `NFL4_DEBUG_LOG` or settings.txt `debug_log` (read before the settings load) or F9, Speech settings, Debug log; format `HH:MM:SS.mmm +elapsed LEVEL thread component run event key=value`. The startup report (about 95 lines, not 60: the contract's list is longer than its estimate) is made on a thread; the writer holds the first lines up to 8 s for it, then writes the header with a note and the late items as `-- late startup report items`; a program closed at once waits 1.5 s for it and marks what is missing `n/a (...)`. (2) WHAT IS LOGGED: keys at the window (name, modifiers, repeat, the screen), at the pump and at read_key; menus (open, enter, close) and hot keys; speech requests (role, backend, accepted ms, the queue length, the text), the end of every waited speech (ms), stops; sounds (slot, ID, file, chain link, how it was reached, volume, pan, frequency, ms; volume changes at trace); file reads, writes, copies and damage in the healing helpers and the Franchise's files; settings changes (old and new, from the rendered text); the seed of the launch generator and its draw count at every season, game and league start (`GameRandom.mark`; at trace every draw with its number); phases and SLOW steps; a 30 s heartbeat; `app welcome_spoken`; the exit reason and the last line `closed`; every caught exception with its traceback (about 40 swallowed exceptions were found and now log at debug or warning; the ones left in `debuglog.py` itself cannot log and are counted). Franchise: one line for every league happening through `news.render` (a trade, an injury, a holdout, a pick, a retirement), one for every league.sav write, the seed mark at a new league (the stage code is another session's open files; a per-stage line can follow in a later session). (3) PRIVACY, one rule in the formatter: the account name after a path separator (also the short 8.3 profile name, accents in either form), the computer name, e-mail addresses, values after password, token, secret, api key, authorization become <user>, <computer>, <email>, <hidden>; text the player typed OR loaded from his files (the coach, the quarterbacks, a renamed player), raw and cleaned, is <typed> as a whole word in any case, and while he types no key and no echoed letter is written (the context "typing: ..." is read by the window, the pump, read_key and the speech log). Decided: the SPOKEN sentences are logged (they are the game's output, and the banter and team names are the player's own files in them), but no file of his is ever copied; the crash log on disk is cleaned when the log closes and at the next start (a native crash cannot be cleaned while it happens) and in every zip. (4) THE REPORT: F9, Speech settings, "Save a problem report" (two choices, with or without saved games, then open the folder), `--problem-report [--with-saves] [--folder=...] [--quiet]` (no window, a message box), zip members `README.txt`, `system.txt`, `logs/` (newest 3 launches, both folders), `crash-log.txt`, `settings.txt`, `customization.txt`, `saved/` only with the second choice; every text member goes through redact(). `clone\tools\read_debug_log.py` (setup in ten lines, problems and SLOW with counts per component and backend, 40 lines before the first error and before the end, health, seeds; `--replay` plays the first game again on the pag rig from the recorded keys and draws; exact at trace, at detailed it holds as long as the timing-dependent ambient draws come out the same). `clone\README_if_something_goes_wrong.md` and `clone\github\ISSUE_TEMPLATE\bug_report.md` are drafts for P11 (the template goes to `.github\ISSUE_TEMPLATE\`; the README section into the README). (5) THE WELCOME: `texts.TITLE_SPOKEN` = "Welcome to Josh Kennedy's N. F. L. football, loosely inspired by Jim Kitchen's original games." built from `GAME_SPOKEN` and `INSPIRATION`; CAPTION unchanged; on the first start the original's " Welcome to " is NOT said (the sentence replaces it and the title; the rest of the first start keeps its place); the SAPI chunker no longer cuts "N. F. L." after an initial (B-S27); STRINGS.md and GAP_REGISTER G-F1 say so; wrong if the owner wants the welcome to start over the intro jingle. (6) NOT DONE, on purpose: sound underruns (pygame's mixer gives no callback, so there is nothing to log), an interrupt flag on speech (the stop line before the request shows it), a list of every one of the 1,811 SAPI voices in the report (engines with counts, and the five chosen voices in full; the zip's system.txt is the same), `debuglog.slow()` at more places than the phases and the file writes. The tools `key_latency_check.py`, `audible_latency_check.py`, `playback_start_latency.py`, `prompt_gap_check.py`, `startup_time_check.py` and `exe_smoke.py` are still Brain's in their menus and strings (they were stale before this session): only their log reading was moved to `tools\logfiles.py` where it was small; P9 rewrites them. The cost was measured on the pag rig (a whole game, 9,500 lines at detailed, 14,500 at trace) and in `test_the_key_to_sound_path_is_not_slowed_with_trace_on` (median under 1 ms with trace on). (7) THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW (a fresh Opus 5.5 subagent) found 3 blockers/majors in privacy that the first tests missed (every typed letter as a key code, the Franchise rename prompt, names loaded from files), the short profile name, the crash log, an unbounded line, a recovery that hid the log from the zip and an unguarded `event()`; all fixed with a test each (fixesforgames B-R9). (8) KIT: the files named in fixesforgames A67 item 6 go into the starter kit at its next refresh; Brain gets them at its next release. Wrong if: the owner's playtest on another PC shows a log line that is missing for a bug, or a file in the zip that holds more than it should.
- **D-078 (2026-10-19, PX SX.8p, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) The whole Franchise's 20-season test with every block on: every invariant judged against the DESIGN's own bands beside the restated ones; no value moved; four design bands restated with the measurement that shows why; the spoken time budget measured (design 16 and 17 SX.8p, 19; entry 1.16 of the design).** Nothing needs the owner (rule 0.8). (0) THE METHOD: a second judge, `work\sx8p\design_bands_sx8p.py`, reads design 16 SX.8f and 5.4 literally on the same runs (I8 the mean rating 68 to 72, the mean age 25 to 28 and the spread of the 32 strengths 1.0 to 6.0 in every run-season; I8b every position group 64 to 76 in every run-season; I11 every strength 60 to 80 and no team among the three weakest of the exact strengths more than 5 seasons in a row in any run; C-F1 2.6 to 3.4 in every season's mean over the runs and in every league's mean, read as D-057 reads 5.4: a single league-season of 16-game records is a sample, a league of a true spread of about 3.0 has about 27 percent of its seasons outside the band by luck, `work\sx8p\cf1_luck_sx8p.py`), names the decision that restates each band it finds missed, and the verdict "design bands" FAILS a run that misses a band no decision restates (`simulate_franchise_sx8f.design_part`, called by the fast and the whole-program runs); a self-test of 28 cases (`design_bands_selftest_sx8p.py`), the numbers of every run-season dumped (`SX8P_DUMP`), `rejudge_sx8p.py` judges a dump again with the judge of today. The research G checks (the cap CP1 to CP5, contract length, the best offer FA2, the firing JB1 to JB7, the cap drift CP4, the injuries IJ1 to IJ8) are judged as they were: they are the research's bands. (1) THE SPREAD OF THE STRENGTHS (I8, design 1.0 to 6.0): MISSED and RESTATED. 25.4 percent of the 42,000 run-seasons of 2,000 leagues are under 1.0 (the lowest 0.54), every season's mean over the runs 0.98 to 1.77: the rosters even out (design 5.2) and the spread sits at its edge in seasons 4 to 10 (1.01 to 1.06, half the leagues under 1.0 in seasons 4 to 8) with a wobble of 0.15 a season (`work\sx8p\analyze_bands_sx8p.txt`). THE SWEEP on the same 300 leagues (`work\sx8p\sweep_sx8p.py`, proven the fast run's leagues row for row by `same_leagues_sx8p.py`; `work\sx8p\sweep_sx8p.txt`): a wider rookie rating (41, 19) 19.0 percent under 1.0 with the quarterbacks outside 64 to 76 in 8.3 percent; the draft's need bonuses 12, 20, 6 25.1; the team level -3 to +3 22.0 (its first seasons only); a noisier progression (a design rule) 13.0; FR_RESIGN_TARGET (the staff's payroll target when it signs players again) 0.55, 0.50, 0.45: 13.8, 7.6, 3.2 percent (every season's mean 1.03, 1.10, 1.18 at least), and the checked runs of 100 leagues at 0.50 and 0.45 show its price: the staff signs again 16.7 and 8.8 percent of the contracts that run out (48 percent at 0.62), the market holds 211 and 232 free agents a league-offseason (131), the league's mean cap use falls to 0.658 and 0.640 by season (CP4's research band starts at 0.65: 0.45 FAILS it), and it still misses the band. No knob reaches the band without breaking another, so FR_RESIGN_TARGET stays 0.62 (SX.8i's market, Q-031) and the band is judged as D-061 restated it (0.8 for the single run-seasons, every season's and every run's mean inside, stretches and a tail). The owner's playtest should listen for teams that sound too close together in the middle seasons (team ratings within about two points, upsets that are not upsets); FR_RESIGN_TARGET 0.55 is the step to try then (13.8 percent, CP4 to be checked). (2) EVERY POSITION GROUP 64 TO 76 (I8b): MISSED and RESTATED. In 2,000 leagues the quarterbacks are outside in 0.37 percent of the run-seasons (75 runs, the longest stretch 7, the highest 78.69), the kickers 0.26, the punters 0.27, the returners 0.04; every season's mean over the runs is inside (QB 70.04 to 72.55). A single-place group's league mean is a cohort of 32 players that keeps a season-to-season correlation of 0.80 to 0.89, so its tails are 1.5 to 2.6 times a plain wobble (0.16, 0.15, 0.17 percent): a few leagues sit near an edge for some seasons, the league does not drift. Judged as D-061 and D-067 restated it (stretches of at most 5 seasons in at most 1 percent of the runs, a hard margin of 3 with its tail). The owner's playtest should listen for a league whose quarterbacks or kickers all sound alike strong or weak for several seasons. (3) NO TEAM AMONG THE THREE WEAKEST MORE THAN 5 SEASONS IN A ROW (I11): MISSED and RESTATED. 367 of 2,000 leagues (18.4 percent) have such a team (the longest 13); one given team, the player's say, has a streak over 5 in 0.61 percent of its 20-season leagues. A team's exact sum keeps a lag-1 correlation of 0.655 from one season to the next (a roster changes by retirement, the draft, the market and trades; the quarterback is half of the variance). The sweep: no recorded knob brings the share under 13 percent of the leagues (the rookie spread 16.7, the draft's need 18.7, the team level 21.3, the payroll target 0.55 to 0.45 14.7, 13.3, 14.7). A plain model of 32 autoregressive teams at that correlation (no draft order) is a reference only: it has 39 percent (the review's finding 5). Judged by the persistence of the bottom three, TIGHTENED from D-061's 0.55 to 0.45 (the whole Franchise has 0.375 in 500 leagues, 0.374 in 2,000, 0.354 in the whole program: a return to the core's 0.48 now fails; `PERSISTENCE_MAX` in `simulate_franchise_sx8f.py`), and a streak of at most 10 in at most 0.5 percent of the runs (3 of 2,000). The owner's playtest should listen for a franchise whose own team stays near the bottom for many seasons although the player trades and signs. (4) THE LEAGUE'S MEAN RATING (I8, design 68 to 72 in every run-season): MISSED in one run-season of 42,000 (67.87; the same leagues as SX.8o's final 2,000, draw for draw), as D-076 found and restated (at most 1 in 10,000 outside, none by more than 0.5, every season's mean 68.99 to 70.41 and every run's inside); the first 2,000-league report failed "design bands" on it because the judge's table did not name D-076 yet: the table names it and the dump was judged again (`work\sx8p\rejudge_2000.txt`: every verdict passes). HELD against the design's own bands with no restatement: the mean age (25.52 to 26.82), every strength 60 to 80 (0 of 1,344,000 team-seasons outside), C-F1 (every season's mean 2.82 to 2.91, every league's 2.65 to 3.11 of 2,000; the single run-seasons 1.62 to 4.05, reported). (5) THE SPOKEN TIME BUDGET (design 19, `work\sx8p\time_budget_sx8p.py`, `work\sx8p\time_budget_sx8p.txt`): the whole program's own speech on the rig, every text the franchise says inside a stage, before a week's menu and before its kick-off, rendered by the computer's first SAPI 5 voice (FlexVoice2 Bill, about 15 characters a second at its own rate 0) as the game renders it, the trailing silence trimmed, 9 offseasons and about 150 weeks a setting, the first answer at every question, in free agency an offer a round (the first measurement ended every round without one: the review's MAJOR finding 1). At rate 0 and rate 3: the default offseason (every stage told, the staff setting off) 14.1 and 10.2 minutes (the longest 15.3; the draft 3.6, free agency 3.6, the expiring contracts 3.4): the top of research H's 10 to 14 and over it in the longest; short mode with the staff off 10.8 and 7.8 (the decisions are still asked); short mode with the staff on 5.1 and 3.7; with item 8 ("the staff makes your other picks") at the first pick 3.7 and 2.7; with the picks told "only yours" as well 3.1 and 2.3; full mode with the staff on 8.5 and 6.1; a week with the default playbooks 35 and 25 seconds (with the "team" playbooks, the game plan item, 50 and 36; with the staff on 23 and 17). By design 19's rule short mode and the staff's choices bring the offseason down, and they do, with nothing cut and no code changed; design 19's core figures (short about 3, the staff's picks and cut under 2 minutes) are restated for nine stages as about 3 at rate 0 and about 2 at rate 3 with the settings above. The defaults (full mode, the staff setting off, design 18) are data the owner may change: SX.9's playtest should judge the default offseason's 14 minutes, whether a new franchise should start in short mode, whether the staff setting should also make the draft's picks, and whether short mode should tell fewer of the other teams' picks. (6) THE RUNS (every report in `work\`): 500 leagues x 20 seasons (`simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx8p_base.txt`, 1,608 seconds) and 2,000 x 20 (`..._fast_2000x20_sx8p_2000.txt`, 5,829 seconds, judged again in `work\sx8p\rejudge_2000.txt`): every restated verdict and every research G check passes (2,000: C-F1 2.88 pooled, CP4 0.695 to 0.833, IJ1 34.64 new injuries a team-season, JB6 5.80 coaches fired a league-season, HO3 4.71, I12 8 of 8 runs repeated draw for draw; 76 verdicts); the whole program, 5 seeds x 20 seasons with the real engine playing the player's games and the staff deciding at every question (`..._full_5x20_sx8p.txt`, 3,088 seconds, judged again in `work\sx8p\rejudge_full5.txt`): every invariant holds, C-F2 -0.60 wins (allowed 0.8), C-F1 2.89, the persistence 0.354, the design's bands missed are the three of (1) to (3). The whole-program run was started before the verdict "design bands" was added (B-T68): its report has the judge's notes, the verdict is in the re-judged file. (7) SAID PLAINLY: the full run takes a season's numbers at stage 1, after its trade window, the fast run at the season's start, so the full run's spread reads a little high and its season 1 is the league after the window (older than this session, the review's finding 7; the fast runs are the design-band evidence). (8) THE CLONE: no value of `extras\tuning.py` moved (the SX.8p block records the sweeps); `data\debuglog.py` names the close's wait for the startup report `REPORT_CLOSE_SECONDS` (1.5 seconds, unchanged) and the start-up tests' close bounds count it: the 64-bit suite failed once with a close of 2.6 seconds against S7.1's 2.5 while the startup report's speech section took 4.4 seconds; the test passed alone 3 of 3 (B-X98); two new tests prove the wait. The golden Classic transcript is unchanged; nothing of the Franchise is reached with Rules = Classic. (9) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx8p_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 2 MAJOR (the budget without offers in free agency; the spread's reason and a knob half tried), 7 MINOR and 3 NOTE; all fixed or decided there. The mutation check (`work\mutation_check_sx8p.py`, after the review's fixes, its two control runs passed first): 19 breaks of the judge (16, against its self-test) and of the close's wait (3, against `tests\test_debuglog.py` and `test_app_startup.py`), all killed (`work\mutation_check_sx8p.txt`; the first run left one break not applied because the table had a new first entry: its text was corrected and the check run again). The suites, after every change, one after the other: 64-bit 4,293 passed (`work\suite_sx8p_64_final.txt`), 32-bit with numpy absent 4,293 passed (`work\suite_sx8p_32_final.txt`), the golden Classic transcript among them. Wrong if: the owner's playtest hears a league of look-alike teams in the middle seasons, a team of his own stuck at the bottom for years, or an offseason too long to sit through at the defaults.
- **D-079 (2026-10-19, PX SX.9, nfl4 only, rule 0.13) The integration of the seven extra features with the Franchise: every switch, every band with everything on, the stress tests, Save Game at the accept-or-decline prompt, the playtest list, About and README, NQ2.** (1) THE SWITCHES (`clone\tests\test_sx9_switches.py`): all 64 combinations of time outs, penalties, onside kicks (the question "always" in half), quarterback runs, challenges and the player's playbooks in seeded whole quick games; eleven of them with the player answering his own prompts, questions and menus; six combinations through the whole program with the real engine in a classic season with the N. F. L. rules and in a franchise; in each, a switch that is off leaves no event, statistic line, word or menu item, and one that is on is heard; injuries only in the franchise; with Rules = Classic no text of the extras or the Franchise is said, no franchise file made (the golden Classic transcript passes unchanged). (2) NQ2 FLIPPED (design 12.1): a new player's settings say the N. F. L. rules (no settings file, a file without the line, a bad value, the Reset item); a file that says classic keeps the original; the test rigs' settings follow their Game, which plays Classic (B-T69); 17 tests that read the old default now name their rule. (3) C10, SAVE GAME WITH A DECISION PENDING: built for the accept-or-decline prompt (S; `extras\penalty_flow.py` `can_save`, `suspend`, `resume`; the hook `resume_choice` and the continuation SAVE_PENDING, Classic no-ops): the file holds the declined board and the choice; the loaded game asks it first; offered when both answers are a new down for the team with the ball before the last second of a quarter or a half (kick-offs included: the review's finding 1), else NXP-22. Not built, by decision: the challenge question (asked from a play menu whose Save Game item is there before and after it: no decision is lost) and the onside question (at a kick-off, where the original has no menu and no Save Game, B-P116). (4) THE BANDS WITH EVERY SWITCH ON (10,000 games each, `work\sx9\`): both sides on the original's plays: SX.6's numbers exactly (12.73 fouls a game, 26.71 seconds a play, 1.349 designed quarterback runs a team-game, 0.270 challenges a team-game with 51.0 percent changed, 0.682 booth reviews with 74.5 percent): every band; a new player's game (his books Classic, the computer's team its own style): 13.34 fouls, 26.31 seconds, 1.509 runs, challenges 0.285 and 51.2 percent, booth 0.701 and 74.4, onside 0.173 a game with 8.3 percent kept, fouls before the snap and scrambles and short yardage in their bands (`allon_*_default_10000.txt`): every band (one property of SX.5's script, "a slide left 5 yards" in seed 4619, is a replay review that moved the spot of a slid scramble: the check predates reviews, not a defect); both teams on their team style (the player chose "team"): 13.86 fouls (band to 13.6), 25.90 seconds a play (band from 26; 34.75 plays a quarter, inside C2's 29 to 36), 1.641 runs (band to 1.6); every pairing in turn: 13.69, 25.99 and 1.592. DECIDED: no value moved. A style calls more passes and quarterback runs by design (D-054's balance: every style 49.9 to 52.4 percent against Classic over 10,000 games, `calibrate_playbooks_sx9_10000.txt`, SX.7's numbers exactly); centring a global rate on the styled game would move the original's plays away from the NFL's targets; the bands are judged on the original's plays and on a new player's game, the styled game is recorded here and named in the playtest list (B-X100). (5) THE FRANCHISE WITH EVERY EXTRA ON: 500 leagues x 20 seasons (`work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_fast_500x20_sx9.txt`) and 5 x 20 through the whole program (`_full_5x20_sx9.txt`): every check passes, the same verdicts as SX.8p (C-F1 2.89, C-F2 -0.60 wins); the design's bands missed are the three D-078 restated. (6) THE STRESS TESTS: `test_sx9_saved_states.py` (a record with every field away from its start through the file and into a game, every field of a wrong kind refused, whole games with every switch on saved at every wait and loaded games played to their end), `test_sx9_league_damage.py` (the damage matrix of 7.10 on league.sav at every stage of a franchise year, both files damaged, the first file with no copy, a restart from a damaged file at every offseason stage), `test_sx9_speech_stress.py` (NVDA closing in the middle of the roster, the SAPI voice failing for a whole franchise year, NVDA closing at the draft with slow speech), `test_nx_pending_save.py`. (7) TEXTS: About has two new pages, the added rules and the NX-R6 notice (Madden and Electronic Arts named only there, `test_sx9_about.py`); F9's help counts the Franchise item; NXP-22, NXP-23 and NXH-47 added; the README section `clone\README_nfl_rules_and_franchise.md` (for P11); the owner's playtest list `PLAYTEST_LIST.md` in five sittings. (8) THE REVIEW (`work\review_sx9_findings.md`): a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer found 2 MAJOR (the kick-off's kept choice refused on load: fixed; the styled bands: decided in (4)), 10 MINOR and 4 NOTE, all fixed or decided there. The mutation check (`work\mutation_check_sx9.py`, after the review's fixes, its control run passed first): 25 of 25 killed (`work\mutation_check_sx9.txt`). The suites, after every change, one after the other with nothing else running: 64-bit 4,518 passed and 6 skipped (work\sx9\suite_sx9_64_final.txt), 32-bit with numpy absent 4,518 passed and 6 skipped (work\sx9\suite_sx9_32_final.txt), the golden Classic transcript among them (the 6 skipped: whole games of the save at the prompt in which no prompt from the chosen one on could be kept). Wrong if: the owner's playtest hears too many flags or quarterback runs with the team playbooks, finds S at the prompt confusing, or wants Classic as a new player's default.
- **D-081 (2026-10-19, P8 S8.3) The stress tests: four test files, six defects found and fixed, the silent-text check of every kind of sentence by one voice of each of the 27 installed engines in both bitnesses, and two emulated voices that no longer die.** Nothing needs the owner. (1) THE TESTS (`clone\tests\test_stress_s83_speech.py` 29 tests, `_audio.py` 30, `_files.py` 295, `_log.py` 10): (a) SPEECH: hostile engine fakes (SAPI, NVDA and JAWS that come and go, refuse texts and raise from every call but `speak`) under 6,000 random manager calls in each of the four modes with an exact count (no text twice), under six whole Classic games, with no engine at all, and with NVDA and then JAWS closed in the middle of a game; the real NVDA backend (a fake controller that comes and goes) and the real JAWS backend (a COM object that raises) under three talking threads; a voice menu of 5,000 voices of 60 engines (an empty name, a blank one, one of 4,000 characters, accents) with 600 random moving keys; (b) AUDIO: a mixer that dies in the middle of a game, the device missing at the start, 20 kinds of damaged wav for every one of the 27 sound IDs, the sounds folder replaced by a file, a locked and a read-only sound, the folder deleted in play, 3,000 added files; (c) FILES: playbook 7.10 through the whole program, 20 files of a data folder (settings, sounds.txt, the intro, three season files, the week counter, the log, a saved game, the commercials note, eight customization files, three playbook files) by 14 damages (deleted, folder gone, emptied, cut in half, random bytes, another encoding, huge, zeros, a bad line, no header, another list's header, read-only, held open, a folder in its place), each started with `app.main()`, the real window and a fake voice: it speaks the welcome, closes with 0 and ends its log with `closed`; (d) LOG AND REPORT: a report from three launches at their 10 MB cap with the account name in every line, a full destination, a destination that is a file, logs that are folders or junk, a report made while the program writes, the program started with a file where its logs folder belongs, with a full disk for the log and with a locked old log, 60,000 events with a small cap. (2) DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED (fixesforgames B-P118, B-A21, B-R11, B-S28, B-X103, B-T74): the speech manager said a text twice when an engine's `queue_length` raised after it took the text (`speech/manager.py`, `_log_request`); the silent output of a run with no sound device kept every sound it was given in memory (`audio/output.py`, `keep=False`), and a device lost in the middle of a run made every sound last 0 seconds and wrote a traceback each time (`audio/engine.py`: after 3 refusals in a row the silent output takes over and keeps the waits, one new notice " The sound system stopped working. The game goes on without sounds. " is queued, CLONE text, GAP_REGISTER section 8); a problem report that was cut short by a full disk stayed on the Desktop as a half zip (`data/problem_report.py`: written as `.part`, renamed when whole); two emulated voices killed the process on pieces of the game's own sentences (`speech/sapi5.py`, `split_chunks`: a token of only stops is removed, and a word over 100 letters and a piece over 300 are cut); and a whole-program test that closed its window after a fixed time failed on a busy PC because the 3.4 s intro plays in real time (the test now closes the window after the first word). (3) G-S9, THE SILENT-TEXT CHECK: a pytest plugin (`clone\tools\speech_corpus_plugin.py`) recorded every text any test made the program say, 139,248 different texts from the whole suite; they were reduced by signature (punctuation, digit shapes, non-ASCII letters, length, spacing) to 439; `clone\tools\sapi_render_check.py` made each into memory (nothing played) with one voice of each of the 27 engines of the owner's PC (1,812 voices), in 64-bit and in 32-bit, with the program's own pieces (`split_chunks`) and repair (`speech_variants`). Result: no sentence of the game is silent on any engine. Two engines needed the repair: Bestspeech Fred (4 texts: two numbers with only a space between them, an accented name) and SmoothTalker (47 texts: the accented surnames Martínez and Márquez, written plain by the ASCII fold). 22 engines rendered in 64-bit, 17 in 32-bit (the others are not built for that bitness or render in real time and were stopped at 90 seconds: Orpheus, Outspoken, Pocket TTS, Prose 2000, the SSI-263 emulation; the program speaks those directly, which this check cannot hear, and S8.2's listening can). SAM and Votrax killed the check's own process in both bitnesses (0xC0000374 heap corruption, 0xC0000409 stack overrun) on a lone full stop and on a word of 4,000 letters; after the fix to `split_chunks` both voices render all 439 texts in both bitnesses. NVDA's and JAWS's readings of the same texts are for the owner's ears at S8.2 (G-S9 stays open for that). (4) THE SUITE: 64-bit 4,936 passed and 13 skipped (`work\s83\suite_s83_64.txt`, 16 minutes), 32-bit with numpy absent 4,936 passed and 13 skipped (21 minutes), 362 tests more than S8.1's 4,581 and 6 (`work\s83\suite_s83_32.txt`); the golden Classic transcript and the conformance map are among them. The 7.10 matrix takes about 6 minutes of each run. (5) LEFT, by decision: the built exe in both bitnesses with the damaged files, a voice that kills the process and a locked data folder (S9.2: `exe_smoke.py`, `who_holds_folder.py`, `clone\tools\voice_crash_check.py` made for this game because `exe_voice_check.py` is Brain's: it writes Brain's settings key); real NVDA and real JAWS closing in the middle of a game and the listening to the voices that are spoken directly (S8.2, the owner); a Franchise league file damaged in the middle of a year is `test_sx9_league_damage.py` (SX.9) and was not repeated.
- **D-080 (2026-10-19, P8 S8.1) The conformance tests: a machine-checked map of every requirement to its tests, two places where the clone did not follow its own decisions fixed, the 10,000 games and the 10,000 seasons, the mutation check.** Nothing needs the owner. (1) THE MAP: `clone\tests\data\conformance_map.tsv` has a row for each of 210 requirements: the leaf sections of GAME_SPEC 1 to 14 (43), the quirks Q1 to Q62, the seven bullets of 17.2 (X1 to X7), the 15 gap entries that close in S8.1, the 26 sounds the owner confirmed by ear (OBS-foot0 to OBS-foot25) and, because this is nfl4 (rule 0.13), C1 to C10 and N1.1 to N7.7 (47) of `spec\NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES.md`; an entry is `<test file>::<regex over its test function names>`, or `NA: <reason>` (6 rows: S1, S2, S14, Q16, Q30, Q40). `clone\tests\test_conformance.py` parses the spec and fails on a requirement without a row, a row for something the spec lacks, an entry that matches no test, or an NA without a reason; `spec\CONFORMANCE_MATRIX.md` is the readable form (`work\s81\matrix_s81.py`, generated, never edited by hand). GAME_SPEC stays 1.3 (one pointer line after 17.2). (2) THERE ARE NO TRANSCRIPTS OF THE ORIGINAL (D-005: the original is not run; Q-005); the observations folder holds the owner's three listening rounds (26 sounds, every one "yes, the name is right"), which are now tests of the names and of the roles (`test_conformance_gaps.py`). The golden Classic transcript (`test_pag_golden.py`) is recorded from the clone at S7.3b and pins regressions only; what makes Classic exact is the lifted code, the P3 checks of every Rnd draw, constant and sentence against the exe, and the tests written from them. (3) THE MAP FOUND FIVE GAPS, all filled in `test_conformance_gaps.py`: Q29 (the interception chance grows with every pass), Q35 (the fake sentences are never spoken), Q58 and G-S5 (the key echo), G-A2 (the safety net of the play noise had no test that it acts), the sound roles. (4) AN INDEPENDENT REVIEW (an Opus 5.5 subagent that had not seen the work; read-only; it read the cited test bodies of the 59 quirk rows and the main S rows) judged 74 of 106 rows sound and listed the rest. TWO were places where the clone did not follow the spec: Q5 and Q6, marked "not reproduced" by D-025 (a) and (b) but built as the original does, with tests that asserted the original. FIXED: F1 in the main menu (and the F1 that a hot-key or F9 menu hands back) says the whole announcement, the week line first (`game\program.py`, `week_intro`; the play-off week says "This is the" first); the start of an overtime period at counter 0 with F4 not pressed since counter 0 speaks 15 minutes and draws nothing (`game\gameclock.py`); `test_program_menu.py`, `test_gameclock.py`, `test_functions.py` and `test_fr_resume.py` follow. The rest became tests in `test_conformance_review.py` (44, several parametrized): Q25 (the overtime kick-off sets the half whatever it was), Q15 (every draw 0 to 9 in quarters 2 and 4), Q18 (F2, F1, Esc and a letter), Q22 (F1 in the try), Q9, Q28 with Q37 and Q39 (a loaded game), Q38, Q60 (a fumbled pass keeps its attempt and its completion or sack), Q61 (the kick-off's noise loops under the run-out question), the lengths of the five phrase histories, GetSexOf, the opponent's snap count; more tests were added to the rows of Q1, Q3, Q45, Q46, Q51, S5.7, S5.11 and S7. LEFT, by decision, because the golden transcript and the S3 and S7 draw checks pin them and each would need a long hand-scripted draw list: the memory of the previous play cleared when the player defends, the quarter-4 and overtime branches of the fourth-down rule beyond its 13 cases, the snap-phrase and replay-list details of 5.8 and 5.11, rady ignoring keys, inrep stopping at the select line, Output or Append of the log, Q2's order, Q14's overtime length, Q27's statistics order, Q54 at the level of a whole play (it is tested in the key layer where the quirk lives), and, in 17.2, that every sentence goes through the right speaker with the right wait, replay and log (each speaker function is tested; the whole order is the golden pin). They are the first candidates if the owner's playtest hears a divergence. NOT A DIVERGENCE: the file layer keeps up to 100 characters of the coach's name while the prompt stores 30 (D-034). (5) G-C1: 10,000 whole Classic games and 10,000 whole seasons (`work\s81\property_games_s81.py` and `property_seasons_s81.py`, the suite's own helpers one game at a time, a stack dump if one game takes 60 seconds): 0 failures, 51 of 51 pause sites reached, 135 games in overtime, no redraw fallback, no safety net. The first run found 2 failures that were not game faults but a too narrow invariant in the suite (B-T71); a second copy of the run beside the first stalled both (B-X101). (6) THE MUTATION CHECK (`work\mutation_check_s81.py`, a copy of the clone, the spec copied beside it): 24 of 24 breaks killed (`work\mutation_check_s81.txt`); the first pass left 2 alive, a test that read the broken constant to compute its own expectation (Q29's floor) and a copy without the spec folder (the league), both fixed. (7) THE SUITES, one after the other with nothing else running: 64-bit 4581 passed and 6 skipped (work\s81\suite_s81_64.txt), 32-bit with numpy absent 4581 passed and 6 skipped (work\s81\suite_s81_32.txt), the golden Classic transcript among them. A first 64-bit run failed 2 tests that did not know Q5 (`work\s81\suite_s81_64_first.txt`), updated. (8) A slip of the author's, said plainly: the session started edit scripts through shell here-documents and `cd` against the prompt, and a Python edit script turned three files to CRLF (B-T73, seen at once in `git diff --stat`, converted back). Wrong if: the owner's playtest hears the F1 repeat of the week line as noise (then set `week_intro` empty and give the tests back the original, D-025 (a) would change), or finds the overtime time at counter 0 odd.
- **D-082 (2026-10-20, P8 S8.2, the owner's playtest) Five owner reports and the rules they made.** (1) F5's key list now names F9 in every mode and the first start's separate F9 sentence is gone; F1 repeats the prompt as the original and is followed by one help-hint sentence (the owner chose "repeat, then name help keys"); CLAUDE.md 0.17, playbook 7.13, B-W13 and B-W14. (2) The exit says "Thank you for playing N. F. L. football by Josh Kennedy, inspired by Jim Kitchen." (the owner's words), the week line says "Josh Kennedy's N. F. L. football", and the football words' "In Jim's NFL football game." says "In Josh Kennedy's N. F. L. football game, inspired by Jim Kitchen."; the welcome sentence is unchanged (it was spoken right in the log); CLAUDE.md 0.18, B-S29. (3) The start-up intro loop ends when the welcome ends in every start-up path and before the first prompt that waits; CLAUDE.md 0.21, B-A22; this changes where the original's loop stopped (the week line) in a Classic later start and is accepted: the owner asked. (4) NEW REQUIREMENT NX-P: all the real NFL plays with the N. F. L. rules, Classic unchanged (CLAUDE.md 0.20, `spec\NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES.md` 18, session SX.10, B-P119). (5) NEW REQUIREMENT NX-N: the real coaches and players are researched and built in, the player types no name (CLAUDE.md 0.19, section 18, session SX.11, B-P120); the accepted defaults: the season of the rosters is the one in use on the day SX.11 runs, recorded with its sources; later draft classes are generated names; the original's own names stay the Classic fallback. P9 waits for SX.10 and SX.11 (NX-R3). Nothing here needs the owner now; the owner listens to the result at the next playtest. Wrong if: the owner wants the intro to play longer, or real names to be optional in the first release.
- **D-083 (2026-10-20, P8 S8.2, the owner's playtest, second statement) nfl4 is loosely inspired by Jim Kitchen's game, no longer a clone; all real and Madden NFL plays; the original's play style for the user stays.** The owner, after hearing the fixed build: "I don't see all of the plays. This game is no longer a clone of Jim Kitchen's games. It is now loosely inspired by Jim Kitchen. I need all real and Madden NFL plays in my version of the game. I just want it to have a play style that is similar to how Jim Kitchen's game behave for the user." Also: "sounds .wav files are fine" (the sound files are confirmed by ear; G-A7 stays as decided). Decisions: (1) the DEFAULT game is the N. F. L. rules mode (already NQ2) and it carries every real NFL play and every play of Madden NFL's playbooks, built in SX.10a to SX.10e (the plan in `spec\NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES.md` section 14); (2) Classic stays as a switch and its golden transcript stays as the guard, it is no longer the game's identity; (3) the original's way of playing for the user is the style to keep (NX-P7); (4) Madden is used as a source of football concepts only: no EA text, art, ratings or exact playbook layouts, the NX-R6 trademark notice stays, Claude is not a lawyer and the owner may ask one; (5) the wording "loosely inspired by" is already the spoken welcome (rule 0.16); the window title, About and README keep the longer 0.5 text, and a later wording change to "loosely inspired by" in About and the README is a text task for SX.10e or S11.1 (recorded, not done). Nothing needs the owner now. Wrong if: the owner finds the menus slower to use than the original's; SX.10b's design listening round answers that.

This session wrote rules and requirements only, no code. Nothing needs the owner now; the owner hears it at the next playtest. L. rules were on, onside kicks on, the onside question setting `sensible`; the question was never asked because the player's team never kicked off while trailing in the second half or leading by 1 to 8 in the last five minutes (design 8.2, D-041), and the computer kicks onside only when trailing late in the fourth quarter (0.17 a game, the NFL's own rate); the onside lines in the log are F5 and F6 help; the file nfl4-crash.log holds only launch markers (no crash). Decisions: (1) the 2026 rule (6-1-6, SRC-OFFICIAL) allows a declared onside kick at every free kick, so the player's team is offered it at EVERY kick-off and safety kick in the N. F. L. rules mode (the setting's default becomes `always`, a player's stored `sensible` that was never chosen is moved to `always` once; built in SX.2b with the T key; in SX.10d the question grows into the 2026 kick-off menu: deep kick first, then short kick into the landing zone, squib, onside), the computer's rule table stays the NFL's practice; (2) NEW REQUIREMENT NX-R7 in `spec\NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES.md` section 18: every rule the N. F. F. L. rules mode, Classic keeps the original's kick-off; (3) OWNER ANSWER (asked once, recommended answer taken): overtime in the N. F. L. rules mode follows the 2026 rulebook (Rule 16: both teams get a possession unless the first drive ends in a safety, one 10-minute period in the regular season that can end in a tie, 15-minute periods in the play-offs, two timeouts in the regular season and three per pair of periods in the play-offs, no coach challenges); D-041's sudden death (NQ4) is SUPERSEDED for the N. - **D-087 (2026-10-20, PX SX.2b, built on Sonnet 5.5) The T key and the onside offer are built.** What was decided while building, on top of D-084 and D-085: (1) the key is taken in the key pump by its CODE (84) and the window thread cuts the speech at once, so the engine's blocking texts do not delay it; (2) a timeout before the snap or the kick is a `PlayWithdrawn` (BaseException) caught only in the run loop, with the clock's `capture` and `restore`, so the play is not run, no draw is made for it and the runoff the snap took is given back; (3) a refusal in the snap count reads the dead-ball period from before the snap; (4) a live-play request is kept in the rules object and granted at H10 or at the next menu, kick-off or choice, and is said only where a question waits (NXT-15, the run-out question, because the kick is live then); (5) a T between the end of a play and H10 is granted at once and stops the whole runoff; (6) at a 'press any key' screen T is that key and its request waits; (7) the Time Out item loses its letter, so no item has T; the number and Enter still call it; (8) at a kick-off the timeout is called where the game is and the kick-off goes on (nothing was called, no freezing of the computer's kicker); (9) the hot-key menus and F9 close on T during a game; (10) the onside question's default is 'always' with a 'chosen' mark, so an old default moves once and a choice of the player's stays; (11) one new text row NXT-15, NXT-16 and the changed help NXH-01 and NXH-05. - **D-089 (2026-10-20, PX SX.14, built on Sonnet 5.5) The start-up choice and saved games are built (NX-S1 to NX-S8, rule 0.23, playbook 7.17).** What was decided while building: (1) the menu is `game/startup_menu.py` on the program's own question (`FranchiseQuestion`: Up, Down, digits, Enter, F1 with the rule 0.17 sentence, F2), asked after the welcome and the intro's stop whenever anything is saved: a save in a slot, an automatic save, a season, a franchise (N. F. L. rules only), a damaged or old save; the first item is what the original did (the newest saved game, else the season or franchise, else "start a new game"); items: continue the saved game or the game that was not finished, choose another saved game (when two can be played), continue the season, continue the franchise, start a new game, delete the saved game (or the damaged one), delete the season, delete the franchise, restore (one item per thing in the backup, or "restore something that was deleted" for several); (2) the files: `data/files/shelf.py` keeps football.sav (slot 1, so the original's file), football2.sav, football3.sav, autosave.sav, `backup\deleted-game.sav` (one level) and `backup\finished-1..3.sav`; a franchise keeps the same set in its own folder; the season (nine files with footplay.txt and its saved games) moves to `backup\season\`, a franchise to `old\league-1.sav` and companions (the three newest); every move is whole or put back; (3) football.sav gets two optional sections after the original's values and the extras: `NX-INFO` (teams, week, kind, time left, the date and slot the shelf stamps) and `NX-RESUME` (the engine's state, automatic saves only); a save without an info section is stamped by nothing (bytes unchanged for a bare sink) and shows the file's date; (4) a save belongs to a week: the classic store judges by `football.gtp`, the franchise by the extras' league/season/week mark; a save of another week is "old", one with no season an "orphan", a damaged or held file is named as such, and NONE of them is set aside or removed by the store any more (the old quarantine and the notice are gone; only the player's delete moves a file); a legacy file without info counts as this week's, as before; (5) NX-S3: loading reads and keeps; the end of a game (played out or conceded) moves every save of that week to `finished` and removes the automatic save, with one spoken sentence; a closed window or a crash keeps everything; Save Game + quit removes the automatic save of the same game; (6) NX-S4: the automatic save is written at the start of every play menu while the game is on (after every play, after a period's statistics, after the half time's kick-off), whole through a temporary file and a rename, no draw, no speech; 1 ms median on this PC (work\sx14_autosave_time.txt), about 11 to 12 KB; a failed write goes to the log and never stops the game; the window-close signal is never swallowed by it (B-W18); the resume part is JSON of the GameState (all fields but the extras), the quarter clock and the generator's state, only four classes may be built from it, refused and ignored whole when unusable, left out above 60,000 bytes; resuming restores it after the set-up has drawn, so the draws match an unbroken game exactly (8 killed games in the tests, Classic and N. F. L. rules); the N. F. L. rules' last ruling is not saved (a resumed game has no play to challenge, as a loaded one); (7) NX-S5 and NX-S6: Save Game asks the place (the game's own first, else the first empty), says what each holds, asks once before replacing another game's, Escape saves nothing, then "Game Saved" and quit (first) or keep playing (the same play menu, the game still on); (8) NX-S7: the new game asks the kind (a new season, a new franchise on the N. F. L. rules; the last kind first); a new season that would replace a season asks first (starting at no) and moves it with its saved games to the backup; the last coach and team are taken by one yes (`SAME_COACH_QUESTION`); a new franchise asks the existing question NXF-02; "a single game between any two teams" is NOT built (Q-033, accepted-default: it needs a mode outside the season); "a game of the season" is the season's own main menu; (9) every mode: Classic keeps its gameplay and its golden transcript (the rigs leave the choice off, B-W20); (10) the key list (F5) says the saved-game questions and Save Game's places in the same line as F9 (`KEYS_ADDED_F9`); (11) a switch of the kind of season in a running program (F9) no longer loads a saved game by itself (`saved_game = False`): it waits for the next start's menu; (12) restore is offered while anything is saved and right after a delete in the same menu; with nothing saved there is no menu (README: copy `backup\deleted-game.sav` by hand); (13) the log writes `save` events for scan, write, load, delete, restore, retire, auto_removed, season_delete, season_restore, franchise_delete, franchise_restore, startup_menu, startup_choice, load_chosen, slot_chosen, slot_cancelled, after_save, new_game_chosen, resumed. Result: see PROJECT_STATE. Nothing needs the owner now; the owner hears it at the next playtest (PLAYTEST_LIST sitting 1 item 6). Wrong if: the owner finds a question at every start with only a season annoying (then the season-only case goes straight on, a one-line change in `StartupMenu.run`), wants the single game, or wants the restore reachable when nothing is saved.
- **D-090 (2026-10-20, PX SX.10b, on Opus 5.5) The play menus and the outcome models of every real NFL play and every Madden NFL concept are designed; the pure modules are built and tested; the owner said yes to the public data.** (1) OWNER ANSWER to Q-P15, asked once with the files, sizes and terms named and the recommended answer first: "Yes, download (Recommended)": the nflverse play-by-play 2024, 2025 and 2026 to week 6 and FTN Data's charting (2024 to 2026) and participation (2024, 2025) files, 163 MB, CC-BY-SA 4.0 with the credit "FTN Data via nflverse", downloaded to the session's scratch folder outside the project, read with the standard library, deleted at the end; only computed numbers enter the game (`extras\plays\model_data.py`, `CREDIT`); hashes in `work\sx10b\MANIFEST.tsv`. (2) Every design question Q-P1 to Q-P20 is decided with its reason in `spec\NFL_PLAYS_DESIGN.md` 1.0 section 0; in short: the original's items stay first and Enter on a family is the playbook's pick (the usual call one or two keys), Right opens and walks a family's lists of at most ten plays, letters F favourites, A again, M more plays, K kneel and P spike when they help, O formation and tempo, N a typed search (T never a letter); concept first with the formation a setting; plain words for personnel; a new outcome layer for every catalog play in the N. F. L. mode while Classic and the "Classic" playbook keep the original's tables; motion, tempo, protection and the defense's personnel as remembered settings; reads drawn from the defense's call; play action as its routes plus the measured fake; trick plays rare; one combined defense call; kicks: deep kick first, Enter on Punt and Field Goal the original's kick with Right for the kinds and fakes, the kick defense a remembered call; the try's list is the goal-line list; the fair catch kick a question at the end of a half; the fouls by the call as start values. (3) BUILT (not yet connected to the engine; Classic untouched): `extras\plays\catalog.py` (282 plays: O001 to O167, D001 to D092, K001 to K036; SX.7's 82 keep their IDs and names; every research name is a play, merged or left out with a reason; no EA name: "Roll Coverage" is on List B, so the concept is "Safety Rotation"), `model_data.py` (every number with its source), `model.py` (the models and their named UNVERIFIED start values), `play_menus.py` (the menus, the kick-off menu, favourites, the search); 302 text rows NXY, NXZ, NXB-11 to NXB-45, NXB-50 to NXB-A17 and NXB-K01 to NXB-K36, generated into `nx_texts.py` and STRINGS_NX.md; `favourites.py` (the player's favourites.txt, read, healed and written; the game reads it from SX.10c); tests `test_sx10b_catalog.py` (292), `test_sx10b_model.py` (50), `test_sx10b_menus.py` (18), `test_sx10b_favourites.py` (5). A re-read of the code while the suite ran found two bugs, fixed with tests: a blocked punt's spot was counted from the wrong goal, and the name search offered the kick-offs and the kicks that only their own question asks (the swinging gate now only on a try). (4) The model meets the data within the tolerances of design 4.11 (league passing 64.7 against 65.1 percent completions, 6.94 against 7.11 yards an attempt; runs by gap within 0.15 yards; field goals, the extra point, punts and the 2026 kick-off within 1 to 2 points); man coverages come out 2 to 3 points easier and Cover 2 3 points harder than their charted totals because a called concept does not change its routes for the coverage (a modeling choice, recorded). Three values moved during the session with their measurements: the coverage cells' shrinkage 150 to 60 attempts (Cover 0 was six points too easy), the trick passes' routes shortened and their fake given plus 6 points (the charted trick passes average 9.2 air yards at 61.5 percent), the interception returns' shares made those of the returns that are not touchdowns. (5) D-088 item 4 done: the README and About now say "The plays are football concepts named in plain football words; many of them are also in the playbooks of Madden NFL. No text, play name, play list, ratings, art or sound of Madden NFL is copied." (the old README sentence "No text, play list ... is used" is gone; `test_sx9_about.py` checks both). (6) The 2026 gaps: the models of G26-1, G26-4 to G26-6, G26-8, G26-9, G26-12 and G26-13 are built; SX.10c to SX.10e put each in the game (design section 6). (7) The plan: SX.10c the offense in the game, SX.10d the defense calls and the kicks of 2026, SX.10e the computer's calling, the bands, 10,000 games and the Franchise test (design section 10); P9 still waits for them, SX.12, SX.13 and SX.11. Nothing else needs the owner now; the owner hears the menus after SX.10c. Wrong if: the owner finds Right or the letters harder than a deeper list of the original's kind (then Right becomes the item "More plays of this kind" in each family, one key more), wants to decide reads at the line, or wants the formation asked before each play.

- **D-092 (2026-10-20, PX SX.10b2) The descriptions and the key help as built.** (1) The row scheme of the design is final: NXY-R01 to R99 and NXY-S00 on (offense), NXZ-R01 on (defense), NXB-D01 to D36 (kicks), NXB-E01 to E25 and E27 to E33 (the original's items, the menu words, the playbook groups), NXB-F01 to F35 (the lists), NXB-G01 to G08 (the settings), NXH-48 to NXH-51 (the added football words of F7); kind help, at most 200 characters, one or two sentences of at most 35 words (a test). (7) `PLAYS_GUIDE.md` and `README_keys.md` are generated by `clone\tools\make_docs.py` and compared by tests.
