- **D-093 (2026-10-21, PX SX.10c) The catalog's offense in the game, as built.** (1) With an offense playbook that is not Classic (a style or the team's) the player's offense menus are the catalog's (`extras\plays\play_menus.py` through `extras\offense_flow.py`): the original's items first with their digits, Right to a family's lists, the letters F, A, M, K, P, O and N, plus and minus, D; SX.7's offense group menu is removed (`PlaybookFlow.offense_items`); the defense keeps SX.7's groups until SX.10d; the Classic playbook keeps the original's items; Classic is unchanged (the golden transcript passes). (2) New hooks, each the original's in Classic: H3 `menu_right`, `menu_heading`, `menu_key` with the key code (a letter only from a letter key: a keypad digit or a function key never reads as its letter, B-W6), `MENU_REFRESH` and `MENU_QUIET`; H19 `model_play` (after the snap sentence), `turnover_return`, `play_text`. (3) The outcome models play only the player's catalog calls; the computer's offense keeps the original's tables with SX.7's bounded changes until SX.10e builds its calling over the catalog, so every band of SX.2 to SX.9 is as it was; SX.10e measures the balance of a catalog offense against them. (4) The call the model plays against is the computer's SX.7 style call (D001 to D030 are the catalog's), else a fixed stand-in per family, D002, D004, D007, D014, D017, D022, with no draw. (5) Enter on a family is, until SX.10e's pick, SX.7's style draw over that family (one draw), said before the snap (NXB-H15); the quarterback items 8, 9 and 0 are O046, O047 and O048 (no draw). (6) The telling: the original's pools, a run by the original entry whose range holds the yards (the defense's own first), a pass by the original's steps (SX.5's scramble and SX.3's grounding where they were), the quarterback's runs by SX.5's sentences and slide; the engine's play number is set for the telling (`narration_family`); the new sentences are rows NXB-H01 to H15. (7) Turnovers (G26-8, G26-9, G26-10's score): an interception is run back from the catch by the model's return, a lost fumble is at its spot, in the own end zone a touch back at the 20, a return to the end zone is the defense's touchdown (`return_touchdown`: the original's steps after TOUCH DOWN, the record's score "return touchdown", no second end point), a try run back gives the defense two points, a fumble the offense keeps ends the play at its spot. (8) Kneel and spike (G26-12) as design 2.7, built as: K when the player leads in the fourth quarter and the time left is at most (downs left less the opponent's timeouts) times 40 seconds, or in the last 40 seconds of the second quarter; P when the clock runs in the last two minutes of a half before fourth down; live time 1 to 2 seconds and 1 second; a kneel never goes into the own end zone. (9) Again and Formation and Tempo are saved with the game (`ExtrasRecord.again`, `play_settings`, checked whole); formation, motion and protection go to the models; the Tempo no huddle (20 to 28 seconds, UNVERIFIED) and hurry up (12 to 20) is the clock's tempo between the player's plays; personnel and the side are kept and change nothing until SX.10d. (10) The ratings door: the ratings' Mod alone, clamped to the same bounds, moves the run (100 hundredths a step), the catch's yards, the completion points, the sack rate (40 per 10,000 a point, UNVERIFIED) and the fumble rate (50 over 50 plus the step). (11) plays.txt has every play of the catalog (282, the K plays too) and `off` takes a play out of every menu; favourites.txt is a file of the Customize layer (header word "favourite plays"), read with the N. F. L. rules and written at once by plus and minus. (12) With the quarterback runs off no list offers a sneak, keeper or designed run (the options stay). (13) Until SX.10d Right on Field Goal and Punt opens nothing and no list offers a special-teams play. (14) The key list has Right, Plus and Minus, the play letters and the search box (feature playbooks); F7 adds NXH-52 to NXH-55. (15) A new player's default playbook stays Classic until SX.10e builds the real pick and measures the bands with it (A70 (5)); the owner reaches the new menus with F9, Rules, offense playbook. Wrong if: SX.10e's bands are off with the catalog's offense (then the stand-in calls, the provisional pick and the UNVERIFIED values move with a recorded measurement), or the owner finds the menus slower than the original's (PLAYTEST_LIST item 10). Evidence: tests/test_sx10c_offense_menus.py, test_sx10c_narration.py, test_sx10c_games.py, the mutation check work/mutation_check_sx10c.py (18 of 18 killed), the golden Classic transcript.
- **D-094 (2026-10-21, PX SX.10d) The catalog's defense and the kicks of 2026 in the game, as built.** (1) When: in a game of the N. F. L. rules where the player uses a playbook (an offense or a defense playbook that is not Classic, `extras\kick_flow.kicks_on`), for both teams' kicks; with both playbooks Classic the kicks stay the original's and SX.4's onside question, so the bands of SX.2 to SX.9 stay as measured until SX.10e makes the team's playbook the default and measures them with all of it (Q-035, Q-036, B-P128). (2) The defense menus: `extras\defense_flow.py` gives PlayMenus' side D through H3 `defense_menu` when the player's defense playbook is not Classic: the six original calls first with their digits (Enter: SX.7's style draw over the family until SX.10e's pick, said before the snap, NXB-H15; a family whose calls are all off is the original's call, and the same for the offense), Right to their lists, F, A, M, P Kick Defense on fourth down, O (Defensive Personnel, Coverage Shade), plus and minus (favourites.txt's second half), D; SX.7's two-level group menu is removed from `playbook_flow.py` and its tests moved. (3) The play: H19 `model_play` asks the offense flow, then the defense flow: the computer's offense play (its style's catalog play when it is of the family the engine plays, else the stand-ins O001, O010, O017, O025, O032 and O046 to O048, no draw) against the player's call through `OffenseFlow.run_model`; the computer's offense family is still drawn by the original's rules (SX.10e). (4) The personnel (Q-P13, `extras\plays\personnel.py`): 3, 4, 5 or 6 defensive backs by the offense's receivers (3 only at the goal line against one receiver or none), the call's box moved one man at most and kept 5 to 8, the player's Defensive Personnel overrides and Coverage Shade puts press or off in place of the call's; the computer's call against the player's play is matched the same way (to the play's or the Personnel setting's people), so SX.10c's offense results move a little; the Side setting changes nothing (a mirror, no data). (5) The kick-off (G26-1, G26-11): H4 answers the new KICKOFF_2026 or ONSIDE_KICKOFF; the player's kick-off menu (deep, short, squib, onside with the switch on, the safety punt after a safety; Enter the deep kick) replaces the onside question; the computer kicks onside by SX.4's rules, else one draw over deep 55, short 42, squib 3 (UNVERIFIED); a kick made again keeps its call (`ExtrasRecord.kick_call`); a touch back at the 35, out of bounds and short of the landing zone at the 40, a catch in the end zone asks the player to run it out unless the model's return is a touchdown (B-X111); from the 20 the catch moves 15 yards and keeps a quarter of the touchbacks (UNVERIFIED); the safety punt is a free kick nobody blocks. (6) Punts (G26-5): the player's kinds and fake punts with Right on Punt; the computer pins within 55 yards of the goal (UNVERIFIED); the receiving call is the player's remembered kick defense (`ExtrasRecord.kick_defense`, K031 to K036, set from P on fourth down or More Plays) or the return; a muffed punt the kicking team keeps draws no foul (a recorded simplification). (7) Field goals and the extra point (G26-4, G26-6, G26-7): any distance (to the goal plus 17), a miss at the spot of the kick or the defense's 20, a block at the spot of the kick or the defense's touchdown (the kicking team's recovery of a block on an early down is not modeled), the try from the 15 (33 yards) that can miss or be blocked and run back for two points. (8) Fakes: the model's 50 percent shape; a fake that works gains the yards to go and 0 to 8 more, a run that fails ends 2 behind to a yard short, a pass that fails is incomplete (UNVERIFIED); the swinging gate on a try is a run of the end that scores when the fake's model says so. (9) The fair catch kick (G26-13, Q-P18): offered after the player's fair catch in the second or fourth quarter with a kick of at most 65 yards when at most 30 seconds are left (UNVERIFIED), a yes or no question starting at no; yes is the next snap (FAIR_CATCH_KICK, the engine's step `fair_catch_kick`); the computer never tries one and the untimed down after a fair catch on the last play is not offered. (10) The kick fouls (G26-14): P34 illegal wedge, P35 illegal kick off formation, P36 leaping, P37 kick catch interference (the receivers' ball 15 yards beyond their catch, only on a punt they caught), rates 0.3, 0.6, 0.2, 0.5 per 100 fitting kicks (UNVERIFIED), drawn only with the 2026 kicks; a missed field goal's declined result is the spot of the kick. (11) The ratings door (H8): the kicker's step of the original's odds is a point of the made rate, the punter's step of the shortest punt a yard of the punt. (12) The engine: `game\pag_kicks26.py` (the steps), hooks H20 `special_kick`, `kickoff_call`, `kickoff_result`, `kickoff_touchback`, `kick_result`, `fake_yards`, `kick_text`, `fair_catch` (Classic: None or never called), `run_it_out` split into `ask_run_it_out`; the ball is put where the kick left it before each end point (A81 (3)). (13) Keys: Right and the play letters cover the defense and Punt and Field Goal, the digits and D the kick-off menu; F6 NXH-56 to NXH-58, F7 NXH-59 to NXH-61; README_keys.md and PLAYS_GUIDE.md regenerated. Wrong if: SX.10e's bands are off with the 2026 kicks (the UNVERIFIED values move with a recorded measurement), or the owner wants the 2026 kicks with the Classic playbooks too (Q-036). Evidence: tests/test_sx10d_defense_menus.py, test_sx10d_kicks.py, test_sx10d_games.py, the probe work/sx10d/probe_games.py (30 games: extra point 95.5 percent, field goals 84.3, kick-off touchbacks 15.6, start 31.5, punts returned 30.1), the mutation check work/mutation_check_sx10d.py, the golden Classic transcript.
- **D-095 (2026-10-21, PX SX.10e) The computer's calling over the catalog, the playbook's pick, the two-point chart, SACK_SCALE and the team's playbook as the default, with every band measured again.** (1) `extras\plays\calling.py` (pure, whole numbers, one draw a call) and its generated data `calling_data.py` (`work\sx10e\make_calling_data.py`: the defense calls' base weights fitted by iterative proportional fitting to the data's coverage, rusher, blitzer, box and defensive-back shares, each within 0.2 points); the offense's list shares, the styles' leans and the situations are in NFL_PLAYS_DESIGN 7.3 (UNVERIFIED shapes, the pass share by down and distance the data's). (2) In a game that plays the catalog (`kick_flow.kicks_on`: the player uses a playbook) every side with a style calls over the whole catalog (`PlaybookFlow.catalog_offense`, `catalog_defense`), SX.5's separate quarterback-run draw is not made (the catalog holds those runs) and the model plays every call (`OffenseFlow.computer_play`, H19); with the Classic playbook the computer keeps SX.7's draw on the original's tables; Classic is untouched (H21 `computer_goes_for_two` answers no with no draw; the golden transcript passes). (3) The playbook's pick behind Enter on a family is the same weights over the family (`PlaybookFlow.pick`). (4) The clock plays and the try are rules: kneel (design 2.7; the computer at the end of the first half only within 30 seconds and 70 yards out), spike (1 in 2, the last minute, no timeout left), the Hail Mary or the laterals on a last play, the try's list. (5) G26-10: the two-point chart of Laura Albert, Punk Rock Operations Research, 6 February 2024 (SRC-SECONDARY: behind by 10, 8, 5, 2 or 1 or ahead by 1, 2, 4, 5 or 7 after the touchdown) in the last ten minutes and overtime, 3 percent earlier (UNVERIFIED, calibrated); the engine's `computer_try_choice` says it (NXB-H16); the simulation rig's side 1 asks the same hook (B-X112). (6) The computer fakes a punt (3 percent) or a field goal (1.5) on fourth and 4 or less (UNVERIFIED). (7) SACK_SCALE 170 (the engine's sacks 6.4 percent of drop-backs; the model's own test keeps 100, B-P130) and QB_SCRAMBLE_FROM_INCOMPLETE_MODEL 12 (scrambles 5.7 percent). (8) The team's playbook is a new player's default (settings, the Rules menu's reset; Q-035 answered); a settings file that never chose (no `nx_playbook_chosen = yes`) and says classic is read as team once (B-W27); with it every new game plays the 2026 kicks (Q-036 answered: Classic is the explicit choice of the original's plays and kicks). (9) Two values of earlier sessions get a catalog game's own value, measured, the old ones kept for the Classic playbook (whose bands are unchanged, the default pairing gives SX.9's numbers exactly): PENALTY_RATE_SCALE_CATALOG 1.07 (SX.3's 1.2 gave 14.42 fouls a game with the catalog's pass mix) and RULING_RATES_CATALOG goal (28, 62), catch (4, 15), line (20, 86) (SX.6's gave 0.351 coach challenges a team-game and 1.023 booth reviews a game: the models end three times as many plays at the goal line); calling.LATE_PASS_FLOOR 7000 (8000 gave quarters 2 and 4 7.02 more plays, band 2 to 7); the sweep is `work\sx10e\sweep_bands_sx10e.py`. (10) Found and fixed: the swinging gate took its list's Field Goal family (B-P131); a style's qb_percent of 0 did not keep its quarterback runs out of the catalog's calling; the catalog's sneak could slide (SX.5's rule never allows it); the 2026 kick-off never asked SX.8o's returner door (the Franchise's check WL2). (11) Measured with 10,000 games each (`work\sx10e\`): the calling's bands (every one passes), the SX.2 to SX.9 bands in three pairings (teams, mixed, default: all pass), SX.3's, SX.4's and SX.5's own bands with every switch on (every band passes; SX.5's property check still names 2 slides in 10,000 games that "left 4 or 5 yards": `work\sx10e\probe_slide.py` shows a replay review moved each slide's spot back after it, a legitimate corrected spot the check did not foresee), the styles' balance against Pro Style (47.4 to 52.2 percent, control 50.1), the Franchise's 20 seasons (fast 500 x 20 and full 5 x 20: only the design bands D-078 already restated miss); kneels come to 3.4 a game against the league's 1.54 (no band; PLAYTEST_LIST 12). (12) Conformance rows NX-P1 to NX-P5, NX-P7, NX-P8 and NX-R7 (the id NX-R7 names two requirements in NFL_EXTRA_FEATURES; one row covers both, B-P132). Wrong if: the owner hears the computer kneel too often or call plays a real team would not (PLAYTEST_LIST 12), or SX.13's audit finds a 2026 rule the calling breaks. Evidence: tests/test_sx10e_calling.py, test_sx10e_games.py, the moved tests, work/mutation_check_sx10e.py, the reports of work/sx10e/.
- **D-096 (2026-10-21, PX SX.12) The 2026 overtime of Rule 16 in the N. F. L. rules mode, with ties in the standings, the season files and the Franchise; Classic keeps sudden death.** Rule 16 was read again from the 2026 book (printed pages 64 to 66, 16-1-1 to 16-1-5, and 4-8-2 (c); NFL_RULES_RESEARCH Part 10, SRC-OFFICIAL). (1) BUILT: `extras\overtime.py` (plain functions, no draw, no word: the phases "first", "second", "sudden", "over", the first team, the tied score at the start, the kick after the first team's score, the play-off periods' next step), its values in ExtrasRecord (H5, saved in football.sav; a record saved before SX.12 holds none and its overtime goes on as sudden death); the hooks H22 in `game\rules.py`, each the original's answer in Classic: `overtime_follows`, `overtime_lines`, `overtime_toss`, `overtime_kicks_first`, `points_end_game` (called after every place points are added: the safety, the penalty safety, the touchdown's six before its try, the defense's return touchdown, the score step), `possession_ends_game` (after the possession sentence), `period_end_step`, `overtime_fourth_down`, `game_over_lines`, and H10's OVERTIME_OVER; the engine's steps `overtime_coin_toss` (the opening toss's code with a flag) and `next_overtime_period`. (2) The toss: the visitors call it (16-1-2, the original's vtrnt), the winner receives or kicks off, no deferral (Q-038); H and T are heads and tails at this toss as at the opening one (no timeout can be called between regulation and the overtime's kick-off; the key list's coin row says so); the computer that wins kicks off 700 times in 1,000 (tuning OT_KICK_FIRST_PER_MILLE, the league's 14 of 20, SRC-SECONDARY), else says the original's " choose to receive ". (3) The possession rule: a first-drive score does not end the game, the try is played and the first team kicks off; that kick is the second team's opportunity even when the kickers keep it (16-1-5 (c)), and so is a punt the receivers muff and the kickers keep (after a scoreless first drive the next score wins; the second team's own kept muff goes on as its possession, Q-038); a safety or a defensive score on the first possession wins; the second team ahead after its touchdown wins with no try (4-8-2 (c)); level after both, or a first possession without points, makes the next score win (the sentence NXC-10 once); the second team's possession ending without points ends the game. (4) Periods: one 10-minute regular-season period (a quick game counts as the regular season), a score as it runs out ends the game (no kick-off), tied at its end it is a tie (" The game ends in a tie. ", NXC-11; the final score's " tied at 20 all" is the original's); play-off periods of 15 minutes go on with the ball, down and distance (16-1-4 (d)), the third (and seventh) begins with a kick-off received by the side that kicked off the last tossed period, the fifth (and ninth) with a new toss (Q-038); the timeouts and the warnings were already SX.2's (two in the period, three per pair of play-off periods). (5) The computer as the second team behind never punts and kicks a field goal only when it ties and is in the original's range (`overtime_fourth_down`; the sim's side 1 too). (6) Ties in the season: `Records.ties` and footbal6.dat (`data\files\tiesfile.py`, 32 values, written with footbal3.dat only while a team has a tie and removed otherwise, a season file for the backup, delete and restore), the standings say " 9, and 6, and 1 tie. ", a tie is half a win and half a loss in every ranking (sort keys 2 x losses + ties and 2 x wins + ties, the original's order when nobody has one), the qualifiers' lines and the week's result "<team> and the <other> tied at 20. " (NXC-12 to NXC-14); a tied week counts only under the N. F. L. rules (the engine's `ties_allowed`), so a Classic tie still goes to the opponent (Q41). The computer's games keep the original's score table, which never ties (the league's rate, about 1 tie in 300 games, would change every season's draws; recorded, not built). (7) The Franchise: league.sav format 11 with the season's ties (a file of format 10 has none), the league check wins + losses + ties = weeks, a result row with equal scores is a tie (only in a regular week), the history entry's "ties" (only for a season with one), the coach's career, the spoken and written records (" 6 and 1 tie" after the losses), the news (a tie is no game for the news and ends streaks), the morale (no change by the result), the draft order (a tie half a win). (8) Texts NXC-06 to NXC-14, NXH-63 and NXH-64 (F6). (9) MEASURED, 10,000 regular-season games with every switch on and the teams' playbooks (`work\sx12\overtime_bands_sx12_1_10000.txt`): 213 overtimes (2.13 percent, the league 5.39: MISS, Q-037), 22 ties (0.22 percent of games, 10.3 percent of overtime games; the league 0.25 and about 4.5, 1 in 14 in 2025), the receiving team 56.8 percent (the league 57.5 of 20), toss winners kicked 69.5 percent, 0.39 timeouts a team in overtime (the league 0.83; 331 none, 24 one, 71 two: the computer's rule 4 calls both or none, recorded), 23.25 points a team-game, no property problem; the end-of-regulation margins (`work\sx12\probe_margins_sx12_3000.txt`) explain the share: 3-point margins 7.5 percent against the league's 15.1, 1-point 7.8, 2-point 5.7. No value moved (the share is the regulation game's, Q-037). (10) PLAY-OFFS, 10,000 games (`work\sx12\overtime_bands_sx12_17_10000.txt`): 191 overtimes (1.91 percent), no tie, one game into a second period, the receiving team 51.3 percent, toss winners kicked 78.5 percent (150 of 191; 70 percent expected, 2.6 standard deviations high, a chance result or the generator's correlation after the coin draw: watched, nothing moved), 0.11 timeouts a team in overtime (15-minute periods without a warning in the first: the league's 0.83 is the regular season's, so this is a report, not a band), no property problem. (10b) THE EARLIER BANDS (`work\sx12\chain_sx12_log.txt`, `work\sx10e\integration_bands_sx10e_teams_10000_sx12.txt`): 10,000 games of the default pairing pass every SX.2 to SX.9 band with the overtime (counted plays 34.32 a regulation quarter, quarters 2 and 4 carry 6.79 more, 26.23 seconds a play, accepted fouls 12.83 a game, coach challenges 0.276 a team-game changing 48.6 percent, replay reviews 0.595 changing 73.1, onside kicks 0.198, designed quarterback runs 1.335, 3.69 timeouts a team, no property problem: the shared base now allows exactly the legal tie, B-X117). THE FRANCHISE's 20 seasons through the whole program (full 5 x 20, `work\simulate_franchise_sx8f_full_5x20_sx12.txt`): I9 (the history and the coach's record, ties counted) and I10 (league.sav loads equal at 900 stage ends, 1,350 cut writes usable) and I12 (the run repeats) pass; the morale checks MO1 to MO3, GG1, HO2 and TD2 failed in a first run because their oracle (`work\people_checks_sx8n.py`) counted a tie as a win, fixed to the rule and passing in the second run; C-F2 missed by 0.03: the player's team 0.83 wins a season under its peers (allowed 0.8, standard error 0.26; SX.10e's run -0.72, the gap is the catalog game's against the computer's score-table games, Q-039; the check now counts a tie as half a win); the design bands I8 spread and I11 streak as restated in D-078. (11) Tests: `tests\test_sx12_overtime.py`; `test_fr_league.py` reads the format from data.py; `test_sx9_saved_states.py` sets the overtime fields; the conformance map's NX-R7 row; the shared measuring base allows exactly the legal tie (B-X117) and the Franchise run's I9 counts ties. Wrong if: the owner hears the overtime as unclear or the computer's choices as wrong (PLAYTEST_LIST 13), or SX.13's audit reads Rule 16 differently (Q-038).
- **D-097 (2026-10-21, PX SX.13) The 2026 rules audit (NX-R7, D-085 (4)): the N. F. L. rules mode read again against the 2026 Official Playing Rules by six fresh reviewers, every finding checked in the book; the differences fixed with tests, the rest decided.** (27 differences fixed, 2 groups of citations corrected, 21 recorded decisions, the rest not modeled.) The book: the official PDF of operations.nfl.com (SHA-256 144cec5b...87c55, the same text SX.12 read), converted in `work\sx13\` and not committed; the audit is `spec\NFL_RULES_AUDIT_2026.md` (A-01 to A-55, every rule number and tag) and the reviewers' reports are `work\sx13\findings_*.md`. (1) FIXED (tests in `clone\tests\test_sx13_rules_audit.py`): no try after a last-second fourth-period touchdown a try cannot change (4-8-2 (c), the regular season's overtime too); the overtime's timing windows (16-1-3 (e), 16-1-4 (h), 4-3-2 (a), (e)(3)); the T key's kept timeout granted before the runoff (3-36-2); a kick ends the dead-ball period (4-5-1 Item 3, hook H18 `ball_live`); a timeout before a try at 0:00; the untimed down in the regular season's overtime and after offsetting fouls (4-8-2 (a), (h)); the untimed fair catch kick (4-8-2 (e), 10-2-5 (a)); no clock for an onside kick the kickers keep (4-3-1 (b)); the 10-second runoff for delay of game (4-7-1 (a), NO_RUNOFF_FOULS empty) and after a reversal of a play that began after the warning (15-5-2, 4-7-4); a defense foul after the warning lets a leading offense run the clock (4-7-1 Item 2); double fouls with a change of possession played again (14-5-2); kick catch interference never takes a punt return touchdown away; the offense's foul behind its own goal line and a run ending there are safeties (14-3-6 Exc. 2 and 3); the ball carrier's face mask after a catch three and one; free kicks not made from the 35 follow the landing zone and 6-2-4's 25 or 30 yards (`model.short_kick_spot`, `ExtrasRecord.free_after_safety`), the safety punt read as a free kick (touchback 35, short of the zone the foul, downed a ball at rest), the computer's safety kick deep; a punt blocked behind the goal line is a safety or the receivers' touchdown (BLOCKED_PUNT_END_ZONE_TD 5000, UNVERIFIED); O155 Fake Kneel is Victory Formation Pass (7-2-1 (c): a simulated kneel ends the play; "fake kneel" LEFT_OUT); no fumble once the ball carrier reached the goal line; a fumbled lateral is the laterals' run, never a sack; the Formation setting never changes the kneel, the spike and the Throwback; the third play-off overtime period's first choice to the toss's loser (16-1-4 (e), `ot_toss_loser`, H22 `overtime_toss_won`); the second team's own punt kept after a muff ends its possession (16-1-5 (c), Q-038). Texts NXB-J25 to NXB-J28 added, NXY-A55 and NXY-S55 reworded, PLAYS_GUIDE.md regenerated; citations P07 7-4-7, P36 12-3-1 (o), P37 14-4-8 Exc. (3), 16-1-4 (g), 11-3-1 (b); the overtime warnings retagged SRC-OFFICIAL. (2) DECIDED, kept as they are, each with what would show it wrong: item 1 the Classic playbook plays the original's kicks and plays inside the N. F. L. rules (Q-036; wrong if the owner wants the book there too: one line in `kick_flow.kicks_on`); 2 the fair catch kick only for the player late in a half (D-094 (9)); 3 no overtime deferral (16-1-4 (e) assumes one; wrong if the casebook allows it); 4 periods 5, 9 begin with a toss even during an initial possession; 6 15-6-2's restored timeout cannot arise (the booth reviews before any timeout; the kept T is the 15-5-2 Note's charged timeout); 7 no timeout in place of the runoff (4-7-1 Item 1); 8 no runoff for grounding (4-7-1 (b)); 9 the kick-off after a score as the first or third quarter ends is played at 0:00; 10 timeouts beyond the limits are refused, so 4-5-1 Item 4 cannot arise; 11 a double foul with a change of possession is always replayed (no clean-hands keep, 14-5-2 Item 1); 12 penalty yards the book carries to the try or the kick-off are not carried (D-050 kept); 13 only the dead-ball option for a kicking team's foul on a kick-off (14-4-7); 14 grounding and an at-snap foul by the same offense keep grounding; 15 no fumble spot for 14-4-3 (a); 16 "1 to go" on the line to gain; 17 11-3-3 Item 4 (c)'s wording; 18 no challenge of the last play of the first or third quarter (D-053); 19 plays with an after-the-play foul are not ruled; 20 no advance after a catch replay finds; 21 no rows for fair catch interference, an invalid signal or the block after a signal. (3) NOT-MODELED lists in the audit (A-16, A-43, A-49, A-50). (4) G26-1 to G26-14 all closed with a playbook (the default). Wrong if: the owner's playtest hears a rule wrongly (PLAYTEST_LIST 14), or a later book changes a rule cited here. Evidence: tests/test_sx13_rules_audit.py, the moved tests (test_nx_review.py M1, test_nx_clock.py's late windows, test_sx10d_kicks.py's safety punt, test_sx12_overtime.py's muff and third period), work/sx13/.
- **D-098 (2026-10-22, PX SX.11) The real head coaches and players, no typing of names (NX-N1 to NX-N7, CLAUDE.md 0.19, playbook 7.14, D-082).** (1) RESEARCH: eight subagents (one per division, text reports, B-X119) fetched the coaches, players and depth chart pages of the 32 teams' own sites on 2026-10-22 (SRC-OFFICIAL, all 32 loaded; the Texans' depth chart is an image, ESPN's is SRC-SECONDARY); the fetch tool returns summaries of the pages, so a reviewer should spot-check spellings (P8). `spec\NFL_NAMES_RESEARCH.md` has the sources, dates and terms. TERMS: the sites are NFL-network sites whose terms bar systematic retrieval into a database without written consent and limit use to personal, non-commercial use; the data kept is facts only. The files are built and used as the program's built-in values; whether they are published in the repository at P11 is the owner's call (Q-040). (2) FILES (`clone/assets/data/coaches.txt`, `rosters.txt`, `respellings.txt`, the player's copies in the customize folder, playbook 7.9/7.10, format 1, a VERSION line, healed like every list): one coach per team; a team's active players of the game's positions in depth order (no linemen or snappers: the game has no such place) with the returners again as RET; a team's lines in the player's file replace that team's built-in lines as a whole (a new season is a file replacement and no old player comes back); respellings by ID with `=> `, `off` switches one off. Built by `work/sx11/make_names_data.py`. (3) THE SET-UP: with the N. F. L. rules the 33 names of both teams are the real players of the player's team (when footplay.txt has to be written; a file the player has wins) and of the opponent (when it is not a Franchise team), chosen by `extras/real_names.py` (receivers WR1, WR2, WR3, TE1, WR4, TE2; a short position takes the players left over); the names are put in AFTER the original's draws, so a game draws exactly what it drew with the original's names (test). (4) THE COACH: the team is chosen first (the coach of a team is known only then; the prompts say "coach" as the original does when nothing was typed), then one question: keep the real coach (Enter), choose another real coach from the list of 32, or type a name (the original's prompt); footbal1.dat holds the chosen name. The same in a new franchise. (5) THE QUARTERBACKS: Enter takes the real quarterbacks of both teams (the questions stay, so a player may still type); the slot of the pool is still drawn. (6) THE FRANCHISE: a new league's players and the other 31 teams' head coaches are the real ones, put in after the draws (the ratings, ages and draws are the league's own, not any game's; three pairs of real namesakes in different teams, Byron Young, Jaylon Jones and Justin Jefferson, keep the second one's place on a drawn surname because a league's active names are unique); later draft classes, stand-ins and coordinators are generated. (7) SPEECH: `sapi_render_check.py` ran 210 names (every name with an apostrophe, hyphen, period or accent, and 120 others) through one voice of each of 27 SAPI engines: 25 engines had no silent name; 4 old voices made no sound for the accented letters (Piñeiro, Jevón, Nuñez-Roches, Estimé: the program's variants healed them), so those four are respelled without the accent; two neural voices (Orpheus Classic, PocketTTS) did not finish in 90 seconds in the tool (a slow start, not a name). Suffixes and St. are respelled as words. How a name sounds cannot be heard here: the owner's playtest adds lines (PLAYTEST_LIST 15). (8) WHOLE NAMES in the play-by-play and the prompts ("Joe Burrow throws to Ja'Marr Chase"), because the Franchise needs unique names and maps its players to the game by name; a last-name form is Q-041. (9) Classic keeps the typed coach and the random surnames; nothing of it is read. Wrong if: the owner finds the whole names too slow (Q-041), a name is said wrong (a respelling line), the owner does not want the data published (Q-040), or a spelling is wrong (the file says how to fix it).
- **D-099 (2026-10-22, P8 S8.2) The owner's answers to Q-040 and Q-041 (the real names).** (1) Q-040: YES, publish the real coaches' and players' names (names, positions, numbers only; no ratings, photographs or logos) in the repository, the installer and the release, with the README's notice (used descriptively, not endorsed, removal on request) and the refresh path kept; Claude is not a lawyer and the NFL network sites' terms (nfl.com/legal/terms 1.3) bar systematic copying without consent, which the owner knew when answering; the S11.1 public-action approval still stands as the one approval per game. (2) Q-041: WHOLE names in the play-by-play and the prompts, as built (no last-name form, no setting). Wrong if: a rights holder asks for removal (then ship the three files empty of players), or the playtest finds the whole names too slow.
- **D-100 (2026-10-22, P8 S8.2) The exit says no menu program is missing (CLAUDE.md 0.26, playbook 7.18, B-S32; the owner's playtest).** The owner, after hearing the exit: remove the "could not find the Kitchensinc game menu" message, for every game. The exit is now foot0, the thank-you sentence (D-082), the release of the sounds and the end; the original's two Beeps, the sentence and the 0.2 s pause after it (Form_Load 0x48a80f to 0x48a9ce, D-006, G-F6, B-X13) are not run, in Classic too (the golden transcript has no exit; the exit's tests were rewritten). The sentence is kept as `texts.NO_GAME_MENU_ORIGINAL`, never said; the Sounds menu still lists the exit beep file (beep_exit, 27 items) because the player's own beep file route (7.7) and its tests are kept, it is just never played at the exit. The exit logs one line. Wrong if: the owner wants the beeps back or the exit beep removed from the Sounds menu.
- **D-101 (2026-10-22, SY.1, owner list after the S8.2 playtest) The radio-style broadcast of the N. F. L. rules (CLAUDE.md 0.27, playbook 7.19).** (1) THE BANTER: the owner: "when comments are turned on: you must replace the comments that Jim Kitchen originally had with standard sports banter, as if from a radio broadcast" (sports games only, global rule 0.27). In the N. F. L. rules the announcer's banter draws its lines from the pool BN of remarks.txt, 142 lines B-numbered BN01 to BN142 (generic: the crowd, weather, band, listeners at home, football sayings, teasing of Ghip; written by three subagents from a brief, checked by work/s82/check_corpora.py: ASCII, at most 150 characters, no digits, no brackets, no duplicates, none of the originals); the draws are the original's (a line n of 142, a name n of 58, rady), so no seeded test or band moved; Classic keeps banter.txt and the 142 jokes word for word (the golden transcript is unchanged). A hand-made NflRules object keeps the original's banter (radio_banter False); the program's rules always have it (rules_from_settings). Switching every BN line off leaves the game without banter, not with the jokes. (2) GHIP: the color commentator the original's banter already addresses ("Ghip, is that a flag on the field or a hotdog wrapper"): 158 lines in 19 pools by moment (CKO CBG CSK CTV CTD CFG CFM CTC CTS CFD CRZ CPN CTM CQE CHT CFN CFT CTT CBO) and the announcer's 12 questions ASK; at each moment a chance in percent (touchdown 85, turnover 80, half time, final and the opening 100, big gain 60, field goal 55, sack 40, third down 15, fourth-down gamble 70, red zone 35, penalty 30, two-minute warning 75, quarter end 60, close game 60, decided game 50), at least two plays between two ordinary lines, ASK before him 40 percent of the time; about 20 lines in a game of 130 plays. He draws only from his OWN generator (GameRandom(random.Random(seed of the game ^ constant))), so a game plays out identically with him on and off (tested: the same draws, trace and score). (3) HIS VOICE: a sixth speaker `color` outside ROLES (tests pin ROLES; the original's F11 and F12 loop over it and Classic must stay exact); settings.txt lines voice_color, volume_color and nx_broadcast; F11 and F12 ask for him after the five speakers only with the N. F. L. rules and the broadcast on; the SAPI engine has one queue, so his voice never overlaps the announcer's; NVDA and JAWS ignore roles (the screen reader speaks his lines in its voice, said in the guides); the first time the game opens, if the player chose no voice for him, he gets the next voice of the announcer's engine (the voice list is read in the background when the game begins) and the choice is saved like the player's own. His line is said asynchronously, is not kept for F2 and is cut by the first key or T. (4) THE SWITCH: F9, Rules, Broadcast (item 10, on by default; Reset is the eleventh item, reached with Down) switches Ghip and the questions to him; F8 silences banter and Ghip together. Hooks: H23 banter_lines(game) and broadcast_moment(state, what), Classic answers none (rules.py); the call sites are the end of a quarter, the two-minute warning, half time, the end of the game and after a score (pag_breaks, pag_results), plus NflRules' own before_kickoff, between_plays, before_snap and after_outcome and the penalty remark. Wrong if: Ghip talks too often or too seldom (the chances are in extras/broadcast.py CHANCE), his voice is not different enough (F12 or a better default), the radio banter is not the kind the owner wants (the lines are data in strings_nx.tsv).
- **D-102 (2026-10-22, SY.2, owner list) The stadium sounds of the N. F. L. rules: realistic sound effects and crowd effects (D-032 revised for this mode, playbook 7.7.1 and 7.8).** D-032 decided that the clone adds no sound to the play; the owner now asks for realistic effects and crowd effects, so in the N. F. L. rules (and only there) the game plays about 15 short sounds beside the original's wavs. SOURCE: generated offline by work/s82/make_sfx.py with numpy (numpy is needed only to MAKE the files, never by the game, rule 0.11): the crowd is a choir of 30 to 38 synthesized voices (vowel formants, vibrato, breath noise) with swells (cheer, roar, groan, boo, gasp, oh) and dense random claps (applause); the impacts are a thump and a crack; the chain and the goal post are inharmonic rings. They are new original work (MIT, marked GENERATED in assets/sounds_nx/README.txt), not recordings and not Jim Kitchen's. NOBODY HAS HEARD THEM (the session cannot hear): objective checks only (work/s82/analyze_sfx.py: duration, peak, rms, dominant frequency, centroid, start and end), so the owner's listening round decides; real recordings with a clear license (CC0) can replace any file, by the player's own file of the same name in the sounds folder or by a new shipped file; downloading them needs the owner's yes per file. WHERE: assets/sounds_nx/*.wav, found beside the exe (build.ps1 copies them); played on the EXTRA channel (nothing was ever played there during a game), non-waiting (GameSounds.play_effect), 15 to 26 dB under the sound volume. WHEN (extras/stadium.py): a touchdown (cheer for yours, groan for theirs, at the six points, hook H23 touchdown), a turnover or a sack (groan for yours, roar for theirs; a sack hits), a gain of 20 or more (roar, or a gasp), a gain of 8 or more (a hit), a completion (the pop of a catch, 60 percent), a first down (the chain), a missed field goal (groan, a sigh, and the goal post in 25 percent), a field goal (applause or a sigh), a penalty (the crowd boos a flag on your team, the flag is thrown on theirs), the end of the first and third quarters and half time (applause). RULE 0.14: a file of the player's with the same name in the sounds folder replaces the shipped one and a new number adds to the family (crowd_cheer2.wav); the draw among them is the stadium's own. NOTHING MOVES: the stadium draws only from its own generator (seed of the game xor a constant), nothing waits, so a game plays out identically with it on and off (tested: the same draws and score). THE SWITCH: F9, Rules, Stadium sounds (item 11, on by default; Reset is the twelfth item). Wrong if: the sounds are not realistic enough or too loud or too frequent (the levels and chances are in extras/stadium.py), or the owner wants real recordings.
- **D-103 (2026-10-22, SY.3, owner list) The referee's microphone and the whistles.** (1) WHISTLES: in the N. F. L. rules every whistle of the game (about 30 call sites, all through GameSounds.play_tackle(8)) goes through a hook that the stadium installs while its game is played (the hook answers None for any other game, so Classic hears foot11): a pea whistle by what happened (the end of a quarter and the final whistle: long, the two-minute warning and a review: double, a time out: short, a penalty: long), else a short or double one seven times in ten and the original's foot11 three times in ten; the pea whistles are generated (D-102), their trill is a 32 Hz amplitude modulation of a 3 kHz tone with its octave; the wait after a whistle stays the original's 2.5 seconds. (2) THE REFEREE'S VOICE: a seventh speaker `referee` outside ROLES (as Ghip, B-S34), saying the flag announcements (there is a flag, the play is dead, the foul and the team), the accept, decline and offset announcements, the time out called, and every sentence of a review (the red flag, going under the hood, reversed, stands, confirmed); with the Broadcast switch off these are the announcer's say4 as before. F11 and F12 ask for his volume and voice after Ghip's; he gets the next free voice of the announcer's engine as Ghip does. (3) THE MICROPHONE: the SAPI engine gives a speaker an effect, `pa` for the referee: his rendered speech is band-limited to about 300 Hz to 4.5 kHz and given two quiet stadium echoes and a short tail (speech/pa_effect.py: audioop at the speed of C, boxes of 4 and 64 samples, no numpy), and the click of the microphone opens his burst and closes it (mic_on and mic_off, generated). The effect reaches only voices the program renders; a slow voice (played directly), NVDA and JAWS speak him plainly. The effect is on only while the Stadium sounds switch is on. NOBODY HAS HEARD IT. Wrong if: the PA sound is unpleasant or hard to understand (the effect is one function with the numbers at its top), or the owner wants the whistles to stay the original's.
- **D-104 (2026-10-22, SY.4, owner list) The player's choice of the receiver (N. F. L. rules, optional).** The owner: "give the ability for the user to choose what receiver to throw to, this would also include if the particular play has a runningback as a receiver option", and later that a choice is never required. FINDING (the code mapping before the build): the outcome model has no target at all (a route, a band and the air yards are drawn), the receiver is drawn at the snap (`state.carrier`, rnd(6), before the model runs) and only named afterwards; the original's sentences name `receivers[carrier - 1]`. DESIGN: the key R in the offense menu of a player whose plays are the catalog's opens a list in the kick-off menu's style (extras/choices.py): the first item is "The play picks the receiver" (so Enter keeps the original's pace and the random pick), then the six receivers by name and position (wide receiver or tight end when the names are real, else "receiver"), then the first two running backs when the play can throw to one (a route of a swing, an angle, a screen or a drag among its routes; any family item or Enter-picked play offers them) and the game is not a Franchise game (its ratings and injuries know six receivers only; recorded limit); Up, Down, Home, End, digits 1 to 9, Enter chooses, Escape leaves the choice as it was, F1 to F4 as everywhere, T calls a time out and leaves the list (tested, rule 0.22). The choice is for the next snap only. AT THE SNAP the engine still draws its receiver (the draws of the game do not move) and `draw_carrier` (hook H17) answers the chosen one when the player's called play is a pass; a back is the target only when the play has a back route (else the drawn receiver, logged). THE MODEL: `Situation.target` ("WR", "TE", "RB", empty unless chosen) filters a pass's routes to those that suit that kind of player (`ROUTE_TARGETS`: a back runs the swing, angle, screen and drag; a tight end the short and middle routes; a wide receiver the rest) and falls back to the play's own routes when none suits; so a choice changes the size of the route draw, and nothing else changes when the player chose nothing (tested: a game with no choice has the same draws and score). The pass sentences name the chosen player (`PlayEngine.receiver` knows codes 7 and 8 for the two backs). Wrong if: a back should be offered in a Franchise game too (needs the ratings and injuries to know backs as targets), or the routes' table is wrong, or the list is too long.
- **D-105 (2026-10-22, SY.5, owner list) The player's choice of the blitzer (N. F. L. rules, optional, random by default).** The owner: "also add the ability if blitzing, what specific player you may want to blitz with", then "you don't have to pick a player to blitz to, a random player can optionally be chosen". FINDING: a blitz is only data (Call.rushers and Call.blitz, no player), and the player named on a sack is `defender1 = rnd(17)` over all defenders, a defensive back included. DESIGN: the key L in the defense menu opens the list of the seventeen defenders (the first item "A player picked by chance", kept by Enter, is the original's draw; then each by name and position when the names are real or the game is a Franchise's: 6 defensive linemen, 5 linebackers, 4 cornerbacks, 2 safeties, else "defender"). The choice is for the next play of the player's defense; it is used only when that call is a blitz (a Call with `blitz`), else ignored and cleared after the play. EFFECT: the chosen player is the one named on a sack (hook H24 `sack_defenders`, used by the one sack sentence that every sack goes through; the second name of a double sack is the one the draw named first), and a linebacker or a defensive back as the blitzer makes the sack likelier (8 and 12 percent of the sack rate, `BLITZER_SACK`; a defensive lineman rushes anyway: 0); nothing else changes, the engine's draws are made as ever. Wrong if: the owner wants a named blitzer to change more than the sack (pressure, coverage), or the percentages are not right.
- **D-106 (2026-10-22, SZ.1, owner's playtest of 0.10.0) The color commentator's name: Ghip by default, 28 researched sports podcasters, or a typed name.** The owner: "The user should be able to change Gip's name or pick from among popular sports podcaster names that claude researches and puts into the game." DESIGN: setting `nx_commentator` (default Ghip); F9, Rules, Commentator (item 12, Reset moves to 13) opens a list (Ghip the default, the 28 names of `extras/commentators.py` alphabetical, the player's own earlier name, "Type a name of your own" with the original's typed prompt), Left and Right step, the name is used from the next game (like every rule). Every default line that said Ghip says `<commentator>` (24 lines; a placeholder of every pool, filled in `say_remark` and in the radio banter's lines), six banter lines lose their he and his ("tell me honestly", "you used to play", "older than the goalposts" are gone: no line may state a fact about a person or assume a pronoun, because a typed name can be anyone), the help says "the color commentator". A remarks.txt of 0.10.0 keeps working: an unchanged default line (the old Ghip wording, `LEGACY_MARKUP` for the six reworded) is read as the default. The names come from published podcast rankings (spec/COMMENTATOR_NAMES.md, SRC-SECONDARY) and are used descriptively. F11/F12 say "the color commentator". Wrong if: the owner does not want real names near the teasing lines (Q-049: ANSWERED 2026-10-22, the owner: "Keep the real podcaster names as they are"), or wants the list as a file the player edits (it is code data; a file is a small step).
- **D-107 (2026-10-22, SZ.2, owner's playtest of 0.10.0) The player's own files for the stadium, referee and whistle sounds are listed, checked, played and put back from the Sounds menu.** The owner: "those are good, ensure the user can customize them by adding custom wav files" (stadium sounds, referee and whistles). The mechanism existed (SY.2, SY.3: same name replaces, a new number adds, rule 0.14) but only CUSTOMIZE.md said so. NOW: F9, Sounds ends with "Stadium and referee sounds" (a list of the 19 sounds in plain words with the file name each takes and whether it is the original, yours or several; Enter plays it at its level in the game, again plays the next of the family; F1 says how) and "Put the stadium sounds back" (one Enter; the files move to "removed sounds", nothing is deleted); the check counts the player's files that cannot be read; a damaged file is skipped and the shipped sound plays (never a silence); wav, ogg, flac and mp3 are read like every other sound of the game, a wav first when two share a number. Wrong if: the player wants to switch one sound off (a silent file does it; `off` is not offered here), or to name any file freely (the names are the contract).
- **D-108 (2026-10-22, SZ.3, owner's playtest of 0.10.0) R and L are documented in F5, F6 and the README keys, pinned by a test for a default game.** The owner: R and L "must be documented in special keys and help". They were since SY.4 and SY.5 (the master list, rule 0.24); a new test (`test_sz3_keys_rl.py`) pins the F5 line, the F6 sentence and the README draft for the DEFAULT settings, and the F5 line of F, A, M, K, P, O and N now says the defense menu has them too. The keys are documented only while the player's books are playbooks (they do nothing with the original's menus).
- **D-109 (2026-10-22, SZ.4, a player's e-mail) The screen reader is found and asked about once; no voice is asked for when it speaks.** The e-mail: "Screen reader is not detected automatically. You are still asked to pick the voice. It should say you are running NVDA or JAWS do you want to use this voice?" FINDING (subagent probe): the default speech output was `sapi` (G-S7: the five voices are part of the game), a running NVDA was never asked, and the first start asked for five SAPI voices that the NVDA user could not hear chosen. NOW: the default is `auto` (NVDA, else JAWS, else SAPI 5); a settings file of the old default (sapi, no `speech_output_chosen`) becomes auto once; when a screen reader runs and the player has not chosen, the program says "You are running NVDA. Do you want the game to speak through it?" in the original's yes or no way (yes first, Y, N, Up, Down, Enter, F1), once, at the first start after the welcome (before the key list and any voice) or at a later start after the welcome; yes keeps automatic and asks for no voice, no uses the Windows voices (the first start then asks the five voices as before); the answer is saved (`speech_output_chosen`) and F9, Speech settings changes it; with no screen reader nothing is asked; F12 and the F9 voices item say "A screen reader speaks the game, so the game's own voices are not used" instead of opening pickers; the color commentator and the referee get no saved voice while a screen reader speaks. Global rule 0.28 (1). Wrong if: a player with a screen reader wants the game's voices by default (then they answer no once).
- **D-110 (2026-10-22, SZ.5, a player's e-mail) More Plays on defense holds every list; the play menus say where the other plays are; "the playbook's choice" is explained.** The e-mail: "Pick more plays on defense. No other plays are listed that I see." and "It says recommended formations but you don't see any recommended play. Is this an oversight? If this is an oversight, fix it. If not, tell the user at least once during game play." FINDING (subagent probe): on defense the six calls were all the menu showed; the other 89 calls were behind Right on a call and nothing said so; More Plays on defense held only the situation defenses and the kick defense; its D description was the offense's. No text says "recommended"; the nearest are "the playbook's choice" (the value of each setting of Formation and Tempo, meaning the called play sets it) and the Franchise's "the staff recommends" (styles). So NOT an oversight: no play is ever recommended before the call; the playbook's pick is drawn at Enter and told after ("the call is Inside Zone"). FIX: More Plays on defense lists all 11 defensive lists, the situation defenses and the kick defense (12 lists); its description is its own (NXB-G09; the situation defenses and the kick defense stay its first two items, then the lists Right opens); a one-sentence hint is spoken after the first item of the offense menu, the defense menu and the settings list, once for each in a program run and in the first three runs only (`nx_menu_hints`), ended for good when the player opens a list with Right or More Plays (NXB-H17 where Right and M are and that Enter lets the playbook pick; NXB-H18 the same for calls; NXB-H19 that each setting starts as the playbook's choice, which means the play sets it, and nothing is recommended); F5, F6 and the README say the defense menu has M, F, A, O and N and that Right opens its lists; hook H25 `menu_hint` (Classic: nothing). No draw, no state of the game (tested with a strict scripted source). NOT built (Q-050): a key that says which play the playbook is most likely to call; over the whole catalog the top play has about 3 percent of the weight, so it would only be meaningful within a family, and Enter still draws. Global rule 0.28 (2) and (4).
- **D-111 (2026-10-22, SZ.6, a player's e-mail) Sentences said twice in a row.** The e-mail: "Sometimes it says onside kick twice or he is on for the field goal twice." FINDING (a subagent's probe over 120 simulated games, 48 played through the menus, and the Classic golden transcript, 1,261 lines, which has none): (1) the computer's onside kick said the declaration (NXO-09) and the line-up (NXO-02) back to back, and ON03 ("the hands team is on the field") repeated the hands team of NXO-03; (2) T called during a field goal's set-up withdraws the kick and the kick called again said "is in for the field goal attempt" and "will hold" again; (3) a field goal made again after an accepted foul on the last play of a half is a new attempt, 16 lines later: left alone (the rule makes the down be played). FIX: `OnsideFlow.kick` says the declaration INSTEAD of the line-up when the computer declares (the player's own kick and a kick made again keep the line-up); ON03 is " a short kick, a bold plan, and every fan in the park knows it. " (`LEGACY_MARKUP` keeps an old file's unchanged line as the default); `PlayEngine.setup_heard/setup_told` skip the field goal's two set-up sentences when the same kick (same quarter, half, down, distance, spot and side) was set up in words and a time out withdrew it (the fair catch kick too); T exists in the N. F. L. rules only, so Classic says every word every time. NOT reproducible with the rigs: a doubled sentence from a speech engine (NVDA's stale-cancel re-ask); the answer to such a report is the problem report file (two identical request lines prove the game said it twice). Test: `test_sz6_no_repeats.py` plays six whole seeded games with the broadcast, penalties, onside kicks, quarterback runs and challenges on and fails on a sentence of more than three words said twice in a row. Global rule 0.28 (3).
- **D-112 (2026-10-22, SZ.7, the owner's third round) The W key: which play the playbook would most likely pick (answers Q-050).** The owner, after hearing the hints: "yes, i want a key. this key must also be documented in game." BUILT: W in the offense and defense menus of the N. F. L. rules with a playbook. On a play type (Line Plunge, Short Pass, Goal Line, Blitz and the rest) it says the play Enter would most likely get and its chance ("Short Pass. The playbook would most likely pick Hitch Routes, about 6 percent of the time."); in a list of plays (opened with Right or listed in More Plays) the plays of that list the playbook most likely calls; on a single play (a quarterback run, a kneel, Field Goal, Punt, a favourite) "X is one play, and Enter calls it"; on Save Game, Time Out, Challenge and the settings what W is for; with the original's items only "W needs a playbook". It uses the weights of Enter's pick (`PlaybookFlow.weights`, the same function `pick` draws from, `PlaybookFlow.likely`) and draws NOTHING (the rig's strict scripted source fails on a draw); it is said in the voice that spoke last, once per press, not written to the speech log, the menu stays on the item; the log line is `key likely`. FINDING that shaped the words: many plays share the greatest weight (the pro style's Line Plunge has three plays at 9.1 percent, End Run four, Screen Pass four at 13.3, Long Pass five at 8.5), so naming the first would be false; W names all two or three tied plays ("about equally often, each about 9 percent of the time"), says "any of 4 plays about equally often, such as X" for four or more, "every time" when one play has all the weight (the defense's Goal Line away from the goal line) and "has no favourite" where the playbook never calls (the kinds of kicks, the kick defense). Words: "most likely" and a chance, never "recommended" (rule 0.28 (4)). The letter W (what would the playbook call) is reserved in the play menus (`play_menus.RESERVED`); free letters were E G H I J Q U V X Z. The hints NXB-H17 and NXB-H18 name W. Test: `test_sz7_likely_key.py` (20,000 draws against the told chance; no draw on every item of every style and every list; the words; F5, F6, README, hints). Classic has no W (its golden transcript is unchanged). Not built by plan: a ranking of the top three plays (Q-052).
- **D-113 (2026-10-22, SZ.7) The keys of the hints are documented in F5, F6 and the README, each on its own.** The owner on item 25 (D-110's hint "Right opens the lists of the play you are on, M opens More Plays, D describes a play, Enter on a play type lets the playbook pick"): "yes, but document these keys in special keys (f5) and in help." FINDING from the real F5 and F6 text: Right and D had their own lines, M was one letter of "F, A, M, K, P, O and N" (and again inside NXH-27), and "Enter on a play type lets the playbook pick the play" was in neither F5 nor F6; the added keys are the last thing F5 says. FIX: master-list rows `enter_pick` ("Enter on a play type") and `more` ("M") of their own, the letters row is now F, A, K, P, O, N, the row `likely` (W); NXH-27 (F5) says Enter on a play type, Right, M and Back in one sentence; F6 and the README keys get one line for each; a test (`test_every_key_a_hint_names_is_a_row_of_the_key_list_and_is_in_f5_and_f6_on_its_own`) reads the hint texts and finds each named key in the list, in F5 and in F6. Global: fixesforgames A92, B-W30; playbook 7.20 (2).
- **D-120 (2026-10-22, SZ.8, a player's e-mail) The team is the Commanders by default.** The e-mail: "They are the Commanders. Unless you want to keep it the Redskins. Change Redskins to Commanders by default." The owner forwarded it. Q-026 (owner-needed since S3.3) is ANSWERED. BUILT: `game/league.py` DEFAULT_TEAM_NAMES T24 = "Commanders" (the original's word stays as `ORIGINAL_T24_NAME` and in STRINGS.md); a teams.txt written by an older version names every team, so the default alone would change nothing for a player who already has the file: `data/customize.py` `migrate_team_names` (called when the customization is made) adds a note to an old file (an old file = the old last note present, the rename note absent), changes `T24 = Redskins` to Commanders once and keeps the player's file as `teams.txt.before-commanders`; a later `T24 = Redskins` is the player's own choice and stays (the note is the mark). New files carry the note. Classic mode says Commanders too (the golden transcript has no team name for T24, tests pass). Tests: `test_commanders.py`. Check: the e-mail's player sees Commanders on the first start of the next version with the old file.
- **D-121 (2026-10-22, SZ.8, a player's e-mail) The Franchise plays the real 2026 calendar.** The e-mail: "It is not using 2026 schedule in franchise mode. You must fix this bug." FINDING: the Franchise used the original's `makes` (16 weeks, random division games and ten random opponents, the other teams meet by chance; research B12 and design 4 had kept it on purpose). The real NFL 2026 regular season is 18 weeks, 17 games and one bye for every team, 272 games. DECISION (the owner: "You must fix this bug"): a franchise plays a real calendar: `assets/data/schedule_2026.txt` (the facts only, read on 2026-10-22 from the public nflverse games table and checked against nfl.com's release story: the opener Patriots at Seahawks on Wednesday 2026-09-09, the Patriots host Miami in week 18; every team has 17 games, one bye in weeks 5 to 14, every division rival twice, once at home), `extras/franchise/fixtures.py` (parse, whole check, relabel, `for_season`), `game/schedule.Calendar`. The season state has `calendar`, `regular_weeks` (16 or 18) and the play-off weeks follow the last regular week (19 to 22); the engine plays the calendar's games in the calendar's order with the original's two score-index draws per game (`_calendar_week`) and a bye week for the player (`bye_week`: the other games are told, nothing else); the original's 16 weeks are untouched (no calendar). The league record is format 12 (`calendar`; a file of format 11 loads as the original's 16 weeks; the offseason news week 16 became 99 because 16 is a regular week now). Every Franchise rule that counted 16 weeks reads the league (`regular_weeks`, `last_prepared_week`, `has_bye`): injuries (a team on its bye draws no game injury), morale, news, the to-do list, workload, staff bounds. A franchise made before keeps its season and plays the calendar from its next season. The tests' rig keeps the original's weeks (`real_calendar=False`), the calendar tests ask for it.
- **D-122 (2026-10-22, SZ.8) Later seasons relabel the 2026 calendar.** The NFL publishes a schedule only when it is released; no future year is known and the rotation of opponents (3-year and 4-year cycles plus last year's standings) is a research task of its own. DEFAULT: season 1 is 2026; a later season plays `schedule_<year>.txt` when a version ships one, else the 2026 calendar with the teams relabeled (each conference's four divisions rotate, the A. F. C. forward and the N. F. C. back, and every fourth season the places turn), which keeps every rule of the calendar (checked for 40 seasons) and gives each team other opponents. The README says so. Q-060 (owner-needed, not blocking): whether to research the NFL's real rotation.
- **D-123 (2026-10-22, SZ.8, a player's e-mail) Free agents can be signed in the regular season.** The e-mail: "You can't sign free agents or do trades. Claude needs to add those abilities." FINDING: free agency was a stage of the offseason only; F9's free agents item said "the market is closed" all season. BUILT: `extras/franchise/signings.py` and `signings_flow.py`: F9, Franchise, free agents opens a menu in the regular season (before the kick-off, never inside a game): sign a free agent (position, player, and when the group has no open place the player of the group to release; one confirmation that says the contract and the dead money) and hear the free agents. The pool is 3 veterans per position group made WITHOUT a draw from a hash of the league's ID, the season, the week and the group (rated 58 to 69, aged 27 to 33, status free, team 0, left 0: no offseason market sees them, stage 6 drops them) plus the players the player released this season (status free, left = the season: they are on the offseason market too). The price is stage 7's asking price; a hurt player cannot be released; the dead money counts against the cap; the cap must hold after both. The roster is put in rating order and the team's strength for the computer's games is brought up to date, as a trade does. Computer teams neither sign nor release in the season (Q-062). The log has `free_agent_signed` and `trade_made` lines (rule 0.15).
- **D-124 (2026-10-22, SZ.8) The trade deadline is told and the trade window is wider.** Trades existed (F9, Franchise, trades; deadline = the kick-off of week 9 of 16) but nothing said where they were or when they closed. NOW: (1) `trades.last_week(league)`: 9 of the original's 16 weeks, 10 of the calendar's 18 (the NFL's 2026 deadline is Tuesday 2026-11-10, the day after week 9, so trades are made up to the kick-off of week 10); (2) trades are open in free agency of the offseason too (stage 8, before the draft begins, `week` 0 in the log), where the offers of the computer teams do not run; (3) the news keeps four sentences (NXX-30 to NXX-33) in its week and tells them with it, and F9's news lists them: week 1 says where trades and free agents are and the deadline, the week before says it is next week, the deadline's week says it is the last, the week after says it has passed; (4) the F5, F6 lines and the closed-trades sentence say the same. Q-063: should trades also open after the draft?
- **D-125 (2026-10-22, SZ.8) How the session worked beside another session.** Another Claude session (SZ.7, the W key) worked in the same folder at the same time; this session stopped its full-suite run by mistake (a process kill by name pattern) and said so at once; the rule since: one pytest run at a time, announced by message; each session takes its own ID ranges (this one D-120 and up, Q-060 and up, A100 and up) and edits shared files with exact-string edits; texts go through the tsv and `work/make_nx_texts.py`.
- **D-126 (2026-10-22, SZ.8) An independent review (a fresh Opus subagent, read-only, with probes) found three bugs and five smaller ones in the calendar, signings and offseason-trade work; all are fixed.** (1) F9 at the START-UP menu: the store is bound to the Game's SeasonState before any season is loaded, so a save there (an F9 trade or a signing; opening the free agents menu saved too) copied the defaults (team 1, week 1, nothing played) into the league: `FranchiseStore.season_current` now says when the state holds the league's season (set by `load_season` and by the engine's and the set-up's writes), `sync` copies nothing before, and opening the menu no longer saves. (2) A signing during the results wait (the week counter is already W+1 but its preparation has not run): a player due back counted as healthy and could be released with his injury row still listed, so the next save failed; free agents now wait for the prepared mark (`NXJ-S19`). (3) In free agency of the offseason the player under the non-exclusive tag could be traded while his offer sheet waited: `trades.problems` refuses him. Smaller: a released player has team 0, so he earns nobody a compensatory pick and `market.resolve` records no move for him; offseason trades are not blocked by the finished season's log (`traded_this_season` counts week 0 only outside the season); the injury report of a bye week names no stand-in; the generated veterans take player IDs on the first look (the docstring said otherwise, now it says so). NOT changed: the staff's par of 8 wins (Q-064).

Practice squads in the Franchise.** `extras/franchise/practice.py` (rules, no draw) and `practice_flow.py` (F9, Franchise, practice squad, the 10th item: hear the squad, sign a squad player to the roster for the minimum, add a free agent of the season, release one). Up to 16 players, at most 6 veterans, salaries 0.2 (young) and 0.4 million (veterans) counted in the cap; formed at the new season (stage 6, before the drop of the unkept players, which now keeps squad players) from the players let go this offseason, each team in a turn that changes each season, its own players first, then topped up with made-up young players (rated 44 to 54, no draw, a hash as the season's free agents); a new franchise and a league opened from format 13 or older get full squads at once; the squads end with the season (stage 1, free agents of the offseason). Elevation: when an injury leaves a group with nobody fit, a squad player of it stands in (a returner's place: a back, a receiver, a cornerback) before a made-up one, at most 2 a team at a time, 3 elevations a player a regular season (counted each week he stands in), no limit in the play-offs; the stand-in draw is still made, so the league's draws stay the same; the weekly injury report tells the player's elevations (NXJ-P18, P19). league.sav format 14. The test rig keeps squads off for the older tests (`practice=False`, like `real_calendar`). The NFL facts are SRC-SECONDARY and marked UNVERIFIED (Q-068); not built: the International Pathway 17th place, other teams signing a squad player to their roster (Q-067), protected players. L.'s 14-team play-offs for a franchise on the real calendar.** `game/playoffs.py` (`real_end_of_regular_season`, `real_seeds`, `real_division_pairings`, `real_championship_pairings`): seeds 1 to 4 the division winners by wins (a tie half a win; equal wins: the division order), 5 to 7 three wild cards by the original's loss order; Q43's place for the player's team is the last wild card's; the wild card round 7 at 2, 6 at 3, 5 at 4 (A. F. C. first), the two seeds 1 have the byes; the division round reseeded (seed 1 hosts the lowest seed left), the better seed hosts the championship; the Super Bowl as before. The format is decided at the end of the regular season (a calendar) and then read from the saved byes (2 teams: the N. F. L.'s; 4: the original's), so a league saved in the play-offs by 0.12.1 finishes its 12-team bracket (`SeasonState.real_playoffs`, `LeagueRecord.real_bracket`, the file check and `engine_consistent`). The seeds are worked out again from the regular season's records at each round. New text NXC-25 (who has the byes), help NXH-76. The NFL's tie-breakers are not modelled (Q-070). F. L. rules, NXJ-Q20). Outside a franchise: the 32 teams, each said with its starter and the quarterbacks behind him (rosters.txt order); Enter on a team lists them, Enter on one makes him the starter from the next game; the choice is the setting `nx_qb_starters` ("T01=Joe Flacco; ..."), by the written name, laid over the loaded names by `extras/quarterbacks.with_starters` (the chosen man takes the team's first quarterback line), so a quick game and a season name and play him (Getqbs' default) and a new franchise gives him its quarterback place; a choice whose player left the team in a new rosters.txt is dropped (logged). In a franchise: every team's starter (the one quarterback place) and its practice squad quarterbacks, with ratings and ages; on the player's own team a squad quarterback becomes the starter by the practice squad's own move (`practice.promote`: he signs for the minimum, the starter is released with his dead money said, one confirmation); the other teams' staffs choose theirs (NXJ-Q18). Not built: a second quarterback place so a starter can be benched without a release (Q-069). Log lines `quarterback_starter`, `quarterbacks_chosen`. Texts NXJ-Q01 to NXJ-Q20, NXH-75, NXH-78.
